---
id: work/alwaad-alhaq
lang: en
source_lang: ar
translators: []
---

## Summary
"Al-Wa'd al-Haqq" (The True Promise) is a Ramadan television programme by Omar Abdelkafy on the Al-Sharq satellite channel, in 44 long episodes in Assabile's recording. It takes the believer from the will and the illness of death through the signs of the Hour, the soul's departure, the grave and barzakh, the resurrection, the reckoning, the Sirat and the Fire to Paradise, and it argues that people should prepare for that journey now. The presenter interviews him in the studio and the sheikh speaks in a mixed Egyptian and standard register. This set holds the episodes written so far; everything in it is the sheikh's speech as it stands in the machine transcript, and what is not traced to a chain or is garbled in the transcript is marked as needing a source.

:::perspectives
- **The speaker's view:** he presents the afterlife material as resting on verses of the Qur'an and sound reports from the Prophet, without personal opinion, and he says himself that some of the barzakh reports are single-narrator reports that other evidence has to strengthen (episode 1).
- **Academic view:** the programme is a popular preaching work in the genre of books on the last things, such as al-Qurtubi's *al-Tadhkira*, which the host mentions; its numbers, its hadith attributions and its anecdotes are the speaker's and are not checked here, which is why they carry source marks.
:::

## Episode 1: the speaker, the programme and the map of the journey
- The first episode opens on the verses that precede the programme, the end of the passage that begins with the idols and their worshippers, where the speaker says the false gods would not have entered the Fire (see [[passage/quran-21-99|al-Anbiya, verse 99]]). The host welcomes viewers to the first episode on the Al-Sharq channel, daily through Ramadan at half past four in the afternoon Gulf time, with a repeat after half past one at night. He presents his guest as a preacher of 35 years who is known for the afterlife and whose work has been printed in three editions. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:01:00}
- The host says the sheikh was nominated by an American magazine among the hundred people who will change the world in the twenty-first century. (needs_source: the nomination is stated by the host alone and the magazine's name is garbled in the transcript) The host adds that the sheikh studied agriculture, took a master's and a doctorate in agricultural sciences, worked at the National Research Centre in Egypt and then in the scientific research academy in Cairo, and has given himself to preaching since 1972. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:05:00}
- The sheikh says he stands close to the subject of the [[concept/akhira|afterlife]] and that the Qur'an speaks of it in about two and a half thousand verses; the figure is repeated in the exchange with the host in slightly different wordings. (needs_source: the exact verse count is not traced to a tally) He says the aim is to answer what we meet after death, since people study every road of this life and neglect what comes after. He says it is not guesswork but rests on the texts of God's Book and the words of the Prophet, and that it is meant to wake the heedless so that Muslims build this world with an eye on the next. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:03:30}
- Asked about himself, he calls himself a servant of the call to Islam, from Upper Egypt, brought up in a balanced, well-known family with a house open to guests, where a village orphan girl was married from their home, and where his mother was seen only with the Qur'an in her hands. He says his father was involved in the affairs of Muslims and the call, and that he is a friend to his own children as to anyone who says that there is no god but God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:12:30}
- He says his doctoral thesis was on a pest of the cotton crop in Egypt. (needs_source: the topic is given in a garbled passage of the transcript) He says he has three sons and two daughters and gives five names as heard: Ahmad, Huda, Nada, Bilal and Umar. (needs_source: names heard once in a passage that loops in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:14:00}
- He says one should honour the people of knowledge and anyone who does good for the people, even when views differ, and gives as examples the respect he saw [[person/muhammad-mutawalli-al-shaarawi|al-Shaarawi]] show to a teacher who had taught him rhetoric at al-Azhar, and the respect he showed to "Sheikh al-Ghazali" (taken here to be [[person/muhammad-al-ghazali-modern|Muhammad al-Ghazali]]) despite differences of outlook. (needs_source: the teacher's name and the details are garbled in the transcript) He adds that God, out of His generosity, has covered our faults before people. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:16:30}
- Asked why he concentrates on the afterlife and on warning, he answers that the Prophet was a bearer of glad tidings and a warner and that the Qur'an combines hope and fear. He gives counts, in the transcript about 213 verses of mercy, Paradise mentioned 157 times and the Fire 158 times, as a sign of that balance. (needs_source: the counts are the speaker's and are garbled in the transcript) He says one must study the Fire so as to know what keeps a person from it, and that he would stop if shown a copy of the Qur'an with nothing in it about the Fire, but the Book has both mercy and punishment. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:21:00}
- He says [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] used to ask [[person/hudhayfa-ibn-al-yaman|Hudhayfa]] by God whether the Messenger had named him among the hypocrites, as a sign of how a man of whom the Prophet had testified to goodness still feared [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:20:00}
- He says every creature has a place in Paradise and a place in the Fire, and that the believers inherit the places of those who are lost. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:20:30}
- He says the Friday sermon on the afterlife is the one topic he prepares in advance, since it is not a matter of opinion but rests on verses and sound texts, and that no one has returned to tell us. He says the reports about barzakh are single-narrator reports that may not found a creed, but other evidence can strengthen them, and he promises documented texts from the Book and the Sunna. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:23:00}
- He says that in every prayer a Muslim recites the Fatiha, with "Master of the Day of Judgement" (see [[passage/quran-1-4|al-Fatiha, verse 4]]) and the request for guidance, so that whoever is guided to the straight path in this world crosses the [[concept/sirat|Sirat]] safely on the day of resurrection (see [[passage/quran-1-6|al-Fatiha, verse 6]]). He says the Muslim recites this at least seventeen times a day. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:22:30}
- Asked what is new this year, he describes a triangle of the text, the reality and the applying of the text to the reality. He says the Muslim reality has changed fast in the last thirty years: where people once went out to pray for rain, technology now lets the world seed artificial rain, and a person can think himself independent of his Creator, so the programme will warn the heedless and help those who remember. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:25:30}
- He speaks of provision for the journey: a traveller takes what suffices for a week or a month, so what has one prepared for the journey that has no end? He imagines the scene on the Day of Judgement when a mother asks her son for a single good deed, and the son answers that he is in the same plight. (needs_source: the exchange between mother and son is given without a source) He recalls the verses on the day when a man flees from his brother and his mother and his father (see [[passage/quran-80-34|Abasa, verse 34]], [[passage/quran-80-35|35]], [[passage/quran-80-36|36]] and [[passage/quran-80-37|37]]) and the excuse "our wealth and our families kept us busy" (see [[passage/quran-48-11|al-Fath, verse 11]]), which he attributes to the Children of Israel. (needs_source: the attribution of that excuse to the Children of Israel is unsupported in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:27:00}
- He says the Qur'an has about 110 verses of acts of worship and over 2,000 verses on building the world and on work, and that the difference lies between building the world to seek the pleasure of its Lord and building it to possess it. (needs_source: both counts are the speaker's and are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:28:30}
- The host recalls that in 1989 at the Asad ibn al-Furat mosque the sheikh could not complete a series on the levels of the Fire. The sheikh says he had planned ten episodes and that after the fourth, seeing the crowds return, he found people in a state of dread, so he stopped and began a series on Paradise of about thirteen episodes. He says that even to speak of the Fire is hard on the soul, with its foods, drinks and clothes. The host says some fifty thousand attended; the sheikh does not confirm the figure. (needs_source: the attendance figure is the host's) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:31:00}
- He says he stops at the Fire only to speak of the fire we have lit for ourselves in this world by distance from God and forgetfulness of the afterlife. Asked about his cheerful manner, he says it comes from the gift of Islam and from following the Prophet, who was never seen but smiling. He tells of a man of sixty who had not entered a mosque for fifty-five years because a grim-faced worshipper once rebuked him and sent him to the last row. (needs_source: the anecdote names no one) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:38:00}
- He says that every person who holds responsibility has a legal, financial and media adviser but none has a religious adviser who tells him whether a matter is lawful, and he advises an employee ordered to do something against the law to ask the people of knowledge about the priorities and balances. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:34:30}
- Asked about the name, he says "al-Wa'd al-Haqq" was chosen because it is from God's Book, that human promises are mostly false, and that the true promise is God's, with Paradise true, the Fire true and the meeting with God true. He says he would ask whoever wishes to oppose the programme to bring his objections. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:43:30}
- He asks the viewer to imagine the angel of death knocking on the door before sunset on the first day of Ramadan and whether one would go with him or ask for time, and says that years have passed over each of us without preparing. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:39:30}
- He gives the plan of the series: the will, starting from the present, since he says a person may be held back in punishment for years for having left no will, and that a will is not only about money. (needs_source: the claim of being held back for lack of a will is unsupported) After this, the illness of death, the minor and then the major signs of the Hour, the departure of the soul, [[concept/al-barzakh|barzakh]], the grave and what it holds, and whether the dead hear us and know our news. He raises the case of a person lost at sea or in a plane crash and asks where such a person's barzakh is. He says Islam is the one religion that answers every question. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:43:00}
- He lists the later stations: the blasts of the horn and their number, the resurrection and the gathering, the scrolls, the setting up of the [[concept/mizan|scales]] and whether there is one scale or many, and each person standing alone to be reckoned. He mentions the sun's nearness, the [[concept/sirat|Sirat]] with its obstacles, the three groups of those who cross it, and the basin of the Prophet. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:45:00}
- He says some people are driven away from the basin and that the Prophet intercedes for them, and then is told he does not know what they did after him, to which he says "away with those who changed". (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:45:30}
- He names the Fire and its seven levels by their names (Jahannam, al-Hutama, al-Laza, al-Saqar, al-Hawiya, al-Sa'ir and the lowest level) and says each fire is a hundred times fiercer than the one above, and that the Fire was kindled a thousand years until it reddened, then a thousand until it whitened, then a thousand until it blackened. (needs_source: the figures and the fire's colours are given without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:46:30}
- He says the last person to leave the Fire will be brought out step by step toward Paradise, and he promises to explain the hadith that scholars call the one narrated with laughter. (needs_source: the report is mentioned by name without a chain) He says every believing monotheist is brought out after receiving his due, that a day there may equal a thousand years of ours, and that repentance is closed at the first major sign of the Hour, when the sun rises from the west. (needs_source: the durations and the closing of repentance are stated without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:49:00}
- He lists the signs of the Hour as the Prophet's own coming, with no messenger, religion or book after him, and then the minor signs: blessing removed from time, the trustworthy treated as a traitor and the traitor trusted, the truthful called a liar and the liar believed. (needs_source: these are quoted by sense without chains) He says nearly all of them have appeared. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:50:30}
- He lists the Paradise topics for later episodes: the maidens, the light, the birds, trees and rivers and the enduring bliss. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:49:30}
- The programme closes with verses on the day the earth is changed (see [[passage/quran-14-48|Ibrahim, verse 48]], [[passage/quran-14-49|49]], [[passage/quran-14-50|50]] and [[passage/quran-14-51|51]]), and the host announces the next episode at half past four. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep01 00:52:00}

## Episode 2: the will, when to write it and how to be fair between children
- The episode opens on the verses about the opening of Gog and Magog and the nearing of the true promise (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|97]]). The host welcomes the sheikh and says today continues what they began yesterday and that they will stop at the will. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:01:30}
- Asked how his academic training in agriculture sits with the religious sciences, he answers that knowledge is taken from its people, that he gained from academic study a method of research and of weighing the opinions of jurists, that his master's was in comparative fiqh, and that the two sides do not conflict. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:03:30}
- He says a person should have a written will in his own hand, signed before witnesses, and cites a saying to the effect that no one should spend a night without his will at his head. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He answers those who think it ill-omened by saying that death is coming in any case. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:05:00}
- He says a supplication for a long life is not reported, what is reported is a supplication for blessing in life, and tells that Umm Habiba prayed for a long life for her husband the Prophet, her father and her brother Mu'awiya, and was told she had asked for fixed terms and written lifespans. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says lifespans are not lengthened but blessed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:06:00}
- He quotes [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] saying that the son of Adam has been demolishing his life since his mother bore him, and likens the road between this world and the next to the road between two cities, where drawing near one means leaving the other. (needs_source: the saying is attributed to al-Hasan without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:07:30}
- He says what makes people avoid the will is a veil on the heart, empty hopes and attachment to the world, and divides [[concept/zuhd|renunciation]] in three: the common people's renunciation of the forbidden, the elite's of the excess of the lawful, and the elite of the elite's of all but God. This is his division. He gives the parable of the fox who dropped a bunch of grapes and thanked God he had not been put in the forbidden, and says [[concept/tawba|repentance]] should be for God, not because a doctor warned a smoker or a gambler lost his money. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:11:30}
- He says Muslims have turned the month of fasting into a month of eating and mentions the rule of a third for food, a third for drink and a third for breath, which he attributes to the Prophet. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He adds that anatomists have put the remaining third for breathing at about a hundred cubic centimetres. (needs_source: the figure attributed to anatomists is unsourced and garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:13:00}
- He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] was brought cold water with dates in it and left it, fearing to be told "you squandered your good things in your worldly life", and says pleasure should be a means to thankfulness, not an end. (needs_source: the incident is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:14:00}
- He says the person making a will should show it to a jurist so that it holds no clause against the law. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:16:30}
- On children's rights he says a son is in his father's charge until puberty and a daughter more so until she marries, that after puberty a son has no claim on his father's maintenance except as a favour, apart from education for the gifted, and that the Prophet worked at thirteen herding sheep to help his uncle Abu Talib. (needs_source: the report is given without a chain and the transcript is garbled) This is his view. He adds that Muslims should learn computing and genetic engineering and support the gifted poor, and that if non-Muslims excel in a field Muslims lag in, all Muslims are sinful. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:21:00}
- On fairness between children he says a child's dutifulness or disobedience is with God, so the dutiful one is not given more and the disobedient one is not deprived, and cites a Companion who gave one son a garden, whose wife objected and asked that the Messenger be made witness, to which the Prophet is said to have replied that he would not bear witness to injustice. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the Companion's name is garbled) He says a person is free with his own money but must not favour one child. He gives a saying of Umar that he seeks refuge in God from wealth his heirs enjoy while he is held to account for it. (needs_source: the saying is attributed to Umar without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:24:30}
- He warns a father against planting trees of grudge among his sons, since favouring breeds envy, and cites [[person/yaqub|Jacob]] and [[person/yusuf|Joseph]] (see [[passage/quran-12-8|Yusuf, verse 8]]) and says one may love one child more but should not show it. He treats the young, the traveller and the sick as special cases, and says a child denied a blessing such as beauty or learning may be compensated within no more than a third of the estate. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:28:30}
- He reports that [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]], asked why he left his sons without money, said that if they were righteous God takes care of the righteous and if not he would not give them what they could use for sin, and that after his death each was married well, and he links this to the verse about those who would fear for weak offspring (see [[passage/quran-4-9|al-Nisa, verse 9]]). (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:30:30}
- He quotes Jesus telling the Children of Israel not to carry tomorrow's worry. (needs_source: the saying is quoted without a chain) He says the inheritance Zechariah asked for cannot have been money because he was a carpenter, but knowledge and piety (see [[passage/quran-19-6|Maryam, verse 6]]), that the "pleasing" one is whom his Lord is pleased with and who is pleased with his Lord, and tells that Gabriel conveyed to [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] that his Lord was pleased with him. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:33:30}
- On the rules of a will he says there is no bequest to an heir, that a bequest stays within a third and goes to good causes such as the poor, a scholarly library or a charitable hospital, and that the rest is divided by the shares. (needs_source: the hadith "no bequest to an heir" is given without a chain) He says a gift depends on circumstances and that giving males and not females, or making sham sale contracts, is trickery against God and not permitted. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:42:00}
- He says a daughter's husband is among the dearest people because he has been trusted with her, and mentions that the Companions said if they had a third daughter they would marry her to Uthman after Ruqayya and Umm Kulthum died. (needs_source: the saying is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:41:30}
- He gives the preface of a will: writing it with a clear mind and in mind of coming before God, and tells that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] dictated his covenant naming Umar. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain) He tells that [[person/al-ghazali-abu-hamid|Abu Hamid al-Ghazali]] asked for water and a white garment on the day of his death, prayed two rak'as and was found dead with a paper of verses under his cheek. (needs_source: the story and the verses are told without a chain) He tells that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz asked his wife to leave because he saw faces in the room that were not human faces. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:45:30}
- He lists the clauses of a will: counsel to the children to fear God and reconcile among themselves, debts stated before burial, who is to wash the body, and no wailing woman, and says Umar struck a hired wailer with his whip. (needs_source: the incident is told without a chain) He cites "he is not of us who slaps the cheeks and tears the garments". (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) He says it is the sunna to make food for the bereaved family. (needs_source: the hadith on food for the family of Ja'far is given without a chain) He tells that Amr ibn al-As asked that people stay at his grave as long as it takes to slaughter a camel and share its meat so that he might be comforted by them. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:50:00}
- He says unjust bequests, such as a father ordering his children to cut off a relative, are not carried out, and that any will that leaves God's Book and the Messenger's words is void. This is his statement. The episode ends with the announcement of the next one. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep02 00:54:00}

## Episode 3: the illness of death, sudden death and visiting the sick
- The episode opens on the verses about the opening of Gog and Magog, and the host welcomes the sheikh and asks to begin with the illness of death (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|97]]). After the break they exchange greetings for the month, and the sheikh says that those who were with them last year may not be with them this year, which is the law of life. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:03:30}
- He says the [[concept/ajal|appointed term]] is nearer to a person than hope, that the worst of people is the one with long hopes, and that God did not leave us aimless but gave a clear path, citing a saying to the effect of "I have left you on a white path, its night like its day". (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:05:00}
- He likens this world to a bridge a traveller crosses and asks about a man who brings contractors to build a house on a bridge, saying such a man is called mad, and that the truly mad is the one far from God's mercy; he attributes to the Prophet the words "do not say mad, say afflicted". (needs_source: the saying is given without a chain) He says the world is a present provision from which the righteous and the wicked eat while the hereafter is a true promise, and that people asking about someone ask about his worldly fortune, not his religion. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:07:30}
- He relates that [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]] said he knocked on the doors to God and found them crowded, and found the nearest door to be the door of lowliness before God. (needs_source: the saying is attributed to Ibn al-Qayyim without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:08:30}
- He gives two similar parables: passengers landing on an island split into three groups, those who returned to their seats, those who loaded themselves with heavy things from the island, and those who forgot themselves and took the island for a home; and workers told to work, split between those who worked, those who relied on the employer's kindness and those who slept until sunset. People likewise are divided between those who see the world as a passage and those who see it as a dwelling. This is his teaching. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:10:30}
- He says sudden death is a relief for the believer and a regret for the wicked. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He explains that the believer does what God commanded as it falls due, that one who dies praying dies with the testimony of faith as his last words, and that the first matter judged on the Day of Resurrection is bloodshed and the first thing asked of a servant is prayer. (needs_source: both reports are given by sense without a chain) He faults those who pray in Ramadan and not in other months. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:13:30}
- He urges viewers, himself among them, to take stock of themselves after every prayer: what did I do from dawn to noon, from noon to the afternoon? He likens reckoning as it comes to washing a dish right after eating, which gets harder if it is left for days. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:14:30}
- He says no one likes illness or death, and mentions a man who said he loved hunger, illness and death, because hunger softens the heart, illness lightens sins and death is the meeting with God. (needs_source: the speaker's name is garbled in the transcript and the saying has no chain) He says whoever loves meeting God, God loves meeting him, and asks those who claim to love God while hating death, comparing them with one who longs to meet an absent son. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense in a garbled passage) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:16:00}
- He says the angel of death is no nearer to the sick than to the healthy, nor to the old than to the young, that death needs no cause, and that place, time and manner were written in the [[concept/qadar|decree]]. He cites a saying to the effect that whoever's death is written in a land will not die in another. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:18:30}
- He tells a story he calls true, from about thirty years earlier, at a level crossing on the sleeper train line from Upper Egypt: a lorry stalled on the rails, people got its driver down, the train struck the lorry and a tyre flew off and fell on the driver's head and killed him at once. He calls this a fixed decree that no prayer changes and distinguishes it from a suspended decree. This is his account. (needs_source: the story has no name or reference and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:20:30}
- He says illness wakes the heedless, that some people are like a cog in a machine carried by their days, and attributes to [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]] a saying that none of you should be a drone by day and a carcass by night. (needs_source: the saying has no chain and its wording is garbled in the transcript) He sums up the lessons of illness as purification from sins, a showing of God's might when the tyrant finds himself weak and asks pardon of those he wronged, and gratitude for health in those who see the sick. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:24:00}
- He lists a Muslim's rights over a Muslim, greeting, answering an invitation, advice, saying a blessing for a sneezer, visiting the sick and following the funeral, and tells a hadith qudsi in which a servant is asked on the Day of Resurrection why he did not visit God when He was ill, and that mercy pours over one who visits the sick while seventy angels pray for him. (needs_source: the reports are given by sense without chains) He adds that a visit should be for God and not so as to be visited in turn. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:26:00}
- He criticises those who see illness with the eye of a servant of the world and cites "wretched is the servant of the dinar, the dirham and the cloak". (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He then turns to fashion shows and the media, saying the viewer holds the remote control and should lower his gaze and that whoever sets an evil precedent bears its burden. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:28:30}
- He forbids gloating over the sick and recites the verses on hastening to forgiveness (see [[passage/quran-3-133|Al Imran, verse 133]], [[passage/quran-3-134|134]] and [[passage/quran-3-135|135]]), says the greater trial is in religion, meaning that the doors of obedience are closed to a person, not in the body, and that a sick person who dies leaves his sins and if he lives God gives him better flesh than his own. (needs_source: the last saying is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:31:00}
- He gives manners of visiting the sick: to give him room in his term, which he attributes by sense to the Prophet, (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) contrasting a Muslim doctor who eases his patient with a Western doctor who lists the surgeons who will operate, and saying it is forbidden to say what drives the patient to despair. He says he read in the history of Islamic endowments that some wealthy men endowed people to stand apart from the sick in hospital telling tales of hope. (needs_source: the endowment has no name or reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:34:30}
- He explains the lawful [[concept/ruqya|ruqya]]: placing the right hand on the sore place (a man for a man, a woman for a woman unless she is a close relative) and reading the Fatiha, and says that Umar entered a sick man's house with the Companions and read it and he recovered, but after Umar died the illness returned and another man read it without effect, which he takes as a sign of the reader's sincerity. (needs_source: the story has no chain and the transcript is garbled) He gives the supplication "O God, Lord of people, remove the harm", a prayer over the dust with saliva, and "in the name of God" three times then seven times "I seek refuge in God's might and power from the evil of what I find and fear". (needs_source: the supplications are given by sense without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:38:30}
- He says a sincere patient complains to God alone, citing [[person/yaqub|Jacob]]'s words "I only complain of my sorrow and grief to God" (see [[passage/quran-12-86|Yusuf, verse 86]]). He tells that when [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]] fell ill and a doctor was brought he smiled and said he had asked for the doctor of heaven, that he visited [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad]] in his illness out of fear for knowledge, and gives two verses he ascribes to him. (needs_source: the two stories and the verses have no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:40:30}
- He says remembering death hastens repentance and brings contentment with little, and counts four pillars of piety: fear of the Majestic, acting by the revelation, contentment with little and preparing for the day of departure. (needs_source: the list is not attributed to anyone in the transcript) He tells that a man asked Ibn Umar whether a poor man would enter paradise half a day before [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]], (needs_source: the report has no chain and the transcript is garbled) and cites "whoever wakes secure in his home, healthy in his body, with his day's food, it is as if the world were gathered for him". (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:43:00}
- The host says the month brings hope to the sick, and the sheikh stresses that ruqya does not replace treatment, telling that Moses asked his Lord whether healing is not from Him, and was told yes, but the doctors eat their provision and comfort souls until His healing or decree comes. (needs_source: the report has no chain) He agrees with a viewer from Abu Dhabi that [[concept/sabr|patience]] is a way of life and that one who dies of a stomach illness is a martyr, God willing. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:45:00}
- Asked why we hate death, he tells that Abu Hazim told Abu Ja'far al-Mansur that we hate it because we built our world and ruined our hereafter, and that Ibrahim ibn Adham led a stranger asking for the inhabited place to the graveyard. (needs_source: both stories have no chain) He says the word is a fearful man's and the deed a secure man's, and that good expectation must come with good action. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:47:00}
- He lists what to fear: [[concept/husn-al-khatima|a bad ending]], one's record of past deeds, which he compares to a criminal record, and creditors taking one's good deeds on the Day of Resurrection so that one is bankrupt. He tells that a Companion the transcript calls Anas grieved at being parted from the Prophet in the hereafter and a verse came down about being with those God blessed (see [[passage/quran-4-69|al-Nisa, verse 69]]), and that the Prophet said he would be found in three places, at the scale, at the reckoning and on the bridge. (needs_source: the reports have no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:49:30}
- The episode ends with the host's announcement that the next episode is at a quarter past four, United Arab Emirates time, and that the programme airs daily to the end of Ramadan with a rebroadcast after midnight. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep03 00:50:00}

## Episode 4: the minor signs of the Hour (1): the Prophet's mission, indecency, cutting kin and bad neighbourliness
- The sheikh begins by saying that the Qur'an's words of quaking and terror alert the heedless, which he likens to an electric shock waking one who has fainted, and announces that today's episode is on the minor and major [[concept/ashrat-al-saa|signs of the Hour]], saying that God left us no excuse and that the Hour has both marks and portents. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:04:30}
- He says the minor signs are a warning for the servant to wake and that the door of repentance is open during them, that they number about a hundred, that their abundance is a mercy from God, and that the sincere believer needs only the first. He says the first of the [[concept/alamat-al-saaa-al-kubra|major signs]] closes repentance, citing the verse on it (see [[passage/quran-6-158|al-An'am, verse 158]]), that they number six, that there is no time gap between the end of the minor signs and the first major one, and that a believer's earlier good deeds keep running for him. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:08:00}
- He counts the Prophet's mission as the first minor sign since there is no prophet after him and no religion after Islam, citing a saying to the effect of "I was sent with the Hour like these two". (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) To those who find the Hour distant after fifteen hundred years he answers that God's reckoning is not ours (see [[passage/quran-22-47|al-Hajj, verse 47]]) and that by it we have not completed a day and a half since "Read" was revealed. This is his reckoning. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:10:30}
- He tells that a man asked the Prophet when the Hour was and was asked what he had prepared for it, which moved him from the important to the more important, and says each person's hour is his death, that when he dies his resurrection has begun, and that Paradise and the Fire are nearer to him than his shoelace. (needs_source: both reports are given by sense without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:12:00}
- He gives a saying that the Hour will not come until indecency and its public talk, cutting of kin and bad neighbourliness appear and the traitor is trusted and the trustworthy is called a traitor. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He says indecency is the act itself and its public talk is speaking of and spreading it, giving fashion shows, actresses and magazines as examples, and suggests picturing oneself sitting beside the Prophet and asking whether he would approve the place. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:16:00}
- On cutting kin he says modern telephones leave no excuse, that blessing is withheld from children, spouses and wealth through it, and that keeping ties is not repaying in kind but joining one who cuts you, and tells that Abu Hurayra complained of relatives who cut him and was told, in effect, "stay as you are". (needs_source: the report has no chain and the transcript is garbled) He says kin include relatives of the first degrees and beyond, then the kin of Islam. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:19:00}
- He tells of a man from Syria who carried his mother on his shoulders on pilgrimage, and says no one has an excuse for leaving his parents and that homes for the elderly are no answer. (needs_source: the story has no chain and the transcript is garbled) This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:21:30}
- On bad neighbourliness he relates that Gabriel kept urging the Prophet about the neighbour until he thought he would be made an heir, and that a man complained of his neighbour and was told to put his belongings on the road so that people cursed the neighbour, who then sought his pardon. (needs_source: both reports have no chain) He tells that a Jew sold his house for a hundred and the neighbourhood of [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] for a hundred, and that a neighbour of Abu Hanifa who drank and sang was jailed, whereupon Abu Hanifa interceded and he was freed and repented. (needs_source: both stories have no chain) He counts prying into neighbours' private affairs as bad neighbourliness. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:25:30}
- He says calling the trustworthy a traitor and trusting the traitor is a sign, gives the example of an employer who bars veiled women from his firm and calls it a betrayal of God and His Messenger, and defines [[concept/al-wala-wal-bara|loyalty and disavowal]] as befriending whoever says there is no god but God and disavowing every enemy of God. This is his definition and opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:28:30}
- He cites the saying that the mountain goats perish and the lowly rise, explaining that people of opinion and thought perish and the dregs remain while the "ruwaybida" speaks, as with one who talks on public affairs without knowledge. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He adds that giving office to the wrong person and preferring those trusted over those skilled is also a sign. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:30:30}
- He says the Prophet put his relatives first in hardships and last in spoils, citing Hamza and a cousin in the wars, and that kin have more claim to kindness from private wealth, not public. He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] put out the state's lamp when a guest came on a private matter, and says a work telephone or car must not be used privately. (needs_source: the reports have no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:34:30}
- He mentions sinking, disfiguring and stoning, and says the Prophet was asked when and said when singing women, instruments and the drinking of wine appear. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He says people call wine "spirits" and that a guest is honoured with what pleases God, not with what pleases him. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:37:30}
- He cites "you will follow the ways of those before you span by span, so that if they entered a lizard's hole you would enter it". (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He says those who claim Europe advanced by abandoning religion repeat a secularist discourse. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:38:30}
- He says he will come to the question about the Mahdi in its place, and the host says the programme takes no calls until the last episode. The sheikh says astrology is a condemned science because only God knows the unseen and that the signs of the Hour are taken from what is authentic from the Prophet, not from scholars' own reasoning. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:42:30}
- He counts further signs as he cites them: greeting only those one knows, the spread of trade, a wife helping her husband in trade, cutting kin, false witness and hiding witness, and the spread of the pen, meaning writing, novels and television serials, and he warns viewers about serials. (needs_source: the signs are given by sense without chains) He tells that the truthful merchant is with the prophets and the truthful, and that in Ali's time people said to deal only with so-and-so. (needs_source: the saying has no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:45:30}
- The episode ends with the promise to continue the minor signs in the next episode with explanation and analysis. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep04 00:46:00}

## Episode 5: the minor signs of the Hour (2): whip-bearers, clothed yet naked women, black dye, the years of deceit and the wish for death
- The episode opens with a recitation of the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and the staring eyes of the disbelievers (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]), followed by a poem on a dying man's washing, shrouding and burial, and then the greeting. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:02:30}
- The sheikh cites a saying of two kinds of people the Prophet had not seen: people with whips like cows' tails who strike people, and women clothed yet naked, swaying and making others sway, whose heads are like the humps of camels, who will not enter Paradise or smell its scent. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He reads the first group as riot police and those who hurt people with electric batons, and calls these two new signs. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:04:00}
- He says the ruler may discipline by discretionary punishment, for acts that carry no fixed penalty, to protect society (see [[concept/tazir-discretionary-punishment|discretionary punishment]]), and that striking people out of arrogance or to show contempt for the law is not that. He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] on a night patrol heard a woman wishing for wine or for a handsome man called Nasr, had the man's hair shaved and then sent him to Syria, and that he limited a soldier's absence from his wife to four months. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:07:30}
- He tells that [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] threatened [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan]] with the whip over a moved boundary marker between two fields and that Abu Bakr answered his father [[person/abu-quhafa|Abu Quhafa]]'s surprise by saying that God raises and lowers people by obedience and disobedience. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He adds that Umar's whip was feared more than the sword of [[person/al-hajjaj-ibn-yusuf|al-Hajjaj]], that [[person/uthman-ibn-talha|Uthman ibn Talha]] once hid the Kaaba key with his mother, and that Satan takes another road when he meets Umar. (needs_source: the reports are told without chains and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:11:30}
- On the second group he explains that the clothed-yet-naked are women in transparent or revealing clothes, that the swaying is an immodest gait, and that "making others sway" means attracting men and leading women to imitate, as a girl copies the actress or singer, and he links high heels to the verse on striking the feet to show hidden adornment. He says what departs from nature departs from our grandmothers' way, and that this is part of following earlier peoples span by span. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:15:00}
- He stresses that he brings only authentic sayings, agreed upon or sound, and addresses women who fall short of what God commanded, noting that schools, doctors and divers each have a uniform. He reads "will not enter Paradise" as a spur to commitment rather than a bar from it, and says the purpose of the signs is that we repent before the major signs and before death. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:17:30}
- He says a community that does not fear God will be humbled, and quotes Umar, in his words, that the Muslims were the lowest of people and God raised them by Islam, so that seeking honour elsewhere brings humiliation, and a letter of Umar to Sa'd at al-Qadisiyya that they win over the enemy by obedience to God and the enemy's disobedience. (needs_source: both reports are told without chains) He adds that God favours no people over another and that nearness to God is what matters. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:20:00}
- He cites a saying about people at the end of time who dye their hair black like the crops of doves and who will not smell Paradise, which he says is in al-Nasa'i with a good, sound chain as he states. He tells that Abu Quhafa, aged about eighty, came to Islam and the Prophet said Abu Bakr should have left the old man for him to visit, then told them to dye his white hair but avoid black, and that dyeing with henna is allowed but not with black. He mentions a saying that no hair turns grey in Islam without God sparing its owner the Fire. (needs_source: the reports are given by sense, the first with a source named but unchecked) This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:22:30}
- He says the Prophet greyed from fear of the Hereafter, and tells that he said Hud and its sisters had made him grey, naming the surahs of the Overthrowing, the Splitting and the Cleaving. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He adds that Ta Ha is not a name of the Prophet and that its meaning is to tread the earth with both feet (see [[passage/quran-20-1|Ta Ha, verse 1]] and [[passage/quran-20-2|verse 2]]). This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:24:30}
- He says the removal of blessing from time is a sign, recalling that Ramadan once seemed near, and that a day will be like an hour and a month like a week. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:26:30}
- He relates the hadith of the good that has smoke in it: after the good comes an evil, then a good with smoke, people guiding by other than the Prophet's guidance, then callers at the gates of Hell, who are from "our skin" and speak "our tongues", and advice to keep to one's house and bite on a tree root if need be. He reads those who speak our tongue with Western thinking as the secularists. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:30:00}
- He tells that Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas, asked why he withdrew from the strife between Ali and Muawiya, said he would fight only with a sword that knew the believer from the disbeliever. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says the truth is found by following scholars of the Qur'an and sunna, that Muslim groups may differ in the methods of call, one stressing conduct, another the sources, another softness of heart, like trees in one orchard, and that none may claim to be the only tree. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:36:00}
- He gives the saying on the years of deceit, when the liar is believed, the truthful is called a liar, the traitor is trusted and the trustworthy is accused, and the lowly "ruwaybida" speaks on public affairs. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He gives soap-opera viewers weeping for a feigned death and a satellite talker asking who forbade dancing as examples, defines "the people" in the verse on those to whom people said that people had gathered (see [[passage/quran-3-173|Al Imran, verse 173]]) as the hypocrites and rumour-mongers, and says three sensible people vouching for a man should be accepted. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:42:00}
- He tells that the people of a funeral praised its dead and the Prophet said Paradise was due, as they were God's witnesses on earth, and that people of truth are more numerous than they seem because no banner has been raised for them, citing a saying that one of them is worth fifty of the Prophet's Companions. (needs_source: both reports are given by sense without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:41:00}
- He tells a story from the Sahihayn, as he says, of a man called Ahban whose sheep a wolf seized, and the wolf told him to fear God and spoke of a prophet behind the palm trees who tells of what was and will be, and Ahban went to the Prophet, who confirmed it was true. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:44:30}
- He cites a saying that the world will not end until a man passes his brother's grave and rolls on it saying he wishes he were in it, not out of religion but from the weight of trials. He tells that the Prophet told his uncle al-Abbas, when he was ill, not to wish for death but to say that God should keep him alive if life is better for him, and says the best are those with a long life and good deeds. (needs_source: the hadith and report are given by sense without chains) He adds that weak faith cannot bear trials, like a weak immune system. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:46:30}
- He tells that the polytheists asked the Prophet for a sign, that the moon split in two so that the Companions said they saw half over Mount Abu Qubays, and that Abu Jahl said he had bewitched them (see [[event/splitting-of-the-moon|the splitting of the moon]] and [[passage/quran-54-1|al-Qamar, verse 1]]). He tells that the Prophet said that if they knew what he knew they would laugh little and weep much, and that the sky groans with no space of four fingers without an angel's brow prostrating. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:50:00}
- The sheikh says the Prophet gave Umar assurance when he feared a danger, that every motion of the Prophet is recorded, and that a later episode will describe him and his death. (needs_source: the report about Umar is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:51:30}
- He closes with three wisdoms of the signs: they confirm the truth of the Prophet's words, they wake the heedless, make the sinner repent and the wrongdoer return what he took, and they prepare a person before the day when he asks to return and is told it is a word he will say. He ends with the example of al-Rabi' ibn Khuthaym and the greeting. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep05 00:53:30}

## Episode 6: five calamities of the Hour, the gold of the Euphrates, the spread of Islam, the years of strife and the lifting of trust and knowledge
- The episode opens with the verses on Gog and Magog and the staring eyes and on the quaking of the Hour (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The sheikh explains to the host that he reads the hadith texts from paper to be sure of every word, as a matter of scholarly trust, and says the programme cites only what is proven from the Prophet. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:03:30}
- He relates the saying addressed to the emigrants about five things, attributing it to Ibn Majah and its grading to al-Albani, and the Prophet sought refuge in God that they should live to see them: indecency spreading and declared openly brings plague and new diseases; cheating in measure and weight brings years of famine, hardship and the ruler's tyranny; withholding the zakat of wealth brings withholding of rain, even from the animals; breaking the covenant of God and His Messenger lets an outside enemy take what they hold; and rulers who do not judge by the Book of God bring strife among themselves. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense and the transcript names two collections, one inconsistent with the other) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:06:00}
- He calls cheating in measure and weight an example of the short measure condemned in the Qur'an (see [[concept/tatfif-short-measure|short measure]] and [[passage/quran-83-1|al-Mutaffifin, verse 1]]) and applies it also to unequal dealings between spouses. He gives as an example a cloth seller in Iraq who, he says, measures a metre as 120 centimetres. (needs_source: the figure is an anecdote with no source) He adds that on the Day of Judgement one will be asked to return even a needle and thread. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:09:30}
- On zakat he says that no poor person goes hungry except through the surfeit of a rich one and that if the rich paid it no poor would remain, tying rain to zakat as both bring growth (see [[concept/zakat|zakat]]). He tells of a drought in the time of Moses in which a sinner was asked to come out, and God sent rain when he repented in secret and told Moses He had shielded him. (needs_source: both the saying and the story are told without chains, the story being among the Israelite reports) He says general mercy comes from individual uprightness. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:13:00}
- He reads the breaking of God's covenant as neglect of prayer, zakat and the law of God, and ties it to the occupation of Muslim lands and loss of control of the land, wealth and decision, which he says is from our own sins and not from God's decree, which the weak believer uses as an excuse. He cites the verses on the town that turned from God and on destroying a town whose rich were commanded and sinned (see [[passage/quran-47-7|Muhammad, verse 7]]). This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:17:30}
- On strife among rulers he says the Qur'an describes the Children of Israel as having harsh strife among themselves, with hearts apart (see [[passage/quran-59-14|al-Hashr, verse 14]]), and says Muslims are hard on one another and weak toward others. (needs_source: a saying about injustice and cowardice is given without a chain) He says Muslim cities differ from Paris and Chicago only in mosques, the call to prayer and some veiled women, and that the question of religion is not the veil alone but what is in the heart confirmed by deed. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:21:00}
- He repeats the whole five-part text and says the Prophet sought refuge from it because corruption makes the committed person a stranger even among his people, and that the remedy is to begin with lowering the gaze in every home and role, with all voices building and none demolishing. He says goodness remains in the community under the ash and the banner of truth has not yet been raised. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:24:30}
- He cites a saying that the Hour will not come until the Euphrates uncovers a treasure or mountain of gold over which people fight, so that ninety-nine of every hundred are killed, each hoping to be the one who survives, and adds that gold may stand for any wealth such as oil. (needs_source: the report is a sense-rendering that he places in Sahih Muslim, unchecked here) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:26:30}
- He cites the saying that this religion will reach as far as night and day reach and that God will leave no house of mud or hair without admitting this religion by honour or humiliation, and says the Companions who heard it did not know the world but believed. He tells that a Muslim family in the Caribbean watches the Sharjah channel and that thirty thousand translated Qur'ans were sent from American libraries before Ramadan. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain and both examples are unsourced anecdotes) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:31:00}
- He tells that the Prophet lived thirteen years in Mecca with eighty-four followers, seventy-two at the pledge of al-Aqaba and, he says, a hundred and fifty at the emigration. (needs_source: the figures are given unsourced and the transcript is garbled) He says the spread of the religion (see [[passage/quran-110-1|al-Nasr, verse 1]]) does not contradict the saying that Islam began as a stranger and will return as one, since a stranger may be a relative who does not fear God. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:32:30}
- He tells of the Prophet in a garden with [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa]] at the door, giving good news of Paradise to [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] and [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] with a trial to strike the last, and calls the killing of Uthman a sign no Arab had seen. (needs_source: the report is told by sense and the transcript is garbled) He adds the saying on Abu Musa's tribe, the Ash'aris, who pooled their food in one cloth and divided it equally. (needs_source: the report is told by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:35:30}
- He cites the saying that the Hour will not come until Muslims fight the Jews, so that the Jew hides behind stone and tree and they call to the Muslim, except the gharqad tree, which is of the Jews' trees, and says ten billion trees were planted after 1967. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the number has no source) This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:36:00}
- He relates the saying on strife like pieces of a dark night in which a man is a believer in the morning and a disbeliever by evening, the sitter being better than the stander and the stander than the walker, with advice to break one's bows and tell a brother to be the better son of Adam.
