(needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) He gives as examples of unbelief those who say the Kaaba is idolatry or deny inheritance rules and the veil, saying that denying what is known of the religion by necessity is unbelief. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:38:30}
- He relates a saying that one who humbles himself before a rich man or king for his wealth loses part of his religion, and tells that Abu Hazim entered on [[person/hisham-ibn-abd-al-malik|Hisham ibn Abd al-Malik]], removed his shoes and greeted him by name, saying God Himself calls His prophets by name and that he removes his shoes five times daily before God. (needs_source: the saying and the story are told without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:40:30}
- He says the Prophet feared for the community not poverty but that the world would be opened and they would compete as earlier peoples had, and that the believers had been tried by hunger in the trench and the ravine. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) He argues that the rich keep wealth circulating among themselves and that the stock market is closed to the poor. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:44:00}
- He gives the saying that trust is taken from hearts, that a man will be called clever and strong though he has not a mustard seed of faith, and that in the Prophet's time he could pledge allegiance to anyone because a man's faith, or his ruler if he were Jewish or Christian, would restore his due, while later he would pledge only to named individuals, and says trust is lifted by people's own conduct, leaving a shameless, hated breed. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says a man who speaks of faith but is without it in deed commits betrayal of trust. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:49:00}
- He cites the saying that among the signs of the Hour are knowledge being lifted, ignorance spreading, adultery spreading, wine being drunk and men dying so that fifty women have one man to care for them. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He says knowledge being lifted means the death of the trusted scholars until people take ignorant leaders who give opinions without knowledge, and that true knowledge is fear of God. He mentions a statistic from an unnamed country, 59 per cent women and 41 per cent men, and reads the men's departure as including moral worth, not only war. (needs_source: the statistic is unsourced and its country unnamed) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:52:30}
- He describes a sifted people left with the dregs, whose covenants and trusts are mixed, and tells that the Prophet laced his fingers to show them, and when asked the way out said to take what they know and leave what they reject, and to attend to their own affairs and leave the public's. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain) He defers further signs to the next episode, which will answer the question of why the signs have not made people take heed. This is his statement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep06 00:57:30}

## Episode 7: the Gabriel hadith, inverted values, the six traits, Adi ibn Hatim's three promises and adorned mosques
- The episode opens with the verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and a verse of poetry on the passing days, then the greeting. The sheikh says the signs are not all evil, that they confirm the Prophet's prophecy and that nearly all have appeared, so the aim is preparation, not fear, and says he checked about four hundred and fifty references in the 1970s and 1980s. (needs_source: the count of references is his own claim) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:03:30}
- He recounts the hadith of [[person/jibril|Gabriel]] who came as a man in white clothes with black hair and asked about Islam, faith and excellence, and then the Hour, to which the Prophet answered that the one asked knows no more than the asker. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain, though he reports it from Umar) He gives as its two signs that the slave woman bears her mistress and that barefoot, naked herdsmen compete in building tall buildings. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:06:00}
- He reads the first sign as an inversion of values rather than the matter of slave women, giving as an example that the ignorant sleep on silk and the learned on dust. He says the second sign is not evil in itself if the builder thanks God, and that the Hour's signs are markers, not condemnations. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:09:30}
- He cites a saying to seek death at six traits: the rule of fools, the sale of judgments, disregard for bloodshed, a multitude of police, cutting of kin, and people who take the Qur'an as flutes to sing with. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He defines the fool as one who does not know what he does and says Qur'an reciters who sing it and do not follow the rules of recitation illustrate the last (see [[concept/tajwid|tajwid]]). This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:24:30}
- On the sale of rule he says he knows of no report of selling office in the early generations and that it marks decline, citing the queen of Sheba's words that kings who enter a town ruin it (see [[passage/quran-27-34|al-Naml, verse 34]]). He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] argued for [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]'s succession because the Prophet had entrusted him with the faith during his last illness. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:17:00}
- On disregard for bloodshed he gives the daily tally of killings in news bulletins and cites the story of Cain and Abel (see [[passage/quran-5-30|al-Ma'ida, verse 30]]) and the verse that whoever kills a soul has killed all people (see [[passage/quran-5-32|verse 32]]). He widens the notion to "moral killing", through harsh treatment of a spouse or an unjust official decision, and says an error in pardon is better than an error in punishment. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:21:30}
- He tells that the sister of [[person/adi-ibn-hatim|Adi ibn Hatim]], [[person/safana-bint-hatim|Safana]], was captured and the Prophet freed her out of respect for her father's noble character, and that Adi came to Islam and the Prophet offered him his cloak to sit on. (needs_source: the story is told without a chain) He adds that [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]] advised Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz that men of religion do not need the ruler and men of the world he does not need, so he should rely on men of honour. (needs_source: the report has no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:27:30}
- He relates the Prophet's three promises to Adi: that the woman travelling in a howdah would go from al-Hira to the Kaaba fearing none but God, that the treasures of the Persian king would be taken, and that wealth would overflow until none accepted it. He says Adi saw the first two and that the third came with Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz, when his treasurer said that his justice had made them rich. (needs_source: the report is told by sense without a chain and the last part is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:38:30}
- He tells of Rib'i ibn Amir before the Persian king after al-Qadisiyya, in patched clothes with his sword, saying God sent them to take people from the worship of men to the worship of God, with the choice of Islam, tribute or the sword (see [[event/battle-of-al-qadisiyya|the battle of al-Qadisiyya]]). He tells that [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid]] denied having a heavenly sword and that Hormuzan, who fled with the keys of the Persian treasury, was caught when his horse slipped on spilled honey, and that al-Bara ibn Azib swore on God for victory. (needs_source: all these stories are told without chains and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:37:00}
- He says one sign is that fornication is done on the road and the best of them says only that they might have moved aside, and that the men of the Prophet's time would not have tolerated it, citing Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas's jealousy and a poem on Ali's jealousy over a toothstick. He contrasts praiseworthy jealousy over God's sanctities with its absence and says screens bring such sights into homes. (needs_source: the saying and stories are given by sense without chains) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:43:00}
- He cites a saying that ruin follows when mosques are adorned and copies of the Qur'an are gilded, and says Umar told builders not to paint red or yellow, only to shelter people. He adds that when mosques were dwarfed giants came out of them, and when they became giants dwarfs came out, and that the Qur'an is a will to be carried out, not an ornament. (needs_source: the saying and the report are given by sense without chains) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:48:00}
- He cites a saying that the Hour will not come until inheritance is not divided and none rejoices in booty, and that a boy will be given a hundred dinars and be displeased, which Umar would have found unbelievable. He gives examples of heirs who delay dividing an estate for ten years and says delay brings quarrels. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:51:30}
- He cites a saying that people will show knowledge and waste deeds, love with their tongues and hate in their hearts, and cut their kin, and says speaking of rulings while taking interest from a bank is an example. He also explains a saying on a son becoming a rage, winter a heat, the generous perishing and the sub-par rising, which he reads in terms of sons' lost support and climate change. (needs_source: both sayings are given by sense without chains and the second is garbled) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep07 00:56:00}

## Episode 8: the close of the minor signs, rulers who are the worst of people, the splitting of the community, much divorce and sudden death, and the sheikh's method of research
- The episode opens with the verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|Al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and the verse on the discovery of the Companions of the Cave so that people may know God's promise is true (see [[passage/quran-18-21|Al-Kahf, verse 21]]), then the greeting. He says the episode is nearing the end of the minor signs and that the messages and interviews he receives leave a good mark in the blessed month. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:02:00}
- He cites a saying that when rulers are the best of people and the wealthy are generous, the surface of the earth is better than its belly, and when rulers are the worst, affairs are in the hands of women and wealth is with the miserly, the belly of the earth is better than its surface. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He reads this as officials of institutions, cities and states being the worst of people, leadership passing to women alone and not to men, and the wealthy being miserly and bidding others be miserly. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:03:30}
- He cites a report that the Hour will not come while someone on earth says God, God, and says the true Muslim is rare, that people are then busy with the world, wealth and trials, and that some Muslims are shy to show their Islam. He recalls the saying that the strong believer is better and dearer to God than the weak believer. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain) He tells that [[person/aisha|Aisha]] saw youths walking sluggishly and asked who they were and was told they were the devout, and that [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] struck a youth walking in a languid way and told him to walk briskly. (needs_source: both stories lack chains and the transcript is garbled) He adds that wealth and family occupy people (see [[passage/quran-48-11|Al-Fath, verse 11]]). This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:06:30}
- He cites a saying that the Hour will not come until the land of the Arabs returns to meadows and rivers, and says the word returns shows it was once so and that geology has shown the peninsula was green before it turned to desert. He says greenery has begun in recent years to cover parts of the desert and that the Arabs have become able to make artificial rain. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain, and the claims on geology, greening and rain are his alone) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:07:30}
- He mentions the saying that the community will split into seventy-three sects, all in the Fire but one, and that the saved are those on what the Prophet and his Companions were on (see [[concept/al-iftiraq-al-ummah|the division of the community]]). (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain, and the transcript of the numbers for the Jews and Christians is garbled) He says whim blinds and deafens, and that a person of whim does not accept the truth. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:08:30}
- He addresses members of da'wa groups, saying the groups present today are not sects but each is like a tree in a large garden, that differing methods of calling are a good phenomenon, and that what is not good is that they criticise and do not love one another, and that each person should renew his intention so that his whim follows what the Prophet brought and not the group. He says the groups may be forerunners of the splitting if whim leads a group's leader to turn it into a sect, that sects appeared from the time of the Khawarij and do not appear all at once, and he points readers to the books on sects and creeds (see [[person/al-shahrastani|al-Shahrastani]]). This is his opinion, and scholars differ on how the sects are identified. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:12:30}
- He tells that [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]], asked after writing his book of law whether it was the truth with no falsehood, replied that it might be falsehood with no truth, that he said of his rulings that this is my view, I think it right but it may be wrong, and the view of others is wrong but may be right, and that he said take the sound hadith and leave my school. He adds that scholars said do not take the word of Abu Hanifa or [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik]] but what agrees with the Book and the Sunna. (needs_source: the sayings lack chains and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:12:00}
- He lists among the signs that people will push one another forward to lead prayer and find no one to lead them, and gives the example of university students who, asked what to do if one prostrates by mistake instead of bowing, cannot answer, so how can they lead (see [[concept/imama|the imamate]]). (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:13:30}
- He mentions the selling of religion for the world, and cites those who said that sale is like usury (see [[passage/quran-2-275|Al-Baqara, verse 275]] and [[concept/riba|usury]]), saying the matter involves naming things by other than their names. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:14:00}
- He counts much divorce among the signs, saying a case of divorce used to shake a whole village, that Umar regulated the matter with his ruling on triple divorce (see [[concept/talaq|divorce]]), and that the rate in some Muslim countries exceeds thirty-six to forty percent and reached fifty percent in some. He puts this down to each spouse not understanding the other's rights. (needs_source: the statistics are his alone, and Umar's ruling on triple divorce is disputed among scholars) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:15:00}
- He counts sudden death among the signs, saying it was rare in recent times and is now frequent, while recalling that the appointed term is fixed (see [[passage/quran-7-34|Al-A'raf, verse 34]]). He says sudden death is a relief for the believer and a regret for the sinner. (needs_source: the comparison is his alone, and the last saying is given by sense without a chain) This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:17:00}
- He counts much slander among the signs and reads it as slander of chaste women (see [[passage/quran-24-4|An-Nur, verse 4]]), saying people make light of the word. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:17:30}
- He lists tall pulpits among the signs, telling that the Prophet's pulpit was at first a palm trunk, that a pulpit of three steps was then made, and that the trunk yearned for the Prophet until its moan was heard by those in the mosque (see [[concept/minbar|the pulpit]]). (needs_source: the story is given by sense without a chain) He says a high pulpit separates the preacher from his listeners' faces so he cannot see his effect on them. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:19:00}
- He counts ruined hearts, through rancour, spite and envy, among the signs, and the suspension of the fixed penalties (see [[concept/hudud-penalties|the fixed penalties]]), saying God set only the cutting of the hand for the thief, and telling that [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Mas'ud]], when his money was stolen, prayed that God bless the thief if he needed it and make it his last sin if he did not. (needs_source: the story lacks a chain) This is his opinion on the penalties. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:21:00}
- He cites the saying that curses the woman who joins hair, the one who has it joined, those who tattoo and are tattooed, those who pluck the eyebrows and those who widen the gaps in their teeth, and gives it as the answer to a woman whose hair fell out through a skin illness and who wished to add hair, linking it to changing God's creation (see [[passage/quran-4-119|An-Nisa, verse 119]]). (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He lists the imitation of women by men and of men by women among the signs (see [[concept/tashabbuh-imitation|imitation]] and [[concept/wasl-al-shaar-fiqh|joining hair]]), links it to leaving human nature citing the people of [[person/lut|Lot]], and says the religion addresses a person whole, not taking part and leaving part. He adds that taking up a trade God does not approve is not allowed. This is his opinion, and scholars differ on hair joining and its details. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:27:00}
- He mentions a person's testimony without being asked to testify and false testimony, saying the false witness is common in homes, and gives the example of a wife who in anger goes to her family, with the husband's mother saying my son is an angel and the wife's mother saying my daughter makes no mistakes. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:29:30}
- He tells the account of the Prophet's anger when [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]] wished to marry the daughter of [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] while [[person/fatima|Fatima]] was his wife, saying the daughter of God's messenger and the daughter of God's enemy do not come together under one roof. The sheikh understands that the Prophet did not forbid taking another wife but feared the anger of Fatima, because the daughter of Abu Jahl had harmed him before she became Muslim, and that Ali would be ruined if Fatima were angry. (needs_source: the hadith and the explanation lack chains and the transcript is garbled) This is his reading, and the details of the hadith are disputed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:32:00}
- He lists among the signs that the believer in the tribe will be humbler than a lamb, and cites verses on a wolf and a lamb to show the strong wronging the weak. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain, and the verses and their attribution are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:34:00}
- He cites a saying that a Companion who left a tenth of what he was commanded would perish, and that one who does a tenth of what he is commanded in the last days will be saved. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain) He then distinguishes denying the prayer from being lax about it, saying one who denies it has disbelieved and one who is lax is a disobedient believer. This is his opinion, and scholars differ on the ruling on one who abandons prayer (see [[concept/abandoning-prayer-takfir|abandoning prayer]]). He says the door of repentance is still open. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:37:00}
- He says the signs named in the references exceed ninety and may reach two hundred, that he made a covenant with God to bring only a sound hadith, and that he thinks they have nearly all appeared, so only repentance remains. (needs_source: the number of signs and the authentication of the hadiths are his alone) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:42:00}
- He describes his method in researching a ruling: he gathers the verses on the question, learns the time and occasion of their revelation and then the language, then consults about fifteen commentaries (see [[person/al-zamakhshari|al-Zamakhshari]], [[person/al-qurtubi|al-Qurtubi]] and [[person/ibn-kathir|Ibn Kathir]]), then weighs the hadith in Bukhari and elsewhere and sets aside the weak, taking into account the setting of the hadith and the person of the Companion narrating, so that on household matters he takes Aisha's account. This is his account of his method. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:41:00}
- Near the end the headings of the minor signs, more than ninety, are shown on screen, among them the spread of obscenity, the cutting of kin, bad neighbourliness, the trusting of the traitor, the believing of the liar, entrusting affairs to the unfit, the withholding of zakat, the Euphrates uncovering a treasure of gold, the killing of Uthman ibn Affan, the spread of trials, the lifting of knowledge, the spread of adultery and wine, the rise of singing girls and instruments, the opening of the treasures of Chosroes, the competing in tall buildings, the adorning of mosques, the gilding of Qur'an copies, much divorce, sudden death and the suspension of the fixed penalties (see [[concept/ashrat-al-saa|the signs of the Hour]]). (needs_source: the list of headings has no chains in the episode) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:46:30}
- He closes by saying he trembles at this reality, that we must study the signs and ask how to support God's religion, that tomorrow's episode begins the major signs, and that the door of repentance is open. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep08 00:48:00}

## Episode 9: the major signs of the Hour: the sun rising in the west, the beast, the smoke, the Dajjal, the descent of Jesus and Gog and Magog
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and lines of verse on this world and the home after death, then the greeting. The sheikh says he wants the Muslim to live with the signs of the Hour and God's promise as the Book and the Sunna present them, to prepare to meet God and to build up this world, and says talk of the afterlife fills more than three thousand six hundred verses, about half the Qur'an. (needs_source: the verse count and the claim of half the Qur'an are his own and the two figures are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:03:00}
- He illustrates how small this world is with a finger put in the sea, and with a drop from Paradise or from the pus of the Fire that would transform or spoil the world if it fell on it. (needs_source: both sayings are given by sense without chains and the transcript is garbled) He says the aim of describing these terrors is not to frighten but to wake the will, not to stop working and sit in the mosque waiting for the Hour, and that the major signs are six, which he sets out in outline before stopping at each. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:05:30}
- The first sign for him is the sun rising from the west. He describes people waking to find no sunrise for two or three days, noticed only by those who pray at night, and then a caller announcing that it has risen in the west. He cites a saying that the first signs are the coming out of the beast or the sun rising from the west, and whichever comes first the other follows, and he leans to the sun first. He says the first day lasts a year, that the Companions asked how they should pray then and the Prophet said to estimate its measure, and that the door of repentance is shut when it appears. (needs_source: the saying and the day lasting a year are given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He rests on the verse that faith does not benefit a soul that had not believed before (see [[passage/quran-6-158|al-An'am, verse 158]] and [[concept/tulu-al-shams-min-al-maghrib|the sun rising from the west]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:09:30}
- He adds that a man goes out with gold to give as alms and finds no one to take it, that the believer continues in his faith and works and the one in disobedience continues in that. He gives the days that follow in an order like the one reported for the days of the Dajjal, the second like a month and the third like a week. (needs_source: the detail is given by sense without a chain and may be confused with the hadith of the Dajjal that closes the episode) He says he needs no scientific explanation of the event because the Prophet's word is truest, and declines to enter astronomical theories or the changing of the earth (see [[passage/quran-14-48|Ibrahim, verse 48]]), since his method is the Book and the Sunna. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:37:00}
- He explains that people may be shaken during that period, that night is needed as rest and calm (see [[passage/quran-28-71|al-Qasas, verse 71]] and [[passage/quran-28-72|verse 72]]), and that people today have turned night into day with electric light. He cites a saying that the Hour will not come until the sun rises in the west, that two men with a cloth spread out will not complete the sale, that a man with the milk of his she-camel will not drink it, that a man mending his trough will not water from it, and that the morsel raised to the mouth will not be eaten. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says he cannot confirm any report fixing the day of the week. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:35:30}
- The second sign for him is the beast (see [[passage/quran-27-82|al-Naml, verse 82]] and [[concept/dabbat-al-ard|the beast of the earth]]). He says scholars have said much in describing it and that he stays with what the Qur'an says, then tells that it carries the staff of Moses and the ring of Solomon and enters from the gate of the Sanctuary; it touches the believer's face with the staff so that it shines and he is called believer, and it seals the unbeliever's face with the ring so that dust covers it and he is called unbeliever; he says it is untrue that the ring lies in the belly of a fish. (needs_source: the story is told by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled, with an unclear attribution to a prophet) He says people draw away from it in fear while a group of believers stand firm. He adds that it marks people on their snouts, so that a man who buys a camel asks from whom and is told from one of the sealed, and that when someone who did not pray goes to pray the beast says to him now you pray. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says it is the Jassasa (see [[concept/al-jassasa|the Jassasa]] and [[person/tamim-al-dari|Tamim al-Dari]]) and promises to return to Tamim's hadith. (needs_source: identifying the beast with the Jassasa is a disputed matter and is his alone) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:40:30}
- The third sign is the smoke (see [[passage/quran-44-10|al-Dukhan, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-44-11|verse 11]] and [[concept/al-dukhan|the smoke]]). He says it covers the people, like a cold for the believer and like fire for the unbeliever and hypocrite, that they pray for the punishment to be lifted and prayer no longer helps, and adds that Iblis then wants to prostrate and the beast treads on him. (needs_source: the tale of Iblis is told in the form of "this is what is said" without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says he does not know the interval between the beast and the smoke, and that the signs follow one another like beads of a broken string. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) He notes that some people die in these days in the state they were in. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:42:30}
- The fourth sign is the false Messiah, the Dajjal. He says every prophet warned his people of him (see [[concept/dajjal|the Dajjal]]) and that the Prophet spoke of him so intensely that the Companions thought him in the date grove, and that it affected Umar deeply. He describes him as a man with the right eye like a floating grape, with kafir written between his eyes, which every believer reads, literate or not. He says whoever meets him should recite the first four verses of al-Kahf (see [[passage/quran-18-1|al-Kahf, verse 1]]) and advises reciting them every night before sleep. (needs_source: the descriptions and the saying are given by sense without chains, and the number of verses is as the transcript has it) He cites the hadith of al-Nawwas ibn Sam'an in Muslim (see [[person/al-nawwas-ibn-siman|al-Nawwas ibn Sam'an]]), noting that the Prophet lowered and raised his voice until they thought him in the date grove and said that something other than the Dajjal he feared more for them, which the sheikh explains as the Dajjal reaping the harvest of the devils among humans and jinn. (needs_source: the hadith and its explanation are given by sense without a chain) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:53:30}
- He says the Dajjal commands the sky and it rains and the earth and it grows, claims prophethood and then lordship, and that the Prophet made clear that our Lord is not one-eyed and will not be seen in this world. He says most of his followers are Jews, hypocrites and women, that a man ties up his wife, mother and daughter for fear they will follow him, and that he has seventy thousand Jews with him as he reports from the Prophet. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain; the description of followers by groups is a report he relays and not the museum's statement; the transcript is garbled) He says the Dajjal has a Paradise and a Fire, his Paradise being a fire and his Fire a paradise, and that the believers' food that day is glorification, praise, the declaration of oneness and magnification, which God puts into them as breath. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) He adds that Medina casts out its impurity so the hypocrites go out to him, that angels bar him from entering Medina, and that none is given power over him until relief comes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:52:00}
- He cites the Prophet's words to Umar that if he comes out while the Prophet is among them he will contend with him, and if after, each man contends for himself and God is the deputy over every Muslim, and says the Prophet used to cry out "servants of God, stand firm" when describing his coming out between Syria and Iraq. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain and the transcript repeats in a garbled loop) He says he stays forty days, a day like a year, a day like a month, a day like a week and the rest like ours, travelling the land at the speed of rain driven by wind, and that the Companions asked how they should pray on the day like a year and were told to estimate it. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain) He says those who follow him are those whose certainty is weak, giving the example of a man in a forbidden job who will not leave it for lack of certainty. He expects civilisation itself to perish before his appearance, citing the verse of the earth taking its adornment (see [[passage/quran-10-24|Yunus, verse 24]]), and, asked about his appearing in the age of flying saucers, answered that civilisation itself may vanish. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:56:00}
- The fifth sign is the descent of Jesus son of Mary (see [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]]) at the eastern minaret of Damascus (see [[place/damascus|Damascus]]) in two dyed garments, with sweat falling from his brow like pearls. He says he enters among the Muslims at dawn prayer, their imam steps back and Jesus pushes him forward and tells him to lead the prayer. He says the Dajjal melts like salt at the smell of him and is killed at the gate of Lydda in Palestine, then that Jesus kills the pig, breaks the cross, rules with justice for forty years and prays as a follower. (needs_source: the story is told by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:26:30}
- The sixth sign is Gog and Magog (see [[concept/yajuj-majuj|Gog and Magog]]). He says God tells Jesus to take the believers to the Tur (see [[place/jabal-al-tur|Mount Tur]]), that the first of them passes the lake of Tiberias and drinks its water and the last says there was water here (see [[place/buhayrat-tabariyya|Lake Tiberias]]). He says Jesus prays against them and worms strike their necks and they die, birds carry their bodies to the sea, heavy rain washes the earth and its blessing grows until a group eats from one pomegranate, then a gentle wind takes the souls of the believers and the worst of people remain, mating in the open as donkeys do, and the Hour comes upon them. (needs_source: the saying is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says they are most likely human, and closes by quoting the opening verses again. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:57:30}
- He remarks that the Prophet in his worship saw these scenes before him, and that when some tell him there is no need for this talk because it frightens, he answers that these are realities we are charged to know and prepare for. He closes saying the matter continues in the next episode. This is his opinion. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep09 00:57:00}

## Episode 10: the Tamim al-Dari hadith and the Jassasa, the Dajjal's chain, Lake Tiberias and Ibn Sayyad
- The episode opens with the verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and the greeting. The host recalls the six major signs from the last episode (the sun rising in the west, the beast, the smoke, the Dajjal, the descent of Jesus, Gog and Magog) and passes on viewers' complaint that the series frightens them and sets them waiting for the Hour. The sheikh answers that the promise is more encouragement than warning, that the world is near its end and that the Muslim should build it with the logic of the afterlife, and says he only relays what the Companions heard from the Prophet. This is his reply. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:04:30}
- He tells the hadith of Fatima bint Qays (see [[person/fatima-bint-qays|Fatima bint Qays]]): the Prophet's crier called the prayer, she went to the mosque and prayed in the first rows of the women behind the men, and the Prophet came to the minbar smiling. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense and he relays it from the narrator without a chain) He draws from it that women attended the Prophet's mosque and lessons with the men in one space and says he cannot see how anyone would keep them from it. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:17:00}
- The Prophet then said that Tamim al-Dari (see [[person/tamim-al-dari|Tamim al-Dari]]) had been a Christian, came, gave allegiance and accepted Islam, and told him something that agreed with what he used to tell them about the Dajjal (see [[concept/dajjal|the Dajjal]]). The sheikh notes the Prophet said he had gathered them neither for hope nor for fear, but because of Tamim, and that the Prophet said that with a smile because Tamim's account confirmed what had been revealed to him. (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He also says he counted a number of occasions on which the Prophet laughed or wept in the two Sahih collections, but the figure is garbled in the transcript. (needs_source: the count of occasions is his own and the number is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:21:00}
- In Tamim's account he sailed with thirty men of the tribes of Lakhm and Judham, the waves played with them for a month, and they came to an island at sunset where a very hairy beast met them, so hairy they could not tell its front from its back, that said it was the Jassasa (see [[concept/al-jassasa|the Jassasa]]) and told them to go to the man in the monastery who was eager to hear their news. The sheikh explains the name as meaning one that roams through the whole land. (needs_source: the explanation of the name is his own and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:54:30}
- They found there the largest man they had ever seen, bound in iron, his hands tied to his neck and his knees to his shoulders, and chained to the monastery wall. He asked who they were and they told him they were Arabs. He asked about the date palms of Baysan, whether they still bore fruit, and was told they did, and said they would soon cease. He asked about Lake Tiberias (see [[place/tabariyya|Tiberias]]), whether it still had water, and said it would soon go. He asked about the spring of Zughar and whether its people still watered their crops from it, then about the prophet of the unlettered (see [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]), and whether the Arabs had fought him and with what result. (needs_source: the story is told by sense without a chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:29:00}
- On learning the Arabs had followed him and that he had prevailed, the man said it was better for them to obey, and added that he was the Messiah, that he was about to be given leave to go out, and that he would reach every town in forty nights except Mecca and Taybah, where an angel with a sword would bar him and angels guard its roads. The sheikh sees Taybah as the Prophet's name for Medina, praises its soil and says it is the place of the Prophet's tread. This is his reading. (needs_source: the details are given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:36:00}
- On the places the man named, the sheikh says the false messiah gives true facts first so listeners trust him, and that the Arabs who heard knew the palms of Baysan, the lake and the spring from their travels. He treats their change as signs of the lesser group and says they pointed to the Dajjal's approach. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:39:00}
- He recalls a newspaper photograph from 1975 of a girl of about ten wading in Lake Tiberias with the water below her knees, captioned as the water having dried up or nearly, which he took as the sign coming true, and says a supplier of his had kept the cutting for some thirty years. (needs_source: the photograph and its date are his own recollection and cannot be checked) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:16:30}
- He says the Qur'an and the Prophet do not dwell on the sea, the island or the place names (compare the people of the Cave), and declines books that name the sea as the Mediterranean, the Atlantic or the Bermuda Triangle and any theory that the beast was a devil or that the Dajjal is tied to flying saucers. His rule is to stop where the Book and the Sunna stop, and he repeats "God said, His Messenger said, then I do not know". This is his method. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:43:00}
- On the Dajjal's nature he says he is in his view a human being God has given a long life, that he has no father or mother known to him, and that he does not know where he now lives. He holds that the matter belongs to the unseen things known only by report, that no prophet or other person has seen him, and that this is meant to be a surprise to all but the believer. He adds that the scent of Jesus will kill every unbeliever who meets it. This is his view. (needs_source: the details of his chaining and age are relayed from books he does not name and are not given with chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:46:00}
- He then tells the encounter of the Prophet with Ibn Sayyad, a Jewish boy near puberty found playing with children at the tump of Banu Maghala in Medina (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] and [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdullah ibn Umar]]), whom the Prophet struck on the shoulder and asked whether he bore witness that he was God's messenger. The boy replied that he bore witness that he was the messenger of the unlettered. Asked what he saw, he said a truthful one and a liar came to him, and the Prophet said the matter was confused for him. The Prophet then hid a verse from him (see [[passage/quran-44-10|al-Dukhan, verse 10]]) and the boy could say only the first letters of the word smoke, after which the Prophet told him to go away and that he would not pass his measure. The sheikh adds that the boy's fortune-telling resembled the Arab soothsayers (see [[passage/quran-72-6|al-Jinn, verse 6]]). (needs_source: the hadith is given by sense, reported from Ibn Umar, without a full chain, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:54:00}
- Umar asked to be allowed to strike the boy's neck, and the Prophet said that if he was the Dajjal he would not be given power over him, and that if he was not there was no good in killing him, and that only Jesus would kill the Dajjal. The sheikh says the Companions doubted whether Ibn Sayyad was the Dajjal, that he was about thirteen at the time and that the Jews of Medina used him to frighten the Muslims. He says Ibn Sayyad died in the time of one of the caliphs and that some readings say he became a Muslim, which he does not accept, and defers the rest to the next episode. This is his account. (needs_source: the age of thirteen, the reports of Jewish fear of him and his death under a caliph are his own and given without chains) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep10 00:57:30}

## Episode 11: Jesus and the Dajjal, the Lydda gate, the age of security, and the first account of Gog and Magog
- The episode opens with the verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and the account of Dhu al-Qarnayn and the barrier (see [[person/dhu-al-qarnayn|Dhu al-Qarnayn]] and [[passage/quran-18-94|al-Kahf, verse 94]]), then the greeting. The host remarks on how heavy the dread of these scenes is and asks whether, after some thirty-five years of speaking and answering calls on the subject, the sheikh has grown weary of it. The sheikh answers that to live with the Book and the Sunna is a blessing, that he enjoys only talk of the religion and of what people will meet after death, and that if his phone stayed silent for five minutes of questions he would suspect a sin of his own. He then thanks his teachers. This is his reply. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:04:30}
- This third episode on the major signs leaves three: the Dajjal (see [[concept/dajjal|the Dajjal]]), the descent of Jesus (see [[concept/nuzul-isa-ibn-maryam|the descent of Jesus]]) and Gog and Magog (see [[concept/yajuj-majuj|Gog and Magog]]). The sheikh says the Dajjal appears only when religion is weak in people's hearts, and takes as a sign the way many Muslims fill the mosques and give charity in Ramadan and fall away from the first day of the festival, which he says is not how the Companions lived. He adds that some women think they stop worshipping when they cannot pray or fast, because religion has been narrowed to prayer and fasting. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:07:00}
- He says the verses on acts of worship in the Qur'an number about a hundred and ten, that the rest concern conduct between people, family, neighbours, employer and ruler, and that Muslims have applied only a small share (he gives figures of about two and a half percent against ninety-seven and a half, but the numbers are garbled in the transcript). (needs_source: the verse counts and percentages are his own and unclear in the transcript) He sees falling religion as the stage on which the Dajjal can appear and says people now follow any new claim, while enemies keep inflating one distraction after another, like balloons, to draw them from the main road. He contrasts the Prophet, who never took up side quarrels, whether called mad or a sorcerer, and only conveyed the message. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:09:30}
- On the purpose of the series he says he wants only to pass on what the Qur'an and the Prophet said, so that a listener who is heedless of these signs, or ignorant of them, is reminded. Each person's own end may come before any sign, so the aim is to prepare for meeting God, when the page is closed and only what was sent ahead remains. This is his stated purpose. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:10:30}
- On Jesus (see [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]]), he points to the verse that none of the People of the Book will fail to believe in him before his death (see [[passage/quran-4-159|al-Nisa, verse 159]]), which he says the commentators read as referring to Jesus and to a belief that will profit when he descends. He also cites the verse describing him as a sign of the Hour (see [[passage/quran-43-61|al-Zukhruf, verse 61]]) and says a non-standard reading gives a different form of the word. (needs_source: the claim about a non-standard reading is given without naming it and the transcript is garbled) He says this is evidence of his descent. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:12:30}
- Asked why Jesus and not another prophet, he gives reasons: to show his followers that he is not divine, to answer those who claimed they killed him (see [[passage/quran-4-157|al-Nisa, verse 157]]), to show the people of the Book that the Qur'an spoke true, and to strengthen the believers who meet him, whose heads he will stroke and to whom he will give news of their places in Paradise. He adds that he will pray behind an ordinary Muslim imam out of courtesy to the Prophet Muhammad (see [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]), so that no one mistakes him for a prophet after Muhammad. This is his explanation. (needs_source: the details of the stroking of heads and the prayer are given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:15:00}
- He quotes a hadith that the prophets are brothers of different mothers with one religion, and another that he is closest of all people to Jesus because no prophet stands between them, and that when people see Jesus they should know him. (needs_source: both reports are given by sense without a chain or a named collection) From the first he argues that there is one religion and several laws, citing the verse that He prescribed for them what He enjoined on Noah, Abraham, Moses and Jesus (see [[passage/quran-42-13|al-Shura, verse 13]]) and the verse that religion with God is Islam (see [[passage/quran-3-19|Al Imran, verse 19]]). He objects to the phrases "comparative religion" and "heavenly religions" as unlawful terms. This is his view. He adds a theatre analogy and a sketch in which God sent punishments such as the flood and the cry while humanity was young and later withheld general punishment, leaving signs on the road as warnings. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:21:00}
- From a long hadith he describes Jesus as a man of medium build, reddish white, wearing two garments, whose head seems to drip even when untouched, with drops like pearls and a fragrant scent, and compares the scent to the pleasant sweat the people of Paradise are said to have in place of waste. He notes the heights of Moses and of Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) and says all people of Paradise will be about thirty-three years old, even those who died as children or in old age. (needs_source: the description is read by sense without a chain, the claim of thirty-three years for all in Paradise is his own, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:16:30}
- He lists what the report says Jesus will do: break the cross, kill the swine, lift the poll tax (see [[concept/jizya|the jizya]]) so that there will be no tax of any kind, call to Islam, end the other communities, and rule for forty years in which there will be peace among animals, so that lions graze with camels, leopards with cows and wolves with sheep, and children play with snakes unharmed, after which he dies and the Muslims pray over him. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain, and the statement that all the communities end is his own) He describes the fortunes of a world made safe, with a man telling his sheep to graze and the sheep not passing a neighbour's field. This is his account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:41:30}
- On the descent, he says some reports place it at Damascus and others near Jerusalem, that he does not mind the place, and that he finds the afternoon prayer the nearest to the texts. The people in the mosque, frightened by the Dajjal, bolt the doors; Jesus asks them to open; the imam steps back and Jesus tells him to lead; after the prayer Jesus strokes the heads of the congregation and gives them news of Paradise. (needs_source: the details of the minaret, the time of prayer and the scene in the mosque are given by sense without chains and the transcript is garbled) He speculates about the weapons of the Dajjal's followers and then adds that God knows best. This is his account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:33:30}
- When the Dajjal sees Jesus he flees, and Jesus follows from Damascus toward Jerusalem until he overtakes him at the gate of Lydda in Palestine, where the Dajjal melts like salt in water at the scent of Jesus and every unbeliever the scent reaches dies. The sheikh stresses that this happens without swords or fighting and compares it to Pharaoh, who could not harm Moses because God put love of Moses in the heart of Asiya. (needs_source: the pursuit, the place and the melting are given by sense without a chain) He urges viewers to remember God often, to be patient and to keep close to scholars and good company. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:39:00}
- In the forty years that follow he says there will be no unjust state, no occupation and no ill-treatment of a wife by a husband, and that people will be equal as servants of God. As an example of blessing under a just ruler he tells that in or after the time of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) grains of wheat as large as date stones were found in the treasury, taken as a sign of the earlier just age. (needs_source: the wheat anecdote is given without a source or chain and the ruler is not named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:42:30}
- The host asks what the world will look like in the time of Jesus, with states, rulers and governments. The sheikh answers that he does not know whether the world will be devastated or reduced to a narrower scene, that the matter seems beyond description, that he has found no verified account and that he repeats only what he hears from the Prophet. He stops where the report stops. This is his method. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:44:30}
- On Gog and Magog he says Yajuj and Majuj are mentioned as peoples of Adam's line (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) who swarm from every height, and gives the meanings he draws from the root of their names: burning, haste, very salt water and surging, with Majuj as something refused. He describes them from a report as small-eyed and says the Muslims will keep fighting others until they come. (needs_source: the etymology is his own and the physical description and the report are given by sense without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:48:00}
- On Dhu al-Qarnayn, he says he lived before Abraham and about four thousand years before the present, that the people he met were barely able to understand speech and asked him to raise a barrier against Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-18-93|al-Kahf, verse 93]] and [[passage/quran-18-95|verse 95]]), and that he agreed to help them with strength. (needs_source: the date is his own and unsupported, and the transcript is garbled) He adds that Dhu al-Qarnayn was a believer and that the title is taken to mean "two horns," perhaps for his long life, and says the barrier was built of iron blocks heated until red and sealed with molten copper (see [[passage/quran-18-96|al-Kahf, verse 96]]). This is his account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:54:00}
- He recounts that the Prophet once woke alarmed saying that a calamity had drawn near the Arabs and that a gap the size of a small opening had been made in the barrier of Gog and Magog. (needs_source: the report is given by sense without a chain or the name of the narrator) He says they tell each other that they will open it tomorrow without adding "if God wills", until one says it and it opens, and draws a lesson to say "if God wills" before any promise, citing Ishmael (see [[passage/quran-37-102|al-Saffat, verse 102]]) and Moses (see [[passage/quran-18-69|al-Kahf, verse 69]]). He cites the verse that the barrier will be levelled when the promise of his Lord comes (see [[passage/quran-18-98|al-Kahf, verse 98]]), whether by their act or at the end of the world. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:56:00}
- Speaking of the saved group, the sheikh says the saved group does not carry any label but that of Muslim and stays within the Book and the Sunna, and advises viewers to teach their children to seek such people. This is his view and is a contested one, since the identity of the saved group is disputed among Muslims. He adds that the Muslim should remember that the question put to him will be what he offered to Islam, answered by deeds. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:58:00}
- The host puts three questions to be taken up next time, whether a person who dies before the signs is better than one who lives to see them, whether the trials expiate sins, and where the awaited Mahdi stands in the account (see [[concept/al-mahdi|the Mahdi]]), and the sheikh defers them to the next episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep11 00:57:30}

## Episode 12: the questions on the major signs, the Mahdi, and the description of the Prophet from the hadiths of Umm Ma'bad and Hind ibn Abi Hala
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the verse that Muhammad was only a messenger before whom messengers had passed (see [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]), then the greeting. The host says he and the sheikh had meant to begin with a talk on the character of the Prophet (see [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]), that the words fail them, and that this episode was meant for his death. The sheikh says love of the Prophet is natural to every Muslim, even a disobedient one. They first finish some points left over from the signs of the Hour. This is the framing of the episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:02:30}
- The host puts three questions left from the day before: whether one who dies before the minor signs is better than one who lives to see them, whether living through them is an atonement or a penalty, and where the awaited Mahdi stands. On the first the sheikh says that when a person dies his own resurrection begins, so he will not see the major signs, which belong to a time of trials from which the Prophet sought refuge.
