This does not make the dead better than the living, since it is God's decree who lives in the trial, and whoever lives through it and endures has a reward because it is a severe test. He pictures people with no food, drink, crops or fruit, and a man coming to say that he has food for whoever believes in him. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:04:00}
- He recalls an exchange about two men, one never tried by trials who worshipped and one tried who worshipped, and says the second belongs to those whose hearts God tested for piety (see [[passage/quran-49-3|al-Hujurat, verse 3]]), because God cleanses His servants through tests. (needs_source: the exchange is attributed in sense to Umar ibn al-Khattab with no source and the transcript is garbled) He concludes that the dead are not better than the living. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:04:30}
- The host recalls that at the first major sign the pens are lifted and there is neither repentance nor penalty. The sheikh answers that one who keeps to uprightness has his reward kept with God, and he gives an image: obedience planted bears obedience and obedience bears good deeds, while disobedience bears disobedience. Whoever endures has the reward of the tried, and all are tried. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:05:30}
- He corrects a point from the previous day on the age of the people of Paradise: he had said that Jesus (see [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Jesus]]) is thirty-three. The children of Paradise are a separate group, which he links to the verse on eternal youths who look like scattered pearls (see [[passage/quran-76-19|al-Insan, verse 19]]), and he calls them servants of Paradise. He asks the viewers' pardon if he skips or hurries some points. This is his own account. (needs_source: the age of thirty-three and the status of the youths are stated without a source or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:06:30}
- On the Mahdi (see [[concept/al-mahdi|the Mahdi]]), he says many scholars hold him to be one of the major signs, but that in his own research he did not find him in the collections of Bukhari or Muslim and so withheld judgement, which he says is not denial. He says there are hadiths on the Mahdi which some scholars have judged sound, that his own heart does not find rest in them, and that he presents what he has verified while others may hold otherwise; he adds that these are not matters known of the religion by necessity, so denying them is not denying something great. This is his own position and others hold the Mahdi to be established. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:08:00}
- Asked how to answer those who test everything by reason, he says God is known through revelation and not by reason, naming a man, whose name is garbled in the transcript, who had a mind and did not find God. He adds that for the Dajjal (see [[concept/dajjal|the Dajjal]]) the point is that he is a sign, whether or not one can picture him living so long; God kept Jesus alive and gives no account of His power. He quotes a line he attributes to al-Maarri that if the resurrection is false nothing is lost to the believer and if true the loss is the denier's. This is his argument. (needs_source: the line attributed to al-Maarri is given from memory in a garbled passage) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:10:00}
- The host reads a viewer's fax thanking the programme and asking the sheikh not to hurry the major signs, and the sheikh thanks her and says he will try to meet her good opinion. The host then turns to the description of the Prophet, asking how he looked and walked before they reach his death. The sheikh answers that the best way in is through two accounts, that of Umm Ma'bad and that of Hind ibn Abi Hala. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:11:30}
- On the Prophet's character (see [[passage/quran-68-4|al-Qalam, verse 4]]), he says the earth's quakes are measured by a scale, lengths by metres and weights by grams, and character by the Prophet's, who reached a human perfection no one reached before him. Then he turns to the Emigration (see [[event/hijra|the Hijra]]). On the road with Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]) and the guide Abdullah ibn Urayqit (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-urayqit|Abdullah ibn Urayqit]]), the Prophet came to the tent of Umm Ma'bad, a well-known resting place between Mecca and Medina. Abu Bakr asked for food and she said the flock had gone off with her husband, leaving a single weak ewe. The Prophet asked leave to milk it, passed his hand over its udder, and it filled with milk; he gave Umm Ma'bad, Abu Bakr and the guide to drink, drank last, and left a full vessel, and then departed. This is his telling. (needs_source: the account of Umm Ma'bad is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:14:00}
- When her husband Abu Ma'bad returned and saw the milk, she described the man she had seen, though she did not know who he was. The sheikh says both were still polytheists then and later accepted Islam and emigrated. (needs_source: the later conversion and migration of the couple is stated without a source) He recites her description in this order: a man of radiant clearness and fine face, with no paunch and no small head, well proportioned. She describes deeply black eyes with long lashes, and mentions a slight huskiness in the voice and arched brows, a long, shining neck and a thick beard. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:18:00}
- She continues that in silence he has dignity and in speech splendour, that his speech is sweet and clear, neither too little nor too much, like strung pearls, and that he is the most radiant of people from afar and the sweetest and finest from near. He is of middle height, neither tall nor short, a branch between two branches who looks best of the three; he has companions around him who listen when he speaks and hasten to what he orders, who attend and gather round him, and he is neither frowning nor foolish. This is the sheikh's recital. (needs_source: the hadith is recited without naming a collection or chain) He also quotes Abu Bakr as having counted seventeen white hairs in him and Umar as saying he could not fill his eyes with the Prophet's face. (needs_source: both reports are given without a source or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:25:30}
- The sheikh draws a point from the Prophet's speech: he spoke with measure because he was on a mission, and it was a mission of emigration. Besieged in the glen of Abu Talib (see [[place/shib-abi-talib|Shib Abi Talib]] and [[event/boycott-of-banu-hashim|the boycott of Banu Hashim]]) for three years, he called to God; in the house of al-Arqam he called to God; driven out, he founded a state. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:21:00}
- He calls her account the most precise description in history, from a polytheist woman in a chance meeting. He then turns to the most exact description by Hind ibn Abi Hala (see [[person/hind-ibn-abi-hala|Hind ibn Abi Hala]]), the son of Khadija (see [[person/khadija|Khadija]]) from her earlier marriage and so the Prophet's stepson, raised in his house. He says al-Hasan ibn Ali (see [[person/hasan-ibn-ali|al-Hasan ibn Ali]]) asked this maternal uncle of his to describe the Prophet, as a boy would remember a figure still clear to him. This is his account. (needs_source: the exchange between al-Hasan and Hind is given without a chain, and the age of al-Hasan in the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:26:30}
- From Hind's description he gives the following: the Prophet was imposing, a face shining like the full moon, a little taller than middling and shorter than the very tall. His hair was soft, curled slightly and reached the lobe of his ears, parted when it parted and cut when long. The sheikh adds that A'isha (see [[person/aisha|A'isha]]) combed and parted it for him and put perfume in the parting. (needs_source: the details of combing and perfume, and of his scent lasting from Friday to Friday on a child's head, are told in sense with no chain) His complexion was fair with a slight ruddiness on the parts exposed to the sun, his forehead wide, his brows arched and fine, with a vein between them that swelled when he was angry. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:32:00}
- He links the swelling vein to the story of the woman of the Makhzum clan who stole, when Usama (see [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]]) interceded for her and the Prophet asked whether he would intercede over a limit of God. He says people saw the vein and knew he was angry, and that he was angered only when a sacred prohibition of God was violated. (needs_source: the story is told in sense and the link to the vein is the sheikh's own) Hind's account goes on: a nose with a light above it that one who did not look closely took to be high, a thick beard, smooth cheeks, a wide mouth and teeth set apart, a neck like a pure silver statue, a body well knit and moderate, belly and chest level, a broad chest, broad apart shoulders, and hair on the shoulders and the forearms. This is the sheikh's recital of the hadith. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:36:00}
- He says the palms were broad and the grip soft yet strong, and that those who shook it found it as if it had come from a perfumer's garden. (needs_source: the saying about the hand is told in sense with no chain) He adds that these were qualities of beauty among the Arabs, so they were not merely felt by those who loved him. On his gait, the Prophet lifted his foot as if descending a slope, and when he turned he turned his whole body. He looked down longer than he looked up, glanced sidelong rather than staring, and was first to greet whoever he met. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:40:00}
- Asked about his speech, Hind says he was continually sorrowful, always thoughtful, without ease, silent for long and speaking only when needed; he opened and closed his speech clearly, in comprehensive words, neither excessive nor short. The sheikh adds, in his own words, that the Prophet smiled with his companions, and that when angry no one could stand before him, since he was angry only for the truth. He was gentle in nature, not harsh, magnified a blessing however small and never found fault with food, and the sheikh cites the verse on his gentleness (see [[passage/quran-3-159|Al Imran, verse 159]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:43:30}
- In Hind's list he was not harsh, nor loud, nor coarse, nor a backbiter, nor a flatterer; he overlooked what he did not want and did not disappoint those who hoped. He had left three things: argument, excess and what did not concern him, and three towards people: that he did not blame them, shame them or seek their faults. He spoke only of what he hoped would bring reward, and when he spoke those with him were silent as if birds were on their heads, then when he fell silent they spoke. He laughed at what they laughed at, wondered at what they wondered at, bore a stranger's roughness, and did not accept praise except in measure. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:47:00}
- The host says that, set against this, they gave the Prophet nothing and know only his name, and the sheikh agrees that these descriptions show how little he is known. Asked by al-Hasan about his going out among people, Hind says the Prophet guarded his tongue except for what brought hearts together, honoured the noble of each people and put them over their people, was wary of people without suspecting them, and asked after his companions; the sheikh gives the example of his missing al-Julaybib (see [[person/julaybib|al-Julaybib]]) after a battle. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:49:30}
- Hind adds that he praised the good and strengthened it, was moderate and did not slack, and that the best in his eyes were the most sincere in counsel and the greatest in rank those who best supported others. The sheikh notes that he made Abdullah ibn Umm Maktum (see [[person/abd-allah-ibn-umm-maktum|Abdullah ibn Umm Maktum]]) governor of Medina. (needs_source: the appointment is stated in sense and the transcript is unclear) The Prophet sat and rose only in remembrance of God, did not keep a fixed seat, sat where the gathering ended and told others to do the same, and a Bedouin coming in asked which of them was the son of Abd al-Muttalib because he had no place of honour. He gave each person present his share, did not withdraw his hand first when shaking hands, and turned no petitioner away without his need or a gentle word. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:54:00}
- His gathering, Hind says, was one of forbearance, modesty, patience and trust, where voices were not raised and no one's honour was violated; people were equal before the truth and distinguished only by piety, modest, honouring the elder, showing mercy to the young, giving precedence to the needy and guarding the stranger. The sheikh connects this to the verse that a messenger has come from among them (see [[passage/quran-9-128|al-Tawba, verse 128]]). He adds that the Prophet gestured with the whole palm, not a single finger apart from the index in the prayer, and when he spoke he tapped his right palm against the left thumb. (needs_source: the gesture is told in sense with no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:57:00}
- The host closes by saying the time is up, and that tomorrow's episode will be on the death of the Prophet and his departure to the highest companion. The sheikh asks God to keep them firm, and the episode ends with the same verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) with which it opened. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep12 00:58:30}

## Episode 13: the death of the Prophet, his last illness, his last sermon and his burial
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and the true promise drawing near (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]), then the host's praise of God and blessings on the Prophet (see [[person/muhammad|Muhammad]]), and announces that the episode is about the Prophet's death. The host admits he is struggling to hold himself together as he approaches it. This is the framing of the episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:02:30}
- The host asks whether the sorrow that comes at the mere mention of the Prophet's death is natural. The sheikh answers that death is a truth every creature admits, and that grief grows with the nearness of the one who dies: a man grieves for his son more than for his brother, and for his brother more than for an old grandfather. Since the Prophet is the nearest person to every believer, he asks the host to imagine its weight on the Companions. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:04:00}
- He gives the example of [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]], who could not bear it and tried to persuade himself and those around him that the Prophet had gone to meet his Lord as Moses did. He says [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] then recited the verse that Muhammad was only a messenger before whom messengers had passed (see [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]), and that people felt they were hearing it for the first time although Umar knew the whole Book. He likens the Prophet to the pull of gravity that keeps hearts steady, and calls his loss for the Companions something like the Resurrection. (needs_source: the report of Umar's denial and Abu Bakr's reply is told in sense with no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:06:30}
- He pictures the Companions with the Prophet as small children with a loving father: a Companion would ask and the sky would answer, they saw Gabriel, they saw the angels fighting at Badr, and rain came when he prayed. He asks the host to imagine the effect on the community today, and says that had the Prophet lived, the groups that now appear and the dregs of the nations that attack Muslim lands and sacred places would not have dared, since he was the point of gathering and stability. This is his view of the matter. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:08:00}
- He ties the death to God's decree (see [[passage/quran-39-30|al-Zumar, verse 30]], [[passage/quran-21-34|al-Anbiya, verse 34]] and [[passage/quran-3-185|Al Imran, verse 185]]), and says God prepared the Companions for the news by sending down the verse that the religion was completed (see [[passage/quran-5-3|al-Ma'ida, verse 3]]). He adds the saying that whoever is struck by a calamity should console himself with the loss of the Prophet. (needs_source: the saying is quoted with no chain) He says only a believer feels the size of the loss, since the Prophet gathered every human role: father, husband, brother, friend, neighbour, scholar, teacher, political leader, caller and worshipper. For other prophets he recalls the verses where the heart trembles at the plea of [[person/ibrahim|Abraham]] (see [[passage/quran-14-37|Ibrahim, verse 37]]) and of Moses (see [[passage/quran-28-24|al-Qasas, verse 24]]), and says that with Muhammad the heart trembles at every moment he is remembered. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:10:00}
- On the story of the death, he begins with the farewell pilgrimage, which he says the Prophet made with all his wives and 113,000 of the Companions. (needs_source: the figure is a statistic given with no source) (see [[event/farewell-pilgrimage|the farewell pilgrimage]]). He says the Prophet then sent out the expedition of [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama]] (see [[event/expedition-of-usama|the expedition of Usama]]) to discipline the Byzantines on the frontier, which he calls the Prophet's last decision as leader of the state. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:11:00}
- He tells that the Prophet began to feel unwell with pain and fever at the house of [[person/maymuna-bint-al-harith|Maymuna]], that when the pain grew he asked his wives for leave to be nursed in the house of [[person/aisha|Aisha]], and that they agreed. Before that, one night he called his servant [[person/abu-muwayhiba|Abu Muwayhiba]], went out to the cemetery of [[place/al-baqi|al-Baqi]] and prayed for the dead of his Companions at length. (needs_source: the night visit to al-Baqi is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:12:00}
- At al-Baqi, as the sheikh gives it, he said that he saw trials coming like pieces of a dark night, and the sheikh reads this as a reassurance to the dead that they were spared them. The sheikh adds the greeting the Prophet is reported to have given the people of the graves, wishing them joy in the place they were in compared with what the living would meet. Then, he says, the Prophet told Abu Muwayhiba that he had been offered the keys to the treasures of this world and to eternity in it and then Paradise, or the meeting with his Lord and Paradise, and had chosen the meeting with his Lord, and he asked forgiveness for the people of al-Baqi. The sheikh calls this the first clear announcement of his death to any of the Companions. (needs_source: the report is read from memory in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:23:30}
- Returning to Aisha's house, he says, she had a headache and the Prophet answered that it was rather his own head, and he began to speak of the poisoned meal of Khaybar (see [[place/khaybar|Khaybar]]) whose effect, in the sheikh's reading, now appeared, so that he said he felt his main artery being cut. The sheikh gives the interval since Khaybar as a little over two years. (needs_source: the interval is stated as a number with no source) He says God willed that the Prophet also receive the reward of martyrdom, beside prophethood and being the intercessor. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:14:00}
- He tells that as the illness grew, the Prophet asked for water from seven wells to wash, then came out leaning on [[person/al-fadl-ibn-al-abbas|al-Fadl ibn al-Abbas]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]], dragging his feet from the pain. The sheikh says illness for the Prophet meant a rise in ranks, while for people like us it is an atonement for sins. This is his view. He says the Prophet then sat on the pulpit and gave a sermon, the last he gave. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:15:00}
- He tells of the Prophet's counsel on the Ansar, and his words that no one had a hand over them that they had not repaid except Abu Bakr, whose hand only God could repay. He says the Prophet ordered every door that opened on the mosque closed except Abu Bakr's door. (needs_source: the words and the order about the doors are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:26:00}
- The sheikh tells the last scene in Aisha's room: the Prophet lay with his head in her lap, looked at a young man holding a tooth-stick, and Aisha understood he wanted it, softened it and gave it to him. He tells that [[person/fatima|Fatima]] bent her ear to his mouth when he whispered to her and she wept, and that when he whispered again she smiled. Later she told Aisha that he had first told her he was going to his Lord, having been given the choice between staying in the world and the highest companion, and had chosen the latter, and then that she would be the leader of the women of the worlds in Paradise and the first of his household to follow him. The sheikh says she died six months after him. (needs_source: the whispered report and the six months are told with no chain and no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:17:00}
- He says Abu Bakr led the people in 17 prayers over three days while the Prophet was ill. (needs_source: the number of prayers is a figure with no source) He tells that once the Prophet felt some relief and came out leaning on Ali and al-Fadl (or on [[person/qutham-ibn-al-abbas|Qutham ibn al-Abbas]]), and the Companions in prayer rejoiced as he looked out from his room that opened onto the mosque. He signalled to them to complete the prayer, Abu Bakr stepped back, and the Prophet sat beside him and prayed. The sheikh adds that the Prophet had never prayed behind anyone except Gabriel when he taught him the prayer and Abu Bakr, which he takes as a sign of Abu Bakr's succession. This is his reading of it, and the succession is a contested subject between Sunni and Shia views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:18:30}
- On the question of who led the prayer, he tells that the Prophet said to order Abu Bakr to lead, that Aisha replied that Abu Bakr was a man who wept much and was tender-hearted, and that the Prophet repeated it. He tells that a Companion, Abdullah ibn Abi Zam'a, came out, did not find Abu Bakr, found Umar and told him to lead, and that the Prophet heard the loud voice and said that it was not Abu Bakr's and that God and the believers would refuse another. The sheikh reads this as a confirmation of Abu Bakr's succession. (needs_source: the exchange is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:34:30}
- He says that when the Prophet entered his room the women with him poured water on him to ease the pain, and that he said that death has its agonies. (needs_source: the saying is quoted with no chain) The sheikh says the believer's soul leaves as easily as a drop of water, and that the Prophet's agony was for the raising of his rank. He says he lost consciousness and revived, and that when Fatima cried over his suffering he answered that there would be no suffering for her father after that day. (needs_source: the exchange is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:39:30}
- He gives the date as a Monday, the twelfth of Rabi al-Awwal in the eleventh year of the Hijra, and the Prophet's age as sixty-three Hijri years. He says the day was also that of his entering Medina at the Hijra. (needs_source: the dates and age are stated with no source) He tells that the Prophet had been better that morning, spoke and smiled at them, and that Abu Bakr asked leave to visit his wife [[person/asma-bint-umays|Asma bint Umays]] outside the city, which the sheikh says he did because he found the Prophet well. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:20:30}
- He says that at the start of the illness the Prophet saw in a dream two gold bracelets on his arms, disliked them and blew on them, and that he interpreted them as the two liars, [[person/musaylima|Musaylima]] and [[person/al-aswad-al-ansi|al-Aswad al-Ansi]], who claimed prophethood when news of his illness spread. (needs_source: the dream and its reading are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:21:30}
- He says the last words of the Prophet were the highest companion, repeated three times, and that the women said that he had chosen. The sheikh pictures the room filled with angels eager to receive that soul. This is his picture. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:40:30}
- He tells how the Companions took the news. He says Ali sat in the room with his head on his hands and did not move, and that [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] is reported to have gone about dazed, unable to see anyone before him. (needs_source: both reports are told with no chain) He says that when Umar heard, he came raising his sword and threatening the people, and that Abu Bakr, who had been away, came quickly, kissed the Prophet's forehead and said that he was good in life and in death, then climbed the pulpit and said that whoever worshipped Muhammad should know that Muhammad had died, and whoever worshipped God should know that God is living and does not die, and recited the verse again (see [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]). He says Umar said afterwards that he felt he was hearing it for the first time. (needs_source: the reports are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:41:00}
- The host asks why God willed this painful manner of death. The sheikh answers that it shows the believer the way to bear loss: if a person falls ill, dies of illness or loses a loved one, he should console himself with the Prophet, since there is no one dearer to us. This is his view. He then turns to Abu Bakr's steadiness, and says that after the Prophet no heart stood firmer than his, although he was a tender-hearted man who wept easily, and that this was good for the community since otherwise no one would have run its affairs. He cites a saying that if the faith of the community were set on one scale and Abu Bakr's on the other, Abu Bakr's would outweigh it. (needs_source: the saying is quoted with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:44:00}
- The host asks why Ali never spoke about that moment. The sheikh answers that he found no description of it from Ali, that the matter is greater than what a person can put into words, and that his heart may have been too tender. He says this is the only explanation he has and that God knows. This is his guess. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:48:30}
- On the washing, he tells that the five present were Ali, [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]], al-Fadl, Qutham and Shuqran, a servant, and that when they asked each other whether to strip him as is done with the dead, a drowsiness fell on them and a voice said not to take off his clothes. He says Aisha is reported to have looked on from behind a partition and seen the men with their cloaks on their chests. (needs_source: the voice and the drowsiness are told with no chain and the transcript is garbled) He says they washed him over his shirt, that nothing was to touch his skin, and that they then removed the shirt and wrapped him in the shroud. He gives the shroud as three garments of white Yemeni cloth, as he had asked in his will. (needs_source: the details of the washing and the shroud are told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:45:00}
- He tells that the Companions asked where to bury him, and that Abu Bakr said he had heard the Prophet say that prophets are buried where they die, so the grave was dug in the same place. He says the men prayed in groups, then the boys, then the women, and that the grave was raised about a handspan above the ground. (needs_source: the saying and the details are told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:30:00}
- On the grave, he gives the five who went down into it as Ali, al-Fadl, Qutham, [[person/anas-ibn-malik|Anas]] and Shuqran. He tells that Fatima said to Anas: how could you bear to throw earth on the face of the Messenger of God. He says the sunna is to throw three handfuls of earth, saying with each of them the words of the verse that from it We created you, to it We return you and from it We bring you out again (see [[passage/quran-20-55|Ta Ha, verse 55]]). (needs_source: the names, Fatima's words and the three handfuls are told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:47:00}
- He pictures Medina on the day of the death as a city that had lost its centre, silent, with the people dazed, and he says God gave Abu Bakr an extraordinary ability to hold the young state together, since the Byzantines were poised and the false prophets had begun to appear. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:36:30}
- On the last sermon, he tells that the Prophet could not climb the pulpit and sat on its first step, with al-Fadl holding his hand. He gives the words: that he praised God, that whoever he had flogged on the back should come and take his due from that back, whoever he had insulted should take his due from his honour, that rancour was not in his nature or his way, and that the dearest to him was the one who took his right from him if he had it or forgave him, so that he would meet God with a clean heart. The sheikh adds that a man said that he was owed three dirhams and the Prophet had them paid, saying there was no time to argue. (needs_source: the sermon and the story of the man are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:25:00}
- He says that when the Prophet recited the chapter of help and conquest (see [[passage/quran-110-1|al-Nasr, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-110-2|verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-110-3|verse 3]]), Abu Bakr wept alone among them, taking it as an announcement of his departure, and that the Prophet then said that a servant had been given the choice between this world and the next and chose what is with God. The Companions answered that they would ransom him with their souls, their wealth and their children. He gives the Prophet's words that the one who had given him most in his company and his wealth was Abu Bakr, and that had he taken an intimate friend it would have been him, but there is the brotherhood of Islam. (needs_source: the reports are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:26:00}
- He gives the Prophet's farewell words, as the sheikh reads them out: a series of prayers for the Companions, then that he counsels them to fear God and entrusts them to God, that he comes to them as a warner and a bringer of good news, and that they must not exalt themselves over God in His servants or His lands. The sheikh takes the warning against pride as the root of a community's misery and cites the verse on the home of the hereafter that God gives to those who seek neither exaltation nor corruption on earth (see [[passage/quran-28-83|al-Qasas, verse 83]]), and the verse that there is a dwelling in Hell for the arrogant (see [[passage/quran-39-60|al-Zumar, verse 60]]). He adds that the Prophet did not know the hour of his own death, which is of the knowledge of the Hour (see [[passage/quran-31-34|Luqman, verse 34]]). (needs_source: the words are read in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:50:30}
- He gives the exchange on the Prophet's burial from the report the host asked him to read: when the Companions asked who would wash him, he said his nearest family first; who would shroud him, in these clothes if you wish, or white or Yemeni cloth; and who would pray over him, he said wait, may God forgive you and reward you well for your Prophet, and they wept and he wept. He told them to wash him and shroud him and set him on his bed at the edge of his grave, then to leave him for a while, and not to hurt him with wailing, lamenting or crying out, so that the first to pray over him would be Ali and the men of his household and then the others. The sheikh says this report shows the Companions asking the Prophet about religion until his last moment. (needs_source: the exchange is read in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:52:00}
- On who would put him in the grave, he reads the answer that his family would, with many angels they could not see. The sheikh asks God to join them to the Prophet and to give them a drink from his hand after which they would never thirst. This is a prayer, not a ruling. The episode ends with the same verses on Gog and Magog with which it began (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]). (needs_source: the answer is read in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep13 00:53:30}

## Episode 14: the first stations of the hereafter, the departure of the soul, washing, shrouding and burial, and the first questioning in the grave
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and on the stupor of death (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]] and [[passage/quran-50-19|Qaf, verse 19]]). The host recalls the warm response to the previous episode on the death of the Prophet (see [[event/death-of-the-prophet|the death of the Prophet]]) and announces today's title, the first stations of the hereafter. The sheikh calls the title apt: the first station is the leaving of the soul and then the grave, and he quotes [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] that if the grave is easy what follows is easier, and if it is hard what follows is harder. (needs_source: the saying of Uthman is quoted with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:02:30}
- The host lists the questions of the episode: how the angel of death looks, whether he has helpers, how he takes souls, whether there are signs by which a believer's soul departs and a disbeliever's, and whether the dying show signs on the body. At the mention of the name Azrael the sheikh says the questions left his mind, as if the angel were in the room. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:04:00}
- The sheikh says every person must leave this world for the next, and speaks of what may make a person stop and reckon with himself: old age, a grave illness, an accident. He quotes an unnamed saying that God may open for you the door of work and close the door of acceptance, or may bring you a sin that becomes the cause of your arrival, and he takes the second to mean that a sin can lead a person to repent and begin again. This is his reading. (needs_source: the saying is quoted with no author or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:05:30}
- He says the angel of death is not nearer to the old than the young or to the sick than the healthy: he carries fixed appointments for the day, place and hour, which none can advance or delay, and he cites the verse of those for whom killing was decreed going out to their resting places (see [[passage/quran-3-154|Al Imran, verse 154]]). This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:06:30}
- On sudden death he says it is a relief for the believer and a regret for the wrongdoer, and that a fatal illness is a mercy from God that expiates sins and raises ranks, though a person should not be glad of illness. He advises remembering death in every prayer and praying as a farewell prayer. These are his views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:08:00}
- Sitting by the bed of a dying person, he says, the tongue may be withheld from us: the person begins to enter another world and sees what we do not see, in line with the verse that the covering is lifted and sight today is sharp (see [[passage/quran-50-22|Qaf, verse 22]]). He tells that al-Basri passed by people weeping over their dead and said they should weep for themselves, since the dead has escaped three things: the sight of the angel of death, the bitterness of death, and not knowing his place in Paradise or the Fire. (needs_source: the report of al-Basri is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:10:00}
- He says two groups of angels descend with the [[concept/malak-al-mawt|angel of death]], angels of mercy and angels of punishment, and that they do not know whether the person is righteous until God makes it known to them. After the soul leaves he asks the two groups who will take it up, with a nod to the verse on the soul reaching the collar bones and the question who will charm it away (see [[passage/quran-75-26|al-Qiyama, verse 26]] and [[passage/quran-75-27|verse 27]]). He says the groups do not quarrel over it, and that each soul has its own sign and scent. (needs_source: the exchange between the groups is told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:12:00}
- On how the angel of death looks, he declines to describe him for lack of a verified description, and says only that he must be fearful. He says the dying person sees him with his own sight, even in a coma, and that the Prophet himself felt the pangs of death (see [[concept/sakarat-al-mawt|the pangs of death]]). He recalls Uthman's words that whoever escapes them escapes a great thing. These are his views. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:13:30}
- On the signs seen at death, he says some sweat, some are quiet, some groan, and he holds that the reported hadiths on sweat on the brow and similar signs are weak, and that he stops at the two Sahih collections of al-Bukhari and Muslim. He cautions against reading a smile or a frown into a face, since the person is already in another world. He gives the picture of an aircraft gathering all its power on the runway, and likens it to the soul gathering to leave a body it lived in, a soul that is of the command of God, as the verse says of the spirit (see [[passage/quran-17-85|al-Isra, verse 85]] and [[concept/al-nafs-wal-ruh|the soul and the spirit]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:15:30}
- He says a life of good conduct points toward righteousness as it appears to us, but that this does not mean the soul leaves easily; the angel may draw it from the feet to the knees, the waist and the chest and then take it. He says the soul is in the whole body, not in one place, and gives the believer a halo of light. A hard departure, he says, can expiate remaining sins, and the righteous pray that God ease the pangs of death. These are his views. (needs_source: the stages of the drawing of the soul are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:17:00}
- He cites the verse that the angel of death who is charged with you takes you (see [[passage/quran-32-11|al-Sajda, verse 11]]), and says his task is only to separate the soul from the body. On visions of the dead in dreams, he says a dream is never a source of any rule of the law, even a dream of the Prophet giving a command, since only waking revelation to a prophet binds. This is his position. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:18:30}
- He tells that [[person/amr-ibn-al-as|Amr ibn al-As]] in his last illness described himself as a sparrow over a pan of oil with fire beneath it, unable to fly away and live or to die and rest, and as a soul coming out through the eye of a needle. He adds that a person may struggle for a day, two or three, and that doctors speak of brain death. (needs_source: the words of Amr are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:19:30}
- On the righteous soul, he tells that angels of mercy come with hanut, which he explains as perfumed cloths, and shrouds from Paradise; the soul is wrapped and rises, the doors of the heavens open for it, and the angels call it by the name it loved best in the world and greet it. He tells that the grave is widened for it as far as the eye can see and a door opened onto its place in Paradise, and that the person then asks God to bring the Hour. (needs_source: the details of the ascent are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:21:00}
- He takes the rising of the soul and of good deeds as a rising of rank, not of place, citing the verse on raising Idris to a high station (see [[passage/quran-19-57|Maryam, verse 57]]), and says this is the creed of Ahl al-Sunna wal-Jama'a. He recalls the question whether the Prophet's ascension brought him nearer to God than [[person/yunus|Jonah]] in the belly of the whale. He declines to settle whether Paradise is in the sky, saying the matter will come later. These are his views. (needs_source: the saying on Jonah is told with no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:22:00}
- On the wicked soul, he says sins have a smell only the angels perceive, and cites the verse that corruption has appeared on land and sea through what people's hands have earned (see [[passage/quran-30-41|al-Rum, verse 41]]). He tells that angels who record a night prayer ask after a person who stopped praying because he was ill, and that when Jonah called out in the darkness the angels of heaven knew his voice (see [[passage/quran-21-87|al-Anbiya, verse 87]] and [[passage/quran-21-88|verse 88]]). For the disbeliever, he says, the doors of heaven are shut in the face of the soul, its deeds not accepted because of idolatry and show. (needs_source: the angels' question and the smell of sins are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:24:30}
- He tells that the angels ask whose foul soul this is and are told its worst name in the world, and that the grave is then narrowed on that person until his ribs cross, with snakes and scorpions, and a door opened onto his place in the Fire, so that he asks God not to bring the Hour. This is one of the reports on the [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|punishment of the grave]], given as he tells it. (needs_source: the report is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:25:00}
- On carrying the bier, he tells that the righteous say hasten me and the wicked say do not hasten me, and he rejects the claim that the bier moves of its own accord, calling it the suggestion of the bearers. He describes the scene at home, with wailing and tearing of clothes (see [[concept/niyaha-fiqh|wailing for the dead]]) and preparing the grave, and says the dead person is like a drowning man who needs a prayer or a good deed. His advice is to pray for the dead at the grave, asking forgiveness and steadfastness for him, since he is now being questioned. These are his views. (needs_source: the sayings of the dead on the bier and the saying on steadfastness at the grave are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:28:00}
- On what to do at a death, he says to open the will first, not after forty days, and that a Muslim must write one naming who washes, who shrouds and who prays and how the estate is divided (see [[concept/wasiyya-fiqh|the will]]). He says there should be few people in the washing room, that the water should be neither very cold nor very hot because the dead person feels, that breaking the bone of the dead is like breaking that of the living, and that the dead knows who washes him. These are his views. (needs_source: the saying on the bone of the dead is quoted with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:29:00}
- On the shroud, he quotes [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]] as saying to shroud him in his old garment, since the new is for the living, and says the shroud should be cheap white cloth without decoration or silk, three pieces for a man and five for a woman as he gives it. He says burial should not be delayed beyond a short wait for the eldest son or a righteous imam to pray, and not a day or two. He says to loosen the limbs of the dead at once, take off the clothes and cover him with a cloth until the washing, as the Prophet did, and to plug the openings with cotton. This is his account. (needs_source: the words of Abu Bakr and the details of the shroud and of the practice are told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:32:00}
- On a spouse washing the other, he says the scholars hold it is permitted but that [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]] differs, holding that the marriage contract ends at death, and that he himself leans to Abu Hanifa's view unless a will says otherwise. This is a contested question and is given as his preference. He adds that the best washer is one who knows the rules and keeps the body covered, that nothing is said during the washing, that the water is poured away without ritual, that perfume is applied to the body and the shroud, that a woman is carried only by those she could not marry, and that hair is not shaved. He says gold dental work and added items should be removed and tattoos left. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:34:30}
- He gives the reward of the funeral prayer: whoever follows the bier, prays and waits until burial gets two measures of reward, the smaller like Mount Uhud. (needs_source: the saying is quoted in sense with no chain) He says one who died in an explosion, a crash, drowning or a car accident goes through the same stages, and that God gave the angel of death the power to take countless souls at the same moment. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:35:30}
- On the angel of death, he says the name Azrael does not sound Arabic and is not established in the Quran or in what is sound, whereas Gabriel, Michael and Israfil are names handed down, and that he does not stress the point. He says the angel of death is a single angel to whom God gave the power to take souls in every land at the same moment. He tells that Gabriel came to the Prophet in the form of [[person/dihya-al-kalbi|Dihya al-Kalbi]], a handsome man whom the Companions also saw, and that the Prophet saw him in his true form with his head in the sky and his wings filling the horizon. He lists the four great angels, [[person/jibril|Gabriel]], Israfil, [[person/mikail|Michael]] and the angel of death. (needs_source: the descriptions are told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:38:00}
- He says angels see us and we do not see them, since we have no need to, and that they protect and record, and that on the Day of Gathering we will see them as the verse says of angels sent down in streams (see [[passage/quran-25-25|al-Furqan, verse 25]]) and see [[concept/malaika|the angels]]. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:38:30}
- On a scattered body, he says God gathers it. He tells of the man of the Children of Israel who told his sons to burn him and scatter his ashes on land and sea, and God told the land and sea to give back what they held and then forgave the man for his fear of Him, and cites the verse that the earth takes nothing from them that is not known (see [[passage/quran-50-4|Qaf, verse 4]]). He adds that the whole body decays except the tailbone, and says he will return to that. (needs_source: the story is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:39:30}
- He says the remains of a person killed in an accident are gathered, washed, shrouded and prayed over, and that only the [[concept/shahid|martyr of the battlefield]] is not washed. He lists those who die as martyrs in the hadith as the drowned, the one under a collapse, the woman dying in childbirth, and those of a plague or an abdominal illness, and says the martyr does not feel the pain of death. (needs_source: the list of martyrs is told in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:40:30}
- On the funeral procession, he says it should go without any voice, not even remembrance of God, and that the Prophet's way was silence, so he rejects a raised voice, single or in a group. This is his view and is a disputed matter. He tells that [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]] asked that no one in red come near him, for fear his soul would take it for fire, and says silence is kept at three times: reciting the Quran, advancing in battle and following a bier. (needs_source: the words of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz and the saying on the three times are told with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:42:00}
- At the grave he reads the long hadith of [[person/al-bara-ibn-azib|al-Bara ibn Azib]]: the Prophet sat by a grave of an Ansari man with his companions still as if birds sat on their heads, scratching the ground with a stick, and said to seek refuge in God from the punishment of the grave two or three times. Then, as the hadith says, a believer leaving the world is met by white-faced angels with a shroud and hanut of Paradise, who sit as far as the eye can see, and the angel of death sits at his head and calls him a good soul to go out to forgiveness and pleasure, and it flows out like a drop from a waterskin. He notes that the version of al-Tirmidhi adds a Lord who is not angry. This account is told with the sheikh's glosses. (needs_source: the hadith is read in sense, the transcript is garbled and no chain is given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:45:30}
- In the same report the angels carry the soul in the shroud, from which comes a scent like the finest musk, and each company of angels asks whose good soul it is and is told its best name; the doors of heaven are opened until the seventh, where God says to record the book of His servant in the Illiyyin and return him to the earth from which he was created. The soul is returned to the body, and two angels sit him up and ask who his Lord is, and he says his Lord is God. (needs_source: the continuation is read in sense with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:48:00}
- He says the soul is returned to the body once the last mourner has left and the grave is closed, and calls the first night in the grave the hardest of a person's life. He quotes a line of verse, with no poet named, that a thousand years of enjoyment would not equal the first night in the grave. (needs_source: the verse is quoted with no poet or source named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:49:30}
- On the two questioning angels, he says people call them Munkar and Nakir (see [[concept/munkar-and-nakir|Munkar and Nakir]]), but that he does not mind the names and stops at what is verified. Their first question is who your Lord is, and he cites the verse that God keeps firm those who believe with the firm word in this life and the next (see [[passage/quran-14-27|Ibrahim, verse 27]]) and the verse that angels descend on those who say our Lord is God and then stand firm (see [[passage/quran-41-30|Fussilat, verse 30]]). He adds that angels attend where good is done and devils where there are songs, dancing and idle shows. This is his remark. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:51:30}
- He tells that before the two angels, a handsome figure comes to the dead person and says it is his righteous deed, that it will sit with him until the Hour, and that two angels more merciful to him than his parents will come and ask him, telling him how to answer, and that God puts the answer in his mouth. (needs_source: the report of the figure is told in sense with no chain and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:52:30}
- The sheikh ends the episode there, to continue next time, with a prayer that God grant repentance and guidance before death, ease at death and honour after it, and make the best of deeds their last, and asks that God keep them firm with the firm word. This is a prayer, not a ruling. The episode closes with the opening verses on Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep14 00:53:30}

## Episode 15: the second episode on the first stations, barzakh life, the questioning and squeezing of the grave, and pictures of the punishment of the grave
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host says this is the second episode on the first stations of the hereafter and that the previous one stopped at the burial of one of the Ansar and the Prophet sitting by the grave. The sheikh says his talk is not meant to frighten or intimidate but to light a lamp before every Muslim so that each knows what awaits when the soul departs, and that knowing this is a duty on everyone. He quotes a saying attributed to a Companion advising his son to learn about death well so that it does not take him by surprise. (needs_source: the saying is attributed with no named speaker and no chain) He recalls that the Prophet told people to seek refuge from the punishment of the grave (see [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|the punishment of the grave]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:03:00}
- The sheikh narrates what happens to the believer's soul: the angel of death takes it, the angels of mercy receive it and place it in perfume and shrouds from Paradise, the doors of the heavens open for it, and the servant's grave is widened as far as the eye can see, furnished from Paradise, with a door opened onto his place there, so that he says, my Lord, hasten the Hour. The two angels then seat him (see [[concept/munkar-and-nakir|Munkar and Nakir]]). He adds that the devout believer sees the sun as if setting and says he nearly missed the afternoon prayer, and is told he is in the world of barzakh where there is no deed, only reckoning. (needs_source: the report is given by the sheikh in his own words with no chain, as are the sun's appearing and what the servant is told) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:05:30}
- The sheikh relates that the earth addresses the believer, saying he was the dearest of God's servants to it while on its back and that it will now show him what it does for him, and says the opposite to the wrongdoer, so that the grave narrows on him until his ribs cross. He mentions that the grave has a squeeze and that if anyone escaped it, Sa'd ibn Mu'adh would have (see [[person/sad-ibn-muadh|Sa'd ibn Mu'adh]]). He says the believer's squeeze is like a mother hugging her child in a moment of tenderness, and that one who constricted people in life is constricted in the grave. (needs_source: the earth's words, the squeeze of the grave and the saying about Sa'd ibn Mu'adh are given with no chain) The host asks whether the seating and questioning is of the soul or of soul and body; the sheikh answers that it is both together. This is his own view of the question. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:07:30}
- On the questioning in the grave, the sheikh says there are four questions (who is your Lord, what is your religion, what do you say of the man sent among you, and a fourth he does not name), and that the answer comes in the language of one's state, meaning what the servant used to do, not merely in words. He relates that Umar ibn al-Khattab asked the Prophet whether he would have his wits about him when the two angels question him, was told yes, and said, then I do not mind. (needs_source: the question of Umar and the Prophet's reply are given with no chain) (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]). The believer answers that his Lord is God, his religion Islam and the man the Messenger of God, adding that he read the Book of God and believed and affirmed it. The sheikh says the Qur'an is the constitution of a community and a way of life, not a book read from one Ramadan to the next, and mentions someone who reviewed five or six parts of the Qur'an every day and kept another reading for reflection. (needs_source: the story of the person who reviewed five or six parts daily has no name given) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:09:30}
- The sheikh continues with what follows a correct answer: a caller from heaven announces that My servant has spoken truly, so his grave is furnished from Paradise, he is clothed from Paradise, a door to Paradise is opened and its fragrance reaches him, and his grave is widened as far as he can see. He says that if one drop of Paradise fell into this world it would perfume it, and one drop of the filth of the Fire would spoil it, and that if one of the people of the Fire came out the people of the world would die of his stench. A handsome man in fine clothes with a sweet scent then comes to the believer, brings him good news and says he is his righteous deed, and the believer asks God to bring about the Hour so he may return to his family and wealth. The sheikh adds that in another report, with a sound chain in his judgement, the deed comes after the questioning of the two angels. (needs_source: the descriptions of Paradise and the Fire and the coming of the righteous deed are given with no chain, and calling the other report sound in its chain is the sheikh's own judgement) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:12:00}
- The sheikh describes the unbeliever's state: black-faced angels come down with sackcloth, the soul is scattered through the body and pulled out like a skewer from wet wool, and the doors of heaven are not opened to it. He recites the verse that says such people do not enter Paradise until the camel passes through the eye of a needle (see [[passage/quran-7-40|al-A'raf, verse 40]]). The soul is then thrown down from heaven like someone falling from an aeroplane, returned to the body, and the two angels question him; to every question he answers, I do not know. A caller announces that he has lied, a bed from the Fire is spread for him, a door to the Fire is opened, his grave narrows until his ribs cross, and an ugly man in ugly clothes with a foul smell comes and says he is his deed. The sheikh says this is a hadith of al-Bukhari from the Prophet, sound and agreed upon. This is his own judgement on the hadith, as he stated it in the episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:17:00}
- The sheikh says that speaking of barzakh life is nothing strange to the learned, and tells stories to fix the meaning. He relates that al-Thawri, whom he calls the commander of the believers in hadith, used to visit the graves every Friday and greet their people, stayed away for a month because of illness, and then saw the dead in a dream dragging their shrouds and saying they had been comforted by his visits. (needs_source: the dream story is given with no chain, and the name of al-Thawri is unstable in the machine transcript (a wrong form appears before his title), so it is not linked to a record) He then tells the story of Thabit ibn Qays at the Battle of al-Yamama (see [[person/thabit-ibn-qays|Thabit ibn Qays]] and [[event/battle-of-yamama|the Battle of al-Yamama]]): his armour was stolen from his body as a martyr, and a Companion saw him in a dream describing where the armour lay and who had taken it and giving instructions in a will; the man told Khalid, who found the armour where it was described, and it was handed to Abu Bakr, who carried out the will. The sheikh says it is the first will after death ever carried out (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]] and [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]). (needs_source: the story of Thabit ibn Qays is given with no chain, and calling it the first will carried out after death is the sheikh's own statement) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:20:30}
- The sheikh then relates the dream of Sa'id ibn Zayd (see [[person/said-ibn-zayd|Sa'id ibn Zayd]]), one of the ten promised Paradise: he was on a campaign, his brother in God made him swear to sleep, and the brother heard him say in his sleep that he did not want to go back and that he was addressing al-Ayna' al-Mardiyya. On waking he said that in the dream he had seen a caller announcing that God was pleased with Sa'id, that two angels had mounted him to his place in Paradise, and that a woman among them had said she was his wife from the houris and that he would meet her in three days, and he asked that it not be told until after his death. The sheikh says he was martyred on the stated day, fasting, at sunset. (needs_source: the dream and the martyrdom in battle are given with no chain, and the details of his death in this account are proposed for checking) He also mentions a Companion who forgot to wash after intimacy and went out to battle and was martyred; the Prophet said the angels had washed him and that he prayed over him on tiptoe because of the crowd of angels. (needs_source: the report of the angels' washing is given with no chain, and the name heard in the machine transcript is Hanzala without a lineage, so it is not linked to a record) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:26:00}
- The sheikh relates that one of al-Shafi'i's students (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]) saw him in a dream after his death and asked how he fared, and that al-Shafi'i answered with verses to the effect that they reckoned with him closely, then showed grace and freed him. (needs_source: the dream and the verses attributed to al-Shafi'i are given with no chain, and the student's name is unstable in the machine transcript) He then announces that a report about what happened to Umar in the grave is weak, says he investigated it and found it weak, and warns viewers of this. He cites the report of the Prophet addressing the dead of the well at Badr (see [[concept/al-barzakh|barzakh]]) and says that rejecters of the Sunna, among whom he names the Khawarij, the Rafida and the Qur'aniyyun, do not accept this chapter. He says the punishment of the grave is discussed not for itself but to learn what saves from it. He cites the verse that Pharaoh's people are shown the Fire morning and evening (see [[passage/quran-40-46|Ghafir, verse 46]]) as evidence of punishment in barzakh. These statements and his classification of the groups are his own view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:29:30}
- The sheikh reads a hadith he reports from Samura ibn Jundab in al-Bukhari (see [[person/samura-ibn-jundab|Samura ibn Jundab]]): after the dawn prayer the Prophet used to ask his companions who among them had seen a dream that night, and he told them he had seen two men who took him by the hand and brought him out to the holy land. The sheikh says the dreams of prophets are revelation, while the dreams of others establish no command or prohibition in the law. The scenes follow: a man whose jaw is torn by an iron hook through to his nape and then the other side likewise; a man whose head is struck with a rock and it rolls and then returns as it was; a pit like an oven, narrow at the top and wide below, with naked men and women in it, a flame rising so that they almost come out and then falling back (he mentions the oven boiling over in [[passage/quran-11-40|Hud, verse 40]]); a river of blood with a man in it who is hit in the mouth with a stone whenever he tries to leave; then a green meadow with a great tree, an old man and children at its foot, a man near it kindling a fire, and a house in the tree finer than any he had seen. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:38:00}
