- The sheikh sets out the explanation of the two angels as he gives it from the hadith: the man whose jaw is torn is the liar who tells a lie that is carried to the horizons; the one whose head is crushed is a man God taught the Qur'an who slept on it at night and did not act on it by day; those in the oven-like pit are adulterers; the one in the river of blood is the devourer of usury (see [[concept/riba|riba]]); the old man at the tree is Ibrahim (see [[person/ibrahim|Ibrahim]]) and the children around him are the children of believers; the one kindling the fire is Malik, keeper of the Fire; and the house in the tree is Paradise. He says these pictures are examples and headings, not the whole punishment of the grave, and that they continue until the Day of Resurrection. (needs_source: the details of the explanation are the sheikh's paraphrase and not a verified text, and identifying the house as Paradise is his own interpretation) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:43:00}
- The sheikh comments on the four, lying, leaving the Qur'an unlearned or unacted on, adultery and usury, saying they are causes of the corruption of societies and the collapse of civilisations and nations, and adds on usury that its devourer, its feeder, its scribe and its two witnesses are equal in burden, counting among them the bank employee who writes the contract. This is his view and his application of the hadith to a contemporary reality. (needs_source: the equal burden of the devourer, feeder, scribe and witnesses of usury is given with no chain, and applying it to the bank employee is the sheikh's statement) He cites the report of the man who comes to take another's wealth: he is to be resisted, then fought; if the defender is killed he is in Paradise, and if he kills the thief the thief is in the Fire. The sheikh compares it with man-made laws and says whoever sets a law of his own in place of God's ruling has fallen into unbelief. This is the sheikh's judgement on a disputed matter, and the museum does not adopt it. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:46:00}
- The sheikh says these pictures continue until the Day of Resurrection and that barzakh passes for the believer like the time between the afternoon and sunset prayers, which he does not feel, while for the unbeliever it passes as ages. (needs_source: the comparison of barzakh time for the believer and the unbeliever is given with no chain) The host asks whether this is for the unbeliever or the disobedient Muslim; he replies that when an unbeliever commits these acts they are sins above his unbelief, and addresses Muslims who died without repenting, saying the declaration of faith must be fulfilled with its due, which is prayer, fasting and keeping away from lying, backbiting, slander, consuming people's wealth, injustice, adultery and usury. This is the sheikh's answer and understanding. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:47:00}
- Asked whether supplication, recitation and charity benefit the dead, the sheikh says that when the son of Adam dies his deeds end except three, ongoing charity, knowledge that benefits and a righteous child who prays for him, and adds that the Qur'an is the constitution of a community and not only for reading at graves. He adds that a father who was the cause of his child memorising the Qur'an receives the reward of all the child recites, and that he and the mother are crowned with the crown of honour on the Day of Resurrection, and urges viewers to have their children memorise the Qur'an. (needs_source: the hadith of the three is given in meaning with no chain, and the report of the crown is given with no chain) Scholars differ on whether the reward of recitation reaches the dead, and what the sheikh states here is his own choice. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:49:00}
- The sheikh closes with a description of the world of souls: the souls of those who died earlier gather around the new arrival and ask about this person and that, and the souls of the learned among them say, let him rest from the toil of the world; if he tells them that so-and-so has died, they say his soul did not pass by them, and the souls of the learned say he has gone to his mother, the abyss, a bad mother and a bad nurse. (needs_source: the report of the souls gathering and asking about the living is given with no chain) He then urges preparing to meet God and repenting today before tomorrow, and recalls the hadith that whoever loves meeting God, God loves meeting him (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). He prays for the dead, that God make their graves a meadow of the meadows of Paradise and not a pit of the pits of the Fire and keep them firm at the questioning. This is a supplication, not a ruling. The host bids viewers farewell until next Saturday. (needs_source: the hadith on loving the meeting of God is given in meaning with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep15 00:53:30}

## Episode 16: the third episode on the first stations, the punishment and bliss of the grave, its proofs from the Qur'an and the Sunna, and some of its causes
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host says this is the third part of the first stations and that its subject is the punishment and bliss of the grave, the proof of them from the Qur'an and the Sunna, their causes and what saves from them (see [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|the punishment of the grave]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:01:30}
- The host asks how to answer someone who denies the whole matter, and the sheikh replies that two revelations came to the Prophet, the Book and wisdom, and that the Sunna explains the Book. He gives the example of prayer, which the Qur'an commands while the number of its units was known only from the Prophet, and of zakat, whose amounts were known only from his mouth: two and a half percent, five, ten and twenty. (needs_source: the zakat percentages are given by the sheikh with no attribution) He cites the verse that the Prophet does not speak from desire (see [[passage/quran-53-3|al-Najm, verse 3]] and [[passage/quran-53-4|verse 4]]), the sending of a messenger among the believers who teaches them the Book and wisdom (see [[passage/quran-3-164|Al Imran, verse 164]]), the command to take what the Messenger gives (see [[passage/quran-59-7|al-Hashr, verse 7]]), and following him as the sign of love for God (see [[passage/quran-3-31|Al Imran, verse 31]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:03:30}
- From the Qur'an the sheikh gives the verse of the wrongdoers in the agonies of death with the angels stretching out their hands (see [[passage/quran-6-93|al-An'am, verse 93]]), reading the word "today" in it as the day of death and the grave. Then the verse of Pharaoh's people exposed to the Fire morning and evening (see [[passage/quran-40-46|Ghafir, verse 46]]), saying the Day of Resurrection is a further stage after this exposure. Then the verse of the nearer punishment before the greater (see [[passage/quran-32-21|al-Sajda, verse 21]]), which he explains as the punishment of the grave. Then the verse that the wrongdoers have a punishment short of that (see [[passage/quran-52-47|al-Tur, verse 47]]) and the verse of being punished twice and then sent back to a great punishment (see [[passage/quran-9-101|al-Tawba, verse 101]]), which he takes to mean the grave and then the hereafter. These are his own explanations of the verses. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:05:30}
- From the Sunna he cites the command to seek refuge from the punishment of the grave, which he says is in al-Bukhari, and the supplication after the tashahhud seeking refuge from the trial of life and death and the trial of the grave. He relates the Prophet passing two graves whose occupants were being punished, one for not cleansing himself of urine and the other for going about with tale-bearing, and putting a green palm frond on each in the hope of lightening it while it stayed moist (see [[concept/namimah|tale-bearing]] and [[concept/tahara|purity]]). He also mentions a grave set aflame over a cloak taken from the spoils without right (see [[concept/ghulul|ghulul]]). (needs_source: the attribution of the command to al-Bukhari and the cloak report are given with no chain, and the names of those named with the report are unstable in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:07:00}
- The sheikh says the punishment of the grave is the nearer punishment, lighter than that of the Day of Resurrection, that some sins are punished both in the grave and in the hereafter, such as not cleansing oneself of urine, and that the form of the punishment in the grave is the same as in the hereafter. He says the punishment of the grave does not cancel that of the hereafter except for some Muslims for whom the grave suffices and who come out purified, and relates a report that most of the punishment of this community is in its graves. (needs_source: the report that most of the community's punishment is in its graves is given with no chain) Of unbelievers he says their punishment continues in the grave and after it, which is his own view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:10:00}
- The talk turns to the meeting of souls in barzakh (see [[concept/al-barzakh|barzakh]]). The sheikh says Abu al-Darda used to pray not to do a deed that would shame him before his maternal cousin Abd Allah ibn Rawaha, who died a martyr (see [[person/abu-al-darda|Abu al-Darda]] and [[person/abd-allah-ibn-rawaha|Abd Allah ibn Rawaha]]), and offers this as evidence that the dead are tied to the deeds of the living. He relates a dream of a father smiling at his son and saying he honoured him among the people of the graves, and of another father biting his fingers and saying his son had shamed him among them. (needs_source: both accounts are given with no chain, and the sheikh's wording of his answer to Umm al-Darda is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:12:30}
- The sheikh tells of two companions, Awf ibn Malik and a brother in God whom the transcript renders as Ibn al-Jassama, who agreed that whichever died first would visit the other (see [[person/awf-ibn-malik-al-ashjai|Awf ibn Malik al-Ashja'i]]). The dead one came after a year in a dream, said their souls are in God's hands, and pointed to black marks on his neck, which were ten dinars owed to his Jewish neighbour that he had meant to repay, left in a niche under a stone. He told him the cat had died in a certain place in the house and that his youngest daughter would catch a fever and join him after six days. Awf repaid the debt, the neighbour said no one knew of it but himself, and the child died on the sixth day. (needs_source: the story is given with no chain and some names in it are unstable in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:15:30}
- The host objects that the dead do not know the unseen (see [[concept/ghayb|the unseen]]), and the sheikh replies that this comes from God's knowledge and that once the soul is free of the earthly body it sees what a person does not. This is his own explanation, not tied to a text. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:16:30}
- He says only the souls of believers gather in the world of souls, as they gathered in congregational prayer, festivals, funerals, gatherings of knowledge and relief work, and that they gather in Paradise too. On whether souls die he says scholars hold two views and he favours that the soul does not die, with the verse that every soul tastes death referring to bodies (see [[passage/quran-3-185|Al Imran, verse 185]] and verses of like wording). He cites the martyrs, alive with their Lord and rejoicing (see [[concept/shahid|the martyr]], [[passage/quran-3-169|Al Imran, verse 169]] and [[passage/quran-3-170|verse 170]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:19:00}
- The sheikh says the punishment in the grave falls on the soul, not the body, and describes the body's decay: after four days the eyes liquefy, after eleven days dark patches appear, after two weeks the body swells and then worms appear and eat the flesh until only the tailbone remains, after which they eat one another. He says the worms do not approach a martyr's body in the same grave. He mentions a report that the flash of swords is trial enough for the martyr, and a tale of a man who trembled before a dug grave, a spread shroud and a drawn sword. He adds that the body sits for the questioning, after which the punishment or bliss falls on the soul alone. He says the account of decay comes from scientists' observations. (needs_source: the figures for the stages of decay, the claim about worms and the martyr, the sword report and the tale of the man are given with no source or chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:22:30}
- On what defends the believer in the grave, the sheikh relates that when the two angels come, prayer stands at his head, the Qur'an speaks for him, zakat comes on his right and hajj on his left, and other deeds at his feet, each saying the angels will not come at him through it, so his grave is spread with silk and a seat from Paradise is opened to him. He mentions that a person's deeds end except three, among them a righteous child's prayer, and says our prayers for the dead descend to them on trays of light that illuminate their graves (see [[concept/sadaqa-jariya|ongoing charity]]). He gives these as what has been reported, with no chain. (needs_source: the account of prayer, the Qur'an, zakat and hajj and the trays of light are given with no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:24:30}
- On the causes of the punishment of the grave, the sheikh says he has gathered from the hadiths more than thirty-five, and recalls the four of the previous episode, the liar, not acting on God's Book, adultery and aggression. He adds not cleansing oneself of urine, neglecting the prescribed prayer, withholding zakat, stirring up discord among people and consuming orphans' property, and tells how Umar attended the death of a Companion and, once the soul left, put out the lamp, saying the oil now belonged to the heirs. (needs_source: the figure of thirty-five and the oil report are given with no chain) He criticises what happens at mourning gatherings, the spending of heirs' money, much talk and smoking during the recitation, which is his own view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:30:00}
- He then names the backbiter and those who tear at people's honour, then ghulul, which he applies to the employee who takes from the workplace without right, giving the examples of a sheet of paper, the work phone and the work car used to take his children to school, and says this is unlawful even if the employer does not provide the housing and marriage the employee needs. This is his own position, and he sets out no other view here (see [[concept/ghulul|ghulul]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:33:30}
- He names the caller to innovation and defines innovation as bringing something of one's own to add to the religion, saying it applies to acts of worship, not to customs such as dress or manner of eating. He quotes the hadith that whoever introduces into this matter of ours what is not of it, it is rejected, and holds that Umar's gathering the Muslims for the tarawih prayer was a return to what they did in the Prophet's time, not an innovation (see [[concept/bidah|innovation]] and [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]). (needs_source: the hadith is given with no chain) This is his view, and other views exist that he does not set out. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:35:30}
- Then gifts to workers: he gives the report of Ibn al-Lutbiyya, who brought the zakat of the people of Yemen and said this is for you and this was given to me, at which the Prophet was angry, mounted the pulpit and said that gifts to workers are ghulul (see [[concept/hadaya-al-ummal|gifts to workers]]). He says Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz put his hand over his nose when the perfume of the public treasury was being shared out, and that Umar ibn al-Khattab ordered a trace of treasury perfume on his wife's finger to be washed off. (needs_source: the three reports are given with no chain, and the wife's name in the last is unstable in the transcript) The sheikh distinguishes a gift among relatives and neighbours from a bribe offered to an office-holder because of his work, holds that pupils' gifts to teachers are a bribe, and says an employee should accept no gift and that giver and taker are both at fault. These are his views (see [[concept/hadiyya-fiqh|the gift]]). He relates that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz said it was a gift for the Prophet and a bribe for us. (needs_source: the saying ascribed to Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz is given with no chain) (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:40:30}
- Then the traitor: he cites a hadith in Sahih Muslim that the traitor's greed is never hidden and he betrays even over the smallest thing, and says whoever betrays in the small will betray in the great (see [[concept/khiyana|betrayal]] and [[concept/amana|the trust]]). (needs_source: the hadith and the saying are given with no chain, and some phrasings are unclear in the transcript) Then the deceiver who deceives in family and money, citing the verse that the hypocrites deceive God and deceive only themselves (see [[passage/quran-2-9|al-Baqara, verse 9]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:42:30}
- Then the one who harms Muslims: he relates a man who moved a thorny branch from their path and was admitted to Paradise, and that faith has some seventy branches, the lowest being the removal of harm from the road, with the example of someone who throws a banana peel in the street. (needs_source: both reports are given with no chain) Then the one who spies on Muslims' faults, with a story of a Companion who buried his sister, remembered he had left his purse in the grave, and found the earth hot enough to burn his hand, his mother then saying she used to listen at her neighbours' walls. (needs_source: the story is given with no chain and none of the people is named) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:45:30}
- Then helping one another in sin, citing the verse not to cooperate in sin and aggression (see [[passage/quran-5-2|al-Ma'ida, verse 2]]), and saying seven men of Yemen killed a man and Umar had them all put to death, saying that if the people of Yemen had joined together against him he would have killed them. (needs_source: the report is given with no chain) Then wrongful deviation in the sacred precinct, citing the verse on whoever intends in it deviation with wrong (see [[passage/quran-22-25|al-Hajj, verse 25]]) and the verse that whoever enters it is safe (see [[passage/quran-3-97|Al Imran, verse 97]]), and saying that merely scaring a pigeon off one's shoulder inside it is deviation (see [[concept/ilhad|deviation]]). (needs_source: the claim about the pigeon is given with no chain, and its narrator's name is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:47:30}
- He lists fear of creatures more than of the Creator, citing the verse that you fear people while God has more right to be feared (see [[passage/quran-33-37|al-Ahzab, verse 37]]) and the verse that they hold more dread in their breasts than God (see [[passage/quran-59-13|al-Hashr, verse 13]]). Then the wailing woman and her listener, relating that a woman who wails over a dead person and does not repent is raised with her hair loose, and is like someone who fought her Lord or pulled down the Kaaba, and giving the hadith that whoever strikes the cheeks, tears the pockets and cries the cry of ignorance is not of us, which he says is in al-Bukhari (see [[concept/niyaha-fiqh|wailing]]). He says grief and tears are not blamed and what is asked is patience at the first shock. (needs_source: the hadiths and their ascription to al-Bukhari are given with no chain) The host closes, to continue the conversation in the next episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep16 00:51:30}

## Episode 17: the fourth episode on the first stations, causes of the punishment of the grave: backbiting, slander, tale-bearing, believing a diviner, delaying the pilgrimage and the prayer, cleanliness from urine, and lying
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host thanks viewers for their response to the programme, and the sheikh says his hope is that the words be applied in real life so that Muslims are seen walking the earth by what they learn. The episode continues the previous one on the causes of the punishment of the grave (see [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|the punishment of the grave]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:02:30}
- The host recalls the causes already given: cleanliness from urine, lying, bribery, usury, eating an orphan's property, inventing innovations and spreading lies among people, and says he is afraid of what he has heard. The sheikh answers that he does not mean to frighten anyone but to light lamps so that people are guided and set right what lies between them and God and then their graves. He asks viewers to take paper and pen and note the causes with him. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:03:30}
- The sheikh lists further causes: building mosques over graves and setting lamps and candles on them, cheating in weights and measures (see [[passage/quran-83-1|al-Mutaffifin, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-83-2|verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-83-3|verse 3]]), then slander and backbiting (see [[passage/quran-104-1|al-Humaza, verse 1]]). He explains the first as one who faults you to your face and the second as one who faults you behind your back, and cites the verses on the slanderer who goes about with tales (see [[passage/quran-68-10|al-Qalam, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-68-11|verse 11]]). He says the word woe names a valley in Hell, which is his statement without a source. (needs_source: the claim that woe is a valley in Hell is given by the sheikh without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:05:00}
- The sheikh stresses that a Muslim society is ruined when tongues are loosed on people's honour, and says the law permitted backbiting in six fixed cases, among them a wronged man complaining to the judge to recover his right (see [[concept/ghayba|backbiting]]). He holds that whoever tracks the faults of others and forgets his own has pride or conceit in his heart. He adds that Islam came to stop the attacks on honour that were common among the Arabs, and that the penalty for slander is eighty lashes for whoever accuses a chaste person without four witnesses, because one word can destroy a family. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:07:30}
- The sheikh relates that Umar ibn al-Khattab asked the Companions what they would say if the commander of the faithful saw a man committing indecency with a woman, whether he should apply the penalty, and that Ali answered that he needs four witnesses or the slander penalty falls on him, and that if three testify and the fourth hesitates the penalty is applied to all four. (needs_source: the report about Umar and Ali is given by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:08:30}
- He tells of two Companions walking behind the Prophet, one of whom said of another man that God had disgraced him. They passed the carcass of a donkey, and the Prophet told them to eat from it, explaining that what they had eaten of their brother's honour was worse. He cites the verse about eating a dead brother's flesh (see [[passage/quran-49-12|al-Hujurat, verse 12]]). (needs_source: the story and the hadith are given by the sheikh without a chain) He adds that covering a brother's fault is better, and says that when people came carrying stones, Jesus said that whoever is without sin should take the first stone. (needs_source: the report about Jesus is given with no ascription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:10:30}
- The sheikh says God did not command spying and that the prohibition is general (see [[concept/tajassus-wa-tahassus|spying and prying]]). He holds that a Muslim society should protect its members by covering and advising rather than exposing and shaming, and that one who finds himself in a gathering where people's honour is discussed should leave it. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:12:30}
- On attacks on the early generations, the sheikh says one cause of the punishment of the grave is speaking ill of Abu Bakr, Umar, Muawiya, Amr ibn al-As and Abu Musa al-Ashari (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/muawiya-ibn-abi-sufyan|Muawiya ibn Abi Sufyan]], [[person/amr-ibn-al-as|Amr ibn al-As]] and [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ashari]]). He calls the arbitration a trial from which God kept our hands clean so that we should not stain our tongues with it, and ascribes this saying to [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]. He holds a Muslim should be silent about the disputes among the Companions and that the hadith of the killer and the killed both being in the Fire does not apply to the generation of the Companions. This is his view, and the matter is disputed among Muslims. (needs_source: the ascription to al-Hasan al-Basri and the hadith are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:16:30}
- Speaking of the Arabs before Islam, the sheikh says usury was established and that the usury of al-Abbas ibn Abd al-Muttalib brought him forty thousand dinars every day. (needs_source: the figure of forty thousand dinars a day is given by the sheikh with no source) He then relates that in the Farewell Pilgrimage the Prophet abolished usury and the blood-claims of the pre-Islamic era, beginning with the usury of al-Abbas and the blood of Rabia ibn al-Harith. (needs_source: the text of the sermon is given without a chain) He holds that the method set out in that sermon is what mends the community and that any departure from the Book and the Sunna brings evil. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:14:30}
- The sheikh turns to the diviner and the soothsayer, relating the hadith that whoever goes to a diviner or soothsayer and believes what he says is free of the protection of God and His Messenger, and a second that whoever goes and does not believe him has no deed accepted for forty days. (needs_source: both hadiths are given without a chain) He lists today's forms: reading palms and coffee cups, the mandal and cowrie shells, and horoscopes. He says none knows the unseen but God (see [[concept/ghayb|the unseen]] and [[concept/kahana|divination]]), citing that the Prophet does not know what will be done with him or with them (see [[passage/quran-46-9|al-Ahqaf, verse 9]]), that had he known the unseen he would have gathered much good (see [[passage/quran-7-188|al-Araf, verse 188]]), and the verse of the five matters (see [[passage/quran-31-34|Luqman, verse 34]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:19:00}
- The sheikh says the jinn used to eavesdrop and were barred when the Prophet was sent, with flames set against them (see [[passage/quran-72-8|al-Jinn, verse 8]] and [[passage/quran-72-9|verse 9]]), and that some men used to seek refuge with men of the jinn and increased them in folly (see [[passage/quran-72-6|al-Jinn, verse 6]] and [[concept/jinn|the jinn]]). Of Solomon he says images were allowed in his law (see [[passage/quran-34-13|Saba, verse 13]] and [[passage/quran-34-14|verse 14]]), that each community has a law and way (see [[passage/quran-5-48|al-Maida, verse 48]]) and that this is now complete, and that that was a community that has passed (see [[passage/quran-2-134|al-Baqara, verse 134]]). This is his own explanation of what he calls the nonsense of magic and undoing spells (see [[concept/sihr|magic]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:21:00}
- The sheikh notes that the sorcerer asks for the name of the visitor's mother, and then relates a hadith urging good names for children, since a person is called on the Day of Rising by his own name and his father's. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) He says the Qur'an names no woman but Mary, for a particular reason, and that this is no slight to others. He then takes up amulets, relating that whoever hangs an amulet will not have God complete his affair, and holds that the Throne verse is recited, not carried, and that the Qur'an protects by reading and acting upon it, not by carrying it. This is his view and scholars differ on the matter. (needs_source: the amulet hadith is given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:23:30}
- The sheikh takes up whoever is able to perform the pilgrimage and does not, relating the hadith that such a person may die a Jew or a Christian. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) He cites the verse on the pilgrimage for whoever is able to make the journey (see [[passage/quran-3-97|Al Imran, verse 97]] and [[concept/hajj-rulings|the rulings of the pilgrimage]]). He advises anyone able to go while young, since it is harder in old age, and answers those who put it off until retirement. On going for one's father, he says the son should first have performed his own pilgrimage, and that he may go for a father who has died or was unable, out of devotion. He mentions a discussion of father and mother and says some scholars put the mother first, without settling it himself. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:26:30}
- The sheikh speaks of delaying the prayer to the end of its time, saying this is a cause of the punishment of the grave when done without excuse. He relates a saying he ascribes to Abu Bakr, though the name is unclear in the transcript, that the prayer has approval at the start of its time, mercy in the middle and forgiveness at the end. (needs_source: the saying and its ascription are given without a chain) He likens the heart to two mirrors, one at the house entrance and one in the bathroom, the second clouded by steam, as the heart is by sins. To those in work that cannot stop, such as a doctor or an air-traffic controller, he says the law follows welfare and a Muslim weighs prayer against harm to others. He cites the prayer of fear as evidence that prayer lapses only for the dead. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:31:30}
- The sheikh relates that a man asked Umar about one who performs ablution and prays, and what of one who does not pray, and Umar looked at him and said he spoke of Muslims. He says the bonds of Islam are undone one by one and the last is the prayer. (needs_source: the report about Umar and the undoing of the bonds is given without a chain) He cites that prayer is a timed duty for the believers (see [[passage/quran-4-103|al-Nisa, verse 103]]). He says God showed mercy by hiding the sound of the punishment of the grave from us, and relates that the Prophet's mule leapt and almost threw him because she heard it, and that all creatures hear it except humans and jinn. (needs_source: the story and the hadith are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:34:30}
- The sheikh speaks of tale-bearing, defining it as carrying words to set people against each other, while backbiting is mentioning your brother in a way he dislikes. He relates a hadith that the worst of God's servants are those who go about with tales, who divide loved ones and who seek faults in the innocent. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) He says God is kind and merciful to whoever knocks and asks forgiveness, but that there is a difference between one who sins ashamed and one who sins, encourages others and mocks the repentant. He cites the verses on those who sinned and laughed at believers (see [[passage/quran-83-29|al-Mutaffifin, verse 29]], [[passage/quran-83-30|verse 30]], [[passage/quran-83-31|verse 31]] and [[passage/quran-83-34|verse 34]]), and Luqman's counsel to his son to enjoin good and bear patiently what befalls him (see [[passage/quran-31-17|Luqman, verse 17]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:38:00}
- The sheikh says that when someone brings words about a brother, one should answer: first that he has clouded the purity between me and my brother, second that he has betrayed a confidence, third that he will carry words about me too, and fourth that his duty was to advise for God's sake. He says provision is in God's hands, citing the verses (see [[passage/quran-51-22|al-Dhariyat, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-51-23|verse 23]] and [[passage/quran-11-6|Hud, verse 6]]). He relates the hadith that one is not of us who turns a woman against her husband. (needs_source: the hadith is given without a chain) He likens the seeker of faults to the fly, which settles on sores. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:42:30}
- The sheikh moves to cleanliness from urine, saying people treat it lightly and that one with a continuing condition has the ruling of the excused (see [[concept/wudu-fiqh|ablution]]). He relates how Fatima bint Abi Hubaysh asked the Prophet about her bleeding, and he told her to stop praying during her days of menses, then wash, make ablution and pray, and says this is in Sahih Muslim. (needs_source: the hadith and its ascription to Sahih Muslim are given without a chain) He cites a saying of Ibn al-Qayyim that prayer is like a slave-girl offered as a gift to a king, who would not be given one with a defect. He holds that purity is a condition of the prayer's validity and not a pillar, since a condition lies outside the thing and a pillar is part of it (see [[passage/quran-5-6|al-Maida, verse 6]] and [[concept/tahara|purity]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:47:30}
- The sheikh closes with lying, saying it is allowed only to reconcile those who quarrel, and that no lie is small in God's sight (see [[concept/kadhib|lying]] and [[passage/quran-24-15|al-Nur, verse 15]]). He adds that whoever claims to love Paradise yet does not obey is a liar, and whoever claims to fear the Fire yet does not leave sins is a liar. He says God did not ask people for prayer through the whole day or fasting through the whole year but legislated what He knew they could bear, and ends with the verse (see [[passage/quran-2-286|al-Baqara, verse 286]]). The episode ends with prayer and farewell and a promise of the next episode. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep17 00:50:30}

## Episode 18: the fifth and closing episode on the first stations, causes of the punishment of the grave: looking, indecency, the orphan's property, usury, neglect of prayer, withholding zakat, preachers of discord, innovation, bribery and treachery
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host recalls the previous episode (tale-bearing, not keeping clean of urine, lying, not acting on the Quran) and turns to indecency (see [[concept/zina|zina]]), putting to the sheikh the claim that some people are driven to it by the cost of marriage: dowry, housing, the deferred dowry and gifts. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:01:30}
- The sheikh begins with the gaze, saying that the look is an arrow of Iblis and that whoever guards it is given a faith whose sweetness he finds, and that whoever lowers his gaze is given insight (see [[concept/ghadd-al-basar|lowering the gaze]]). He says that habit dulls the conscience, and that religion came down to a society, so that the whole society must restrain those who spread indecency on screens, giving the parable of the people who cast lots for places on a ship. (needs_source: the ship parable and the saying on the look as an arrow of Iblis are given by the sheikh without a chain) He adds that preachers should be lamps and not stars, that the community falls ill but does not die, and that respectable households still exist, and he urges parents to teach children how to use the screen. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:05:30}
- On mixed workplaces and universities the sheikh says that dealings between the sexes should stay within the limit of need, and that a woman who works or studies goes for a purpose, knowledge or a living, and should fear God in it. He recalls the two women who watered their flock in the story of Moses (see [[passage/quran-28-23|al-Qasas, verse 23]]) and says, as his own view, that a woman's first place of work is raising the children. He criticises following the latest fashion without thought, linking it to the saying that the community will follow those before it. (needs_source: the saying that the community will follow the ways of those before it is cited by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:09:00}
- The sheikh holds that the look, touch and walking toward it are the preliminaries of indecency, and that a man who looks for twenty years and calls it the first glance deceives himself, since the first glance is the one he turns away at once. For one who has fallen he gives repentance, asking forgiveness, many good deeds and praying for the guardian of the woman he wronged, adding that the wronged man will receive those good deeds on the Day of Resurrection. He quotes a saying that adultery with ten women is lighter than with a neighbour's wife. (needs_source: the saying about the neighbour's wife is given by the sheikh without a chain) He says the repentant, man or woman, is by God's leave spared the punishment of the grave (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:11:00}
- On the cost of marriage the sheikh says, as his own view, that the first outlet for zakat today is helping young Muslims to marry (see [[concept/zakat|zakat]] and [[concept/nikah-fiqh|marriage]]), following what he says Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz did, and that late marriage is a great indecency for which the whole society bears blame. (needs_source: the report about Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz paying for marriages from the zakat is given by the sheikh without a chain) He proposes that each wealthy person marry off a few young men from his zakat and that each of them later do the same for another. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:12:30}
- The sixth cause is eating the property of orphans (see [[concept/yatim-fiqh|the orphan]]). The sheikh gives sayings that God is angered when an orphan weeps, that mercy covers whoever strokes an orphan's head, and that the best house is one where an orphan is honoured. (needs_source: the sayings on the orphan are quoted by the sheikh without a chain) He tells a story from the Children of Israel in which Isa sees Iblis holding honey and ashes. (needs_source: the story of Isa and Iblis is given by the sheikh without a chain) He urges listeners to be a father to an orphan, take him on trips and buy him new clothes for the feast. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:14:00}
- On usury (see [[concept/riba|riba]]) the sheikh says it is one of the two matters over which God announced war (see [[passage/quran-2-279|al-Baqara, verse 279]]). It is his view, and he states it as the agreement of the fiqh academies of the last fifty years, that modern bank interest is usury, and he rejects the argument that the Quranic prohibition does not cover it; other contemporary scholars differ on this question. He says whoever deals in it has no excuse, and cites a report of an economic conference in Paris in 1988 that said inflation would end only if interest returned to zero. (needs_source: the report of the Paris conference of 1988 is given by the sheikh from memory without a reference) He says God opens lawful alternatives such as partnership, profit-sharing, sale with a stated margin and the good loan, and that the repentant should return his capital and leave the interest. He cites the report that the first usury the Prophet waived was that of al-Abbas ibn Abd al-Muttalib (see [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]]). (needs_source: the report on al-Abbas is given by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:20:00}
- On the neglect of prayer the sheikh tells of a woman who swept the Prophet's mosque, whose death went unnoticed, and of how he prayed over her grave and heard that the best deed she found was tending the houses of God. (needs_source: the story of the woman who swept the mosque is told by the sheikh without a chain, and the name he gives her is not confirmed here) He says that no one should be imposed on people as imam, and quotes a saying that the worst of people before God is a man whom his people dislike as imam. (needs_source: this saying is quoted by the sheikh without a chain) He compares the voluntary prayers to the garden in front of a house, which wakes the house before the thief reaches the door. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:24:30}
- On one who has missed many prayers the sheikh recalls the opinion attributed to Imam Ahmad that one who abandons prayer is outside the faith (see [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]] and [[concept/abandoning-prayer-takfir|abandoning prayer]]), and gives his own advice: to make up one or two prayers with each time, according to ability, and, if he intends to start today and dies before doing so, that sincere intent counts. He says there is no despair for one who missed a prayer, a fast or zakat. The ruling on making up missed prayers is a matter on which the schools differ, and this is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:27:30}
- On withholding zakat the sheikh says one may not give it to those one is bound to support, meaning parents and grandparents and children and grandchildren, while a wealthy wife may give it to a poor husband. He tells of Zaynab, wife of Abdullah ibn Masud, who sent Bilal to ask the Prophet this question (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Masud]]). (needs_source: the report of the wife of Ibn Masud is related by the sheikh without a chain) He adds, as his own view, that a husband should not take his wife's earnings, since she is not bound to spend. He recounts that Moses, reading the tablets, asked to belong to the community of Ahmad, naming Ibn Abbas as a source. (needs_source: the story of Moses and the tablets is related by the sheikh with only a mention of Ibn Abbas and no chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:31:00}
- On the preachers of discord the sheikh distinguishes the preacher who acts on what he says from one who is not qualified, and warns against those who raise suspicion around a known person, set people against rulers or rulers against people, and unbalance the rights of man and wife or parent and child. He urges advice by wisdom and fair exhortation. He gives as examples Khalid ibn al-Walid after his dismissal under Umar and Umar's saying at his death that women could not bear another like him, and the withdrawal of Abu Dharr to al-Rabadha after he differed with Uthman, his death there and his burial by Ibn Masud. (needs_source: the accounts of Khalid, Umar and Abu Dharr are told by the sheikh without chains) He also gives sayings of the Prophet on Abu Dharr and Jibril's greeting to him. (needs_source: the sayings on Abu Dharr are quoted without a chain) (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]] and [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr]]) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:36:00}
- The sheikh turns to the one who takes from his workplace what is not his (see [[concept/ghulul|the stolen cloak]]), saying he should return it without exposing himself, or, if he cannot, give its value in charity without expecting a reward for it. He says that when a servant repents, God replaces his sins with good deeds and makes the angels, the earth and his own limbs forget what they witnessed. (needs_source: the claim that God makes the witnesses forget is stated by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:38:30}
- On innovation (see [[concept/bidah|innovation]]) the sheikh says the religion is complete and that keeping to the Sunnah is better than striving in innovation. He says that anniversaries of a death, obituary notices and reciting suras to give the reward to the dead are not part of what the early generations did, and that a person should work for himself instead of relying on others. The question of whether such rewards reach the dead is one on which scholars differ, and this is his view. He says lineage will not speed one whom his deeds have slowed, and tells that the Prophet told his Companions not to curse the father of Ikrima ibn Abi Jahl, since it hurts the living and does not reach the dead (see [[person/ikrima-ibn-abi-jahl|Ikrima]] and [[person/al-walid-ibn-al-mughira|al-Walid ibn al-Mughira]]). (needs_source: the sayings on lineage and on Ikrima are quoted without a chain) He recites the verses on al-Walid ibn al-Mughira (see [[passage/quran-74-11|al-Muddaththir, verse 11]] to [[passage/quran-74-14|verse 14]]) and says that his son al-Walid ibn al-Walid became a Muslim, and that Muslims should be good children so that their good deeds reach their parents. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:44:00}
- On bribery the sheikh rejects the claim of necessity, saying that whoever pays shares in corrupting the official, and gives the saying that God's provision is not sought through disobedience. (needs_source: the saying on seeking provision through obedience is quoted by the sheikh without a chain) He says the guardian who learns of it should dismiss the offender and that reform begins with oneself. He then speaks of treachery in prayer, in the gaze, in wealth, in office and in dealings with people (see [[concept/khiyana|treachery]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:47:30}
- The sheikh adds deceit, cunning, harming Muslims, seeking out people's faults, helping in sin and aggression, and being among the helpers of the unjust, giving the story of a man who sewed a ruler's clothes and asked if he was one of them. (needs_source: the story of the man who sewed clothes is told by the sheikh without a chain) He says each of these should be left and repented of. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:50:00}
- The host recites the whole list of causes covered through the series: not keeping clean of urine, lying, not acting on the Quran, indecency, eating orphans' property, usury, neglect of prayer, withholding zakat, preachers of discord, backbiting, plunging into people's honour, taking from the workplace, calling to innovation, bribery, treachery, deceit, cunning, harming Muslims, seeking out faults, helping in sin, impiety in the sanctuary, putting one's opinion before the Sunnah, wailing and listening to it, building mosques over graves, cheating in measure, slander, insulting the early generations, going to diviners and soothsayers, helping the unjust, not performing the pilgrimage though able and delaying prayer to its last moment. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:52:00}
- The sheikh closes by saying that he gave this list so that listeners wake from heedlessness, repent and work for good, and that for the one who fears God, necessity becomes light. He adds that supplication at dawn is answered, thanks the host, and says the next episode begins the deeds that save from the punishment of the grave. The episode ends with farewell. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep18 00:54:00}

## Episode 19: the first station closes with the remaining causes of the punishment of the grave (hair, highway robbery, obscene speech, fatwa without knowledge, cruelty to animals, theft, the stand-in husband, abandoning the oppressed, judging by other than revelation) and the first rescuers begin: dutifulness to parents and wudu
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the verse on the thief (see [[passage/quran-5-38|al-Ma'ida, verse 38]]). The host says the series is about to turn to the rescuers from the punishment of the grave, but that a number of causes have not yet been mentioned and the sheikh agrees to cover them in a few minutes, since knowledge is a trust. The host recalls that more than thirty-three causes have been counted so far, the last being preoccupation with the faults of others. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:03:00}
- The sheikh names the woman who shows her hair to those she is free to marry, whom he calls the stranger, and says this is a cause of the punishment in the barzakh; he gives a saying of the Prophet on women who are clothed yet bare and sway as they walk. (needs_source: the description of the punishment attached to the hair and the saying on women who are clothed yet bare are given by the sheikh without a chain) He argues that because only the hair is named, the rest of the body is graver still. Asked about a woman who is devout in all else yet does not cover, he says that whoever denies a matter known from the religion by necessity leaves the faith, and that a woman who does not cover has no claim on the verses addressed to believing women (see [[passage/quran-24-31|al-Nur, verse 31]] and [[concept/hijab|hijab]]); he says the scholars agree that the permitted display is either the face and hands or, in another view, the whole body. This is his view and his statement of consensus; the conditions for declaring a person outside the faith, and the extent of what may be shown, are matters on which scholars differ. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:06:30}
- The next cause is highway robbery (see [[concept/hirabah|hirabah]]), which he describes as taking people's property and lives with arms and a gang. He adds the one who fears the creature and not the Creator, such as the employee who works while the manager is present and slacks when he is away, and says that Islam is sincerity and being watched by God whether or not the employer is present. (needs_source: the saying that the believer is neither indecent nor foul-mouthed is quoted by the sheikh without a chain) He then names the foul-mouthed, saying that harm done by the tongue also harms the angels, and that the remedy is repentance, reading the Quran often and sitting with people of knowledge. He cites a saying of Abu Musa al-Ash'ari, as the transcript renders it, that he loved the world only for three things: a verse he recites, a saying he hears from the Prophet, or sitting with people who choose the best words as one picks the best fruit (see [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]]). (needs_source: this saying is attributed by the sheikh without a source, and the speaker's name is uncertain in the transcription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:09:00}
- On giving a fatwa without knowledge the sheikh says that the mufti must have studied the Book and the Sunnah and the principles of jurisprudence, and that a specialist in hadith, biography or rhetoric is not for that reason a mufti. He gives a saying that the boldest of you in giving a fatwa is the boldest toward the Fire and says that the early imams answered "I do not know". (needs_source: the saying on boldness in fatwa is related by the sheikh without a chain) He says that the Companions asked the Prophet only thirteen questions, and he counts it better to say "I do not know" and enter Paradise than to give a fatwa without knowledge and enter the Fire; he tells of nights without sleep over a fatwa given in doubt. (needs_source: the count of thirteen questions and the figure of six answers out of forty-two are stated by the sheikh without a reference) He adds that Umar, as he transcribes it, named Mu'adh ibn Jabal for jurisprudence, Ubayy ibn Ka'b for the Quran and Ali for inheritance, and himself for wealth (see [[person/muadh-ibn-jabal|Mu'adh ibn Jabal]], [[person/ubayy-ibn-kab|Ubayy ibn Ka'b]] and [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali]]). (needs_source: this report on the specialities named by Umar is given without a chain, and the transcription is unclear on the person named for inheritance and the Quran) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:13:30}
- The sheikh turns to cruelty to animals, saying God gathers the animals on the Day of Resurrection and that the harm done to them is accounted for; he tells that Umar, finding children playing with a bird tied by a string, cut the string and freed it, and that Ibn Abbas saw him in a dream a year after his death and heard that God forgave him because he had freed a creature (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]]). (needs_source: the story of Umar and the bird and the dream of Ibn Abbas are told by the sheikh without a chain) He says that animals are told on that day to become dust, and that the unbeliever then wishes he had been dust. (needs_source: the words said to the animals are given by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:16:00}
- On the act of the people of Lut (see [[concept/liwat-fiqh|liwat]] and [[person/lut|Lut]]) the sheikh describes a torment in the barzakh, and says that in the classical juristic view the offender is thrown from the highest tower of the city; he says no law should bless it in the name of personal freedom, and that civilisations fall when their morals fall, referring to a work on the story of civilisation without naming its author. He applies the same ruling to women with women. This is his statement of a classical position; scholars differ on the penalty and on its application. (needs_source: the reference to the story of civilisation and its author is not named by the sheikh) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:18:00}
- On theft he says the hadd penalty exists but has long conditions in fiqh (that the property be secured, among others), and that the ruler or jurist judges a claim of need (see [[concept/hudud-penalties|hudud penalties]]). He then gives the muhallil, the man who marries a woman divorced three times only so she may return to her first husband, quoting a saying that this is the borrowed billy-goat and that God cursed the muhallil and the one for whom it is done. (needs_source: the saying on the borrowed billy-goat is quoted without a chain) He calls such a marriage void and says it was practised before Islam and returned among Muslims in later times (see [[concept/talaq|talaq]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:20:00}
- The sheikh gives the one who devises a pretext to drop an obligation (see [[concept/ihtiyal|ihtiyal]]), as when a person pleads illness to skip a duty, and gives the story of the Children of Israel who set their nets on Friday for the fish that came on the Sabbath. He adds the one who passes an oppressed person who asks for help and does not help, saying that Islam is positive and that one helps any person, of any faith, since injustice is common to people, and that a Muslim who wants the wider injustice lifted should first lift it between spouses, neighbours and employer and worker. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:21:30}
- On judging by other than what God revealed, the sheikh says that one who holds his own law better than God's law is in grave error, and that one who rules by another law without holding it better is a transgressor, since he sets aside God's words. This is his view; scholars differ on the grading of each case. He tells how Umar, missing a man from the dawn prayer, struck him with the whip when the man answered that he was ill and had slept. (needs_source: the account of Umar and the absent man is told by the sheikh without a chain) He adds harming one's neighbours and the wife who loads her husband beyond his means with demands, and says each cause has its own picture of torment in the barzakh and on the Day of Resurrection, but that he does not wish to dwell on the images, only to ask listeners to repent now. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:24:30}
- The host and the sheikh agree that some viewers were frightened by the series. The host recites the whole list of forty-seven causes: tale-bearing, not keeping clean of urine, lying, not acting on the Quran, indecency, eating orphans' property, usury, neglect of prayer, withholding zakat, preachers of discord, backbiting, plunging into people's honour, taking from the workplace, calling to innovation, bribery, treachery, deceit, cunning, harming Muslims, seeking out faults, helping in sin, impiety in the sanctuary, putting opinion before the Sunnah, wailing and listening to it, building mosques over graves, cheating in measure, slander, insulting the early generations, going to diviners and soothsayers, helping the unjust, not performing the pilgrimage though able, delaying prayer, preoccupation with others' faults, uncovered hair, harming neighbours, burdening the husband, highway robbery, fearing the creature and not the Creator, foul speech, fatwa without knowledge, cruelty to animals, the act of the people of Lut, theft, the muhallil, abandoning the oppressed, judging by other than revelation and devising pretexts to drop obligations. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:28:00}
- The sheikh cites a saying of Ibn Abbas, who was asked about sitting with people who frighten, that sitting with one who makes you fear God, so that you find safety on the Day, is better than sitting with those who reassure you and leave you in fear then. (needs_source: this saying of Ibn Abbas is given without a chain) He says that knowing the end should help us begin well, and proposes to treat the rescuers briefly. For the forty-seven he suggests a practical plan: give a week to each, abstaining one week from lying, another from theft, another from harming neighbours and so on, so that within about a year each is dropped; if a person keeps to it, God will have accepted his repentance by his intention. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:34:00}
- The sheikh begins the rescuers by saying that they are easy for those whom God makes it easy, and quotes the verse that prayer is hard save for the humble. He tells of a Muslim villager in Russia who understood no Arabic, who was lucky in a draw to keep the village's single copy of the Quran for a night, and who would open it and say that it was great speech without knowing a word; after his death at ninety a white dove was seen over the burial and a fragrance was smelled by those present. (needs_source: the story of the villager and the dove is told by the sheikh from a relative's account, with no further source) He then tells that God told Moses to tell a servant who had disobeyed for twenty years and been absent for twenty more that if he returns he will be accepted. (needs_source: this report is related by the sheikh without a chain) He quotes it in a rhythmic form in which God says the penitent is a beloved, the one who does not repent is one whom He heals, and that He is more merciful to them than a mother. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:33:30}
- The first rescuer is dutifulness to parents, from a saying of the Prophet, given as a dream seen at night, of a man of the community to whom the angel of death came and was turned back by his dutifulness to his parents. (needs_source: the saying of the dream and its parts, the rescue by dutifulness to parents, the wudu, the dhikr, the prayer, the fast of Ramadan, the ghusl, the pilgrimage, charity and joining kin, is quoted by the sheikh without a chain, and the same applies to the individual parts below) The sheikh says the angel of death has names, time and place, and that this is the one case in which he is turned back; he says that no amount of good will bring the undutiful child into Paradise and no evil will bar the dutiful one, and urges returning to one's parents even if they have wronged or expelled one, while keeping to the command to keep company with them in a good manner (see [[practice/birr-al-walidayn|dutifulness to parents]]), noting that this applies even if they urge unbelief, in which case one does not obey them. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:38:00}
- The second rescuer is wudu, from the part of the saying about the man over whom the punishment of the grave was spread like a carpet, saved when his wudu came (see [[practice/wudu|wudu]]). The sheikh says that research in hospitals in America found that nervous patients calmed after wetting hands, face and head, and quotes a World Health Organization statistic that skin cancer should be common in the Middle East because of the sun yet is not, which he attributes to wudu. (needs_source: the American hospital research and the World Health Organization figures, whose numbers are unclear in the transcript, are stated by the sheikh without a reference) He urges going out with wudu, and gives the sayings that sins fall from the limbs with each wash and that the Prophet knows his followers who come after him by the brightness on their faces and limbs from wudu. (needs_source: the sayings on sins falling and on the marks of wudu are quoted without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:41:30}
- The third is the remembrance of God (see [[practice/dhikr|dhikr]]), the saying that the one whose tongue is moist with it has sins that fall away. (needs_source: the saying on the moist tongue and the tree planted in Paradise is quoted by the sheikh without a chain) He gives that a tree is planted in Paradise for whoever goes to his market and says the words of glorification, and the two words light on the tongue and heavy in the scale. He adds the story of David, in which the angels could not find the reward for his praise, as befits God's majesty, and God told them to write what he had said and He would reward him. (needs_source: the story of David and the angels is related by the sheikh without a chain) He says each situation has its own phrase, and that a person who makes it a habit finds his tongue moist. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:45:00}
- The fourth is prayer (see [[practice/salah|prayer]]), in its time, with humility and in its pillars, which put out the fires of sin. The sheikh says that the Prophet rested his eye's coolness in prayer, and tells of a righteous man who spent twenty years urging himself into prayer and twenty more unable to leave it. (needs_source: the account of the righteous man and the sayings on the night prayer are given without a chain, and the name of a scholar quoted at the end is unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:47:30}
