- The fifth is the fast of Ramadan (see [[practice/sawm-ramadan|the Ramadan fast]]), which quenches the man turned from the basin in thirst; the sixth, ghusl after major impurity (see [[practice/ghusl-al-janaba|ghusl]] and [[concept/janaba|janaba]]), which he says should be taken without delay; the seventh, the pilgrimage and the umra (see [[practice/hajj|hajj]]), which bring one out of darkness into light; the eighth, charity, which shields from the Fire, even if small (see [[concept/sadaqa|sadaqa]]); and the ninth, joining kin, which causes believers to speak to a man to whom they had not spoken. The sheikh adds that he stops at joining kin and will continue the next day, and calls on viewers to do these things at once. The episode ends with the sheikh thanked and farewell. (needs_source: the parts of the saying on fasting, ghusl, pilgrimage, charity and joining kin are quoted without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep19 00:51:30}

## Episode 20: the rescuers from the punishment of the grave continue, from enjoining good and gentleness to good character, fear and hope, the children who died young, tears, a good opinion of God, blessing on the Prophet and the testimony of faith, seventeen in all
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the verses on kindness to parents (see [[passage/quran-17-23|al-Isra, verse 23]] and [[passage/quran-17-24|verse 24]]). The host recalls that the previous episode stopped at the rescuers beginning with dutifulness to parents, wudu, prayer, dhikr, the Ramadan fast, ghusl, the pilgrimage and the umra, and joining kin. He passes on a message from a woman viewer, Umm Ahmad, who thanks the sheikh and asks him not to frighten people, and the sheikh replies that he leans toward ease and mercy, but that what the Book and the Sunnah say has to be made clear. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:03:00}
- The sheikh returns to the dream the Prophet related, in which the sheikh counts about seventeen kinds of rescuer; he holds that the dream of a prophet is true and a kind of revelation, and that these acts join worship to conduct and so form a strong Muslim who is saved in this world, in the barzakh and on the Day of Resurrection (see [[concept/al-barzakh|the barzakh]] and [[concept/adhab-al-qabr|the punishment of the grave]]). The first rescuer of this episode is enjoining good and forbidding evil: a man of the community surrounded by the zabaniya, the angels of the Fire, is rescued from them by his enjoining good and forbidding evil, and the angels of mercy take him. (needs_source: the saying of the dream and each of its parts are quoted by the sheikh without a chain, and the same applies to every rescuer below) He describes the zabaniya as carrying maces, hooks and chains (see [[passage/quran-22-21|al-Hajj, verse 21]]), under Malik, keeper of the Fire, with nineteen over it, and says the people of the Fire call to Malik to let their Lord make an end of them (see [[passage/quran-43-77|al-Zukhruf, verse 77]]) and that he answers after a thousand years. (needs_source: the length of the wait for Malik's answer and the description of the Fire's guardians beyond the verses are given by the sheikh without a reference, and the passage is partly garbled in the transcription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:06:30}
- On enjoining good and forbidding evil (see [[concept/amr-bil-maruf|enjoining good]]), the sheikh gives three principles: that the one who enjoins knows what he commands and forbids, acts by it, and is gentle. For knowledge he sets a minimum that every Muslim should hold, not the whole of the sources, which he leaves to scholars and callers: the rules of purity and of water, the pillars, sunnas and invalidators of wudu and of prayer, the prostrations of forgetfulness and of recitation, the pillars of the fast and questions such as eye drops, nose drops, enemas and glucose (see [[practice/sawm-ramadan|the Ramadan fast]]), zakat al-fitr and when to pay it (see [[practice/zakat-al-fitr|zakat al-fitr]]), the zakat on crops watered by rain and by irrigation (see [[concept/zakat|zakat]]), the rites of the pilgrimage (see [[practice/hajj|hajj]]) and the rules of sale, for which he would have every Muslim state require trained traders before licensing them. He gives the example of a mother who tells her daughter not to pray for forty days after childbirth even after the bleeding has ended, and says the four imams hold that she should pray. This is his statement of the schools' position, which is a matter for the jurists to confirm. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:13:00}
- He says that someone without that knowledge should neither command nor forbid, and warns against answering a question such as praying eight rak'ahs in congregation after Ramadan with "it is all blessing", or arranging a prayer on New Year's eve for the people of evil, which he calls innovations (see [[concept/bidah|innovation]]); this is his judgement of those practices. He gives the story of a man who asked al-Hasan al-Basri to speak on the merit of freeing slaves; the preacher stayed silent for three days, and a year later, in Ramadan, spoke of it, explaining that he had been poor, that God had enriched him, and that he had bought slaves and freed them, so his words came from the heart (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]). (needs_source: the story of al-Hasan al-Basri and the slaves is told by the sheikh without a source, and its wording is partly garbled in the transcription) The second principle, acting by what one commands, he illustrates by the person who tells others to eat less while himself eating too much, or who calls backbiting forbidden and then speaks ill of someone. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:16:30}
- The third principle, gentleness, he grounds in three points: that the person addressed is his brother and so dear to him, that those people have left themselves to him that he may plant good in them and so have a claim on him, and that the person is weak, in his sight, in prayer or in giving. He recommends starting with love, a smile and gentle words, quoting the scholars or a story rather than rebuking directly, and recalls the Prophet's instruction not to cut off the man who began to urinate in the mosque. (needs_source: the story of the man who urinated in the mosque is related by the sheikh without a chain) He adds that harshness, even that of Musa, brought no result, and relates that the Prophet on the night journey heard a loud voice and Jibril said it was his brother Musa, whose sharpness God accepted because of those he was with. (needs_source: this account of the voice of Musa on the night journey is given by the sheikh without a source, and the transcription of it is unclear) He gives household examples, an unveiled wife or a husband who does not pray, advising a fitting moment and an approach through the person's own interest (see [[person/musa|Musa]] and [[person/jibril|Jibril]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:20:30}
- He speaks sharply against those he calls wolf-hearted, who see only faults and are quick to declare others outside the faith, and says no one should be declared an unbeliever except on clear proof or on the person's own statement of it (see [[concept/takfir|takfir]]). He quotes the call to the way of the Lord with wisdom and good counsel (see [[passage/quran-16-125|al-Nahl, verse 125]]) and notes that wisdom is named first. He likens the sins that cover a Muslim's heart to dust on a piece of gold with the testimony of unity in it, which needs a skilled hand to clean it without scratching the gold. On the saying that whoever sees an evil should change it by his hand, he says that this does not mean beating, chains or smashing shops but a constructive act: if one dislikes a school one founds a better one, and if one dislikes an interest-bearing bank one starts an Islamic bank; changing by hand belongs to those with authority, such as a ruler, who he says may use the lash as Umar did against a legal violation, and to parents at home, and the citizen's part toward the ruler is to advise him privately and with sincerity. This is his position, and the limits of changing wrong by hand and the manner of advising rulers are matters on which scholars differ. (needs_source: the saying on changing evil by hand and the saying on Umar's whip are quoted without a chain, and the pious man's remark on the ruler is unnamed) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:25:30}
- Asked how the zabaniya can surround someone who did enjoin good, the sheikh says they take it that he did not, and find in his record many sins; the record then shows that he enjoined and forbade, and the angels of mercy take him. He says that enjoining need not be spoken, and gives the example of a student whose mother is unveiled and who adjusts her own veil before the mirror, and of the son who prays while his father does not, with the call to prayer reaching the father; he adds that a speaker must consider whom he is addressing, sitting with a footballer for half an hour about his training before turning to religion. The number of acts needed he leaves open. (needs_source: the claim that the angels find the enjoining in the record is the sheikh's explanation, not a reported text) He recalls that the Prophet's letters to other rulers offered a choice between accepting Islam, paying the poll tax or the alternative, and he urges looking for the key to each person, one through money, one through food, one through a sport. The part of the transcript that follows is lost to repetition. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:30:00}
- As the sheikh tells it, the wrestler Rukana was challenged by the Prophet, thrown twice, and said that only God's power had overcome him; later he accepted Islam, and the Prophet then said, "who will buy this slave?", to which Rukana answered that he was free and the Prophet replied that he was dear to God (see [[person/rukana|Rukana]]). The sheikh draws from this that the door to a person is learned by close attention, and that it is bound to good character. (needs_source: the story of Rukana is related by the sheikh without a chain, the order of events is unclear in the transcription, and the wording of the Prophet's reply is uncertain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:31:30}
- The next rescuer is good character (see [[concept/khuluq|khuluq]]): a man kneeling with a veil between him and God, whose good character takes his hand and brings him in. The sheikh says the purification of character is one of the tasks of the Prophet, and reads the order of the verses on the Prophet's mission as putting purification before teaching the Book and wisdom (see [[passage/quran-2-129|al-Baqara, verse 129]] and [[passage/quran-62-2|al-Jumu'a, verse 2]]); he adds that he counts it a quarter of the mission. (needs_source: the claim that character is a quarter of the mission is the sheikh's reading, and the transcription of his argument is uncertain) He warns of the pride of the person who feels better than all others on entering the mosque, or who says only he follows the Book and the Sunnah and the rest are people of innovation, and calls that a great sin that voids deeds, which is his judgement. As remedies he gives measuring oneself against the Book, against the Sunnah, and sitting with a godly scholar. He cites the Prophet's saying that the closest to him in the assemblies on the Day of Resurrection are those of best character and gentlest manners, and that God hates the harsh and arrogant, which he places in the sound collection of Muslim. (needs_source: both sayings are quoted without a chain, and the wording is garbled in the transcription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:35:00}
- He relates that men came to the Prophet one after another asking for counsel and were told to spread peace, not to be angry, to feed others and to pray at night while people sleep, and reads each answer as addressed to the person's weakness: the angry, the proud, the miserly, the hard of heart. He says good character is acquired and grows, that it cannot be split between a harsh self at home and a smiling one outside, and that this division is hypocrisy, which he says will be punished; the religion is one garden in which each command is a tree. (needs_source: the sayings of the men who asked for counsel and the reading of each answer are given by the sheikh without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:38:00}
- The next rescuer is fear of God (see [[concept/khawf|khawf]]): a man whose record is handed to his left hand takes it in his right because of his fear. The sheikh defines godliness as God not finding one where He forbade and not missing one where He commanded (see [[concept/taqwa|taqwa]]), compares fear to the goad that drives a camel and says that fear should be stronger in the young. He gives the account of a man of the Companions who asked who would reckon him, and, told it was God, said the Generous pardons when He reckons. (needs_source: the definition of godliness and the account of the man asking who would reckon him are quoted without a source or chain) He cites the verse of Musa hastening to his Lord (see [[passage/quran-20-83|Ta Ha, verse 83]] and [[passage/quran-20-84|verse 84]]) against putting off good, and the verses of being drawn on gradually (see [[passage/quran-7-182|al-A'raf, verse 182]] and [[passage/quran-68-44|al-Qalam, verse 44]]) and of the person who calls on God in distress and forgets afterwards (see [[passage/quran-10-12|Yunus, verse 12]] and [[passage/quran-29-65|al-Ankabut, verse 65]]) against trusting that God will forgive without fear. He distinguishes a slave of worship from a slave of compulsion who turns to God only in need, which is his own distinction. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:45:00}
- He lists what a person should fear: Iblis, the pile of sins eating up good deeds, the sudden coming of the angel of death, the grave, the creditors on the Day of Resurrection, and that an act thought accepted may not be, which is why forgiveness is sought after worship (see [[concept/istighfar|istighfar]]). He says that if people feared God they would not wrong others, deal in interest, take their siblings' inheritance or leave prayer. He closes the point with a story told to children of a father who tested three sons for the post of imam by sending each to slaughter a rooster where no one could see; two did so behind closed doors and the third brought it back, saying he saw God with him wherever he went (see [[concept/muraqaba|muraqaba]]). (needs_source: the story of the father and the three sons is told as a family tale without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:46:00}
- The next rescuer is the children who died young: a man whose scale is light is helped when his children weigh it down. The sheikh says such a child stands at the gate of Paradise and refuses to enter without his parents, and that God says to those who were patient, "join them to their offspring"; he adds that God asks the angel of death whether he took the child of His servant, and, hearing that the father praised God and said "we belong to God", has a house built for him in Paradise called the House of Praise and widens his grave as far as the eye can see. He quotes the saying that whoever is struck by a calamity and is patient has the reward, and whoever is struck and is not patient has the pain, the command being carried out either way. (needs_source: the report on the children at the gate of Paradise, the house of praise and the saying on the calamity are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:47:30}
- Hope in God (see [[concept/raja|raja]]) is the next rescuer: a man standing on the edge of Jahannam is pulled back by his hope. The sheikh separates hope from wishing, which is hope without effort, like a failing pupil who expects to come first, and calls it blameworthy; he says hope is a wish joined to deeds, quoting a saying that the incapable person follows his desires and wishes upon God, and the verses that the matter is not by wishes (see [[passage/quran-4-123|al-Nisa, verse 123]]) and the one devoted at night who fears the hereafter and hopes for his Lord's mercy (see [[passage/quran-39-9|al-Zumar, verse 9]]). He gives the picture, which he credits to the great scholars, of the heart as a bird whose head is love of God and whose two wings are fear and hope. (needs_source: the saying that the incapable follows desire and the likeness of the bird are given without a source or an author) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:49:00}
- Weeping from fear of God is next: a man who has fallen into the Fire is rescued by the tears he shed in fear. The sheikh quotes that those who wept even a tear the size of a fly's head will be brought out, and that two eyes the Fire will not touch are the eye that wept from fear of God and the eye that stayed awake on guard, or in another version the eye that was lowered from what God forbade. He calls the first saying sound. (needs_source: both sayings on tears and the eyes are quoted without a chain, and the sheikh's grading of the first as sound is his statement) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:50:30}
- A good opinion of God follows (see [[concept/husn-al-zann-billah|husn al-zann billah]]): a man trembling on the Sirat like a palm frond in a strong wind is steadied by his good opinion of God (see [[concept/sirat|the Sirat]]). The sheikh adds that a good opinion has to go with good deeds, and recalls God's saying that He is as His servant thinks of Him. (needs_source: the saying that God is as His servant thinks of Him is quoted without a chain) Then blessing on the Prophet: a man crawling on the Sirat is set on his feet by his blessings on him, and the sheikh gives that the stingiest of people is the one who hears his name and does not bless him. (needs_source: the saying on the stingiest of people is quoted without a chain, and the passage is garbled by repetition in the transcription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:52:00}
- The last rescuer is the testimony that there is no god but God: a man who reaches the gates of Paradise and finds them shut is let in when the testimony comes (see [[concept/shahada|the shahada]] and [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]). The sheikh says it must be said in sincerity and with its right, meaning that one makes no law that God did not make and does not declare lawful or unlawful what He did not. The host then reads the seventeen: dutifulness to parents, wudu, prayer, the remembrance of God, the Ramadan fast, speed in ghusl after major impurity, the pilgrimage and umra, joining kin, enjoining good and forbidding evil, good character, fear of God, the children who died young, hope in God, weeping from fear of God, a good opinion of God, blessing on the Prophet and saying there is no god but God often. He suggests devoting a week to each, and the sheikh adds that whoever errs should repent and seek forgiveness (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). The episode closes with a prayer asking forgiveness, healing, righteous spouses for the viewers' children, and release from the Fire, and with the host's farewell. (needs_source: the sayings on the testimony as a rescuer and on those who regret a sin are quoted without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep20 00:56:00}

## Episode 21: the good ending (husn al-khatima) begins before the barzakh is left: sincerity of intention and its renewal, free will and guidance, hastening to good, and the diseases of the heart (pride, showing off, grudge, envy, false security, despair)
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the chapter of the afternoon (see [[passage/quran-103-1|al-Asr, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-103-2|verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-103-3|verse 3]]). The host says that each day they draw a step nearer the promise and approach its closing, and the sheikh adds that speakers are trustees over people's minds and over the transmission of revelation, so every word must be exact. They say they wish to stop, before leaving the barzakh, at the good ending, and the transcript of this opening is partly lost to repetition. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:03:00}
- The sheikh notes that the talk falls in the last ten days of Ramadan, a month of good tidings, and likens Ramadan to Yusuf among his brothers: Yaqub did not regain his sight when he saw the shirts of his eleven sons but when the shirt of Yusuf reached him (see [[passage/quran-12-94|Yusuf, verse 94]]), so the heart in these days scents repentance and a good ending (see [[person/yaqub|Yaqub]], [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] and [[concept/husn-al-khatima|the good ending]]). He says that a good ending is what everyone should seek, that one who plants a thorn does not harvest amber, and that no one who wrongs and despises people and holds himself above them should expect it; the Prophet lived as one among all and a Muslim should follow him in outlook, conduct and action. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:05:30}
- Asked whether a person is free or compelled, the sheikh quotes a saying he gives to Jafar al-Sadiq, that God willed something for us and willed something from us: what He willed for us, decree and destiny, He veiled from us, and what He willed from us, deeds, He made plain, so one should not busy oneself with what is for us at the expense of what is asked of us. He concludes that a person is compelled in what he is not held to account for and free in everything he is (see [[passage/quran-18-49|al-Kahf, verse 49]] and [[concept/qadar|qadar]]); the good ending is therefore also the person's own act. He says that God knows what each will do, but did not write disbelief on the unbeliever; the unbeliever wrote it on himself by choice, and knowledge is not compulsion. (needs_source: the saying given to Jafar al-Sadiq is quoted by the sheikh without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:08:00}
- On guidance he cites that the Prophet does not guide whom he loves while God guides whom He will (see [[passage/quran-28-56|al-Qasas, verse 56]]), that a person is shown the way, grateful or ungrateful (see [[passage/quran-76-3|al-Insan, verse 3]]), and that those are the ones God guided (see [[passage/quran-6-90|al-Anam, verse 90]]). He holds that God's will for guidance follows the servant's own will, and that the door of God has no keeper and no lock: people knock and it is opened. As evidence he sets Abu Bakr, who sought, knocked and entered, beside Abu Jahl and Abu Lahab, who turned away, though the Prophet gave them the same message; and he says the Prophet prayed for Islam to be strengthened by the dearer of the two Umars, Umar ibn Hisham and Umar ibn al-Khattab, and that the Prophet spent a long while with the powerful of Quraysh while the Quran says of one who thinks himself self-sufficient that the Prophet attends to him (see [[passage/quran-80-5|Abasa, verse 5]] and [[passage/quran-80-6|verse 6]]). The Ansar, in the Prophet's speech to them, were left to their faith as others were. (needs_source: the account of Abu Bakr and Abu Jahl, the prayer for the dearer of the two Umars and the speech to the Ansar are told by the sheikh without a chain, and the transcription of the verse and the speech is partly garbled) (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]], [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]] and [[concept/hidaya|guidance]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:11:30}
- He says that guidance is not left to prayer alone: when told "I pray for him to stop smoking", he answers that one must also act, and that the way out comes to the one who plants the tree and fears God (see [[passage/quran-65-2|al-Talaq, verse 2]]), without asking for the exit first. He tells a tale in which Iblis tells Isa to throw himself from a mountain to see how God would save him, and Isa answers that God tests His servants and the servant does not test his Lord (see [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Isa]] and [[person/iblis|Iblis]]). He relates that a man asked the Prophet to be his companion in Paradise and was told to help him against himself by much prostration, and compares this to a pupil whose father knows the school's head: the connection helps only one who also does his own work, as intercession raises a mark of forty-eight to a pass but not zero. (needs_source: the tale of Iblis and Isa and the report of the man who asked for companionship in Paradise are given without a chain or source, and the school comparison is the sheikh's own) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:13:30}
- The first element of the good ending, he says, is truthfulness with God (see [[concept/al-sidq-truthfulness|truthfulness]]), and its first part is sincerity of intention in every act, such as a visit to a relative that should be for God and not for the sake of meeting someone there. He cites the call to seek the abode of the hereafter in what God gave, without forgetting one's share of the world (see [[passage/quran-28-77|al-Qasas, verse 77]] and [[concept/niyya|intention]], [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]), and teaches that the world is a farm and the hereafter its harvest. He says that the saying "work for your world as if you will live forever and for your hereafter as if you will die tomorrow" has no basis, that no one of the righteous said it, that it is in no book of hadith and not even among the fabricated reports, and that it spread among people and is wrong from its start because no one lives forever. (needs_source: his statement that the saying is in no collection, not even of fabricated reports, is made without naming a book and has not been checked) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:16:00}
- As a model of sincere intention he tells of Imam Malik and the caliph Abu Jafar, who asked him to write a book that would ease matters, avoiding the leniency of Ibn Abbas, the strictness of Ibn Umar and the rare reports of Ibn Masud; the result was the Muwatta (see [[work/muwatta-malik|al-Muwatta]], [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik ibn Anas]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Ibn Umar]] and [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Masud]]). He says Malik, before he spoke of the Prophet, would wash, perfume himself, wear white and speak at the Prophet's grave, and that when the caliph proposed to copy the book and send it to the provinces, Malik doubted his own sincerity and asked that it be placed on the roof of the Kaaba for a year, to be copied only if no word was effaced; when it was found whole, the sheikh says, his intention was proved. (needs_source: the account of Malik and the caliph, his dress before narrating and the year on the roof of the Kaaba are told by the sheikh without a source, and the transcription is uncertain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:18:00}
- The second part is renewing the intention, and he argues that the prayers spread through the day give a chance to stop and ask what one has done since the last: whether one spoke ill of someone, wronged a wife, preferred one child, raised one's voice, or took a sheet from work. He recalls the saying that if a river ran by one's door and one washed in it five times a day nothing of the dirt would remain, and says God removes sins by the five prayers in the same way. He urges taking oneself to account before being accounted, and asking each morning why one goes to work. (needs_source: the saying of the river and the five prayers is quoted without a chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:21:30}
- For one who slips he gives his view that a Muslim exists to plant good and build life by God's pleasure, so if circumstances turn him from the path he should renew his intention, seek forgiveness and begin a new page, even a hundred times (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]] and [[concept/istighfar|istighfar]]). He recalls the divine saying that if a person came with sins filling the earth and did not associate anything with God he would be met with as much forgiveness, and a prayer of a righteous man, "I am the one who returns to sin and You are the one who returns to forgiveness, so treat me by Your favour and not by my deserving" (see [[concept/hadith-qudsi|the divine saying]]). (needs_source: the divine saying and the prayer of the righteous man are quoted without a chain or a name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:22:30}
- He says that intention precedes action, and that someone who resolves to give charity before Ramadan ends and is prevented is written a reward; he adds a tale that Musa was told to tell a man who had said that if the mountains were food he would feed God's servants that his charity had been accepted. He distinguishes the attributes of majesty, such as the compeller and the subduer, which belong to the persistent unbeliever and the oppressor, from the attributes of beauty with which a servant should live: the merciful, the loving, the forgiving, the pardoner and the appreciative (see [[concept/sadaqa|sadaqa]] and [[person/musa|Musa]]). The host asks how God turns the sins of the thief, murderer, adulterer or backbiter into good deeds, and the sheikh replies that they are still on truthfulness with God and defers it. (needs_source: the saying that the one who intends good and does not do it is written a reward, and the tale of Musa and the man with the mountains, are given without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:25:00}
- The next part is truthfulness of the limbs, which he explains through wudu (see [[practice/wudu|wudu]]): he gives that the lady Sukayna said of Imam al-Shafii that he performed wudu well, and reads it as meaning that his hands were clean of bribes and the forbidden, his mouth rinsed from backbiting and lying, his nose not scenting the forbidden, his eyes and ears turned from evil, the wiping of the head a mind that thinks only of God and the religion, and the washing of the feet a walk to the mosque, to gatherings of knowledge and of good, to visit the sick, to follow a funeral and to give congratulation or condolence. This he calls true completion of wudu, as opposed to washing the body while the mouth keeps its sins; the ruling on completing wudu is not his subject here and he points only to a conduct matching it (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]]). (needs_source: the remark attributed to the lady Sukayna about al-Shafii is told without a source, and the transcription of it is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:27:00}
- The third part is filling time with obedience, so that even sleep becomes an act of worship: he gives a saying of a righteous man, "I count my sleep with God as I count my standing", since one sleeps in order to rise to obedience again. He says that prayer that does not restrain from indecency and wrong shows the wrong in a person's conduct (see [[passage/quran-29-45|al-Ankabut, verse 45]]), and describes the Prophet's Companion, named in the transcript as Hanzala, who felt himself in heaven in the Prophet's company and back in the world when he left him, and was told it was an hour and an hour; the sheikh adds that the Prophet's own faith rose until he was above the horizon. Asked whether work can excuse praying late, he answers that nothing delays the prayer, as each time has hours, and the one excuse he names is death. (needs_source: the saying about sleep, the account of Hanzala and the Prophet's words to him are given without a chain, and the transcription of the Companion's name is uncertain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:30:30}
- Asked about the sins of the heart, he lists pride, self-admiration, showing off, feeling safe from God's plan, despair of His mercy, and want of fear of Him (see [[concept/kibr|pride]], [[concept/ujb|self-admiration]] and [[concept/riya|showing off]]). He describes the one who shows off as praying behind his manager but not when the manager is absent, or speaking of kindness to wives before people while wronging his own at home, and grudge as holding oneself a wolf and others sheep, watching for a gap to attack (see [[concept/hiqd-grudge|grudge]]). He holds that such diseases are fed by the person, like bats, owls and vermin in a shut and ruined house, which flee when doors are opened and sun and air come in, as they flee a heart which the light of faith enters. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:33:30}
- On envy he defines it as wishing the end of another's blessing (see [[concept/hasad|envy]]), and gives verses he ascribes to al-Shafii on the one who spent the night envying him, who offended God in what He gave and so was repaid with an increase for the poet and a closed door of asking for the envier. He counts two calamities in the envier: that he objects to God's justice in the universe, and that he consumes himself, like a fire that eats itself when it finds nothing else, and advises patience since it kills him; the envier has no contentment and is like one who drinks sea water. (needs_source: the verses ascribed to al-Shafii and the line on the fire are quoted without a source, and the transcript is partly lost at the start of the passage on the calamities) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:36:00}
- He separates envy from admiration of another's good, which wishes to be like him in character, knowledge or religion without wishing the other to lose it, and which he says is lawful; he cites the saying that the believer admires and the hypocrite envies. He tells that some Arabs had men skilled in envy, and that one was asked to look at a she-camel so that she would be slaughtered. As remedies, one who sees a blessing says that what God wills has happened and there is no power but with God (see [[passage/quran-18-39|al-Kahf, verse 39]]), one does not wish for what God has favoured others with, the share being what each has earned, and one asks God for His bounty (see [[passage/quran-4-32|al-Nisa, verse 32]]); when one meets a scholar or a righteous ruler one prays for him and supports him and does not tear him down. (needs_source: the sayings on the believer who admires and the hypocrite who envies and on the skilled envier and the camel are given without a chain, and the transcript of the second is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:39:30}
- Feeling safe from God's plan he calls a great sin, and says that the thought that the one billion and thirty million Muslims will all enter Paradise is an example; none feels safe from it but the losers (see [[passage/quran-7-99|al-Araf, verse 99]]). He adds that the Companions promised Paradise grew in fear, not in security, and gives that someone asked, when is rest, and was answered, when the left foot is beside the right inside the threshold of Paradise. (needs_source: the figure of one billion and thirty million Muslims is stated without a source, and the account of the question about rest has an unclear speaker and is garbled in the transcription) Despair, he says, comes from the thought that one's sins outnumber God's pardon, though His pardon encompasses sins; and want of fear is the root of every sin, since fear makes the heart keep to the limits. The transcript of this section is thin. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:42:00}
- Returning to decree, he says God did not write unbelief on the unbeliever though He knew it, that the servant's deeds mark him as of the people of Paradise or of the Fire, and he speaks of the blind man, whose insight he says could be shared among a billion who see. As he tells it, God says that when He takes the two beloved ones of His servant He has no reward for him but that He shows him His face, and the sheikh calls this a divine saying and the highest reward of Paradise, relating it to the more that God promises beyond the reward (see [[passage/quran-50-35|Qaf, verse 35]], [[concept/ruyat-allah|the sight of God]], [[concept/hadith-qudsi|the divine saying]] and [[concept/jannah|Paradise]]). He suggests that a sighted man might have been proud of his eyes, and that those with a lack are often creative. He distinguishes God's prior knowledge from the servant's freedom to choose, saying one goes to the mosque or the cinema by his own feet, and that every child leaves the womb with a blank page. (needs_source: the divine saying on taking the two beloved ones is quoted without a chain, and the passage is partly garbled in the transcription) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:45:00}
- The next sign of a good ending is hastening to good deeds and not delaying them. He tells that the Prophet came out and found al-Abbas, Abu Bakr and Umar at his door and asked what they were speaking of; each said what he took to be the best of kindness: Abu Bakr said hastening it, Umar said concealing it, and al-Abbas said making it small. The Prophet is told to have said that in kindness there are three things: if it is hastened it is made pleasant, if concealed it is completed, and if belittled it is made great. The sheikh explains each: not saying "I will do it for you in five years", not telling others what one has done, and not counting it as anything. (needs_source: the account of the three Companions at the Prophet's door and his words on kindness are told without a chain, and part of the transcript is lost to repetition at the start) A statistic that he says comes from a respectable institution is promised at this point, but the transcript of it is lost. (see [[person/abbas-ibn-abd-al-muttalib|al-Abbas]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:48:00}
- Following the Sunna comes next (see [[concept/sunna|the Sunna]] and [[concept/ittiba|following]]): he says that the Prophet never betrayed, lied or deceived anyone, so that he is the model to follow. Then he names remembering the hereafter, giving a saying of one of the righteous that if he missed remembering it for an hour his heart would spoil, and the recalling of death (see [[concept/akhira|the hereafter]] and [[concept/mawt|death]]). The episode closes with a prayer for repentance and guidance before death, for ease at the moment of death, and a return to God unafflicted, neither disgraced nor regretful; for righteous spouses for the viewers' daughters and sons; for the removal of the devils of men and jinn from Muslim societies and for good counsellors for rulers; and for shade on the Day of Resurrection, a drink from the Pool, entry to Paradise without punishment and a good ending. The host says they will meet again on Saturday, and the opening verses are repeated. (needs_source: the saying of the righteous man on remembering the hereafter is quoted without his name or a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep21 00:51:00}
## Episode 22: the causes of a bad ending: long hope, injustice, heedlessness of remembrance, being deceived by the world, false security from God's plan and showing off; the kinds of hearts; and repentance at the end of Ramadan
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host presents the day's topic, a bad ending, as a title that tightens the chest yet relieves it for whoever wishes not to be among its people. The sheikh compares speaking of a Muslim's faults to the doctor who must enter the operating room and open the patient's belly to remove the disease, and says the aim of the episode is to give every Muslim guidance for improving his ending, since at the end of life a person goes either to a good ending that brings him to Paradise or a bad one that brings him to the Fire (see [[concept/husn-al-khatima|the good ending]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:02:30}
- The sheikh describes a good ending as God sending, when He wishes a servant well, an angel in his last days to guide and set him straight so that he dies in the best state, in prayer, in a gathering of knowledge, in jihad or in something God approves; and he says that for one God wishes ill, a devil is sent who leads him astray so that he dies in the worst state, sometimes with death arriving suddenly while he is in wrongdoing. He adds that deeds are judged by their endings. (needs_source: the report of the angel sent to a servant in his last days and the devil sent to another is told by the sheikh without a chain or a book) He explains a bad ending as sealing the record with what blackens it, without having erased the past with a good deed. A sentence about a prayer that is invalidated during bowing runs through this stretch, but the transcript there is garbled, so it is not carried over. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:04:30}
- He holds that the ending depends on the whole run of earlier deeds, not on the last deed alone: God does not undo a servant's work for one slip, and whoever dies unrepentant, proud, unjust or slanderous has died on what he lived by all his life. He gives the example of the tree that is planted and, after a time, bears its known fruit, and of a hadith he relates in which the believer who recites the Qur'an is like the citron, sweet in scent and taste, the believer who does not recite it like the date, with no scent and a sweet taste, the hypocrite who recites like basil, with a good scent and a bitter taste, and the hypocrite who does not recite like the colocynth, with a bad scent and a worse taste (see [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]). (needs_source: the hadith on the kinds of reciters, the citron, the date, the basil and the colocynth, is related by the sheikh without a chain, and its wording in the transcript is shortened and garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:06:00}
- The first cause that blackens the record, for him, is long hope (see [[concept/tul-al-amal|long hope]]): a person says he will not die now because his father lived ninety years, his grandfather past a hundred and his mother a hundred and ten, and that he will find guidance in his last days. He says long hope turns the world into an end rather than a means, so its owner falls into usury, speaking ill of people and a foul tongue. He quotes some people's saying that the wise man does three things: he leaves the world before it leaves him, pleases his Creator before meeting Him, and builds his grave before entering it. (needs_source: the saying on the three things the wise man does is quoted from "some of them" with no name or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:08:00}
- He tells that Ata ibn Abi Rabah was ill and a follower he put forward to lead the prayer excused himself, saying that if he led them now he would not lead them again; Ata replied that he was long in hope and therefore unfit to be imam, since one who enters prayer planning another prayer does not pray as one taking leave (see [[person/ata-ibn-abi-rabah|Ata ibn Abi Rabah]]). He relates that the Prophet passed a Companion who had bought a young camel for two months and said, "Do you not wonder at this man, he is long in hope," and that the Companion's name is garbled in the transcript in a form resembling Usama. He says long hope hardens the heart and makes one forget the Hereafter. (needs_source: the report of Ata and the one he put forward and the report of the Prophet and the Companion with the young camel are told without a chain or source, and the names of the follower and the Companion are garbled in the transcript so they are not asserted) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:09:30}
- He notes that some people dislike speaking of the true promise because it reminds them that they will leave wealth, banks, buildings and power, and that one who sits on the chair finds the sweetness of nursing and the bitterness of weaning when he leaves it. He then says injustice is of three kinds (see [[concept/zulm|injustice]]): a person's injustice to himself, his injustice to his religion by neglecting obligations and overstepping God's limits, and his injustice to others, whoever they are, animal, plant or human, Muslim or not. He holds the worst calamity is that the unjust person is not convinced he is unjust, and gives the example of one who takes more than his due of his father's inheritance, arguing that he worked with his father all his life without pay; he says injustice is putting a thing in the wrong place and the worst oppressors are those who wrong someone who has no helper but God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:12:30}
- He illustrates with a husband whose wife has lived with him thirty years and whose family has died, and who mistreats her because he knows she has no one behind her, counting himself in the right by his guardianship; he says this is the calamity. This is an example the sheikh gives, not a report about a particular person. Next he turns to heedlessness of remembrance (see [[practice/dhikr|remembrance]]), citing God's command to His Prophet to keep himself patient with those who call on their Lord and not to obey one whose heart God has made heedless of His remembrance (see [[passage/quran-18-28|al-Kahf, verse 28]]), and says remembrance belongs in every state: if you are afraid say "O Strong One, secure me", and if poor say "O Rich One, enrich me". He contrasts the person invited to a party with the one invited to a lesson of knowledge, and notes that hearts find rest in the remembrance of God (see [[passage/quran-13-28|al-Ra'd, verse 28]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:15:00}
- He counts being deceived by the world among the causes of a bad ending: a person who wants to trample people in order to arrive himself, and one who pities cats and polar bears but not human beings. Examples of rich countries throwing away food to protect prices run through the talk, but the text there is garbled and repetitive and is not carried over. He relates a hadith that one who keeps the prayer with its ablution, bowing and prostration is gathered with the prophets and the truthful, and one who neglects it is gathered with Qarun, Haman and a man named ibn Khalaf, and he says nothing normally distracts from prayer except kingship, ministry, trade and wealth (see [[person/qarun|Qarun]], [[person/haman|Haman]], [[person/ubayy-ibn-khalaf|Ubayy ibn Khalaf]] and [[practice/salah|prayer]]). (needs_source: the hadith on being gathered with the prophets or with Qarun and Haman is related by the sheikh without a chain, and the start of the list is garbled in the transcript so not every name is known) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:16:30}
- He returns to false security from God's plan and asks how a prayer is accepted. He says the obligation is discharged by performing its pillars, words and actions, while the reward is as much as one was humble in it and understood of it, with an illustration that one humble in one rak'a has a quarter of the prayer's reward and in two rak'as a half, and he says this illustration is his own with no text behind it. He says that if the mind wanders, one seeks refuge in God from the devil and continues without repeating the Fatiha or the rak'a; this is his view on the point. He says humility begins with the heart's humility to God's commands before prayer, so one who is suspicious of people, proud over them, eats what is forbidden and wrongs them cannot be humble in prayer. He mentions that Ibn Umar would bow at the Ka'ba and birds would land on his back, and that the Prophet bowed so that a vessel of water placed on his back would have stayed still (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Ibn Umar]]). (needs_source: the reports of Ibn Umar with the birds and of the Prophet with the full vessel are told without a chain, and the percentages are the sheikh's own illustration) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:20:00}
- He says the main cause of false security is God's veiling of the servant: one who disobeys, eats what is forbidden, takes bribes and wrongs, and is neither exposed nor punished is led on step by step, and feels that God is pleased with him (see [[concept/istidraj|being led on]] and [[concept/ghurur|self-deception]]); he says one of the greatest calamities is to be pleased with oneself, and that you should know a man pleased with himself is deceived. He relates that the righteous were fearful, and reports a righteous man whose sons told him to go easy on himself and who answered that he had wept for this day for sixty years, though the man's age is garbled in the transcript. (needs_source: the report of the righteous man who had wept sixty years has no name or source, and the age in the transcript is garbled) He cites that if God took people to account for what they earned He would leave no creature on the earth (see [[passage/quran-35-45|Fatir, verse 45]]), and holds that in religion a person looks to those who are less than he, and in worldly things to those better off, whereas the right way is the reverse. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:22:30}
- He takes up the trust that man carried (see [[passage/quran-33-72|al-Ahzab, verse 72]] and [[concept/amana|the trust]]), saying, in his understanding, that it was carried in the world of spirits and that Adam stands for all people, and that man is unjust and ignorant because he did not know the size of what he carried. (needs_source: his saying that the trust was carried in the world of spirits is an interpretation he gives without attribution, and the text there is garbled) Then he says every person has four states: obedience, disobedience, blessing and trial; the deceived person, on seeing obedience, does not obey, on seeing disobedience commits it without seeking forgiveness, on seeing a blessing does not give thanks, and on seeing a trial does not endure it, and he says the worst thing God can do to a servant is let him be deceived by himself (see [[concept/sabr|patience]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:25:30}
- Asked about the verse on God's willing the destruction of a town, he answers that God's will is tied to the servants' deeds: He does not destroy a town until it has done wrong, been rebellious and drifted from His path (see [[passage/quran-17-16|al-Isra, verse 16]] and [[passage/quran-7-96|al-A'raf, verse 96]]). He says God sent messengers to bring people out of misguidance, that lands are of two kinds, one that honours scholars and is blessed and one that expels them and is punished, and that the story of the people of the town in the Qur'an is an example (see [[passage/quran-36-14|Ya Sin, verse 14]]). He says contentment with oneself comes only through obeying God, and quotes his own teacher as saying that whoever leaves the world for God has the world run after him, while wealth gives its restless, depressed owner no ease. (needs_source: the saying attributed to his teacher on the world running after those who leave it is quoted without a name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:28:00}
- The host asks whether a bad ending extends to the community, and the sheikh answers yes, relating a hadith that if the community does not say to the wrongdoer "you are a wrongdoer", it has been given up on. (needs_source: the hadith that a community which does not say "wrongdoer" to the wrongdoer is given up on is related without a chain) He says the address is to everyone who says there is no god but God, not to scholars alone, and that whoever now controls the people's media was given the field because the community abandoned its post. Phrases about outside parties run through this stretch, but the text there is garbled and is not carried over. He proposes a large conference called the Rising of the Community, held in stages: the generation over fifty leads a stage, then after twenty years the next generation takes over, until the community matures after a century; he says the start is the Qur'an and Sunna and that a believer naturally loves his homeland, as the Prophet loved Mecca. He compares how a national football defeat sets off committees while the community's repeated defeats over centuries do not. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:32:00}
- He counts showing off among the causes of a bad ending (see [[concept/riya|showing off]]) and distinguishes it from hypocrisy; he says calling someone a hypocrite is grave and no one should be so called unless the conditions are complete (see [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]), so a man who promises and cannot keep it for an excuse is not a hypocrite, and breaking a promise means promising with the intention not to keep it. He describes showing off as dressing oneself up before people, such as treating a wife well in company and wronging her at home, or praying a minute at home and an hour in the mosque. He cites those who show off (see [[passage/quran-107-6|al-Ma'un, verse 6]]) and those who love to be praised for what they did not do (see [[passage/quran-3-188|Al Imran, verse 188]]). He quotes an unnamed teacher as saying that if asked who is free of showing off, none would be found, and that a little showing off is shirk. (needs_source: the sayings that a little showing off is shirk and that it was rare among the early generations are quoted without a chain, and some phrases are garbled) He says one who tries and fails has the reward of the intention. This stretch also refers to al-Ghazali and to a Shafi'i view that a prayer lengthened for show may be invalid, and to a saying he attributes to Ibn Abbas that taking means does not contradict reliance on God, but the text of both is garbled (see [[person/al-ghazali-abu-hamid|Abu Hamid al-Ghazali]] and [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:38:00}
- He returns to the heart first and the limbs next, and says the scholars divided hearts into four: a heart that loves only God, which is the Companions' hearts; a heart inclined to the world that is content if given and angry if not; a heart in between; and a heart that wavers between the Hereafter and the world, its light now shining and now dim. (needs_source: the division of hearts into four is attributed to "the scholars" without naming any) He advises those who kept up worship in Ramadan not to stop after it, and says the five prayers, the Friday prayer and Ramadan expiate what lies between them if major sins are avoided (see [[practice/sawm-ramadan|the Ramadan fast]]). (needs_source: the hadith that the prayers, Friday and Ramadan expiate what lies between them is related by its meaning without a chain) He says the night may be the Night of Power (see [[concept/laylat-al-qadr|the Night of Power]]) and urges a sincere repentance in the hours of Ramadan that remain (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:41:30}
- He tells of a man in an expedition who asked the Prophet whether, if he became Muslim and fought and was killed, he would enter Paradise, and was told yes; he spoke the testimony, fought, was struck by an arrow and died, and the Prophet said he had taken his place in the highest Paradise though he had not prostrated to God once. (needs_source: the report of the man who became Muslim in battle and was killed and entered Paradise without a prostration is told without a chain or name, and the place of the expedition is garbled in the transcript) He says the Qur'an has 213 verses of mercy and 213 of punishment, and mentions Paradise and Hell 57 times each. (needs_source: the numbers given for verses of mercy and punishment and for mentions of Paradise and Hell are stated without a count or source and are his own) He warns against relying on intercession alone, since no one has a credit to rely on, and asks where the night prayer and the care of orphans and widows are (see [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:44:30}
- He gives his view that the prayer of a wronged woman, widow, divorcee or wife, is grave for a society, that men today have become weaker than women in the laws and courts through the weakness of their faith, and that the wronged woman's prayer falls on those who taught them, scholars, preachers, media and ministers, and on a whole state. The sheikh defers the detail of this topic to another episode and counts leaving such injustice in society among the signs of a bad ending. Views differ on this, and it is here the sheikh's opinion, not a ruling. (needs_source: his saying that men have become weaker than women in the laws and courts and that a wronged woman's prayer falls on a whole state is his own with no source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:45:30}
- Asked how to prepare for a good ending, he answers that our affairs will be set right only by what set right the first of this community (the word is garbled in the transcript), and that the start is the Qur'an and Sunna and the end is to them. He says what we apply of religion, prayer, inheritance, the headscarf and mosques, is by his count three percent of it, and that a Muslim should be a corrected copy walking on two feet in what he is commanded and forbidden. He says memorising the Qur'an while eating usury, taking inheritance and wronging a neighbour turns the Qur'an into a proof against its holder on the Day of Resurrection, and that the Companions did not ask many questions, only about thirteen, because they acted. (needs_source: the number thirteen for the Companions' questions and the three percent of religion are figures he gives without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:48:30}
- He says patience is not a set time but the mending of oneself through a whole life, that the bribe-taker or thief, had he waited, would have received the money lawfully, and cites that whoever turns from God's remembrance has a narrow life (see [[passage/quran-20-124|Ta Ha, verse 124]]) and that some of those who associate partners wish to be given a thousand years (see [[passage/quran-2-96|al-Baqara, verse 96]]). He says one who does not live so lives like insects and crawling things. He holds that the preacher who is persecuted, expelled and scattered inherits from the prophets, and that the Hereafter is lit only if the world is burned, that is, if desires and the tongue are brought under control. He cites those who strive in God's cause (see [[passage/quran-29-69|al-Ankabut, verse 69]]). (needs_source: the saying that the Hereafter is lit only if the world burns is quoted as a rule with no attribution, and his remarks on persecuted preachers have no specified source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:51:00}
- He relates that when the prohibition of wine came down the Companions poured it out in the streets of Medina, so that its smell lingered for a week, and that some spat out the mouthful they held (see [[concept/khamr|wine]]); he sees this as self-striving, and that too many questions and too much argument have set us back. He says we must not declare anyone an unbeliever and that it is wrong to say an unveiled woman is an unbeliever, and he recommends advising by the Book and the Sunna. (needs_source: the report of the wine poured in Medina's streets with its smell lasting a week is told without a chain) He tells that a man asked a Companion to teach him Islam and the Companion recited a chapter to him, the man wept and was content with it and later was martyred, but the name of the Companion and the chapter are garbled in the transcript, so neither is asserted. The sheikh closes by saying they do not attack anyone and are a speck in the building of the greater Islam. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep22 00:54:00}
## Episode 23: the terror of the Rising (hawl al-matla'): the two blasts of the Trumpet, the white earth, the bodies raised, the gathering and the standing
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the verses of the earthquake (see [[passage/quran-99-1|al-Zalzala, verse 1]] to [[passage/quran-99-8|verse 8]]). The host says that after the earlier tours the series now draws near its end, and the sheikh answers that today's stop is the start of the hard scenes, which he asks God to make light and to keep them steady through. The topic is the terror of the Rising (hawl al-matla'), the first sight met on coming out of the graves (see [[concept/yaum-al-nushur|the Day of Spreading]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:02:30}
- The sheikh says people will be spread out from their graves and will then look upon the scenes heard of in the Book of God, in the words of the Messenger and in the explanations of scholars, and how they read the Qur'anic and prophetic texts on the scenes that turned the Prophet's hair grey. He relates that whoever wishes to see the states of the Hereafter should read the surahs on the sun being wrapped up and the sky splitting open (see [[passage/quran-81-1|al-Takwir, verse 1]] and [[passage/quran-82-1|al-Infitar, verse 1]]). He adds that people come out of the graves unable to recognise one another, or recognising and denying, and that even the mountains will move. He ties the whole matter to precision in presenting it and, above all, to preparing for it. (needs_source: the report that whoever wishes to see the Hereafter should read those surahs is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:05:00}
- Taking up where the series stopped, at the world of the barzakh (see [[concept/al-barzakh|the barzakh]]), he describes a wind that takes the last of the believers after the final of the great signs, leaving only the worst of people (see [[concept/ashrat-al-saa|the signs of the Hour]]). He says there are strong reports of a great fire coming out of Aden that gathers people to the land of gathering, which he says is in the text of a hadith in Sahih Muslim, and that where it lies God knows, some saying the land of Syria and the holy house. He lists the Qur'anic names of the first cry and calls them the shout, the cry, the thunderclap, the quaking, the deafening blast and the overwhelming calamity, and recalls the verses on the caller who calls from a near place on the Day of Emergence (see [[passage/quran-50-41|Qaf, verse 41]] and [[passage/quran-50-42|verse 42]]). He gives the caller's words as an address to the decayed bones and scattered flesh to rise for judgment before God, and he says the caller is Jibril or Israfil, with God knowing best. (needs_source: the caller's address to the bones and flesh is relayed without a chain, and the identity of the caller is left open by the sheikh himself) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:07:00}
- He explains that there are two blasts (see [[concept/nafkhat-as-sur|the blasts of the Trumpet]]). At the first, everything alive on the earth, and those in the heavens, falls dead except whom God wills. The sheikh says the martyrs are excluded because they are alive with their Lord and provided for, and that the prophets are the more entitled to it; he adds that some scholars take the exception to be the four great angels, Jibril, Israfil, Mikail and the angel of death (see [[concept/malak-al-mawt|the angel of death]]). He relates the hadith in which the Prophet says he will be the first from whom the grave is split and will find Moses holding the leg of the Throne, and that he does not know whether Moses was stunned and then raised or was spared the first stunning by the one at the mountain, and he sees in that reply the manners of prophecy in saying "I do not know". He recalls the verse of Moses asking to see God and the mountain (see [[passage/quran-7-143|al-A'raf, verse 143]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:09:00}
- He says the earth then stays so for forty years between the first and second blasts, and that in that span the sky is folded like a scroll for writings (see [[passage/quran-21-104|al-Anbiya, verse 104]]), the sun is wrapped up and the sky splits, and the earth casts out what it holds and gives ear to its Lord (see [[passage/quran-84-1|al-Inshiqaq, verse 1]] and [[passage/quran-84-2|verse 2]]). Between the blasts, he says, nothing at all happens and all are dead. (needs_source: the forty years between the blasts is stated without a chain or book) He says the earth is then changed (see [[passage/quran-14-48|Ibrahim, verse 48]]) into a land white and pure, like melted silver, which he relates from the Prophet in reply to a question about where people go, a land with no landmark for anyone, with no hollow and no hill to hide in (see [[passage/quran-20-106|Ta Ha, verse 106]] and [[passage/quran-20-107|verse 107]]), on which no sin was done and no drop of blood spilled, and where true justice begins.
