(needs_source: the description of the land as white like melted silver is attributed to the Prophet without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:11:30}
- The sheikh says that when the second blast comes, souls return to their bodies (see [[passage/quran-81-7|al-Takwir, verse 7]]), and he answers the question of one eaten by fish, burned, scattered by the wind or lost in a crash by saying that the God who created the first time restores, and that the scholars hold the body is rebuilt from the tail bone (see [[passage/quran-36-78|Ya Sin, verse 78]]). He relates that people are raised barefoot, naked and uncircumcised, and that Aisha put her hand to her head and asked about the women's nakedness, to which the Prophet answered that each has that day a concern enough for her (see [[passage/quran-80-37|Abasa, verse 37]]). He tells, from a physician, a story of an earthquake in Cairo in which a mother forgot her child on the doctor's examining table and ran, as a picture of the great earthquake of the Hour. (needs_source: the report of the raising barefoot, naked and uncircumcised and Aisha's question is told without a chain; the physician's story of the earthquake is a personal anecdote with the year as the sheikh gives it) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:14:00}
- He recalls the verse in which the earth casts out its loads (see [[passage/quran-99-2|al-Zalzala, verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-99-3|verse 3]]) and says people will come out without guidance, splitting into two kinds: the unbeliever and the sinner say who raised us from our resting place, and the believers answer that this is what the Merciful promised (see [[passage/quran-36-52|Ya Sin, verse 52]]). He says the steadiness God gives is for the believer alone, because he upheld God's religion on the earth and so God makes his heart steady in the fright, each according to his faith (see [[concept/khawf|fear]]). He says the whole of humanity from Adam, billions upon billions, rises on this same earth, changed in form and heading for the land of gathering (see [[concept/ard-al-mahshar|the land of gathering]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:17:00}
- He explains that the earth shines with the light of its Lord, not the light of the sun or moon (see [[passage/quran-39-69|al-Zumar, verse 69]]), since the sun is wrapped up and the stars scattered. He states that the seas are turned to fire, arguing that water is made of hydrogen and oxygen, which burn, and that God removed this property in the world out of mercy. (needs_source: the claim that the seas become fire through the burning of hydrogen and oxygen is the sheikh's own reading and is given without a source) He says a fire drives people to the land of gathering, some on foot, some two or three to a camel, and some on their faces, relating the question of how one walks on his face and the answer that He who walked him on his feet can walk him on his face. He says the sun draws close to the heads, that God gives people the power to bear it, and that the believer is steady because he lived so. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:19:30}
- The sheikh describes Israfil as created only for the task of blowing the Trumpet, with eyes fixed toward the heavens like bright stars, and says the first blast shows God's power to give death and life: one blast kills and one wakes. (needs_source: the description of Israfil's eyes like bright stars is relayed without a chain or book) He says people stand up as one, hearing and sight restored, from Noah and Adam to the youngest, the wronger and the wronged, and then turns to the unborn child killed unlawfully, who in his account will be asked and will question those who killed her (see [[passage/quran-81-8|al-Takwir, verse 8]] and [[passage/quran-81-9|verse 9]]), holding that the child is a person from the first day, not only after four months. This is the sheikh's view and a matter on which scholars differ. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:23:30}
- He says the angels of the heavens come down and surround the gathering, and that the matter lasts thousands of years, a day with God standing in the next world for fifty thousand years (see [[passage/quran-70-4|al-Ma'arij, verse 4]]). He relates the saying of Salman that three things made him weep, among them the terror of the Rising and the standing before God. The sheikh says the scenes turned the Prophet's hair grey, that we hear them as if reading something ordinary, and that whoever takes them to heart repents at once and settles the wrongs between people before it is too late. He says what was said before were only introductions and that the serious part of the promise has begun. (needs_source: the saying of Salman is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:26:30}
- Asked how long the great signs take, he gives about two hundred years at most, counting forty years in one stage and forty in another and the forty years of Isa (see [[concept/nuzul-isa-ibn-maryam|the descent of Isa]]); he does not give a text. At the first blast the earth is emptied of people, animals and buildings, and all die including the angel of death. He says he had thought the angel of death was last, but narrations to that effect he looked into and found weakly transmitted. Then God asks whose is the kingdom today and answers Himself, to the One, the Prevailing (see [[passage/quran-40-16|Ghafir, verse 16]]), the mighty kings and pharaohs gone and only the face of God remaining (see [[passage/quran-55-26|al-Rahman, verse 26]] and [[passage/quran-55-27|verse 27]]). (needs_source: the period of two hundred years for the great signs is the sheikh's estimate without a text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:29:00}
- He says Israfil is brought back to life and blows again, and that all hear it. He relates a hadith that the last to be gathered are two men, one of Muzayna and one of Juhayna, whom two angels drag on their faces. He says that on the day of crowding, with six billion alive on the earth in 2005 as he gives it and every person who ever lived, all are gathered into one place so that each finds only the place for his feet, and he asks himself and the viewers what each has prepared. (needs_source: the report of the two men of Muzayna and Juhayna is relayed without a chain; the figure of six billion in 2005 is a statistic given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:31:00}
- He relates that Abraham was asked whether he feared the scenes of the Rising and replied that the terrors would make him forget his friend; the transcript there is garbled, so the exchange is not carried over. He says it is reported that Moses will say he asks not about Harun, Isa not about Maryam and Abraham not about Ismail, each having a concern enough for him (see [[passage/quran-80-37|Abasa, verse 37]]), and that the only steady one is the Prophet, who says "my community, my community" (see [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]). He says whoever follows his guidance should not drop his Sunna, and cites Ibn al-Qayyim's book on the Garden as a title he admired for calling the Prophet the one who spurs souls on to lands of joy. (needs_source: the report of what Moses, Isa and Abraham will say is relayed without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:33:00}
- The sheikh turns to the lesson: those who delay prayer or resist the veil should heed the series as a warning, and he says the episodes should alert the community. He says the Hour is a name of the whole standing and promises another episode on the names of the Hour in the Qur'an. He recalls that those who enjoyed the world will feel they stayed an hour of the day (see [[passage/quran-10-45|Yunus, verse 45]] and [[passage/quran-30-55|al-Rum, verse 55]]), and quotes the cry that we were in heedlessness of this (see [[passage/quran-21-97|al-Anbiya, verse 97]]), saying that heedlessness, even when forgetfulness is chosen, brings regret, and he thanks God for the sheikh who warns him. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:35:00}
- To the question of why so much terror, he answers that God is forgiving and merciful and His torment is the painful torment, and that the day is the compensation for believers who were wronged: he names Noah, who lived 950 years with eighty believers by the sheikh's account, and the Companions Bilal, dragged over the hot sand, and Khabbab, and he names Abu Jahl as their wrongdoer. He says the world is a passing loan eaten from by the good and the wicked, and the Hereafter a true promise in which a just King rules, recalling the earthquake of the Hour when every nursing mother is distracted and every pregnant one drops her load (see [[passage/quran-22-2|al-Hajj, verse 2]]). He says that scene belongs to the second blast. (needs_source: the number of eighty believers with Noah is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:38:30}
- He relates that people rise hungrier, thirstier and barer than ever, and that whoever fed, gave drink or clothed for God's sake will be fed, given drink and clothed by Him, and he tells those who slander people and sour their lives that they win only loss and lasting torment. He relates that God will call on the Day of Rising: "I listened to you long, so listen to me today", set aside the lineages men raised, saying the noblest is the most pious, and ask where the pious are. The same segment tells of Adam being told to send out the share for the Fire, nine hundred ninety nine of every thousand, leaving one of a thousand, and he says people are in heedlessness although their reckoning has drawn near (see [[passage/quran-21-1|al-Anbiya, verse 1]]). (needs_source: the call of God to the people of the gathering and the share for the Fire are relayed without a chain or book; the figure, garbled in the transcript, is given as the well known hadith figure) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:41:00}
- In the second part he says each is raised in the state he died: a man who died drunk rises drunk, and he relates the hadith of the man in a state of pilgrim sanctity thrown by his mount, whom the Prophet had washed and not perfumed or covered at the head, since he will be raised calling out the response of pilgrimage. He adds that those who died in the cause of God will be steady that day. (needs_source: the report of the drunk man and the man in sanctity is relayed without a chain; the hadith is told by the sheikh from memory) He says the earth splits swiftly for them (see [[passage/quran-50-44|Qaf, verse 44]]), threw out what it held and emptied itself (see [[passage/quran-84-3|al-Inshiqaq, verse 3]] and [[passage/quran-84-4|verse 4]]), and reasons that if every grave stayed a grave the living would find no place on earth, since bodies decay. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:46:30}
- He points to the earth revived by rain (see [[passage/quran-22-5|al-Hajj, verse 5]] and [[passage/quran-41-39|Fussilat, verse 39]]) and to the second creation incumbent on God (see [[passage/quran-53-47|al-Najm, verse 47]]). He draws attention to the words that they will then be standing looking on (see [[passage/quran-39-68|al-Zumar, verse 68]]), standing and not sitting, and says that in the moment the unbeliever asks who raised him from his resting place, the believer is steady. He says steadiness comes by following every command of the Book and leaving what it forbids, by fearing God in people's rights and by contentment with God's allotment, which he calls the beginning of the gathering. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:49:30}
- He says people look for anyone to guide them, with no friend asking after a friend (see [[passage/quran-80-34|Abasa, verse 34]] to [[passage/quran-80-37|verse 37]]), and that the knowledge of certainty becomes the eye and then the truth of certainty. He compares the believer awaiting that day to the top student waiting to be called for the certificate. He says people are not gathered believers with believers but mixed, standing so long that some would wish to enter the Fire rather than wait, and that Adam is asked to intercede only so that the reckoning may begin. (needs_source: the report that the standing is long enough that some wish for the Fire and the intercession of Adam only for the reckoning to begin are relayed without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:52:30}
- The sheikh relates the hadith that if people knew what the Prophet knew they would weep much and laugh little, and says this is a call to quick repentance and to good dealing with Muslims and non-Muslims, wanting Islam's greatness shown. He tells that Anas wept while pouring water for the Prophet, fearing he would not be with him on the Day, and that the Prophet answered with the verse on being with the prophets, the truthful, the martyrs and the righteous (see [[passage/quran-4-69|al-Nisa, verse 69]]), and that there are three places where the Prophet may be found: the flying of the record pages, the setting up of the scales and the crossing of the bridge, where no friend asks about a friend. (needs_source: the hadith of Anas weeping and of the three places is relayed without a chain, and the wording is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:56:00}
- He says the time spent in the land of gathering is not known and is not counted in our days, since the sun and moon that measured them are gone, and that God knows. He says the angels of the heavens surround the land and that the angels who were with the good servant in the world are, in some reports, with him at the grave and then leave, with Jibril standing at the scale in what is said. On the stage after the gathering he says people go to Adam, who is asked to intercede with God for the reckoning to begin, though the length of the wait is unknown. (needs_source: the reports of the angels who accompanied the servant and of Jibril standing at the scale are relayed without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:59:30}
- He says that whatever comes to mind about God, God is other than that (see [[passage/quran-42-11|al-Shura, verse 11]]), and that God is the creator of time and place and not bound by them, so that minds stop in prostration, not in confusion. The episode closes with his supplication for repentance, an easy death, forgiveness, firmness in the firm word, a good ending, a drink from the Prophet's basin and the sight of God's face (see [[concept/al-hawd|the basin]]). A verse-like poem recited at the end is garbled in the transcript and not carried over. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep23 01:01:00}

## Episode 24: the gathering continued: the going to the prophets, the intercession, the flying of the pages, the scales and the settling of wrongs
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and the verse on the record fastened to every person's neck (see [[passage/quran-17-13|al-Isra, verse 13]] and [[passage/quran-17-14|verse 14]]). The host recalls that the last episode stopped at the land of gathering (see [[concept/ard-al-mahshar|the land of gathering]]) with the people turning to Adam and his sons so that the reckoning might begin, and asks what comes next. The sheikh says the subject is the reckoning and the flying of the pages (see [[concept/al-hisab|the reckoning]]), and that the whole episode is about the gathering, its meaning and its signs. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:02:30}
- He describes people on coming out of the graves as locusts spread abroad (see [[passage/quran-54-7|al-Qamar, verse 7]]), and the sky changing its colour like a rose (see [[passage/quran-55-37|al-Rahman, verse 37]]). He says the angels of the heavens then come down and ring the gathering, the dwellers of each heaven larger than the one below, until seven rings surround all who are gathered, and he explains that a person tends to deny what he has not seen. (needs_source: the claim that each heaven's dwellers are a hundred times larger than those below is given without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:05:00}
- He says even the beasts of prey come with bowed heads, lion, wolf and elephant among them, blameless but afraid, so that fear is the mark of the whole gathering. (needs_source: the report that the beasts of prey come with bowed heads is relayed without a chain or book) He explains the word hashr as a gathering by compulsion and not by consent, a gathering that holds people as a hand is held pinned in a doorway, and he notes that the Qur'an uses it for people (see [[passage/quran-18-47|al-Kahf, verse 47]]) and for wild beasts (see [[passage/quran-81-5|al-Takwir, verse 5]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:07:00}
- He dwells on the hush of the scene: voices lowered before the Merciful so that only a whisper is heard, and faces bowed before the Living, the Self-Subsisting, with the wrongdoer having failed (see [[passage/quran-20-108|Ta Ha, verse 108]] and [[passage/quran-20-111|verse 111]]). He points out that wrongdoing is named there and that the Hereafter is defined by there being no wrong that day (see [[passage/quran-40-17|Ghafir, verse 17]]), and he contrasts this with the magicians of Pharaoh, who in the face of his threat spoke only of what lay beyond this life (see [[passage/quran-20-72|Ta Ha, verse 72]] and [[passage/quran-20-73|verse 73]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:09:00}
- He recalls Iblis speaking before the people and disowning those who followed him, saying that he had only called and they had answered, so they should blame themselves (see [[passage/quran-14-22|Ibrahim, verse 22]]), and the parable of the devil and the man who disbelieves and then disowns him (see [[passage/quran-59-16|al-Hashr, verse 16]] and [[passage/quran-59-17|verse 17]]). He adds the verses on those who followed disowning their leaders when the ties are cut (see [[passage/quran-2-166|al-Baqara, verse 166]]), and the plea of those in the Fire to be sent back, which is refused (see [[passage/quran-23-107|al-Mu'minun, verse 107]] and [[passage/quran-6-28|al-An'am, verse 28]]), and he says that Iblis speaks only because God allows him, so that his followers hear him disown them. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:11:30}
- He relates that voices rise, said to be those of the righteous scholars of every nation, advising people to go to the one who can deliver them or intercede, and that the people go to Adam, who says his Lord is angry today as never before and never again, that he was forbidden the tree and ate, and that they should go to another. The sheikh says this is in the sound collections, and that Nuh, the longest lived of the prophets, answers that he made a prayer against his people and fears its outcome, and sends them on (see [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]). (needs_source: the report of voices of righteous scholars advising the people and the name given to them is relayed without a chain or book; the account of Adam and Nuh is told from memory without citing a collection) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:14:00}
- The sheikh adds that on the day God gathers the messengers and asks what answer they received, they will say they have no knowledge (see [[passage/quran-5-109|al-Ma'ida, verse 109]]). He relates that the people then go to Abraham, who recalls his three so-called lies, among them his saying that he was sick (see [[passage/quran-37-89|al-Saffat, verse 89]]), which the sheikh reads as being sick at heart at the worship of idols, and that Abraham too sends them on. The account then names another prophet, whose name is garbled in the transcript, and Isa, who each say that his Lord is angry as never before and sends them on, all saying that they are not for this place. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:16:30}
- He tells that the people go at last to the Prophet, who says he is for it, walks a few steps and prostrates beneath the Throne, where God opens to him praises that were not opened to anyone before and says to him to raise his head, ask and it will be given, intercede and it will be accepted (see [[concept/al-maqam-al-mahmud|the praised station]] and [[passage/quran-17-79|al-Isra, verse 79]]). He holds that this is the station every prophet envies, and he sets the steadiness of the Prophet against the fear of the others. He says the Prophet asks for his community, and is told to take those of it who have no reckoning and bring them in by a gate of the Garden, in which they share the other gates. (needs_source: the account of the Prophet's prostration beneath the Throne and the order to take those with no reckoning is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:18:00}
- He says the Prophet knows his community by their ranks in worship as a man knows his sons, and he relates that when one is called by his own and his father's name only he rises, as in the case of Talha (see [[person/talha-ibn-ubaydullah|Talha ibn Ubaydullah]]), so that every Talha knows who is meant, and he calls this a divine ability far beyond a computer entering a record. The sheikh adds that the Garden is near on this same earth. (needs_source: the story of Talha being called by name and the claim that the Garden lies on this same earth are given without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:19:30}
- He tells of those taken to the gates of the Garden without a reckoning, among them the martyrs and the patient (see [[concept/sabr|patience]]), and relates that Ridwan stops them and asks, and they answer that they do not wait for a book or a scale, citing the verse that the patient are paid their reward without count (see [[passage/quran-39-10|al-Zumar, verse 10]]). He relates their reply that they bore wrong, were forbearing when wronged, thankful when given and sought forgiveness when they sinned, and he adds that a widow left with children who raised them is among those he means. He says the Prophet leads that group in and returns, since the remaining community stands for the reckoning. (needs_source: the exchange of Ridwan with the patient and their words are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:21:30}
- The reckoning now begins with the flying of the pages. The sheikh dwells on the word used for the record fastened to each person, as one that cannot be given to another or mixed up (see [[passage/quran-17-13|al-Isra, verse 13]]), and likens it to a certificate with the grand total, while the detailed pages, folded in this life, are spread out again (see [[passage/quran-81-10|al-Takwir, verse 10]]) and set down (see [[passage/quran-18-49|al-Kahf, verse 49]]), at which the guilty say what is with this book that leaves out nothing small or great. He says the beginning of al-Isra shows that safety lies in staying with the Qur'an (see [[passage/quran-17-9|verse 9]] and [[passage/quran-17-10|verse 10]]), and says that reading it without reflection is a failure of respect. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:26:00}
- He says the pages fly from beneath the Throne like flocks of doves, over all the billions, and come down to settle on each person's neck, so that the book is taken in the right hand, in the left, or from behind the back (see [[passage/quran-69-19|al-Haqqa, verse 19]], [[passage/quran-69-25|verse 25]] and [[passage/quran-84-10|al-Inshiqaq, verse 10]]). He says the hand moves without will, as in the answers of the grave where the state speaks and not the tongue, and that the one whose book is full of sins is terrified to find in it things he forgot (see [[passage/quran-18-49|al-Kahf, verse 49]]), the neglect of prayer and the wronging of kin, while one who takes it in his right hand is reassured. He says a small sin is the smile and a large one the laugh, and that an angel reassures the one with few sins. (needs_source: the report of the pages flying like doves from beneath the Throne, the saying that the small sin is the smile and the large the laugh, and the angel's reassurance are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:29:00}
- At the flying of the pages the Fire comes forward and breathes out over the gathered three times, he says, with a roar and a rasping as the verse describes (see [[passage/quran-25-12|al-Furqan, verse 12]]). He relates that it has seventy thousand reins, each drawn by seventy thousand angels, that one spark would burn the earth and all in it, that long necks of the Fire pick unbelievers out of the crowd as a bird picks grain, that at the second breath every nation kneels (see [[passage/quran-45-28|al-Jathiya, verse 28]]) and at the third people fall on their faces looking sideways. He calls those whom it takes its children, the ones who kindled the fires of this world among people by hatred, slander, backbiting and oppression, in his own phrase. (needs_source: the three breaths, the seventy thousand reins each drawn by seventy thousand angels and the long necks picking unbelievers are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:32:30}
- Asked how all are reckoned for their number, he answers with the report that God feeds and provides for all, so the reckoning is as easy for Him. He separates the book, the summary of each person's deeds which is given first, from the pages with the detail, which are shown at the scale, and he says that from there each person is brought to the rights of others, such as a greeting unanswered or a visit not paid, and he adds that each of us will take his brother by the neck over what was owed. (needs_source: the report of how God reckons so many and that each will take his brother by the neck is relayed without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:34:30}
- The reckoning of the messengers comes first. He relates that God asks Nuh whether he conveyed the message, Nuh says yes, and his people deny it, so Nuh is asked for a witness and names Muhammad and his community (see [[passage/quran-2-143|al-Baqara, verse 143]]). The people of Nuh ask how this community can bear witness, and it answers that it read the Book of God and knew that Nuh had conveyed it. He says this is the praised station of the community, and warns that anyone busy with his own sins cannot hope to give. (needs_source: the exchange between Nuh, his people and the community is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:37:30}
- He tells that the messengers are questioned in turn until Isa is asked whether he told people to take him and his mother as gods (see [[passage/quran-5-116|al-Ma'ida, verse 116]]), and that Isa glorifies God and answers that he said only what he was commanded (see [[passage/quran-5-117|verse 117]]). The sheikh reads his words that if God punishes them they are His servants, and if He forgives, He is the Mighty and Wise (see [[passage/quran-5-118|verse 118]]), as the speech of one in terror who does not even ask for mercy, handing the matter to God, not as an intercession. He relates that when al-Hasan al-Basri read the verse on the day when the truthful profit from their truth (see [[passage/quran-5-119|verse 119]]) he fainted, and says that if the truthful are questioned about their truth, the judge too will wish he had never judged between two. (needs_source: the report of al-Hasan al-Basri fainting and the saying about the judge are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:40:30}
- He says the scales are then set up for the community, and that they are not asked as the earlier nations were. He cites the verse on the just scales of the Day of Rising (see [[passage/quran-21-47|al-Anbiya, verse 47]]) and the three scales of this life, the sky raised and the scale set so that none cheat in it (see [[passage/quran-55-7|al-Rahman, verse 7]] to [[passage/quran-55-9|verse 9]]), and says that scholars differ on whether it is one scale or several and whether it is a true scale or a figure for justice (see [[concept/al-mizan|the scale]]). He says it is set on this same earth, that none leaves it except for the Garden or the Fire, and that Adam's descendants are called one by one over a day of fifty thousand years (see [[passage/quran-70-4|al-Ma'arij, verse 4]]), which the guilty will take for a single hour (see [[passage/quran-30-55|al-Rum, verse 55]]). (needs_source: the claim that nothing of the Hereafter resembles the world but names is relayed without a chain, and the pace of the calling is the sheikh's own account) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:44:00}
- He relates that each person is called by his own and his father's name and the herald says that whoever has a grievance against him should come (see [[concept/mazalim|grievances]]), and he gives the examples of one wronged by a man who stood by and did not help, and one whose right was eaten, down to a needle and thread. He tells that God will take redress for a hornless sheep from a horned one that butted it, and that He would break one mountain for striking another, while mercy belongs to the one who sent forward good. He says God knows who is called first. (needs_source: the reports of the needle and thread and of the two sheep are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:45:30}
- He says that each person has scrolls as far as the eye can see, and that the sight of that day is sharp, so that God says to the servant to read his book (see [[passage/quran-17-14|al-Isra, verse 14]]) and the servant turns the pages and denies none of it. He relates three questions put to him: whether the recording angels wronged him, whether they left any good deed unwritten, and whether he has an excuse. The sheikh says the Prophet is reported to have said that none accepts an excuse more than God, and that no excuse will hold, not being deceived, nor lack of time for prayer, nor the demands of a job, since the Qur'an calls on the soul that purifies itself to succeed and the one that corrupts it to fail (see [[passage/quran-91-9|al-Shams, verse 9]] and [[passage/quran-91-10|verse 10]]). (needs_source: the report of the scrolls and of the three questions to the servant is relayed without a chain or book; the transcript gives a number of years per scroll that is garbled and not carried over) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:50:00}
- He relates the hadith of the bankrupt, the one who comes with prayer, fasting and charity yet has wronged people, so that his creditors take his good deeds, then are given of their own sins to carry, until he is brought to nothing, and he says that Jibril then announces that so and so is wretched and will not be happy after it. He turns to those who take their book in the left hand, cries of regret, and to the binding in chains (see [[passage/quran-69-25|al-Haqqa, verse 25]] to [[passage/quran-69-27|verse 27]] and [[passage/quran-69-30|verse 30]] to [[passage/quran-69-32|verse 32]]). The sheikh says that for the one whose scales are heavy the creditors are few, because God carries the debts of those who turned to Him and served His religion with sincere intent, and that such a person lifts his book with the right hand (see [[passage/quran-69-19|verse 19]] to [[passage/quran-69-24|verse 24]]) and knows his place in the Garden better than his own house, which is a distance of forty years for a lean horse between the two leaves of its gate. (needs_source: the hadith of the bankrupt, Jibril's announcement and the distance of forty years between the leaves of the gate are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:53:00}
- The episode ends with his promise to take up the Garden and its smell in the next episode, and with a long supplication for firmness on the day of the great terror, repentance, an easy death, a book in the right hand, a drink from the basin and the sight of God's face (see [[concept/al-hawd|the basin]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep24 00:54:30}

## Episode 25: the Sirat: the five remedies, the four held up as proof, the kinds of record, the zakat of wealth, the seven bridges of the Sirat and the bridge of wrongs
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and the staring eyes of the disbelievers (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]). The host reports that viewers have split into two camps: those who like the programme's style even though it warns, and those who reject it and ask to hear about Paradise. The sheikh answers that he neither encourages nor frightens from himself, but presents the words of God and of His Messenger, and that any headache or pressure caused by fear sits in the scale of that person's good deeds. He says the verses of mercy equal the verses of punishment, that the warning is not for the disbeliever alone but for the disobedient, for those who owe rights to the Creator and to creatures, for those who wronged people and ruined mosques, and he recalls God's words that most people do not believe in God except while associating partners (see [[passage/quran-12-106|Yusuf, verse 106]]). He promises not to leave the viewers until he brings them to the door of Paradise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:05:00}
- The host recalls that earlier episodes reached the flying of the pages, the reckoning and the scale, and that today's episode is on the Sirat (see [[concept/sirat|the Sirat]]). The sheikh says the order, as he understands it from the verses, the hadiths and the sound reports, is the reckoning, then the scale, then the Sirat, that many people picture the scale after the Sirat, and that some scholars differ on the order. He reminds the viewer that a Muslim says at least seventeen times a day in prayer the words asking to be guided to the straight path (see [[passage/quran-1-6|al-Fatiha, verse 6]] and [[concept/al-sirat-al-mustaqim|the straight path]]), and he holds that whoever walked the straight path in this world crosses the Sirat on the Day of Rising and is saved, while whoever was bent aside by other paths will not pass. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:07:30}
- He tells of a man who came to al-Husayn ibn Ali (see [[person/husayn-ibn-ali|al-Husayn ibn Ali]]) complaining that whenever he wanted to repent he went back to his sin, and al-Husayn said he had five remedies. The sheikh gives the remedies as he relates them: whoever wants to disobey God should disobey Him in a kingdom that is not His, then should not eat of His provision, then should disobey Him where He does not see, then, if the angel of death comes, should ask for a delay in order to repent, and then, if gathered before God and asked why he disobeyed, should say that he did not disobey. The man answers that all this is impossible and calls God to witness his repentance. The sheikh sees in this the pillar of entering upon God, and compares the disobedient to a guest who breaks his host's furniture, eats his provision and then defies him. (needs_source: the story of the man, al-Husayn ibn Ali and the five remedies is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:10:30}
- He says four are held up as proof against four, leaving no one an excuse: the wealthy are answered by Sulayman (see [[person/sulayman|Sulayman]]), the afflicted by Ayyub (see [[person/ayyub|Ayyub]] and [[passage/quran-38-44|Sad, verse 44]]), the young and handsome by Yusuf (see [[person/yusuf|Yusuf]] and [[passage/quran-12-101|Yusuf, verse 101]]), and the poor by Isa (see [[person/isa-ibn-maryam|Isa]]), who, he says, put his hand under his head as a pillow and called himself the richest of the rich. He sees no excuse, holds that the prophets were sent to lead people on this path, and that a person can do what he thinks beyond him if he trains, as the body is trained, and he says the heaviest thing placed in the scale is a gulp of anger swallowed by a believer who could have acted on it. (needs_source: the four prophets as proof, the saying of Isa that he is the richest of the rich, and the report of the gulp of anger are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:15:00}
- He then describes the ways records are taken: in the right hand, in the left hand, or from behind the back (see [[passage/quran-84-7|al-Inshiqaq, verse 7]] to [[passage/quran-84-12|verse 12]]). He holds that one given his book from behind has no hope and no intercession, which is his own view, and he says that those of the disobedient monotheists who enter the Fire spend a term and then God's mercy reaches them and they come out, and God knows best. He recalls that the disbeliever will wish he were dust when the animals are told to be dust (see [[passage/quran-78-40|al-Naba, verse 40]]). He relates that the Prophet told Aisha (see [[person/aisha|Aisha]]) that the easy reckoning is merely the presentation before God and that whoever is closely questioned is destroyed (see [[passage/quran-84-8|al-Inshiqaq, verse 8]]). (needs_source: the report of the animals told to be dust and the hadith of Aisha on the easy reckoning are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:17:30}
- He relates that the Prophet said God takes from every thousand nine hundred and ninety-nine to the Fire, that the Companions were afraid, and that he told them not to fear, since among those taken are two great multitudes which he identifies as Gog and Magog and Iblis with his soldiers. The sheikh distinguishes a positive fear that drives a person to act and repent from a negative fear that makes him turn away. (needs_source: the report of nine hundred and ninety-nine of every thousand and the two multitudes named is relayed without a chain or book, and its details are unclear in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:18:30}
- He then turns to a hadith in Sahih Muslim, as he names it, about the owner of gold and silver who does not pay their due: plates of fire are made for him and his forehead, side and back are branded with them (see [[concept/zakat|zakat]]) on a day lasting fifty thousand years until judgement is passed between people. He advises a man of wealth to use an accountant to work out his zakat and to show it to a scholar of fiqh. He says the hadith covers money, camels and land, mentioning the owner of camels who does not pay their due being trampled by their feet and gored by their horns, and one who takes people's land unjustly carrying it on his back (see [[passage/quran-6-31|al-Anam, verse 31]]). (needs_source: the hadith of the camels and the report of carrying land taken unjustly are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:25:00}
- He says every nation is called with its imam (see [[passage/quran-17-71|al-Isra, verse 71]]), that whoever followed the imam of good is saved and his book is raised (see [[passage/quran-69-19|al-Haqqa, verse 19]] to [[passage/quran-69-21|verse 21]]), and that whoever followed the imam of evil goes with him to the Fire, so each person must choose his imam. He then relates that a caller calls from God for the people to cross the Sirat, that the crossing begins with the Prophet at the right of the Sirat, and that those of his community who cross without a reckoning he takes with him (see [[passage/quran-21-104|al-Anbiya, verse 104]]). (needs_source: the call of the caller and the Prophet's place at the right of the Sirat are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:27:30}
- The host asks who enters Paradise without a reckoning, and the sheikh cites one of his teachers that among them is one who washes his garment and finds no other. He goes on to say that God loves to see the trace of His blessing on His servant, that thankfulness is of the kind of the blessing, and that whoever has a surplus of provision should return it to one who has none. He mentions a coming winter and children in Pakistan, Iraq and Palestine without enough to cover them, says Muslims are like one body, and grieves over the persecution and occupation that Muslims suffer. (needs_source: what he relays from one of his teachers about who enters Paradise without a reckoning) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:31:00}
- He relates that the Prophet says he stands at the right of the Throne facing the Fire, with Ibrahim the Friend of God on his left facing Paradise, and that the Companions asked why he was not facing Paradise, and he said so that he might see those of his community going to the Fire and intercede for them. He adds that the Fire lies beneath the Sirat and is black, not red, so that the one crossing sees nothing and only his faith lights his way, and that their light runs before them and on their right (see [[passage/quran-57-12|al-Hadid, verse 12]] and [[passage/quran-57-13|verse 13]]). (needs_source: the report of the Prophet and Ibrahim standing at either side of the Throne and the black colour of the Fire are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:34:30}
- He says those who cross the Sirat are of three kinds: the one who is saved, the one who is scratched and the one who is piled up. On the Sirat are hooks like the thorns of the sa'dan plant, one particular hook for each sin that snatches its owner. Whoever has few sins and many good deeds is caught by the hooks, rises and goes on, while whoever's light goes dark stops and is snatched away. He says the speed of crossing is according to faith: some pass like the loosed wind, some like a lean horse, some crawl, and some have light only the size of the thumb of the foot, which flickers on and off, being the servant who worships his Lord on the edge. He relates that the Fire calls to some believers to pass quickly, since their light has covered its flame. (needs_source: the report of the hooks, the differences in speed and the call of the Fire to the believer is relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:38:00}
- He says there are seven bridges on the Sirat, at each of which the servant is made to stand. The first is the bridge of tawhid, where one whose tawhid is mixed with smoke, associating partners or showing off falls (see [[concept/tawhid|tawhid]]), then prayer and its fruit, then zakat (see [[concept/zakat|zakat]]), then fasting, then the pilgrimage, then enjoining good and forbidding evil (see [[concept/amr-bil-maruf|enjoining good]]), and the gravest is the bridge of wrongs (see [[concept/mazalim|the wrongs]]). He cites that God wrongs no soul at all (see [[passage/quran-36-54|Ya Sin, verse 54]]). (needs_source: the number and order of the seven bridges are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:40:30}
- He dwells on the bridge of wrongs and says God says to the servant that he saw Him wronged and did not help Him, and could have covered Him and did not, and the sheikh asks where the brotherhood and Islam went. He says the angels judge wrongs at once, that every wife, son and wronged person clings to the one who wronged them (see [[passage/quran-30-41|al-Rum, verse 41]]), and he sees the day as one in which the wrongs of family, neighbours and ruler and ruled are settled. He says whoever helped the wrongdoers, even by handing an inkwell, shares in their wrong, and cites a man (see [[person/abu-hazim-al-araj|Abu Hazim al-A'raj]]) who refused to hand the commander of the faithful the inkwell so as not to become his partner, and says someone asked al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) whether he was among the helpers of the wrongdoers and was told that he was among the wrongdoers. (needs_source: the stories of Abu Hazim and al-Hasan al-Basri with the wrongdoers are relayed without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:44:30}
- He contrasts this with the devil disowning the human being once he disbelieves (see [[passage/quran-59-16|al-Hashr, verse 16]]), explains the difference between the delegation to which the God-fearing are gathered (see [[passage/quran-19-85|Maryam, verse 85]]) and the watering-place to which the criminals are driven (see [[passage/quran-19-86|verse 86]]), and says the criminals are stopped and questioned before being guided to the path of the Furnace (see [[passage/quran-37-23|al-Saffat, verse 23]] and [[passage/quran-37-24|verse 24]]) and then go down into the abyss (see [[passage/quran-101-10|al-Qari'a, verse 10]] and [[passage/quran-101-11|verse 11]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:46:30}
- He closes with a reflection on his own responsibility, saying that he does not give an opinion of his own but presents what he read in the Book of God and understood from the words of the Messenger and from his teachers, that he wants to bring the community back to its Islam in a good way, that they are building stones and not demolition tools, and that the Prophet was a bringer of good news and a warner, so both roads must be made clear. He ends with a prayer that God make their feet firm on the Sirat on the day feet slip, and grant repentance and guidance before death and honour after it. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep25 00:51:00}

## Episode 26: the Basin and intercession: the bridge of wrongs, who drinks from the Basin and who is driven off, the elements, kinds and limits of intercession
- The episode opens with the recitation of the chapter of al-Kawthar (see [[passage/quran-108-1|al-Kawthar, verse 1]] to [[passage/quran-108-3|verse 3]]) and with the host recalling that the previous meeting stopped at the last bridge on the Sirat, the bridge of wrongs, and that today moves to a new stage, the Basin (see [[concept/al-hawd|the Basin]]) and then intercession (see [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]). The sheikh says there are two accounts of the bridges: some hold that there are not several bridges above the Sirat, only one main bridge between the Sirat and the Basin, the bridge of wrongs, which is the most important of them. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:03:00}
- He says that what lies between a servant and his Lord rests on God's pardon, and quotes one of the righteous as saying that his hope in God with his sins outweighs his hope in Him with his deeds, because with sins he relies on forgiveness and with deeds on himself. He holds that the rights of others must be settled before leaving this world. (needs_source: the saying of one of the righteous on hope with sins and with deeds is quoted without naming the speaker or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:05:00}
- He says every prophet has a basin, and that the Prophet said he would boast of his community's numbers on the Day of Resurrection. The sheikh explains this as boasting in the number who come to the Basin and drink from it, not in head-count alone, since not everyone who goes to the Basin drinks. He relates that angels drive some people away and hold them back, and that the Prophet likened himself to a man who lit a fire while moths fall into it as he holds them by their waists and they slip from him. He says the Prophet stands on the right of the Sirat, sees people plunging into the Fire and wants to bring them to him, but is prevented because intercession has limits. (needs_source: the hadith that every prophet has a basin, the moths hadith and the report of the Prophet standing on the right of the Sirat are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:07:00}
- He relates that some people are driven from the Basin, the Prophet wishes to intercede for them and is told he does not know what they innovated after him, and he says away with whoever changed. The sheikh holds that these are those who altered God's religion, added to it and did not uphold His law, gives as an example someone who prays at home but not abroad, and says that those who reject penalties such as cutting the thief's hand, calling them savage, fall under this. This is his own view. (needs_source: the hadith of those driven from the Basin and told away is related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:08:30}
- He describes the effect of drinking: spite, rancour and envy leave the heart, and God removes the rancour from hearts (see [[passage/quran-15-47|al-Hijr, verse 47]]), then the person grows to the build of Adam, sixty cubits, free of illness and defect. He says the Basin purifies what remains of the dregs of this world in the heart of someone whose faith was not strong enough to burn away desires and grudges, so that it serves as a healing remedy before entering Paradise. (needs_source: the report of growing to sixty cubits after drinking is related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:24:00}
- He explains that al-kawthar in Arabic means a great abundance, and tells of a Bedouin woman asked about her son who said he had come back with kawthar. He says the Prophet described the Basin's size as the distance between Mecca and Jerusalem, and in another account between Ayla and Aden (see [[place/ayla|Ayla]] and [[place/aden|Aden]]), and that he researched this and found that the Prophet answered each questioner with a distance that questioner knew, and that references will be given at the end of the series. (needs_source: the reports of the Basin's size are related without chain or book; the explanation of the difference by the questioner is the sheikh's own view) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:12:00}
- He describes the water of the Basin as sweeter than honey and whiter than milk, with cups as many as the stars, so that when a believer stretches out his hand he finds a cup, and says whoever drinks never thirsts again. He mentions a higher degree in which the Prophet gives some believers to drink with his own hand instead of the angels. (needs_source: the description of the water and cups and of the Prophet giving to drink by hand is related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:14:00}
- He then asks who drinks from the Basin and lists several kinds: one who accepts people's excuses and seeks seventy excuses for his brother, one who does not quarrel with people, one who is a key to good and a lock against evil, while the reverse, one who locks good and opens evil, does not drink. He adds that whoever harms the weak who have no helper but God does not drink, and cites the hadith of Paradise and the Fire disputing, with Paradise saying the weak enter it. He says the reward matches the deed. (needs_source: the qualities of those who drink and the hadith of Paradise and the Fire disputing are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:18:00}
- He says whoever drinks from the Basin is made to know his home in Paradise, and gives two senses of the words that God will have made it known to them, either knowing the place or its fragrance. He holds that God shows the righteous servant the place he would have had in the Fire had he disobeyed, then tells him that since he obeyed he should go to his place in Paradise, and that others take over places in turn. He says the wisdom is that every person has a place in Paradise and a place in the Fire. (needs_source: the report of showing the servant his place in the Fire before entering Paradise is related without chain or book, and the transcript is unclear in this stretch) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:21:00}
- He says people recognise one another at the Basin, and that on the Day of Resurrection there are three places where no one asks after a relative, the flying of the pages, the scale and the Sirat (see [[passage/quran-80-34|Abasa, verse 34]] to [[passage/quran-80-37|verse 37]]). He says people of disobedience gather on disobedience and then disown one another, while those who visited and loved one another for God are called by a caller as neighbours of God and enter Paradise. (needs_source: the hadith of the neighbours of God and the caller, and the three places where no one asks after a relative, are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:26:00}
- He then turns to intercession, saying it requires four things: an intercessor, the one interceded with, the one interceded for and the matter interceded in. He cites that intercession is only with God's permission (see [[passage/quran-2-255|al-Baqara, verse 255]]) and gives the example of an employee who intercedes with a manager because of his standing with him. He names the great intercession as the first kind, the Prophet's intercession that the reckoning begin for all nations. (needs_source: the hadith of the great intercession is related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:29:00}
- He mentions the Prophet's intercession for those whose good and bad deeds are equal, the people of al-A'raf, and says the Prophet places the card in the pan of good deeds so that it outweighs, and that the declaration of faith has rights and is not only words. He then turns to intercession to lighten punishment and relates the case of Abu Talib (see [[person/abu-talib|Abu Talib]]), when al-Abbas asked the Prophet whether his deeds had benefited him, the man who had defended him, and he answered that he was placed in a shallow part of the Fire wearing two sandals that boil his brain. Scholars differ on whether Abu Talib believed, and this is what the sheikh relates. (needs_source: the card report and the report of Abu Talib in the shallow fire are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:32:30}
- He relates the hadith of the seventy thousand who enter Paradise without reckoning, with the words of Ukkasha ibn Mihsan and the Prophet's reply, and says he searched the hadith and found that some hypocrites were in the gathering, so the Prophet did not wish to open the door to others. He says every prophet has an answered prayer and that the Prophet kept his as intercession for those who committed major sins in his community. (needs_source: the hadith of the seventy thousand, the report of Ukkasha and the prayer kept for intercession are related without chain or book; the sheikh's explanation of why the second man was not answered is his own view) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:34:00}
- He says God pardons what concerns His own right in major sins and accepts the Prophet's intercession, that this intercession comes before the Sirat, at the scale and while he stands beside the Sirat for those he sees he can intercede for, and that it is for groups, not named individuals. He sets as a condition that the sin owes no wrong to anyone among the servants. He says the rights of spouses, children and parents are to be settled in this world before death, and that someone whose father was harsh to him should not let that stop him doing good. (needs_source: the times of the Prophet's intercession for major sinners and the condition that the sin owe no wrong to others are the sheikh's own view) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:37:30}
- He relates the account of two men, a wrongdoer and the wronged, to whom God shows palaces in Paradise; they ask whom the palaces belong to and are told they belong to whoever pays the price, that one forgive the other, and when they say they have forgiven each other they are told to take their brother's hand and enter. (needs_source: the account of the wrongdoer, the wronged and the palaces is related without chain or book, and the transcript is unclear at its opening) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:39:30}
- He then names intercessors other than the Prophet: the martyr intercedes for seventy of his family, and a Companion and a scholar for those they know. He relates that after the intercession of the prophets, the truthful, the martyrs and the scholars, God says only His own remains and He is the Most Merciful, and takes a handful from the Fire and enters them into Paradise. He says what concerns God's essence and attributes is beyond our minds. He relates the hadith of the Bedouin who asked the Prophet whether he had asked his Lord, and was told that God gave him seventy thousand with each of the seventy thousand and three handfuls of His handfuls, and that Umar put his hand over the Bedouin's mouth. (needs_source: the hadith of the intercession of martyrs and scholars, of the handful and of the Bedouin and the handfuls are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:42:30}
- He says God brings out of the Fire people who never did any good but also did no evil and harmed no one, and contrasts them with someone who prays, fasts and performs the pilgrimage while his heart holds rancour against Muslims, so his deeds are lost. He says those taken in the handful first fell into the Fire, and a day with God is fifty thousand years (see [[passage/quran-70-4|al-Ma'arij, verse 4]]), so they spend their share of punishment and are then purified. (needs_source: the hadith of those who did no good at all and the Muslim report named are related without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:44:30}
- He names those intercession does not help, putting first the arrogant and those who presume upon God and legislate on their own, and says intercession is not absolute but restricted by prior deeds, likening it to a mercy committee that raises the marks of someone who nearly passed but not of someone who scored very low. He cites that whoever intercedes with a good intercession will have a share in it (see [[passage/quran-4-85|al-Nisa, verse 85]]). On the question of usury and banks he says he tells those who ask that the verdict is that it is forbidden, and this is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:47:30}
- He says the first to enter Paradise are the people of affliction, since every servant is tried (see [[passage/quran-29-2|al-Ankabut, verse 2]]), and that those who were called to prostration in this world while sound cannot do it in the next (see [[passage/quran-68-42|al-Qalam, verse 42]] and [[passage/quran-68-43|verse 43]]). Turning to the verses of al-Furqan on abiding in punishment (see [[passage/quran-25-68|al-Furqan, verse 68]] to [[passage/quran-25-70|verse 70]]), he says the Fire has seven levels, six whose people abide and one, Jahannam, from which monotheists do not abide, and that intercession concerns only its inhabitants. (needs_source: the division of the Fire into six levels of abiding and a seventh not abiding for monotheists is the sheikh's own view, stated without attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:51:30}
- He relates that the Prophet told Fatima (see [[person/fatima|Fatima]]), his aunt Safiyya (see [[person/safiyya-bint-abd-al-muttalib|Safiyya bint Abd al-Muttalib]]) and al-Abbas to work, since he cannot avail them anything against God. He answers the host's question about the disbelieving nations such as Gog and Magog, Pharaoh and Haman by saying they are driven to the Fire from the start. He closes with verses on fearing injustice to others and promises another meeting after the feast. (needs_source: the report 'work, for I cannot avail you' is related without chain or book, and the sheikh's remark on the fate of the disbelieving nations is his own view) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep26 00:54:00}

## Episode 27: those promised the Fire: Iblis and his story with Adam, the meaning of Satan, pride, envy and hatred, and the methods and steps of Satan and who is protected from him
- The episode opens with recitation of verses from the chapters of al-Anbiya and al-Kahf (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]] and [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]], and [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]]). The host begins by mentioning media reports of bombings that struck Amman and killed innocent people. The sheikh says that harming the secure in Muslim lands is something Islam does not know, and that those who came from outside the Muslim world entered under a pledge of security which, even if unwritten, is written in hearts. He puts it down to a lack of understanding of the law among some young people, and perhaps a hidden hand from non-Muslims that uses some of the ignorant, says it is never part of Islam to terrify a person, and prays that those with authority in Jordan will handle the matter wisely. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:04:30}
- The host then states the plan for the new stage: earlier episodes stopped at the Sirat, the Basin and intercession, and what remains is the Fire and its levels, then Paradise and its degrees. The sheikh says the aim is neither to terrify the secure nor to frighten Muslims, but to spread what is in the Book of God and what came from the Prophet so that truths become clear. He begins with those given tidings of the Fire, people who reached the furthest extent of disobedience a person can imagine, just as those promised Paradise reached the highest obedience, and first among them is Iblis. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:07:30}
- The sheikh explains the meaning of shaytan in the language: from shatan, remoteness, so anyone who draws people away from the road of good is a shaytan, whether human or jinn (see [[concept/shayatin-al-ins-wal-jinn|devils of humans and jinn]]), and he gives the example of someone who dissuades people from prayer or from the headscarf. Iblis, he says, is from iblas, being cut off from good, and he notes the Qur'an uses the word mublisun. He says the word Iblis occurs eleven times in the Qur'an and seeking refuge with God also occurs eleven times, and that the word shaytan occurs eighty-eight times, as does the word angels. (needs_source: the counts of how often words occur in the Qur'an are figures the sheikh gives without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:09:30}
- He presents two views on Iblis's origin. One holds he was of the angels, because the command to prostrate was addressed to the angels and he was excepted from it (see [[passage/quran-2-34|al-Baqara, verse 34]]), and he attributes this to some major scholars.
