The other, to which the sheikh inclines on the text of the Qur'an, is that he was of the jinn and acted wickedly against his Lord's command (see [[passage/quran-18-50|al-Kahf, verse 50]]); he holds that before this Iblis was a chief of the jinn and a worshipper. It is a disputed matter among scholars (see [[concept/sujud-al-malaika-li-adam|the prostration of the angels to Adam]]). (needs_source: the attribution of the view that Iblis was an angel to major scholars is made without naming them) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:11:00}
- He returns to the story of Adam (see [[person/adam|Adam]]) and Hawwa (see [[person/hawwa|Hawwa]]) in Paradise, saying that jannah in the language means a garden, and that some scholars said Adam and Hawwa wore a light that covered their nakedness and left after they ate from the tree. He likens the believer to the Kaaba surrounded by rings of God's covering, one ring uncovered after another as he sins. He tells of a youth whose hand was cut for theft, whose mother said to Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]) it was his first time; Umar replied that God would not disgrace him the first time, and the youth later admitted to twenty-one thefts, which the sheikh calls gradual drawing-in (istidraj). (needs_source: the scholars' statement about a light on Adam and Hawwa, the likening of the believer to the Kaaba, and the story of the thief's mother and Umar are told without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:15:00}
- He shows how Iblis entered through human weakness and the love of lasting: he told them the tree would make them angels or immortals (see [[passage/quran-7-20|al-A'raf, verse 20]]), pointed them to a tree of immortality (see [[passage/quran-20-120|Ta Ha, verse 120]]), swore he was a sincere adviser (see [[passage/quran-7-21|al-A'raf, verse 21]]) and led them on by deception (see [[passage/quran-7-22|al-A'raf, verse 22]]). He says scholars explain gharur (deception) as what deceives you then harms you, and relates from books of biography that Seth said to his father Adam that he had not thought anyone would swear falsely by God. (needs_source: the words of Seth son of Adam are related from biography books without naming the book, and the scholars' explanation of gharur is given without naming them) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:17:30}
- The sheikh sees two great flaws in the human being, forgetfulness and weak resolve (see [[passage/quran-20-115|Ta Ha, verse 115]]), and gives the example of someone who promises repentance at the end of Ramadan then forgets. He mentions that Umar said to Abu Musa (see [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]]) "come, let us have faith for an hour", and that the Prophet asked Ibn Mas'ud to recite to him and wept. (needs_source: Umar's words to Abu Musa and the Prophet's request to Ibn Mas'ud are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:19:00}
- He says Hawwa was created from a living thing, links the name Adam to the surface of the earth, and stresses that male and female complement rather than contradict one another, each with its own role (see [[passage/quran-3-36|Al Imran, verse 36]]), warning against confusing them. He notes that the first thing that appeared on Adam and Hawwa after eating was their nakedness, and tells that his small son asked why women do not run in the sa'i, to which he answered that Hajar (see [[person/hajar|Hajar]]) ran on women's behalf. He rejects the claim that Hawwa was the cause of leaving Paradise, saying it is in neither the Qur'an, nor exegesis, nor the hadiths. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:21:30}
- He says Adam and Hawwa stopped as soon as their nakedness showed and returned to God confessing their fault (see [[passage/quran-7-23|al-A'raf, verse 23]]), that God chose him and turned to him (see [[passage/quran-20-121|Ta Ha, verse 121]], [[passage/quran-20-122|verse 122]] and [[passage/quran-2-37|al-Baqara, verse 37]]), and concludes that the human being came down to earth forgiven, with no inherited sin. He says inherited sin is a doctrine among those outside Islam and has no place in Islam; this is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:23:30}
- He sets out Iblis's argument: he reasoned by analogy and claimed to be better than Adam because he was made of fire (see [[passage/quran-38-76|Sad, verse 76]]). The sheikh holds that whoever relies on reason without the texts goes astray, and that the analogy itself is wrong in his view, since clay is better than fire; jinn and humans, unlike angels, are charged and free to choose (see [[passage/quran-55-31|al-Rahman, verse 31]]). He says some jurists listed eighteen kinds of angels, among them a group called "the exalted" occupied only with glorifying God, to whom the command to prostrate was not addressed; he says this is his view, and mentions another reading of the exalted as meaning height and pride (see [[passage/quran-38-75|Sad, verse 75]]). He relates that the Prophet said seventy thousand angels enter the Frequented House every day (see [[concept/bayt-al-mamur|the Frequented House]]). (needs_source: the division of angels into eighteen kinds including "the exalted" is attributed to some jurists without naming them, and the report of the Frequented House is related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:26:30}
- He says Iblis's hatred began at that moment, and that the first sins by which God was disobeyed were pride, hatred and envy (see [[concept/kibr|pride]] and [[concept/hasad|envy]]). Pride appeared in his refusal to prostrate, envy in the angels prostrating to Adam and not to him, and hatred was born of that. He holds that anyone with something of these three has a trait of Iblis, with the example of someone asking why so-and-so is rich and he is not. He mentions that whoever has an atom's weight of pride in his heart does not enter Paradise. (needs_source: the report that one with an atom of pride does not enter Paradise is related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:30:30}
- He states the difference between the disobedience of Adam and that of Iblis (see [[concept/fariq-bayn-maasiyat-adam-wa-iblis|the difference between the disobedience of Adam and Iblis]]): Adam erred in a "training stage" through heedlessness and misunderstanding, repented and asked God's forgiveness, while Iblis disobeyed out of pride and persisted. He says scholars said seeking forgiveness for doing what is forbidden is easier than for disobeying a command. He recalls that God receives the repentant, relates that the Prophet said God is more merciful to His servants than a captive woman who laid her child on her belly, and tells that Moses asked his Lord about admitting some servants to the Fire and was told to sow a crop. (needs_source: the scholars' statement on the difficulty of repenting from disobeying a command, the report of the woman and the child, and the story of Moses and the crop are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:45:30}
- He says Iblis admitted he has no power over the sincere (see [[passage/quran-15-40|al-Hijr, verse 40]] and [[passage/quran-15-42|verse 42]]), distinguishes the servants of the Merciful (see [[passage/quran-25-63|al-Furqan, verse 63]]) from servants "of ours" (see [[passage/quran-17-5|al-Isra, verse 5]]), and the mukhlis (sincere) from the mukhlas (chosen sincere), saying the chosen servant is connected to God like Joseph (see [[passage/quran-12-24|Yusuf, verse 24]]). He quotes Ibn al-Qayyim (see [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn Qayyim al-Jawziyya]]) as saying to his soul "be sincere and be freed", and says Iblis was among the witnesses to Joseph's innocence. (needs_source: the phrase "be sincere and be freed" is attributed to Ibn al-Qayyim without naming a book, and counting Iblis a witness to Joseph's innocence is the sheikh's own statement) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:33:30}
- He says Satan has offspring and followers among humans and jinn (see [[passage/quran-17-64|al-Isra, verse 64]], [[passage/quran-114-6|al-Nas, verse 6]] and [[passage/quran-36-60|Ya Sin, verse 60]]) and is alive, and he states that in the United States there are about five hundred thousand devil worshippers and in Europe about seven hundred thousand. He reads the hadith of women "clothed yet naked, inclined and inclining" as describing a satanic trait, which is his reading of the hadith. (needs_source: the numbers of devil worshippers are statistics the sheikh gives without a source, and the hadith of the clothed yet naked is related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:36:00}
- He relates that Satan sets his throne on the water every night and sends out his troops, the nearest to him being the one who causes the greatest trial, and that one says he stayed with a man until he divorced his wife, whereupon Iblis draws him near. The sheikh adds advice to husbands and wives not to treat divorce lightly. In another hadith he relates that the Prophet went out at night to al-Baqi' (see [[place/baqi-al-gharqad|al-Baqi']]) and asked "has your devil come to you?", saying everyone has one, even himself, except that God helped him against it so it submitted (aslama). The sheikh says some jurists held that the devil became Muslim, and he himself inclines to the meaning that he is safe from its evil. He mentions that the Prophet seized Satan during prayer until he felt the coolness of its spittle on his hand. (needs_source: the report of Iblis's throne on the water, the hadith of al-Baqi', and the report of seizing Satan in prayer are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:40:00}
- He gives his view on the jinn: he rejects marriage between jinn and humans, saying that once this door is opened it will not close, and affirms that jinn marry jinn and that among them are Muslims and the deviant (see [[passage/quran-72-11|al-Jinn, verse 11]]), that there are believing jinn who worship God and pray, that the rebellious unbelievers among them are under Iblis's command with the work of leading humans astray, and that Iblis is the father of the jinn as Adam is the father of humans (see [[concept/jinn|the jinn]]). These are unseen and disputed matters on which nothing is decided. (needs_source: the worship, prayer and fasting of believing jinn and the denial of marriage between jinn and humans are the sheikh's statements given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:42:00}
- He lists names of Satan: al-gharur, the deceiver (see [[passage/quran-31-33|Luqman, verse 33]]), and the taghut, which he says is anything worshipped besides God or that turns people from God, then says whoever rules by other than what God sent down has made himself a taghut, restricting unbelief to one who saw his own law as better than God's law. This is his view, and defining what is meant is disputed among scholars. He explains fisq from a ripe date slipping out of its skin, so the fasiq is one who steps out of God's command. He cites a hadith that one legal penalty carried out on earth is better than seventy autumns of rain. (needs_source: the hadith that carrying out one penalty is better than seventy autumns of rain is related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:48:30}
- Among his methods, he says, Satan first commands indecency and makes the forbidden look attractive, with the example of an employee who dresses up and warms to a female colleague under the name of courtesy, then he sows doubt about wudu and prayer. The sheikh sees congregational prayer as the remedy, says a woman receives the reward of the congregation if her husband goes to it, says he would like women to go to mosques, that Arab customs have overcome acts of worship, and mentions the Prophet's saying not to keep God's maidservants from God's mosques. (needs_source: the hadith not to prevent God's maidservants from His mosques is related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:51:30}
- He says Satan runs in a person as blood runs and slinks away when God is remembered, citing the command to seek refuge at an incitement (see [[passage/quran-7-200|al-A'raf, verse 200]]) and noting that a person praying who is touched by an incitement interrupts the recitation, seeks refuge, then carries on. He links this to the one who consumes usury (see [[concept/riba|riba]]), like one whom Satan drives to madness by touch (see [[passage/quran-2-275|al-Baqara, verse 275]]), saying he was Satan's partner in this world; this is his view. He mentions that Satan ties three knots on the head of the sleeper, loosened by waking, then wudu, then prayer, and that one who sleeps until morning has Satan urinate in his ear, and says he himself recites the first four verses of al-Kahf each night (see [[passage/quran-18-1|al-Kahf, verse 1]] to [[passage/quran-18-4|verse 4]]). (needs_source: the reports of Satan running like blood, the three knots and the urination in the ear are related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:56:30}
- He mentions satanic seduction (see [[passage/quran-6-71|al-An'am, verse 71]]) and criticises what he sees as corruption in music videos and entertainment, citing the love of spreading indecency (see [[passage/quran-24-19|al-Nur, verse 19]]). He mentions that on the Day of Resurrection Satan will say he promised them and failed them and had no power over them except that he called and they answered (see [[passage/quran-14-22|Ibrahim, verse 22]]). This is his application of the verses to present reality. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 00:59:00}
- He advises seeking refuge with God on entering the toilet, sleeping and going out, and reciting al-Baqara in the house to drive out devils, and relates that when someone enters his house without greeting or naming God the devils say there is no lodging or supper for them, that the Prophet seized the hand of a girl who reached for food without naming God, saying the devil's hand was with hers, that one who eats without naming God is joined by Satan, and that the weak devil of a believer met the devil of an unbeliever and complained that his household names God over everything. (needs_source: the reports about the house, naming God over food, the girl's hand and the meeting of the two devils are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:02:30}
- He sets out the steps of Satan (see [[passage/quran-24-21|al-Nur, verse 21]]) in order: first he calls a person to leave the faith by casting doubt on the hadiths and the afterlife; if he fails, he calls to innovation in acts of worship, which the sheikh holds applies to worship and not customs, with no such thing as a good innovation; if he fails, he draws him into major sins; if he fails, he makes small sins attractive, citing (no small sin with persistence, no great sin with seeking forgiveness); if he fails, he busies him with many permitted things; then he sets his troops of humans and jinn on him. He cites the promise of poverty (see [[passage/quran-2-268|al-Baqara, verse 268]]) and of false hopes (see [[passage/quran-4-120|al-Nisa, verse 120]]). (needs_source: the saying "no small sin with persistence, no great sin with seeking forgiveness" is given without attribution) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:08:30}
- He says protection is by holding fast to God, sincerity, keeping to the Book, the Sunna and the congregation, and remembrance, and that whoever does so has no power over him, and that when the aim is deliverance from the Fire and entering Paradise everything else becomes light. He gives figures he says are counts of words in the Qur'an: the righteous six times and the wicked three, life and death each 145 times, good deeds and evil deeds each 167, this world and the next each 115, hardship and patience each 102, Jahannam and Paradise each 77, light and darkness each 24, and says this is among the signs that the Qur'an is in pairs. (needs_source: the counts of word occurrences in the Qur'an are all figures the sheikh gives without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:14:00}
- He gives the parable of a father walking with his small son in the dark: the child forgets his father is beside him, and when the light comes he is sure he is holding his father's hand. In the same way the servant in the darkness of disobedience does not see that his Lord is near, but when the moon of faith rises he sees that his Lord is nearer than the jugular vein. He says Iblis lives until the day they are raised (see [[passage/quran-15-36|al-Hijr, verse 36]] to [[passage/quran-15-38|verse 38]]) and that the jinn used to listen for news of the heaven until the Prophet was sent and it was filled with shooting stars. He promises the next episode will speak of the valleys of the Fire, including the valley of zamharir, closes with supplication and promises a meeting next Monday. (needs_source: the description of the valley of zamharir and similar is promised by the sheikh without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep27 01:18:30}

## Episode 28: those promised the Fire, part two: the peoples and groups given tidings of the Fire, from the people of Noah to the people of the Trench, Thamud, Aad, the people of Lot, the false claimants to prophethood, Abu Lahab and his wife, Cain, Pharaoh, Qarun and the Samiri
- The episode opens with recitation of verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and on the peoples who denied before (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]], [[passage/quran-38-12|Sad, verse 12]] and [[passage/quran-38-13|verse 13]]). The host says this is the second part of "those promised the Fire", after Iblis, and that the first part drew a warm response from viewers. The sheikh stresses that Satan works against people twenty-four hours a day and does not leave them in obedience or in disobedience, so the believer must seek refuge with God. He then says that those promised the Fire are of two kinds, groups and individuals, named in the Qur'an and in agreed hadiths. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:05:00}
- Asked to choose, the host starts with groups and recalls that in an earlier meeting the sheikh said he belongs to no group and follows the Book and the Sunna. The sheikh says all Muslims are the jama'a of the Muslims (see [[concept/luzum-al-jamaa|keeping to the community]]), everyone who says there is no god but God and that Muhammad is His messenger, and distinguishes the community from the sect (firqa). He cites the hadith that the Jews split into seventy-one and the Christians into seventy-two branches and the Muslim community will split into seventy-three, all in the Fire but one, namely those on what the Prophet and his Companions were on (see [[concept/al-iftiraq-al-ummah|the splitting of the community]]), and says a misguided sect is one that brings a new version of the religion. (needs_source: the hadith of the splitting of the community is related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:07:30}
- The sheikh says the groups active today differ in the means of calling to God and that each, in his view, has its merits and its faults: one stresses conduct and the training of the individual and enters representative councils, another goes back to the roots and holds to the plain text. He says there is no official spokesman for the faith other than the Prophet, that no individual or group may say "I am the community of the Muslims", and that each group is a tree in the great garden of Islam, a page of the book and not all of it. He estimates that more than ninety or ninety-five percent is agreed on, and holds that a group is a human product; he thanks God that he has coloured himself by none, so that leaders of groups take his advice with an open heart. This is his own view. (needs_source: the estimate that ninety to ninety-five percent is agreed on is given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:13:00}
- He says the schools of law are about jurisprudence, differences of understanding and of which texts reached whom, and that today one can reconcile them, and that a jurist trained in one school only should not preside over a great fatwa. He relates that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) said it was a mercy that the Companions did not agree, since had they all followed Ibn Umar's way or Ibn Abbas's way it would have been harder, and that al-Shafi'i (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]) changed some of his positions when he moved from Iraq to Egypt, as a fatwa changes with time, place, circumstance and person. He says he loves all the groups and forgives those who wrong him, and loves one who advises him more than one who flatters him. (needs_source: the saying of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz about the Companions and the change of al-Shafi'i's school in Egypt are related without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:15:30}
- The first people given tidings of the Fire are the people of Noah (see [[person/nuh|Noah]]). The sheikh recalls that Noah stayed among them a thousand years less fifty (see [[passage/quran-29-14|al-Ankabut, verse 14]]) and that few believed, and says the books of biography and exegesis put those who believed at about seventy men and women. He notes the oddity of building a ship in the mountains or the desert far from the sea, and that every elder who passed mocked him, which he calls a law of truth and falsehood, citing the verses of those who laughed at the believers (see [[passage/quran-83-29|al-Mutaffifin, verse 29]]); they called him mad and rebuffed him (see [[passage/quran-54-9|al-Qamar, verse 9]]). He says Noah is the eighth grandson of Adam (see [[person/adam|Adam]]), seven fathers between them. (needs_source: the count of about seventy believers and Noah's descent as the eighth grandson of Adam are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:19:00}
- He likens humanity to a sick person to whom God keeps sending a physician, which is why the Children of Israel received so many prophets, with many ills at once. He speaks of three kinds of heart, the sound, the sick and the dead, and says the sound heart is the only one fit to meet God on the day when neither wealth nor sons avail except one who comes with a sound heart (see [[passage/quran-26-88|al-Shu'ara, verse 88]], [[passage/quran-26-89|verse 89]] and [[concept/qalb-salim|the sound heart]]), free of malice, envy, pride, crookedness and despair of God's mercy, and adds that worship is not accepted from one who is unjust to people. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:22:30}
- On the Flood, he explains the sign that the oven gushed (see [[passage/quran-11-40|Hud, verse 40]]) as water coming out of the heated oven. After the long years, Noah called "I am overcome, so help me" (see [[passage/quran-54-10|al-Qamar, verse 10]]), and the sheikh urges every wronged person to pray so sincerely. He remarks that the verse speaks of the gates of the sky being opened and the earth made to burst with springs (see [[passage/quran-54-11|verse 11]] and [[passage/quran-54-12|verse 12]]) and that gates suggest a rush, and that Noah rode the ark of planks and nails (see [[passage/quran-54-13|verse 13]]) which he built alone, as he says no biography names a helper. He recalls the hadith that on the Day a prophet will come with a small band, one with a man or two, and one with no one, and says from his own particular view that Noah, not Ayyub, is the prophet of patience (see [[concept/sabr|patience]]). (needs_source: the hadith of the prophets who come with few or no followers and the claim that no biography names a helper of Noah are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:27:30}
- Noah's wife and son are also among those given tidings of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-66-10|al-Tahrim, verse 10]]). The sheikh reads the wife's treachery as taking the secrets of the house outside it and says this is common in homes today, citing a hadith that among the greatest betrayals is a man confiding in his wife and she spreading it, or the reverse. Of the son, he says Noah was tried through his people, his wife and his son, and that Noah called him to the ark but he said he would take refuge on a mountain (see [[passage/quran-11-42|Hud, verse 42]] and [[passage/quran-11-43|verse 43]]); the waves came between them, and the sheikh sees mercy in Noah not watching his son drown. He relates a saying that whoever measures by reason alone goes astray. (needs_source: the hadith of the greatest betrayals and the saying that whoever measures by reason goes astray are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:31:00}
- He turns to Noah's plea for his son and God's answer that he was not of his family (see [[passage/quran-11-45|Hud, verse 45]], [[passage/quran-11-46|verse 46]]), and reads it as meaning that family is by righteous deed, and that kinship of religion is stronger than kinship of blood; he adds that Noah asked out of a father's love and was corrected with the highest courtesy of prophethood. He relates that Abu Lahab (see [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]]) is said to have his punishment lightened every Monday because he freed a slave girl who brought him news of the Prophet's birth, and that nothing done is lost with God. (needs_source: the report that Abu Lahab's punishment is lightened on Mondays is related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:35:00}
- Asked whether Noah was sent to all people, the sheikh answers that every messenger was sent to his own people except Muhammad, and that God does not punish until a messenger is sent (see [[passage/quran-17-15|al-Isra, verse 15]]). He explains the Flood as the fate of a humanity that drowned itself in sin before it drowned in water. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:38:00}
- The second example is the people of the Trench (see [[concept/ashab-al-ukhdud|the people of the Trench]]). The sheikh tells of the king who was worshipped, his sorcerer who grew old, and the boy sent to learn from him; on the way the boy sat with a monk and was told to say he was held up by his family or by the sorcerer. Brought before the king, the boy said that God is his Lord and the king's; boat and mountain failed to kill him, and each time he prayed "God, suffice me against them as You will". He told the king that he could only be killed by taking an arrow and saying "in the name of the Lord of this boy" before all the people, and when the king did so the people believed. The king then dug trenches and lit fires, and a woman with an infant hesitated until the infant spoke to her, upon which she threw herself in (see [[passage/quran-85-4|al-Buruj, verse 4]] to [[passage/quran-85-8|verse 8]]). The sheikh draws the lesson that the caller must be patient (see [[passage/quran-31-17|Luqman, verse 17]]) and that dying on the truth is the best death. (needs_source: the story of the boy, the monk and the king is related without naming the hadith book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:45:30}
- He mentions the people of Saba, who reached a civilisation no one had reached but did not give thanks (see [[passage/quran-34-15|Saba, verse 15]] and [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]), and says he sees ingratitude to God, to parents and to those who do good in children today and holds that it removes blessing. He then tells of the people of Thamud, whose prophet Salih (see [[person/salih|Salih]]) brought a she-camel as sign, with a turn of water for her and a turn for them (see [[passage/quran-54-28|al-Qamar, verse 28]]), and the only order was not to harm her; there were nine men in the city (see [[passage/quran-27-48|al-Naml, verse 48]]) and one among them hamstrung her (see [[passage/quran-54-29|al-Qamar, verse 29]]). God razed them for their sin (see [[passage/quran-91-14|al-Shams, verse 14]] and [[group/thamud|Thamud]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:52:00}
- From Thamud he draws that the whole people was punished because the rest did not take the hand of the few who did the deed; he gives the parable of the people on a ship, those at the upper and lower decks, and says he does not remain silent while corruption spreads, as that removes blessing and ruins homes. He repeats the hadith that one rider is a devil, two riders two devils, and three are a company, and says Islam loves the community when it is on the truth. (needs_source: the parable of the ship and the hadith of the one, two and three riders are related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:52:30}
- Of Aad, he says some exegetes read the verse on those who build great works hoping to live forever as meaning they hoped factories would prolong life (see [[passage/quran-26-129|al-Shu'ara, verse 129]] and [[passage/quran-26-130|verse 130]]), and that the barren wind left nothing standing (see [[passage/quran-51-41|al-Dhariyat, verse 41]] and [[group/aad|Aad]]). Of the people of Lot (see [[person/lut|Lot]] and [[concept/qawm-lut|the people of Lot]]), he says the stones were marked (see [[passage/quran-11-82|Hud, verse 82]]) because they broke the natural disposition, and that when Lot said "these are my daughters" (see [[passage/quran-11-78|Hud, verse 78]]) he spoke as a father of his people, which is his reading. He rejects the objection that such punishments are harsh, saying God punishes only after sending a warner, and notes that the series gives headlines and not details, "alarm bells". (needs_source: the exegetical reading about factories and the reading of "my daughters" are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 00:59:00}
- Among the groups he lists the false claimants to prophethood. Musaylima, he says, wrote to the Prophet asking to share the earth with Quraysh and was answered from Muhammad to Musaylima the liar. He says the people who refused zakat after the Prophet had taken that the order "take from their wealth" (see [[passage/quran-9-103|al-Tawba, verse 103]]) was meant only for him, and Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]) treated them as apostates (see [[event/ridda-wars|the Ridda wars]] and [[concept/zakat|zakat]]). The sheikh holds that whoever accepts part of the Qur'an and rejects part, or denies a verse, has left the religion, and that saying there is no verse on the veil (see [[passage/quran-24-31|al-Nur, verse 31]] and [[concept/hijab|the hijab]]) denies something known from religion by necessity, which he distinguishes from a woman who admits she falls short and asks for guidance; this is his view and contested among thinkers. (needs_source: the letters between Musaylima and the Prophet are related without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:05:00}
- He tells of Sajah, a woman claimant who joined Musaylima, was offered marriage by him and came out saying he had given her as dowry the removal of the dawn and night prayers from his followers; a man of his tribe said he knew he was a liar but that a liar from Rabi'a was better than a truthful man from Mudar (see [[person/musaylima|Musaylima]] and [[person/sajah|Sajah]]). He compares this with the stance of Abu Jahl (see [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]) in the rivalry of Banu Hashim and others over giving water and carrying the banner, who said that since a prophet had come from them they would never believe. Khalid ibn al-Walid (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]) fought Musaylima at al-Yamama (see [[event/battle-of-yamama|the battle of al-Yamama]]), where the sheikh says seventy memorisers of the Qur'an fell, and Wahshi (see [[person/wahshi-ibn-harb|Wahshi]]) killed Musaylima with the spear that had killed Hamza. He credits Abu Bakr with restoring the Arabian peninsula and with compiling the written copy of the Qur'an, and names al-Aswad (see [[person/al-aswad-al-ansi|al-Aswad al-Ansi]]) among the claimants. (needs_source: the words of the man of Rabi'a, the dowry story and the number of memorisers killed are related without a chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:24:00}
- Among individuals, the sheikh names Abu Lahab and his wife. He says Abu Lahab's name was Abd al-Uzza ibn Abd al-Muttalib and that he was called Abu Lahab for his ruddy, fiery face, that his wife Umm Jamil (see [[person/umm-jamil|Umm Jamil]]) was Arwa bint Harb, sister of Abu Sufyan (see [[person/abu-sufyan-ibn-harb|Abu Sufyan]]), and not Arwa bint Abd al-Muttalib, aunt of the Prophet. When the message came, he says Abu Lahab ordered his two sons to divorce Ruqayya and Umm Kulthum (see [[person/ruqayya-bint-muhammad|Ruqayya]] and [[person/umm-kulthum-bint-muhammad|Umm Kulthum]]); one of them, whom he calls Utba or Utayba, spat toward the Prophet, who prayed that a beast be set on him, and he was killed by a lion on a journey to Sham, after which Abu Lahab told his companions that Muhammad's prayer is answered. He adds that Abu Lahab alone of Banu Abd al-Muttalib joined the boycott (see [[event/boycott-of-banu-hashim|the boycott of Banu Hashim]]). (needs_source: the identity of the sons, the lion story and Abu Lahab's role in the boycott are related without a chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:13:30}
- He cites the surah of Abu Lahab (see [[passage/quran-111-1|al-Masad, verse 1]]) and advises giving children good names, since each has a share of his name. Of Umm Jamil he says she hated the Prophet and once came with a large stone near the Kaaba while he sat by Abu Bakr and said "where is your companion?", and did not see him, which he links to the verse about those who look at you and do not see (see [[passage/quran-7-198|al-A'raf, verse 198]]). He says the case of Abu Lahab and his wife shows that rebelling against God and openly opposing the truth bring punishment, and that the wives of Noah and Lot were alike in betraying secrets and in disbelief. He also warns that whoever lies about the Prophet will take his seat in the Fire. (needs_source: the story of Umm Jamil and the stone is related without a chain or source, and the warning is a hadith related without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:16:30}
- Of Cain (see [[person/qabil|Cain]] and [[person/habil|Abel]]), the sheikh says he was the first to establish killing, and so bears a share of the sin of every killer; he buried his brother after seeing the crow and was left with a regret that did not help (see [[passage/quran-5-31|al-Ma'ida, verse 31]] and [[passage/quran-5-32|verse 32]]). He says the cause was malice and envy (see [[concept/hasad|envy]]) at what God had apportioned (see [[passage/quran-4-32|al-Nisa, verse 32]]), and recites verse attributed to al-Shafi'i that he who envies him has offended God in His work. (needs_source: the verse attributed to al-Shafi'i is related without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:20:00}
- Of Pharaoh (see [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]), he says he slaughtered the sons of the Children of Israel and used their labour unpaid, and had a tomb built for twenty years; the sheikh reads his request for a tower to reach the God of Moses (see [[passage/quran-28-38|al-Qasas, verse 38]]) as tyranny, and holds that his declaration of belief when drowning was not accepted (see [[passage/quran-10-90|Yunus, verse 90]] and [[passage/quran-10-91|verse 91]]). He says Pharaoh, Haman and their soldiers were wrongdoers together (see [[passage/quran-28-8|al-Qasas, verse 8]]), and that one who carries out a tyrant's orders bears his own sin while the sin is also placed in the scale of the one who ordered it; this is his view. (needs_source: the twenty years of building the tomb are given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:21:00}
- Of Qarun (see [[person/qarun|Qarun]]), he recalls his claim that he was given his wealth because of knowledge he had (see [[passage/quran-28-78|al-Qasas, verse 78]]) and the earth swallowing him (see [[passage/quran-28-81|verse 81]]), saying he withheld zakat and, in a tradition he repeats, was an alchemist turning base metals into precious ones. He applies "Qarunism" to those who treat wealth as their own achievement and hoard it, and "Pharaohism" to tyranny. He relates that Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) wept on seeing the mat's marks on the Prophet's side, and the Prophet said that Kisra and Qaysar lived in luxury while his was prophethood, not kingship. (needs_source: Qarun's alchemy and the report of Umar and the mat are related without a chain or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:23:30}
- He says Amr ibn Hisham was given the name Abu Jahl after the mission, around the plot to confine, banish or kill the Prophet (see [[passage/quran-8-30|al-Anfal, verse 30]]), and notes Amr ibn Luhayy (see [[person/amr-ibn-luhayy|Amr ibn Luhayy]]), first to bring idol worship to Arabia, and the Samiri (see [[person/al-samiri-ibn-sinina|al-Samiri]]) who made the calf from the Egyptians' gold and threw in a handful from the Messenger's track (see [[passage/quran-20-96|Ta Ha, verse 96]]), saying he died of a skin disease. The host thanks the sheikh and says the next meeting is on Friday. (needs_source: the name given to Abu Jahl, the Samiri's death and the plot are related without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep28 01:26:30}

## Episode 29: fear of the Fire and the ways of guarding against it: manliness (muru'a) and what breaches it, performing the obligations and the voluntary acts, fear of God and of those owed rights, five kinds of people of the Fire, and a project for the revival of the community
- The episode opens with recitation of verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and on the Fire whose enclosure surrounds its people (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]] and [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]]). The host recalls that earlier episodes covered the levels of the Fire, its kinds and the food and drink of its people, and asks why we do not fear even the mention of the Fire. The sheikh says a heart's fear of the Fire is necessary and has nothing to do with the number of one's sins, since when the Fire was mentioned the Prophet's heart and chest shook like a boiling pot. He relates that Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) had two lines under his eyes from weeping in fear of God, that al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) would faint when the verse of al-Kahf that opened the episode was recited before him, and that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) felt the blaze of Jahannam on his face and stood in prayer all night. He argues that everyone must pass over the Sirat above Jahannam (see [[passage/quran-19-71|Maryam, verse 71]] and [[concept/sirat|the Sirat]]) and that no one has been told he is saved, so fear is needed (see [[concept/khawf|fear]]). (needs_source: the report of the two lines under Umar's eyes, al-Hasan al-Basri fainting and Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz's night vigils is told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:04:00}
- Asked why this fear does not turn into deeds that save, he answers that a sick heart does not receive speech as a sound heart does, and that the Companions' hearts were clear, each of them like a walking copy of the Mushaf. He says hearts exist for remembrance of God, fear of Him, hope, love, sincerity, certainty, patience and trust, but people occupied them with the world and they filled with envy, rancour, pride, showing off and despair (see [[concept/riya|showing off]] and [[concept/qalb-salim|the sound heart]]), like a machine that runs on fuel and has been filled with water. He holds that whoever connects with his Creator is spared his worries, and that weeping at a sermon does not last unless it becomes action. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:06:30}
- The sheikh relates that God created Paradise and told Gabriel to look at it; Gabriel said no one who hears of it will fail to enter, so God surrounded it with disliked things; then Gabriel saw the Fire and said no one would enter it, so God surrounded it with desires. He lists the disliked things: the dawn prayer, restraining the tongue, lowering the gaze, leaving places of sin, patience with harm, giving to one who deprived you, joining one who cut you off and pardoning one who wronged you, and says that whatever goes against the self's whim brings one closer to God and whatever agrees with it takes one away (see [[concept/hawa|whim]]). He ties this to the verse that God tests people to know the truthful and the liars (see [[passage/quran-29-2|al-Ankabut, verse 2]] and [[passage/quran-29-3|verse 3]]). He adds that the believer moves among lights, in his speech, deeds, entering, leaving, grave and Sirat, and the disbeliever among darknesses to match. (needs_source: the hadith of Paradise surrounded by disliked things and the Fire by desires, and the counting of the lights and darknesses, are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:09:30}
- The sheikh says that if Muslims feared the Fire as they fear poverty they would all be saved, and argues that truth is never absent from any age. He tells that Khalid ibn Abdallah al-Qasri (see [[person/khalid-ibn-abdallah-al-qasri|Khalid al-Qasri]]) preached on Eid al-Adha and said he would sacrifice al-Ja'd ibn Dirham (see [[person/jad-ibn-dirham|al-Ja'd ibn Dirham]]), who had said that God did not take Abraham as a friend or speak to Moses, then came down and killed him. The sheikh calls al-Ja'd a criminal, which is his own judgement of the man; the report circulates in the books and its details and its assessment are disputed. (needs_source: the story of Khalid al-Qasri's sermon and the killing of al-Ja'd is told without naming a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:12:00}
- The sheikh tells that Nur al-Din Zangi (see [[person/nur-al-din-mahmud-ibn-zangi|Nur al-Din Zangi]]) saw the Prophet in a dream telling him to save him from two men, so he consulted advisers and travelled to Medina (see [[place/medina|Medina]]) and distributed charity; asked whether anyone had been missed, he was told of two men from the Maghrib living beside the mosque, the two he had seen, and in their house were books and money and, under a mat, a tunnel running toward the noble body, and they were punished. He says some people are rooted in evil, while everyone's basis is the natural disposition and monotheism, which they have buried (see [[concept/fitra|the fitra]]). He adds that a small sin makes a black spot on the heart, and when sins repeat the heart blackens and is overcome by rust (see [[passage/quran-83-14|al-Mutaffifin, verse 14]]), so matters become confused, and when God alone is mentioned they recoil and when others are mentioned they rejoice (see [[passage/quran-39-45|al-Zumar, verse 45]]). (needs_source: the story of Nur al-Din Zangi, the two men and the tunnel is told without chain or book, and the hadith of the black spot is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:16:30}
- He moves on to say the greater disaster than weak fear is the loss of muru'a, which the forebears had, and defines it as generosity of spirit, chivalry, standing beside the weak, bearing burdens and trust in what is with God. He says a righteous man who wanted to lower his brother's standing would ask his need from someone else, and relates that al-Shafii (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]]) was so glad that he freed his maidservant when she told him that his friend had taken something from his purse and left, because his brother had acted without formality. He mentions that al-Ghazali (see [[person/al-ghazali-abu-hamid|al-Ghazali]]) set eleven conditions for true brotherhood. (needs_source: the report of al-Shafii freeing his maidservant and the number of al-Ghazali's conditions are told without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:19:00}
- The sheikh lists what he calls breaches of muru'a: taking pay for teaching the Qur'an without need, following whim in a gathering against what the truth says, delaying prayer, sampling perfumes in shops without intending to buy, asking a guest to act against his profession, such as asking a doctor guest to examine a child, browsing magazines in shops, and eating in streets and markets, noting that some jurists, the Hanafis in one view, rejected the testimony of someone who eats in the road and laughs loudly. These are his views, and some are matters on which jurists differ; they stand in disagreement, not consensus. (needs_source: counting these as breaches of muru'a and attributing the rejection of testimony to some jurists and the Hanafis are told without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:25:00}
- He adds a man kissing his wife in front of people, attending feasts repeatedly uninvited, belittling one's wife in public and calling her foolish, keeping bad company, and exposing arms and shoulders before others. He tells a joke about an elderly sheikh who asked every visitor about his family, to show that people are careless with modesty. He says that speaking about breaches of muru'a and returning to muru'a is necessary, and that he addresses it to Muslims. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:30:00}
- On the hijab the sheikh says the head of a household is responsible for his family, so if his daughter does not wear it he is questioned, and a mother who called from Abu Dhabi said she had failed to persuade her daughter. He holds that the hijab is an obligation (see [[concept/hijab|the hijab]]), that university study is no excuse for leaving it, and that firmness is needed from the start, before the girl grows up and becomes independent, and that one who leaves it for years and then wants firmness has fallen short. This is his view, and contemporary thinkers differ on how to apply it. (needs_source: the report of the mother's call from Abu Dhabi is not identified) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:31:30}
- He remarks that people dislike hearing about the Fire and prefer hope, and says God mentions the Fire in the Qur'an as often as Paradise, seventy-seven times each as he puts it. He says God could have guided everyone, but people led themselves astray and followed Iblis, and that God forbade nothing without opening a lawful alternative. He thinks it is easy to build a veil between oneself and the Fire if one strives with oneself. (needs_source: the count of seventy-seven mentions each of Paradise and the Fire in the Qur'an is given without a tally or source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:34:00}
- The first step in that veil, he says, is establishing the obligations, those of worship and those of dealings between people. He cites a hadith that God set limits, so do not transgress them, imposed obligations, so do not neglect them, and kept silent about things out of mercy, not forgetfulness, so do not ask about them. He says one who does not worship well will not deal well, that a prayer that does not restrain its performer from indecency and wrongdoing (see [[passage/quran-29-45|al-Ankabut, verse 45]]) and does not make him humble, truthful and trustworthy is of little benefit, and that God's pleasure is gained by bringing joy to a neighbour, a friend or a brother. (needs_source: the hadith of God's limits, obligations and silence about things is told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:37:00}
- Then the voluntary acts: he relates a sacred hadith that the servant keeps drawing near to God by voluntary acts until He loves him, and when He loves him He becomes his hearing and his sight (see [[concept/hadith-qudsi|the sacred hadith]] and [[concept/nawafil|voluntary acts]]). He gives as examples two rak'as in the depth of night, a portion of the Qur'an, seeking forgiveness, keeping family ties, visiting the sick, ongoing charity and sponsoring an orphan, and says such a person hears only good, looks only on good, eats only what is wholesome and hurries to God's pleasure. (needs_source: the sacred hadith on drawing near by voluntary acts is told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:39:00}
- On fear he tells of the mother of Moses (see [[person/umm-musa|the mother of Moses]] and [[passage/quran-28-7|al-Qasas, verse 7]]), who first nursed him in fear and later nursed him at Pharaoh's order in safety, and concludes that one who fears God is made safe. He says Ibn Umar (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Ibn Umar]]) kept sugar in his pocket and gave it to children, acting on the verse about spending from what one loves (see [[passage/quran-3-92|Al Imran, verse 92]]). He then says one tear shed in fear of God puts out seas of Jahannam, that the first veil is fear of God, then fear of a bad end, then fear of those owed rights, and warns that some people backbite and wrong others without knowing it. (needs_source: the report of sugar in Ibn Umar's pocket and the hadith of the tear that extinguishes seas of Jahannam are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:43:00}
- He tells the story of al-Fudayl ibn Iyad (see [[person/al-fudayl-ibn-iyad|al-Fudayl ibn Iyad]]), who was a highway robber and climbed into a house, heard a reciter read the verse on the time having come for hearts to be humbled to the remembrance of God (see [[passage/quran-57-16|al-Hadid, verse 16]]) and said it was time, O Lord. He then tells of a notable of his people at a drinking party, where a visitor asked the maidservant whether the owner was free or a slave; she said free, and he said no, he was a slave, left the gathering barefoot and went to learn. He says a person may have the door of deeds opened and the door of acceptance closed, and a sin may come to him and become the reason for his repentance (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). (needs_source: the story of al-Fudayl and the story of the man who left the gathering barefoot are told without a source and are mixed together in the recording) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:44:30}
- Asked whether disbelief and injustice are rooted in a human being from creation, he answers that every child is born on the pure natural disposition, the disposition of monotheism, and that people make themselves disbelieve, associate partners and go astray through environment, whim, Iblis and the commanding self. This is his view, and a hadith with this sense is reported. (needs_source: the hadith that every child is born on the fitra is cited without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:45:30}
- Describing the punishment, he takes the verses that they cry for relief and are given water like molten metal that scalds faces (see [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]]), explaining it as boiling pus, that they are given scalding water that cuts their bowels (see [[passage/quran-47-15|Muhammad, verse 15]]), that those who eat the property of orphans eat fire in their bellies (see [[passage/quran-4-10|al-Nisa, verse 10]]), and that they have canopies of fire and garments cut from fire (see [[passage/quran-39-16|al-Zumar, verse 16]] and [[passage/quran-22-19|al-Hajj, verse 19]]). He says Malik answers them after a thousand years (see [[passage/quran-43-77|al-Zukhruf, verse 77]] and [[concept/malik-keeper-of-jahannam|Malik, keeper of Jahannam]]) and that they beg the people of Paradise for water (see [[passage/quran-7-50|al-A'raf, verse 50]]). He describes those who deserve this as people who humiliated and starved others and took orphans' money. (needs_source: Malik's reply after a thousand years and the description of the burning vessels are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:48:00}
- He says God created the human being on monotheism and sent prophets and a clear book, but the person shut the door on himself and did not follow one whose heart God made heedless of His remembrance (see [[passage/quran-18-28|al-Kahf, verse 28]]), and that the Messenger went to his people as Noah did (see [[person/nuh|Noah]]), and they put their fingers in their ears (see [[passage/quran-71-7|Nuh, verse 7]]). He explains the verse about one who takes his whim as his god (see [[passage/quran-45-23|al-Jathiya, verse 23]]) to mean that his whim leads him to drink, take bribes and look at what is forbidden, and adds the verse about those whose effort is lost while they think they do good (see [[passage/quran-18-104|al-Kahf, verse 104]]) and the verses about the astonishment of the people of Shu'ayb (see [[person/shuayb|Shu'ayb]] and [[passage/quran-7-88|al-A'raf, verse 88]]) and of the polytheists that gods were made one God (see [[passage/quran-38-5|Sad, verse 5]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:51:30}
- He places individual responsibility on the community, and says the callers ask no wage (see [[passage/quran-26-109|al-Shu'ara, verse 109]]) and that Noah called his people in secret and openly until he had given them every excuse (see [[passage/quran-71-9|Nuh, verse 9]]), then prayed against them (see [[passage/quran-71-26|verse 26]]) after he was told that none of his people would believe except those who already had (see [[passage/quran-11-36|Hud, verse 36]]), and says it was they who closed the door. He relates that al-Hasan al-Basri was asked how long he would tire himself and said he sought His rest by it. This is his reading. (needs_source: the saying attributed to al-Hasan al-Basri about rest is told without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:53:30}
- He recalls that the Fire has seven levels and that "al-Nar" is the name for all of it, whose names include Jahannam, Saqar, al-Hutama, Laza, al-Hawiya and al-Sa'ir (see [[concept/jahannam|Jahannam]] and [[concept/fire|the Fire]]). Paradise has seven gates as well (see [[passage/quran-15-44|al-Hijr, verse 44]]), each with an allotted share, and the Fire has a gate for one who raised a weapon against the community of Muhammad; he says it covers whoever led people by swords and bombs, whatever their religion, even a Muslim. He cites a hadith he relates of a Companion who slept under a tree and whose brothers took his sword in jest, so he woke terrified, and the Prophet said not to frighten believers and that whoever frightens a believer, God will frighten him on the Day of Resurrection, and he warns against surprising one's wife at home to see what she is doing. (needs_source: the hadith of the Companion whose sword was taken and the hadith on frightening believers are told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:55:00}
- He then turns to the camera and cites a hadith that the people of the Fire are five, and says they are: the weak one with no restraint, who is a follower seeking neither family nor property, which he explains as the "imma'a" (one with no mind of his own); the treacherous one whose greed is never hidden, even for little, including taking a pen or a sheet of paper from the workplace; the miser; the liar; and the fifth, one foul in speech. He gives the example of Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]), who drank milk, doubted its source and vomited it, saying he sought refuge in God from what had mingled with the flesh and veins, because his stomach was used to nothing but the lawful. He cites the Prophet's saying not to be an imma'a, saying "if people do good we do good and if they do wrong we do wrong," but to resolve oneself. (needs_source: the hadith of the five people of the Fire, the report of Abu Bakr's milk and the hadith of the imma'a are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:58:30}
- He tells that a boy of twelve entered on Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz with a delegation and said that when God grants a servant a retaining heart and an eloquent tongue He has chosen good for him, and advised him to treat the eldest Muslim as a father, the middle one as a brother and the youngest as a son, and to distribute surplus wealth to God's servants, whereupon Umar lowered his head. The sheikh draws the contrast with the imma'a. (needs_source: the story of the boy and Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz is told without chain or name) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:00:00}
- He describes the miser as far from God, from people and from Paradise and near to the Fire, relating that it is written on the gate of Paradise that no miser enters, that miserliness is a tree whose root is in the Fire, and that the worst of people with God are those whom people shun out of fear of their evil. He then cites the woe to worshippers who are heedless of their prayer, those who show off (see [[passage/quran-107-4|al-Ma'un, verse 4]] to [[passage/quran-107-6|verse 6]], [[concept/al-bukhl|miserliness]] and [[concept/riya|showing off]]), and the description of the hypocrites who stand to prayer lazily, showing off to people (see [[passage/quran-4-142|al-Nisa, verse 142]] and [[concept/nifaq|hypocrisy]]). (needs_source: the hadith of what is written on the gate of Paradise, the tree of miserliness and the worst of people are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:02:30}
- On lying he says it is a calamity: a Muslim may be a miser but should not be a liar, and lying is not allowed except to reconcile quarrellers. He tells of a man who hid under a pile of fibre from people seeking him, and in the sheikh's view truth saved the one who did not give him away; he says truthfulness saves (see [[concept/al-sidq-truthfulness|truthfulness]]). He mentions that Abu Bakr was asked about the Prophet during the hijra and answered that he was a man who guides me on the road, saying Abu Bakr was speaking about his own intention, and that the Prophet, asked who he was, said he was from water. (needs_source: the story of the man hidden under the fibre, the hadith permitting lying to reconcile, and the exchange on Abu Bakr's reply and the Prophet's answer "from water" are told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:05:00}
- He says a heart full of wisdom sends out words like a ladle taking from a pot, and relates that Ali (see [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]) said to someone who praised him, "I am above what you say and below what you think," and that al-Miqdad (see [[person/al-miqdad-ibn-amr|al-Miqdad ibn Amr]]) spoke a true word to the Prophet before a battle from a heart full of faith. (needs_source: the saying of Ali and the words of al-Miqdad are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:06:30}
- Assessing the community, the sheikh thinks it has begun to awaken, citing the return of young people to the mosques, women taking the hijab and the memorising of the Qur'an, and says the hard blows it takes from its opponents are a sign that the body is still alive. He adds that the community needs an organised Islam to meet organised disbelief, and a clarifying of priorities and avoiding of dispute. This is his view, and the question is a matter of judgement and disagreement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:08:30}
- The sheikh says that fifteen years earlier he consulted senior scholars about issuing a book on the fiqh of the first century before Abu Hanifa, without drawing people into the maze of the schools, since Muslims lived that century at the height of Islam's greatness (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]), and asks viewers to send suggestions on the causes of the community's stumbling and the ways to rise, to be put to scholars and then to those who bind and loose, so that this generation takes one step and leaves the next to later generations. (needs_source: the date of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz's death and his age in the episode are stated without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:11:30}
- The host closes by saying the next episode will move to the verses on the levels of the Fire, its people, its gates, its drink and its food. The sheikh says he still shrinks from speaking about the Fire, and that his fear grows as he ages from what he has learned from the Book and the Sunna and from the loss of his elders, and that it will stay with him until he meets God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep29 01:13:00}

## Episode 30: the description of the Fire in the verses of the Qur'an, part one: from al-Baqara to al-Ra'd, and the people of the Fire among followers, leaders, wrongdoers and the people of al-A'raf
- The episode opens with recitation of verses on the opening of Gog and Magog and on God's creation. The host says the programme has come closer to describing the Fire, that the source of the description is the Book of God taken in order from the start of al-Baqara, and that the sheikh will choose some verses from each sura and explain them; the talk is about the Fire in general, not its levels, as the place for those who disobeyed God, from unforgiven major sins to associating partners with Him. The sheikh says the word "the Fire" occurs in the Qur'an 138 times, that talk of the Fire shakes the hearts of believers, and that whoever is not moved by it should look for a heart, which he calls the first lesson in speaking of it. (needs_source: the number of times the word Fire occurs in the Qur'an is given without reference to a count) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:04:00}
- He begins with al-Baqara and the claim of the Jews that the Fire would touch them only for a number of days, the days they worshipped the calf, which the Qur'an answers by saying they hold no covenant from God to that effect and that the people of disbelief abide in it (see [[passage/quran-2-80|al-Baqara, verse 80]] and [[concept/jahannam|Jahannam]]); he says a time limit on punishment has no place in Islam. He then says some Muslims wish wishes on God and think everyone who says there is no god but God will leave the Fire, and cites a hadith that some of them remain in the Fire for as long as the world has lasted since Adam; he describes anyone who sees a human law as better than the Sharia as setting himself above God and legislating from himself, which is his own judgement. He cites the hadith in which the Prophet likens himself to a man who lit a fire while moths and locusts fly into it and he holds them back by their waists. (needs_source: the hadith of some monotheists staying in the Fire for the length of the world and the hadith of the moths and locusts are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:07:30}
- He then passes to Abraham's prayer for Mecca, that its people be provided with fruits, and to God's words about the one who disbelieves, that He will let him enjoy a little and then drive him to the punishment of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-2-126|al-Baqara, verse 126]]). He says provision in this world goes to believer and disbeliever alike by the mercy of the Merciful, and that disbelief has degrees, from ingratitude for blessings and denial of them to the disbelief that takes one out of the faith (see [[concept/kufr|kufr]]), and gives the example of a son who denies the favour of the father who spent on him for years. He notes that every soul comes with a driver and a witness (see [[passage/quran-50-21|Qaf, verse 21]]), that thanks for a blessing means acting on it, spending from it and showing its trace on the person, and links this to all of life belonging to God (see [[passage/quran-6-162|al-An'am, verse 162]], [[passage/quran-6-163|verse 163]] and [[concept/shukr|gratitude]]). He cites the hadith "do not belittle any good deed, even meeting your brother with a cheerful face".
