(needs_source: the hadith on not belittling any good deed is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:12:30}
- On the verses where those who were followed disown those who followed them when they see the punishment (see [[passage/quran-2-166|al-Baqara, verse 166]] and [[passage/quran-2-167|verse 167]]), the sheikh says Satan tempts a person with a post, an administration or a governorship in return for giving up principles, and that the leader who is followed is also held to account because he misled others and was forbidden to be an imam of error, so he bears a double share for his sin and for his misleading. He says religion should be taken as medicine is, from a known doctor and not from the pavement, and that follower and leader each disown the other. He gives an exchange between a man and the commander of the faithful about piety, which the latter explains as walking through a thorny land, rolling up one's sleeves and striving. (needs_source: the exchange about piety and the thorny land is told without chain and without naming the questioner or the answerer) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:16:30}
- He takes up those who conceal what God sent down of the Book and eat only fire into their bellies (see [[passage/quran-2-174|al-Baqara, verse 174]]). In the sheikh's view concealment includes presenting some rulings of the religion and being shy of others, calling the cutting of a thief's hand savagery and the punishment of the drinker a limit on personal freedom, and taking one law from the religion and another from oneself; this is his view. He adds that the community does not agree on error, that the group in the juristic councils is guarded from the mistake an individual may make, and that whoever stands apart is like the stray sheep eaten by wolves. (needs_source: the hadiths that the community does not agree on error and of the stray sheep are told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:20:30}
- He mentions those who ask only for this world and those who ask for good in this world and the next and for protection from the punishment of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-2-200|al-Baqara, verse 200]] and [[passage/quran-2-201|verse 201]]). He says God is the owner of this world and the next, that what veils a person from God is imagining that someone shares the command with Him, that there is no god but God means no one controls the universe but Him, and that God ran causes through people's hands so one should not forget the Lord of causes. He then cites those who disbelieved, whose patrons are the idol, who bring them out of the light into the darkness (see [[passage/quran-2-257|al-Baqara, verse 257]] and [[concept/taghut|taghut]]), likens uprightness to a main road from which one turns into a narrow lane that leads to a narrower one, and joins this to the prohibition of following the other paths (see [[passage/quran-6-153|al-An'am, verse 153]]). (needs_source: the saying that what veils a person from God is imagining that someone is with Him is given without naming who said it) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:24:30}
- On the verse about those who consume usury, that they are the people of the Fire and abide in it (see [[passage/quran-2-275|al-Baqara, verse 275]] and [[concept/riba|riba]]), the sheikh reads the word abiding as meaning they will not leave it even by intercession; this is his reading, not that of every scholar. He then gives the example of banks: if fifteen trustworthy scholars said they were not usury and two said they were, doubt would enter a person's heart, and he says this is a place for precaution, like taking a meningitis vaccine for one's health, and that the question needs episode after episode, and cites the hadith to leave what causes doubt for what does not. This is a matter of reasoned judgement on which scholars differ. (needs_source: the hadith "leave what makes you doubt" and the saying that a little that suffices is better than a lot that distracts are told without chain, and the number of scholars is a hypothetical example) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:28:30}
- From Al Imran he cites that the wealth and children of those who disbelieved will not avail them against God at all and that they are the fuel of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-3-10|Al Imran, verse 10]]). He says the fuel is people and stones, that the keepers of the Fire are harsh and severe angels who do not disobey God in what He commands them and have no mercy in their hearts, and that God made them without hearts so that no pity would take them in His religion. (needs_source: the claim that God created the keepers of the Fire without hearts is told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:30:30}
- Then the verse on holding fast to the rope of God, which recalls that the believers stood on the brink of a pit of Fire and God saved them from it (see [[passage/quran-3-103|Al Imran, verse 103]]). He reads it as the fire of enmity in this world between tribes, the Aws and Khazraj, families and states, and carries it to people today who track the affairs of neighbours, their money and children, recalling the prohibition of pursuing what one has no knowledge of (see [[passage/quran-17-36|al-Isra, verse 36]]). He links security to faith (see [[passage/quran-6-82|al-An'am, verse 82]]) and says that when he reads about the Companions he feels as if he sits beside Abu Bakr as he weeps over the Qur'an, beside Umar as he fears, and Uthman with modesty marking his movements (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]] and [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman]]). (needs_source: the pictures of Abu Bakr weeping, Umar's fear and Uthman's modesty are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:33:30}
- He cites the believers' prayer that whoever is made to enter the Fire has been disgraced and the wrongdoers have no helpers (see [[passage/quran-3-192|Al Imran, verse 192]]), and says the believer's helper is God. He tells of an unlettered man who could neither read nor write who came to a scholar complaining of being wronged within his family, saying he had fasted Mondays and Thursdays for thirty years, stood in the last third of the night with what he knew and listened to the Qur'an all day, and that he did not want to go to court but asked for a prayer; the scholar reminded him that God defends those who believe (see [[passage/quran-22-38|al-Hajj, verse 38]]). (needs_source: the story of the unlettered man is told by the sheikh without naming him or giving a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:35:00}
- He says the wrongdoers whom the Fire threatens include those who associate partners with God, which is a great wrong (see [[passage/quran-31-13|Luqman, verse 13]] and [[concept/shirk|shirk]]), as well as showing off (see [[concept/riya|riya]]), infringing others' rights, putting a thing in the wrong place and spending an institution's money on promoting what is not permitted; a wrongdoer may be a Muslim, with a fire in measure of his wrong, and God has prepared for wrongdoers a fire whose enclosure surrounds them (see [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]] and [[concept/zulm|wrong]]). He relates that Abu Hazim went in to Muawiya (see [[person/muawiya-ibn-abi-sufyan|Muawiya ibn Abi Sufyan]]) and called him "the hired man"; those present objected, and Muawiya said to leave Abu Hazim, for he knew what he said, and Abu Hazim said God had hired him to govern them, being good to them, kind to their young and respectful to their old. (needs_source: the report of Abu Hazim and Muawiya is told without chain, and the identity of Abu Hazim and of the caliph needs checking) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:37:00}
- From al-Nisa, whoever disobeys God and His Messenger and transgresses His limits He will cause to enter a fire to abide in (see [[passage/quran-4-14|al-Nisa, verse 14]]). He explains disobedience as rebellion, leaving a command or doing what is forbidden, and God's limits as all His commands and prohibitions, not the penal limits alone, and cites the hadith that every king has a preserve and God's preserve is His prohibitions. He says the wrongdoer is trodden on by the people of the Fire, that the Fire has snakes like mules whose bite leaves its venom in the body for forty years, and relates that the Prophet heard a sound and said it was a stone thrown into Jahannam seventy years ago that is still falling and has now reached its bottom. He compares this to states that treat high treason as a grave crime, and asks what of one who betrays the Creator of the earth. (needs_source: the hadith of the kings' preserve, the snakes of the Fire like mules and the length of their venom, and the hadith of the stone falling for seventy years are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:40:30}
- He calls for a return to the Book of God and reflection on it, to read the Qur'an as if it were sent down to oneself alone, which he ascribes to Ibn Masud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Masud]]), and a return to the sources to apply Islam as the Companions did. Then al-Ma'ida, that whoever associates others with God, God has forbidden him Paradise and his refuge is the Fire and the wrongdoers have no helpers (see [[passage/quran-5-72|al-Ma'ida, verse 72]]). He mentions the wish of the disbelievers to be sent back and God's word that if they were sent back they would return to what they were forbidden (see [[passage/quran-6-27|al-An'am, verse 27]] and [[passage/quran-6-28|verse 28]]), gives the example of someone who vows on a Friday and then does not keep the vow, and advises people not to burden themselves with vows. (needs_source: the ascription of "read the Qur'an as if it were sent down to you" to Ibn Masud is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:45:00}
- From al-An'am, God's words on the day He gathers them all, "company of jinn, you have taken much of mankind", the reply of their allies, and then "the Fire is your abode, abiding in it" (see [[passage/quran-6-128|al-An'am, verse 128]] and [[concept/jinn|jinn]]). The sheikh says he believes what is in the Book of God about the jinn and not the tales and fancies, that protection lies in the morning and evening remembrances and seeking refuge on entering the toilet, and that the house in which al-Baqara is recited is one Satan flees; he advises anyone complaining of an affliction to turn to the Book of God and not to those who deal with the jinn, which is his view. (needs_source: the hadith that Satan flees a house in which al-Baqara is recited is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:47:30}
- He tells of a dream he ascribes to Abu Ayyub (see [[person/abu-ayyub-al-ansari|Abu Ayyub al-Ansari]]) in which he saw a man resting in Paradise; he asked the man's wife about his deeds, and she said he gathered orphans every Friday night, fed them by his own hand, wiped their heads and told them to pray for their uncle. He concludes that one should not think any good act small, and cites the hadith "do not belittle any good, even a sheep's trotter". (needs_source: the dream of Abu Ayyub and the hadith on a sheep's trotter are told without chain, and the details of the report are not clear from the delivery) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:49:30}
- From al-A'raf, "enter among nations that passed before you of jinn and mankind into the Fire; every time a nation enters it curses its sister, until when they have all caught up with each other the last say of the first, our Lord, these misled us, so give them a double punishment of the Fire" (see [[passage/quran-7-38|al-A'raf, verse 38]]). He says each nation curses the one before it, because the first is the one who planted the evil tree, and gives as examples of the imam of evil Cain, the first killer, Pharaoh, the unjust ruler, Qarun, the rich man, and Abu Jahl, the obstinate (see [[person/qabil|Cain]], [[person/qarun|Qarun]] and [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]). This is his explanation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:51:30}
- The sheikh prays that God make him sincere in what he says, so that he leaves this world having given something to his community, and then moves to the call of the people of Paradise to the people of the Fire, "we found what our Lord promised us true, did you find what your Lord promised you true? They said yes", after which a caller announces that God's curse is on the wrongdoers (see [[passage/quran-7-44|al-A'raf, verse 44]]); he says this call comes after both groups are settled in their abodes. He cites a hadith qudsi that God forbade wrong to Himself and made it forbidden among people, and urges a person to measure himself against the Book of God so that insight shows him his faults and he returns to the truth. (needs_source: the hadith qudsi on forbidding wrong is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:57:00}
- Then the call of the people of the Fire to the people of Paradise to pour on them some water or what God has provided, and the answer that God has forbidden both to the disbelievers (see [[passage/quran-7-50|al-A'raf, verse 50]]), and the people of al-A'raf turning their eyes toward the people of the Fire and saying "our Lord, do not place us with the wrongdoing people" (see [[passage/quran-7-47|al-A'raf, verse 47]]), men who have not entered Paradise though they hope (see [[passage/quran-7-46|verse 46]]). He gives two views of the people of al-A'raf: that they are witnesses from each nation, or that their good and bad deeds are equal and they await God's mercy and the Prophet's intercession (see [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]), a point on which exegetes differ. He says what lies between a servant and God He forgives except associating partners, whereas wronging people does not pass, and warns of the prayer of the wronged. (needs_source: the two views on the people of al-A'raf are given without ascription to a named exegete) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:00:00}
- From al-Tawba, "do they not know that whoever opposes God and His Messenger, for him is the fire of Jahannam, abiding in it, that is the great disgrace" (see [[passage/quran-9-63|al-Tawba, verse 63]]). He explains opposition as enmity, which he sees in those who say they have better laws and that humanity has matured and can run itself apart from revealed law, and holds that a Muslim has no right to take from the Sharia what suits him, like someone demanding a private school with no uniform and no fees. He presents the hijab as freedom (see [[concept/hijab|hijab]]), recalls that this world is not worth a mosquito's wing to God, and gives the example of one who endures five minutes of heat and then lives ten years in cool air-conditioning. This is his view, and the matter is one of reasoned judgement and disagreement. (needs_source: the hadith that this world is not worth a mosquito's wing to God is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:05:30}
- He says a woman puts on her hijab for those who see her and those who do not, and promises a coming episode on humility in prayer at the request of viewers, in which the one praying enters by saying the takbir into a "house of freedom", throws the world behind his back and turns to his Lord. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:05:30}
- Also from al-Tawba, "is he who founded his building on piety from God and His good pleasure better, or he who founded it on the brink of a crumbling bank so that it collapsed with him into the fire of Jahannam" (see [[passage/quran-9-109|al-Tawba, verse 109]] and [[concept/taqwa|taqwa]]). He invites attention to the precision of the wording, the building on piety being firm and the one on a crumbling bank falling, likens one who builds his world on pride and distraction to building on sand, and says the first takes the Book and the Sunna, endures and seeks reward, and stands at a frontier post of Islam. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:08:00}
- In Hud he cites "is he who is on a clear proof from his Lord" (see [[passage/quran-11-17|Hud, verse 17]]), then tells that the paternal aunt of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) came asking for her special allowance, which had been paid to her before him, and he said "in which copy of the Qur'an do you find that?"; she left saying to the Umayyads that they should blame only themselves, for they had married into the line of Umar ibn al-Khattab and it had produced this man. He tells that Umar took a tiny coin of the treasury from the hand of a young relative, and that he refrained from cold water for fear of being told "you wasted your good things in your worldly life" (see [[passage/quran-46-20|al-Ahqaf, verse 20]]), which the sheikh sees as schooling the soul. (needs_source: the reports of the aunt, the coin and the cold water are told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:11:00}
- He then says that this is the truth from God though most people do not believe, and relates that Umar ibn al-Khattab found a page of the Torah and the Prophet said "are you in doubt, son of al-Khattab? I brought it white and pure, and by the One who sent me with the truth, were my brother Moses alive he would have no choice but to follow me". He says comparative study of religions is for the firmly grounded scholars and not for one whose foot is not yet fixed. (needs_source: the hadith of the page of the Torah with Umar is told without chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:13:00}
- He cites God's words on the last day, that no soul will speak except by His leave and some will be wretched and some happy, and that the wretched will be in the Fire with sighing and sobbing (see [[passage/quran-11-105|Hud, verse 105]] and [[passage/quran-11-106|verse 106]]); he says sighing and sobbing is the motion of the people of the Fire for as long as they remain in it. Then "do not incline to those who do wrong lest the Fire touch you" (see [[passage/quran-11-113|Hud, verse 113]]), which he explains as leaning toward the wrongdoers even a little, objecting to those who would keep religion in the mosques and out of economics and art, and relates that if the people of the Fire found a fire of this world they would rest in it and sleep. (needs_source: the saying that the people of the Fire would rest in a fire of this world is told without chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:15:00}
- He then passes to al-Ra'd, "those have iron collars on their necks and those are the people of the Fire, abiding in it", about the deniers of resurrection (see [[passage/quran-13-5|al-Ra'd, verse 5]]), and says denial of resurrection is disbelief in God. The host closes by saying the next episode will continue the description of the Fire as it is in the verses and then as it is on the tongue of the Prophet, and that the description will come in several parts and episodes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep30 01:16:30}
## Episode 31: the description of the Fire in the verses of the Qur'an, part two: from al-Kahf to al-Ahqaf, the drink, food, garments, canopies and permanence of the Fire, and the tyrants, their followers and the weak
- The episode opens with recitation of verses on the opening of Gog and Magog, and the host says the programme continues the description of the Fire and that the idea of the Fire is still remote from the minds of many Muslims today. He relates an exchange in which Abu Hazim was asked why people dislike the hereafter and love the world, and answered that they built up their world and ruined their hereafter, and a person does not like to move from a built-up place to a ruin. He mentions a saying of al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) that his own self prefers shade to sun, so why should it not prefer Paradise to the Fire. (needs_source: the exchange about disliking the hereafter is told without a chain or a named questioner, and the saying of al-Hasan al-Basri is quoted without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:03:00}
- The host recalls that the previous episode began reviewing the verses on the Fire, and the sheikh says the verses he has gathered on the Fire with its levels and its dialogues number two hundred and seventy-one, a very large number that must have a wisdom, since God does not send down His verses idly but to leave His creatures without excuse and to show the two roads. He compares a person who denies being near the Fire to a sick man who does not think himself sick: admitting the illness is half the cure, turning to the doctor is a quarter, and taking the medicine is the rest. He notes that some people hold on to the verses of mercy and intercession and leave the verses of the Fire, and asks where the other verses are. (needs_source: the number of verses on the Fire is given without reference to a count, and a different, apparently garbled figure occurs later in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:10:00}
- He relates that some of the Followers complained to Ibn Abbas (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Ibn Abbas]]), who recited the verse of al-Kahf in which some will say there were three, the fourth their dog (see [[passage/quran-18-22|al-Kahf, verse 22]]), which says few know them, and said he was among those few. He then cites a divine saying that God does not gather two securities and two fears in His servant: whoever fears Him in this world He makes secure in the next, and whoever feels secure from Him in this world He makes fearful in the next; and he says that sitting with one who makes you fear and finding safety on the Day of Resurrection is better than sitting with one who reassures you and finding fear. (needs_source: the report about Ibn Abbas is garbled in the transcript and told without a chain, and the divine saying about the two securities and two fears is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:05:00}
- He tells that Harun al-Rashid went to visit al-Fudayl ibn Iyad (see [[person/harun-al-rashid|Harun al-Rashid]] and [[person/al-fudayl-ibn-iyad|al-Fudayl ibn Iyad]]); al-Fudayl looked at him from a small window and asked who was knocking, the answer was "open for the commander of the believers", and al-Fudayl said they had come to keep him from the remembrance of God. When the door was opened and he shook Harun's hand, al-Fudayl patted it and said how soft this hand is if only it escaped God's punishment on the Day of Resurrection, and Harun wept. The sheikh says the hearts of the early generations were pure, so a word moved them and a sin frightened them, like a polished mirror that shows the slightest blemish, whereas a sick heart neither knows the good nor rejects the wrong. (needs_source: the report of Harun al-Rashid's visit to al-Fudayl is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:07:00}
- He says the Companions and the early generations left the world and it followed them, and he cites a saying on the tongue of the world that it tells whoever serves it to be used and whoever uses it to be served; he says God owns this world and the next, so whoever wants one should ask Him for the next and He will give both (see [[passage/quran-7-176|al-A'raf, verse 176]]). On the verses he says they are recited but many do not reflect on them, and God asks whether they do not reflect on the Qur'an or whether there are locks on their hearts (see [[passage/quran-47-24|Muhammad, verse 24]]); the Companions reflected and were afraid, and he tells that Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) passed by night in Medina, heard a woman reciting "has the story of the overwhelming event reached you" (see [[passage/quran-88-1|al-Ghashiya, verse 1]]) and wept. He mentions that Ibn Mas'ud used to recite the Qur'an as if it had been sent down to him alone (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Mas'ud]]), and calls for training the self in the Prophet's school, whose doors are still open. (needs_source: the saying ascribed to the world, the report of Umar passing by night and weeping at the verse of al-Ghashiya, and the description of Ibn Mas'ud's recitation are told without a chain, and the transcript does not make clear whether the figure is Umar or another) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:11:00}
- He says many Muslims complete the Qur'an in Ramadan with their tongues without it passing their ears, that the completions may be evidence against a person, and he cites a transmitted saying that many a reciter of the Qur'an is cursed by the Qur'an. He then tells of Sheikh Ibrahim al-Akhdar, imam of the Prophet's Mosque, who held a Qur'an competition in Algeria every three years, and relates there the story of a poor boy whose mother ordered him to memorise the Qur'an with a sheikh; the sheikh required him first to serve the mosque and the animals, and the boy turned back sad, then saw the Prophet in a dream telling him to return and say to the sheikh that the sign was "zumar zumra"; when he told him, the sheikh wept and kissed him, and said he had seen the Prophet years before and asked how the memorisers of the Qur'an who act on it enter Paradise, and was told "in groups, group after group" (see [[passage/quran-39-73|al-Zumar, verse 73]]). He adds that the person in charge made the competition annual and gave each participant fifty thousand in a currency the transcript does not make clear. (needs_source: the story of the boy, the sheikh, the dream and the agreed sign is told without a chain and is attributed to words of Sheikh Ibrahim al-Akhdar as the speaker relates them; the amount and currency are unclear in the transcript; the transmitted saying on the reciter of the Qur'an is told without a chain and its verb is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:16:00}
- He says the remedy of the heart lies in remembering God, keeping righteous company, being near godly scholars and reflecting on the Qur'an; that a person's enemies are Iblis, the world, the self and desire; and that salvation is with the Lord of salvation, so whoever is with the Creator is the victor. He then takes the verse of al-Kahf "say, the truth is from your Lord, so let whoever will believe and whoever will disbelieve" (see [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]]), and holds that the will here is the servant's will, that putting belief before disbelief in the verse shows how clear the truth is, that both roads lie in front of a person, that truth can come only from God, and that the most a creature says is what is correct, not the truth. He cites God's words on the Day of Resurrection that the angels in ranks will not speak except those whom the All-Merciful permits and who say what is correct (see [[passage/quran-78-38|al-Naba', verse 38]] and [[passage/quran-78-39|verse 39]]), and says that even Gabriel that day will say what is correct, not the truth, because he is a creature. This is his understanding of the verse. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:21:00}
- He then comes to the rest of the verse, "We have prepared for the wrongdoers a fire whose canopy encloses them" (see [[passage/quran-18-29|al-Kahf, verse 29]] and [[concept/zulm|wrongdoing]]), and says the Fire is prepared for the wrongdoers, not for the disbelievers alone, because associating partners and disbelief are forms of wrongdoing, and wrongdoing is putting a thing in the wrong place; he explains the canopy as a great wall surrounding them. He says that if they call for relief from the heat they are given water like molten metal that scorches the faces, and explains it as boiling pus; he cites that when the cup is brought near the face the skin of the face falls off, and that the people of the Fire are overcome by hunger and are brought al-dari' (see [[passage/quran-88-6|al-Ghashiya, verse 6]]), which he describes as a thorny plant that sticks in the throat, cannot be swallowed or brought up, so they cry for help. He holds that the mouth that never spoke the truth in this world is punished in this way. (needs_source: the report of the cup and the falling of the skin of the face, the report that hunger overcomes the people of the Fire, and the description of al-dari' as a thorn eaten by camels in the desert are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:26:30}
- He criticises the phrase "the wrath of nature" used by some people and holds that it is an unlawful way of speaking. He cites a hadith that God said that some of His servants woke up as a believer in Him and a disbeliever, so whoever said "we were rained on by God's mercy" is a believer in Me and a disbeliever in the star, and whoever said "we were rained on by such-and-such a star" is a disbeliever in Me and a believer in the star. He advises checking four things: glances, utterances, thoughts and steps, and says whoever keeps them enters Paradise. This is his view on usage and it bears on the question of attributing events to God. (needs_source: the hadith of the believer and the disbeliever and the star is told without a chain, and the saying on checking the four things is told without a chain or a speaker) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:27:30}
- On the host's question about where God's mercy stands beside this punishment, he says one must not take a verse and leave what surrounds it, since God said "My mercy encompasses all things, so I will prescribe it for those who are mindful of God" (see [[passage/quran-7-156|al-A'raf, verse 156]]), making it general and then specific. He relates that when the verse came down Iblis said "I am something", and the specification followed. The sheikh says the will mentioned in the matter of guidance is the servant's will, that a person is born on the natural disposition and is led astray by his desire, his devil and his self that commands evil, that an unlettered farmer who reads the Book of God receives its meaning in his heart more than a great scholar might, and that whoever closes his heart has what he earned rusting over it (see [[passage/quran-83-14|al-Mutaffifin, verse 14]] and [[concept/qadar|qadar]]). This is a theological question on which scholars differ. (needs_source: the report of Iblis saying "I am something" when the verse came down is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:32:00}
- From al-Hajj he cites "these are two adversaries who disputed about their Lord, and those who disbelieved will have garments of fire cut out for them" (see [[passage/quran-22-19|al-Hajj, verse 19]]), and explains the cutting as matching what a person wore of the unlawful in this world; he says whoever covers a Muslim, God covers him, and he cites a hadith that the Fire complained to its Lord and was given leave for two breaths, one in winter and one in summer, ascribing it to Sahih Muslim, and says the severest heat and cold in this world come from its breath (see [[concept/jahannam|Jahannam]]). He adds that a disbeliever's molar will be like Mount Uhud and the thickness of his skin a journey of three days, and that skins are replaced and others cut for him. (needs_source: the hadith of whoever covers a Muslim, the hadith of the Fire's complaint and its two breaths, and the hadith of the disbeliever's molar and skin are told without a chain, and some are ascribed to a book without naming the place in it) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:36:00}
- From al-Qasas he cites "We made them leaders calling to the Fire" (see [[passage/quran-28-41|al-Qasas, verse 41]] and [[person/firawn|Pharaoh]]), and says Pharaoh became the pattern of every tyrant, and that in the sheikh's view whoever removes a ruling of God and sets up a ruling of his own claims divinity in deed. He mentions Pharaoh's exalting himself in the land, dividing its people and weakening one group of them (see [[passage/quran-28-4|al-Qasas, verse 4]] and [[passage/quran-28-5|verse 5]]), and tells that Moses (see [[person/musa|Moses]]) asked God to bring him near Jerusalem, and that the Prophet said that were he there he would show them his grave. He says every tyrant through history shares in that. (needs_source: the hadith "were I there I would show you his grave" and the report of Moses asking to be near the Holy Land are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:38:30}
- From al-Ahzab he cites "the day their faces are turned over in the Fire" (see [[passage/quran-33-66|al-Ahzab, verse 66]]), and says the verb "turned over" is in the present tense and so indicates renewal, and that a face used only to disobey God is punished through its eyes, ears and mouth. Then from Saba' "so today none of you has power over another for benefit or harm, and We say to those who did wrong, taste the punishment of the Fire that you used to deny" (see [[passage/quran-34-42|Saba', verse 42]]); he says the Qur'an is explained only by the Qur'an. This is his understanding. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:41:00}
- From Fatir "for those who disbelieve is the fire of Hell; it will not be decreed for them to die, nor will its punishment be lightened for them" (see [[passage/quran-35-36|Fatir, verse 36]]), and he says God does not put an end to them; he cites the words of the people of the Fire "O Malik, let your Lord put an end to us" (see [[passage/quran-43-77|al-Zukhruf, verse 77]]), that Malik answers after a thousand years that they will remain, and that they are then put in coffins of fire which are shut. He cites a hadith that Death is brought as a white-grey ram and slaughtered between Paradise and the Fire, so the people of Paradise grow in joy and the people of the Fire in grief (see [[passage/quran-87-13|al-A'la, verse 13]]). (needs_source: the report of Malik answering after a thousand years, the report of the coffins and the hadith of the slaughter of Death are told without a chain, and the speaker ascribed some of them to al-Bukhari and Muslim without naming the place) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:44:00}
- From Sad he cites "this; and for the transgressors is a worst place of return, Hell, which they will burn in, and wretched is the resting place" (see [[passage/quran-38-55|Sad, verse 55]] and [[passage/quran-38-56|verse 56]]), and explains transgression as rebellion against God, listing its forms as transgression against the Messenger of God, scholars, the righteous, young Muslims, families, the departments one heads and God's creatures. Then "this, let them taste it: scalding water and filthy discharge, and other torments of its kind in pairs" (see [[passage/quran-38-57|verse 57]] and [[passage/quran-38-58|verse 58]]); he says hamim is what is intensely hot and ghassaq what is intensely cold, and that the kinds of torment come in pairs. Then "this is a troop rushing in with you" (see [[passage/quran-38-59|verse 59]]), which he takes to be the troop of followers like the tyrants through history, and says transgressors may be Muslims who testified that there is no god but God without fulfilling its due. He says that among those who incurred wrath one might think of Abu Jahl, Abu Lahab, Uqba ibn Abi Mu'ayt and Umayya ibn Khalaf, and among those who went astray the false claimants such as Musaylima and Sajah, and that reflecting on this belongs in prayer (see [[passage/quran-1-7|al-Fatiha, verse 7]], [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]] and [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]]). This is his reading of the verses. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:48:00}
- From the same sura, "they will say, why do we not see men we used to count among the wicked, did we take them in mockery" (see [[passage/quran-38-62|Sad, verse 62]] and [[passage/quran-38-63|verse 63]]); he says the people of the Fire ask after those believers they used to mock, and links it to "those who did wrong used to laugh at those who believed" (see [[passage/quran-83-29|al-Mutaffifin, verse 29]] and [[passage/quran-83-30|verse 30]]) and to "that is indeed true, the disputing of the people of the Fire" (see [[passage/quran-38-64|Sad, verse 64]]), which he takes to be argument and tension among them. He recalls "We will remove whatever of ill feeling is in their breasts" (see [[passage/quran-15-47|al-Hijr, verse 47]]) and says there is a veil between the people of Paradise and the people of the Fire, and that one who had a companion in the world looks down and sees him in the midst of Hell (see [[passage/quran-37-51|al-Saffat, verse 51]] and [[passage/quran-37-55|verse 55]]). He says intercession is restricted, not unlimited (see [[passage/quran-39-19|al-Zumar, verse 19]] and [[concept/shafaa|intercession]]). This is his view, and the matter is disputed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:57:30}
- From al-Zumar, "they will have canopies of fire above them and canopies beneath them; with that God puts fear into His servants, so, O My servants, fear Me" (see [[passage/quran-39-16|al-Zumar, verse 16]]), and he says a canopy is taken for mercy but is harsher than the Fire, that the call is to all servants, and that God watches a servant where He forbade him and does not miss him where He commanded him. Then from Ghafir the believing man of Pharaoh's people who said to his people "how is it that I call you to salvation and you call me to the Fire" (see [[passage/quran-40-41|Ghafir, verse 41]] and [[passage/quran-40-43|verse 43]]); the sheikh notes that disbelievers do not call the Messenger himself a liar but reject God's verses (see [[passage/quran-6-33|al-An'am, verse 33]]), and gives as an example al-Walid ibn al-Mughira and the words "I will make him climb a steep slope" (see [[passage/quran-74-17|al-Muddaththir, verse 17]]). He recalls the hadith of the leaf of the Torah with Umar, and says defending the Shari'a against those who attack it is something that should be done; this is his view. (needs_source: the hadith of the leaf of the Torah with Umar is told without a chain, and the identity of the "boy" taken to be al-Walid ibn al-Mughira rests on a garbled transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:02:30}
- He then says, citing Ghafir, that the way back is open to whoever transgressed against himself, and recalls "do not despair of God's mercy" (see [[passage/quran-39-53|al-Zumar, verse 53]]); he cites "they are exposed to the Fire morning and evening, and on the day the Hour is established, make the people of Pharaoh enter the severest punishment" (see [[passage/quran-40-46|Ghafir, verse 46]]), and says the exposure is in the grave and that the ruling is not limited to the people of Pharaoh but covers all who are like them. He mentions the disputing of the people of the Fire, in which the weak say to those who were arrogant "we were your followers" (see [[passage/quran-40-47|Ghafir, verse 47]]), and the words of those in the Fire to the keepers of Hell "call upon your Lord to lighten for us one day of the punishment" (see [[passage/quran-40-49|verse 49]]); he says that is the most they wish for, and that the weak may include disobedient Muslims. He also cites "in the scalding water, then in the Fire they are set ablaze" (see [[passage/quran-40-72|verse 72]]). (needs_source: the naming of the sura of Ghafir is uncertain and the transcript is garbled here, and the passage on the number "a thousand Pharaohs" is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:07:00}
- From Fussilat, "that is the reward of God's enemies, the Fire, in which they have the abode of permanence" (see [[passage/quran-41-28|Fussilat, verse 28]]), then "those who deviate in Our verses are not hidden from Us; is one who is thrown into the Fire better, or one who comes secure on the Day of Resurrection" (see [[passage/quran-41-40|verse 40]]); he explains deviation as altering the verses and interpreting them by desire to serve certain parties, and holds that separating religion from politics and the state belongs to it, and says that whoever feared in this world is secure in the next. This is his view on a political question that is disputed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:09:00}
- From al-Ahqaf, "on the day those who disbelieved are exposed to the Fire, you exhausted your good things in your worldly life and took pleasure in them" (see [[passage/quran-46-20|al-Ahqaf, verse 20]]), and he says the exposure is before entry, and contrasts the wine of Paradise in which there is no intoxication (see [[passage/quran-37-47|al-Saffat, verse 47]]); he says part of the brain secretes a substance like morphine to ease pain and that drugs disable it, a claim that needs a scientific reference. He cites a hadith that pride is My cloak and greatness My wrapper, and the hadith of the man whom the Prophet told to eat with his right hand and who said he could not, and was told "may you not be able", and only pride had stopped him, so he never raised it to his mouth. He tells that a man named Hazn refused to change his name when the Prophet named him Sahl, and that his grandson Sa'id ibn al-Musayyib said the harshness had remained among them; the sheikh says the man should have accepted what the Prophet chose for him. He cites the hadith of the Companion who threw away his gold ring after the Prophet said that one of you picks up a coal of fire and puts it in his hand. (needs_source: the hadith of pride as the cloak, the hadith of the man who could not eat with his right hand, the report of the name and Sa'id ibn al-Musayyib and the hadith of the gold ring are told without a chain or a book, and the report on the brain and morphine is given without a scientific reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:12:30}
- He cites "on the day those who disbelieved are exposed to the Fire, is this not the truth, they say, yes by our Lord" (see [[passage/quran-46-34|al-Ahqaf, verse 34]]), and says Islam came confirming what was before it and as a guardian over it (see [[passage/quran-5-48|al-Ma'ida, verse 48]]), that the proof has been established on people today since the message reached them, and that it is desire that prevails. This is his view. He then cites a hadith that God will say to the one with the lightest punishment among the people of the Fire, were you to have all that is on the earth, would you ransom yourself with it, and he will say yes, and God will say that something easier than that was asked of him. The host ends with the supplication of one seeking refuge with God and promises to continue in the next episode. (needs_source: the hadith of ransoming oneself with what is on the earth is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep31 01:15:30}
## Episode 32: the description of the Fire, part three: knowledge and responsibility, the lasting of the Fire, the faces of al-Ghashiya, the five supplications of the people of the Fire and the many names of the doomed
- The host opens with recitation of the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and the verses of al-Ghashiya (see [[passage/quran-88-1|al-Ghashiya, verse 1]]), and says this is the third episode on the description of the Fire. The sheikh begins by saying that the one who disobeys God knowing differs from the one who disobeys not knowing, in reward and in punishment alike (see [[passage/quran-39-9|al-Zumar, verse 9]]). He holds that learning the minimum of the faith is a duty and that neglecting it is itself a loss, and relates that a man told Abu Hurayra (see [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]]) he feared losing knowledge if he learned it, and was told that leaving knowledge is already to lose it. (needs_source: the exchange with Abu Hurayra is told without a chain or a book) He cites the verse on a party going out to learn the religion (see [[passage/quran-9-122|al-Tawba, verse 122]]) and the first command of revelation (see [[passage/quran-96-1|al-Alaq, verse 1]]), arguing that a command addressed to the Prophet is general in wording. He adds that repentance is accepted from the learned and the unlearned alike, since the one who accepts it is the Lord of all worlds, and that a person skilled in worldly affairs is accountable for neglecting the affairs of his religion. This is his view. He then explains the two boundary words of the Qur'an: for commands it is the warning against overstepping (see [[passage/quran-2-229|al-Baqara, verse 229]]) and for prohibitions the warning against approaching (see [[passage/quran-2-187|al-Baqara, verse 187]]), with the example of wine (see [[passage/quran-5-90|al-Ma'ida, verse 90]]), and uses the comparison of a farmer's boundary marker and of building without blocking a neighbour's air and light. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:09:00}
- Asked whether the Fire is renewed or burns out and ends, the sheikh answers that when it subsides God increases it (see [[passage/quran-17-97|al-Isra, verse 97]]), that it is not decreed to perish, and that its people neither die nor live in it (see [[passage/quran-87-13|al-A'la, verse 13]]). He ties the Fire to the injustice people do one another in the world, and says part of God's mercy is that grievances are removed in Paradise: he mentions the companion who doubted the resurrection and is seen in the midst of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-37-51|al-Saffat, verse 51]], [[passage/quran-37-55|verse 55]]) and the removal of rancour from the hearts of the people of Paradise (see [[passage/quran-7-43|al-A'raf, verse 43]]). He says that natural affections are also lifted, so that Prophet Nuh would not grieve in Paradise for a son who entered the Fire, and that all suffering in the world falls into fear of tomorrow and grief for what is past, which are taken away from the people of Paradise (see [[passage/quran-35-34|Fatir, verse 34]]). (needs_source: the removal of Nuh's fatherly feeling in Paradise is stated as fact without a text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:13:00}
- The host asks why punishment is endless when the life of sin was finite, given the Qur'anic principle of retribution. The sheikh answers with the comparison of reward: a servant who worships for twenty years is not told he will leave Paradise after a multiple of those years, and he explains this by God's foreknowledge that the believer would have obeyed for ever had he lived for ever and the disbeliever would have disbelieved for ever, citing the verse that they would return to what they were forbidden (see [[passage/quran-6-28|al-An'am, verse 28]]). He recalls the people of Pharaoh asking Musa to pray for removal of the plague and promising to believe (see [[passage/quran-7-133|al-A'raf, verse 133]], [[passage/quran-7-134|verse 134]]), and a report that smoke afflicted Quraysh early in the mission (see [[passage/quran-44-10|al-Dukhan, verse 10]]). The sheikh says this is the secret of eternity in Paradise or the Fire. This is his view on a question on which the theological schools differ. (needs_source: the report of the smoke on Quraysh is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:16:00}
- The host reads the verse that no ransom will be accepted from the disbelievers and hypocrites and that the Fire is their guardian (see [[passage/quran-57-15|al-Hadid, verse 15]]), and the sheikh explains that the Fire becoming one's guardian means it takes charge of him, as plants are watered with water and these are watered with fire. He gives the linguistic sense of the zaqqum, swallowing with difficulty (see [[passage/quran-37-64|al-Saffat, verse 64]]; also [[concept/zaqqum|zaqqum]]), and a report that Abu Jahl (see [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]) asked for dates and butter to swallow it. (needs_source: the report about Abu Jahl and the dates is told without a chain) On the verse that the people of the Fire and the people of Paradise are not equal (see [[passage/quran-59-20|al-Hashr, verse 20]]) he says the wording is meant to encourage the believer and wake the strayer, and remarks that the Qur'an says "companions" of the Fire, not residents or victims. He relates a hadith of the last man out of the Fire who is offered a place the size of the world and doubts God is mocking him. (needs_source: the hadith of the last man out of the Fire is retold without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:20:30}
- On the Qur'anic description of the Fire's punishment as "gharam" (see [[passage/quran-25-65|al-Furqan, verse 65]]) and doubled punishment (see [[passage/quran-25-69|al-Furqan, verse 69]]), the sheikh says the doubling is for the disbelief itself and again for mocking and harming believers, and asks where the suffering of early Muslims such as Ibn Mas'ud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Mas'ud]]) and Khabbab (see [[person/khabbab-ibn-al-aratt|Khabbab ibn al-Aratt]]) is accounted for. He says the world weighs little with God, comparing it to a mosquito, so that one who asked for the world in Paradise would be refused. (needs_source: the saying that the world is of little weight with God and the report of the request are told without a book; the transcript is garbled here) He then turns to the wives of Nuh and Lut (see [[passage/quran-66-10|al-Tahrim, verse 10]]) and reads "treason" broadly as disclosing the secrets of the household, by husband or wife alike, and adds a hadith, which he places in Muslim, on spouses spreading what passes between them. (needs_source: the hadith on spreading the secrets of spouses is named only as being in Muslim, without a number) He warns that curiosity about homes leads from backbiting to slander and to treason, and says there is reward for keeping a secret, that betrayal of God and His messenger is also treason (see [[passage/quran-8-27|al-Anfal, verse 27]]), and that the verse mentions entering the Fire without stating a length. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:28:00}
- On the greater Fire (see [[passage/quran-87-12|al-A'la, verse 12]]) the sheikh explains "al-ashqa", the one who excels in rebellion (see [[passage/quran-87-11|al-A'la, verse 11]]), and says one should not call a lively child "wretched", for among people some are wretched and some happy (see [[passage/quran-11-105|Hud, verse 105]]). He relates a saying of Ibn Umar (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Ibn Umar]]) that he would love nothing absent more than death if he knew God had accepted a single prostration from him. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Umar is told without a chain or a book, and the surrounding quotation of al-Hasan al-Basri is garbled) He says the greater Fire spans all the levels because such a person left no sin undone, and that the punishment of each person follows how far he strayed, a person with a small lapse staying shortly and a tyrant, killer and one who ruined God's religion staying longer. He warns against judging intentions, citing the account of Usama (see [[person/usama-ibn-zayd|Usama ibn Zayd]]) and the Prophet's question of whether he had split open the man's heart, and a saying of Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) that people were judged in the time of revelation but now by what they show. (needs_source: the account of Usama and the saying of Umar are told without a chain or book) He adds that Ibn Hazm (see [[person/ibn-hazm|Ibn Hazm]]) said a person is known by his character and dealings. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:35:30}
- On the opening verses of al-Ghashiya (see [[passage/quran-88-1|verse 1]], [[passage/quran-88-2|verse 2]]) the sheikh says "khashi'a" means humbled, and relates that the Fire has three exhalations on the day of gathering, at the first of which everyone falls to his knees, even angel and prophet, and at the second all fall on their faces, while the believers are told not to fear (see [[passage/quran-41-30|Fussilat, verse 30]]). (needs_source: the three exhalations are told without a chain or a book) He recalls a hadith on the earlier nations whose believers were sawn in two without turning from their faith, and Musa's counsel to patience (see [[passage/quran-7-128|al-A'raf, verse 128]]), and reads "working and weary" as acting for other than God and being tired in the Fire for it, with the verse on every human striving toward his Lord (see [[passage/quran-84-6|al-Inshiqaq, verse 6]]). He gives the example of three people awake at night, one praying, one a guard paid for it and one a thief, whose results differ because of intention, and relates a hadith of two eyes the Fire will not touch, one that wept from fear of God and one that kept watch in His way. (needs_source: the hadith of the earlier nations and the hadith of the two eyes are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:39:00}
- The sheikh describes the Fire as having seven levels, and says its sparks are like great castles (see [[passage/quran-77-32|al-Mursalat, verse 32]]) and that the hypocrites are in the lowest level (see [[passage/quran-4-145|al-Nisa, verse 145]]). He gives a multiple of seventy by seven for the lowest level, a figure the transcript garbles and he gives without a source. (needs_source: the figure of four hundred and ninety times is garbled in the transcript and given without a source) He says that God changes the body of the disbeliever while the spirit stays as it was, so he knows who he is, and that friends on that day are enemies of one another (see [[passage/quran-43-67|al-Zukhruf, verse 67]]). (needs_source: the claim that the body of the disbeliever is changed while the spirit stays is stated without a text) He describes the people of the Fire entering thirsty and being given scalding water, and says that one who never gave a Muslim a kind word to drink is thirsty from the start, recalling the verse on the pious gathered to the Merciful and the sinners driven thirsty to Hell (see [[passage/quran-19-85|Maryam, verse 85]], [[passage/quran-19-86|verse 86]]). This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:42:00}
- Returning to al-Ghashiya, the sheikh says "dari'" is a thorny plant that camels eat and choke on, neither fattening nor satisfying (see [[passage/quran-88-6|al-Ghashiya, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-88-7|verse 7]]), and that one reason for this torment is that the person ate what was forbidden, so that he advises being content with a little lawful. He draws attention to the grammar of the passage, in which the Fire is entered actively as a warning to souls in the world, while the drink is given in the passive, with a spring of boiling water (see [[passage/quran-88-5|al-Ghashiya, verse 5]]): angels give the drink, and the food is described for the group together, since they gathered as one in unbelief. He contrasts this with the Paradise drink, also in the passive, where it is an honour (see [[passage/quran-83-25|al-Mutaffifin, verse 25]]). He adds that the Fire is closed over them (see [[passage/quran-104-8|al-Humaza, verse 8]], [[passage/quran-104-9|verse 9]]) while the doors of Paradise are open, comparing a school with walls and a guard that a pupil wants to leave to an open public park, and reading it as a closing of the doors of good that they had themselves done in the world. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:50:00}
- The sheikh turns to the verse on the fire that blazes (see [[passage/quran-92-14|al-Layl, verse 14]]) and the verse that those who disbelieved among the People of the Book and the idolaters are in the Fire of Hell and are the worst of creatures (see [[passage/quran-98-6|al-Bayyina, verse 6]]). He explains in his own account that the Torah holds legislation and the Gospel ethics, that each was meant to complete the other, that the Jews rejected the Gospel and so split the two, and that the Qur'an is the last book, so that the People of the Book should have believed in it, citing a saying of the Prophet that if Musa and Isa were alive they would have to follow him. This is his account of the matter and is contested between religious communities. (needs_source: the saying of the Prophet about Musa and Isa is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:53:30}
- The sheikh reads Surat al-Masad (see [[passage/quran-111-1|al-Masad, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-111-5|verse 5]]), saying that Abu Lahab (see [[person/abu-lahab|Abu Lahab]]) was named so for his flushed face and that names can match their bearers. He gives other reports about names: that Abu Bakr's mother, asked why she named him Atiq, answered that God would free him from the Fire, and that Umar once questioned a man whose name and clan carried the meaning of fire and told him to go to his family, who had burned. The transcript garbles the names in the latter report, and the sheikh adds that the Prophet changed the names Harb and Sakhr, which Ali wished for his sons, to Hasan and Husayn. (needs_source: the reports on the name of Atiq, the name of the man questioned by Umar and the renaming of Ali's sons are told without a chain or book, and the names are garbled) He says the wife of Abu Lahab used to put thorns in the Prophet's way and reads the rope of palm fibre as a rope round her neck. Ibn al-Jawzi (see [[person/ibn-al-jawzi|Ibn al-Jawzi]]) is quoted as saying that the flesh of the learned is poisoned, a saying he attributes to him. (needs_source: the saying about the flesh of the learned is attributed to Ibn al-Jawzi without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 00:59:30}
- The sheikh reads the five supplications of the people of the Fire in the Qur'an. The first is that God caused them to die twice and live twice and they now confess (see [[passage/quran-40-11|Ghafir, verse 11]]), answered that this is because they disbelieved when God alone was called (see [[passage/quran-40-12|Ghafir, verse 12]]); and he notes that the belief of Pharaoh at the moment of drowning did not profit him (see [[passage/quran-10-90|Yunus, verse 90]]). The second is that they have seen and heard and ask to return (see [[passage/quran-32-12|al-Sajda, verse 12]]), answered that they forgot the meeting of that day (see [[passage/quran-32-14|al-Sajda, verse 14]]). The third is a request for a little more time (see [[passage/quran-14-44|Ibrahim, verse 44]]). The fourth is a request to be sent back to do good unlike what they did (see [[passage/quran-35-37|Fatir, verse 37]]), answered that they were given a life long enough and a warner came to them. The fifth is that their wretchedness overcame them (see [[passage/quran-23-106|al-Mu'minun, verse 106]]), answered with an order to be silent (see [[passage/quran-23-108|al-Mu'minun, verse 108]]). He adds that each supplication begins with "our Lord", and says God gives the argument in full in the Qur'an so that no one is left with a plea. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:06:00}
- The host asks why the Qur'an gives the doomed so many names, such as hypocrite, wrongdoer, disbeliever and idolater. The sheikh answers that people differ in nature and sin comes in colours, that God coloured acts of obedience so that we do not tire and that Satan coloured sins to lead people astray, so that each listener finds himself addressed. He lists five groups, those who oppose God and His messenger, those who deny His verses, those who cut ties of kinship, wrongdoers and hypocrites, who are in the lowest level (see [[passage/quran-4-145|al-Nisa, verse 145]]), and says the Fire speaks, asking for more (see [[passage/quran-50-30|Qaf, verse 30]]), and recalls the report that it complained to God and was permitted two breaths in a year. (needs_source: the hadith of the complaint of the Fire and its two breaths is told without a chain or a book) Asked about a recent earthquake, he prays that it be no sign of God's anger, and relates that when the earth shook in the time of Umar, he stood and told people to fear God, since the earth shakes only for sins done on it. (needs_source: the report about Umar and the earthquake, and the saying that the earth boasts to its neighbours of the one who mentions God on it, are told without a chain or a book) He says the whole universe glorifies God and that a person who strays falls out of that harmony, and announces that the next episode begins the first level of the Fire. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep32 01:13:30}
## Episode 33: the levels of the Fire, part one: Jahannam, Sa'ir, al-Hutama, Laza and Saqar, and who is sent to each
- The host opens with recitation of the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]]) and the verses of Surat al-Naba on Jahannam as a place of ambush (see [[passage/quran-78-21|al-Naba, verse 21]], [[passage/quran-78-30|verse 30]]). He says he feels an unusual trembling because the series now turns to the levels of the Fire, and the sheikh answers that a Muslim who feels no fear of them has already felt safe from God's plan (see [[passage/quran-7-99|al-A'raf, verse 99]]), a fear that moved the Companions, the Followers and the Prophet. The host then asks whether the seven names, Jahannam, Saqar, al-Hutama, al-Hawiya, the lowest level, al-Jahim and the rest, name levels, doors or only describe the Fire. The sheikh answers that the scholars understood them as levels or doors of the Fire, and that some hold Jahannam to be the name of the whole Fire. This is his account of a question on which scholars differ. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:04:00}
- The sheikh gives his own view, which he offers as the result of study and not as a ruling: that each level is assigned to particular groups according to what they did, and that Jahannam is the first level, where the disobedient among the monotheists are placed. He relates a hadith that a day will come when the doors of Jahannam creak and it empties, (needs_source: the hadith of the doors of Jahannam creaking is told without a chain or a book) and says he held this view at first, then found in the Qur'an that Jahannam also holds people who are not of that group. He says he consulted more than twenty-five commentaries and his teachers in exegesis and hadith, and presents the result as an effort that is from God if sound and otherwise from himself. (needs_source: the number of commentaries consulted is his own account) He concludes that every level will hold people of every kind, the sinful among the monotheists included, and that those monotheists leave through Jahannam because it is the last door, as one leaves a well by its first step, while the remaining levels close on those who stay for ever. He mentions in passing a report that someone stood a night on one verse about a witness from every nation (see [[passage/quran-4-41|al-Nisa, verse 41]]) and wept, and that al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) completed a recitation of the whole Qur'an every few days.
