(needs_source: the report of the night spent on one verse is told without a chain or a book, and the name is garbled in the transcript) He sets out three kinds of Muslim at the reckoning: those whose good deeds outweigh their wrongs go to Paradise, those whose wrongs outweigh go to the Fire, and those whose deeds are equal are the people of al-A'raf. This is his own reading, and the schools differ on who leaves the Fire and when. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:10:30}
- Asked whether the sources of the faith assign each name to a given level or fix their number, the sheikh says they do not. He turns to Jahannam, which he says is named seventy-seven times in thirty-nine surahs. (needs_source: the count of seventy-seven mentions in thirty-nine surahs is a statistic given without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:11:30}
- The sheikh begins with the verse that when a man is told to fear God, pride in sin takes hold of him and Jahannam is enough for him (see [[passage/quran-2-206|al-Baqara, verse 206]]), and gives the example of a man who disinherits a relative and is warned by another. He says that the right answer to such advice is to thank the adviser, and that people have often found a reminder more hateful than a rotting carcass. He recalls that the same words were said to the Prophet in al-Ahzab (see [[passage/quran-33-1|al-Ahzab, verse 1]]) and that he did not take offence, and relates that when a boy told Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar]]) to fear God, Umar wept. (needs_source: the story of the boy and Umar is told without a chain or a book) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:16:00}
- The sheikh turns to the verse on those who earn God's anger, and says wrath is not limited to the disbeliever but reaches any person who neglects prayer and almsgiving, preys on people's honour, devours orphans' wealth or misuses public funds. On the verse that the one who kills a believer deliberately is repaid with Jahannam and God's anger (see [[passage/quran-4-93|al-Nisa, verse 93]]), he says that retribution belongs to the ruler and not to individuals, because Islam arose as a community and leaving it to individuals would spread disorder, and he cites the report to obey even a leader who is an Abyssinian slave. This is his view on a legal question. (needs_source: the report to obey a leader who is a slave is told without a chain or a book, and the wording is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:20:30}
- On the verse that whoever kills one soul is as if he killed all people (see [[passage/quran-5-32|al-Ma'ida, verse 32]]), the sheikh reads it as killing the humanity in that person. He extends the sense, as a figure and with no worldly penalty, to a moral killing: ruining a person by rumour, belittling him, or making his life a lasting torment through harm to a spouse. He advises the wronged spouse to be patient and recalls the verse that the patient are paid without reckoning (see [[passage/quran-39-10|al-Zumar, verse 10]]), adding a report that those who always praised God will be told to enter Paradise by any door they wish, joining Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]), who alone is called from all eight. (needs_source: the report of those who praised God entering by any door is told without a chain or a book) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:22:30}
- On the verse about those whom the angels take while wronging themselves (see [[passage/quran-4-97|al-Nisa, verse 97]]), he says wronging oneself means abandoning obedience and barring others from the path. Those who answer that they were weak and helpless are asked whether God's earth was not wide enough to emigrate in. He argues that Islam asks for action and not for passive excuses, and that a person is not powerless if he can move, adding the remark that in his time migration is hindered by visas and security rules. He quotes Ibn Taymiyya (see [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]) as saying that his garden is in his chest, and that imprisonment is retreat with God, and killing martyrdom. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Taymiyya is told without a book) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:25:00}
- The sheikh reads the verse that for those who deny God's verses and are too proud for them the gates of heaven are not opened and they do not enter Paradise (see [[passage/quran-7-40|al-A'raf, verse 40]]), and says their prayer is not answered and that the comparison with the camel and the needle means they will not enter. He says the sinful monotheists will leave the Fire when intercession or mercy reaches them, and that the disbeliever and the proud are the ones who stay; he notes that scholars hold many views on this and says that he hopes God's mercy is near. This is his view on a question on which the schools differ. He then reads the verse on the many of the jinn and men made for Jahannam, who have hearts that do not understand, eyes that do not see and ears that do not hear (see [[passage/quran-7-179|al-A'raf, verse 179]]), saying that this is not a lack of the faculties but of using them for the truth and that arguing for its own sake blocks understanding. He says that everything in creation glorifies God (see [[passage/quran-17-44|al-Isra, verse 44]]), so that the only one who is out of tune is the person who denies it, and relates a saying of Ibn Mas'ud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Ibn Mas'ud]]) that a hearer who could grasp the glorification of things would be struck by it. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Mas'ud is told without a chain or a book, and the wording is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:31:30}
- The sheikh reads the verse that those who disbelieve spend their wealth to bar people from God's path, will regret it and then be overcome, and are gathered to Jahannam (see [[passage/quran-8-36|al-Anfal, verse 36]]), and reads it as about wealth spent to lead people away from their faith, which ends in loss, saying it is worse still when a Muslim does this. He asks whether Jahannam as the first level is for the disbelievers or for the sinful monotheists, and answers that the disbelievers may move between its places of torment. On the verse that God does not destroy towns unjustly while their people reform (see [[passage/quran-11-117|Hud, verse 117]]) he says the destruction comes when people do wrong, and adds that God set epidemics against some of them, which is his own reading of events. On the verses that if God wished all people would be one community and that He created them for that difference (see [[passage/quran-11-118|Hud, verse 118]], [[passage/quran-11-119|verse 119]]), he says difference in nature, colour and law is there so that each person freely chooses, and that the verse ends with Jahannam being filled with jinn and men together. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:36:00}
- Citing the verse in al-Isra on Jahannam as a prison for the disbelievers (see [[passage/quran-17-8|al-Isra, verse 8]]), the sheikh says Jahannam is therefore not only for the sinful monotheists. He says the Qur'an is never worn by repetition and that the Companions heard the verse on the Prophet being only a messenger (see [[passage/quran-3-144|Al Imran, verse 144]]) as if for the first time when Abu Bakr recited it at his death. (needs_source: the account of the Companions hearing the verse after the Prophet's death is told without a chain or a book) He reads the verses on one who wants only the present world and is given Jahannam, and one who seeks the Hereafter and strives for it as a believer (see [[passage/quran-17-18|al-Isra, verse 18]], [[passage/quran-17-19|verse 19]]), and says belief is not a matter of wishes or of trust in intercession alone. He adds the verse that a person should seek the Hereafter through what God gave him without forgetting his share of this world (see [[passage/quran-28-77|al-Qasas, verse 77]]), and relates that Umar and his friends each named a wish in turn, one a mountain of gold spent in God's way, one horses for jihad, and Umar a mosque full of men like the best of the Companions. (needs_source: the story of the wishes is told without a chain or a book, and a name in it is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:40:00}
- The sheikh turns to the verse that for those whom God leaves astray there are no protectors, and that He gathers them on their faces, blind, dumb and deaf, with Jahannam as their refuge, which is stoked whenever it dies down (see [[passage/quran-17-97|al-Isra, verse 97]]). He explains that they closed their eyes, mouths and ears to the truth in the world and so lost them, and that guidance and misguidance are both with God and by His knowledge, advising the listener to gather every verse on misguidance before drawing a conclusion (see [[passage/quran-76-3|al-Insan, verse 3]], [[passage/quran-91-8|al-Shams, verse 8]]). This is his reading. He reads the verse on God presenting Jahannam to the disbelievers whose eyes were veiled from remembrance (see [[passage/quran-18-100|al-Kahf, verse 100]], [[passage/quran-18-101|verse 101]]), and says presenting means they will enter, and that a Muslim in this state could enter with them. He relates the Prophet's address to his house, to Banu Abd Manaf and Banu Hashim, to al-Abbas, Safiyya and Fatima, that he could not avail them against God, and the prayer of Isa in the verse on those who are punished being servants of God (see [[passage/quran-5-118|al-Ma'ida, verse 118]]). (needs_source: the address of the Prophet to his relatives is told without a chain or a book) He adds the verses on the disbelievers taking servants as guardians and on the greatest losers in deeds, who think they do well (see [[passage/quran-18-102|al-Kahf, verse 102]], [[passage/quran-18-103|verse 103]], [[passage/quran-18-104|verse 104]]), and says that following one's whims is the mark of this. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:46:30}
- The sheikh reads the verses of Maryam on God drawing out from every group the one most defiant of the Merciful and burning them first (see [[passage/quran-19-69|Maryam, verse 69]], [[passage/quran-19-70|verse 70]]) and says the first of each group to enter makes the next tremble, a vivid detail that he takes from the wording. He explains the verse that there is none of you who will not come to it (see [[passage/quran-19-71|Maryam, verse 71]]) as being about all people, believer and disbeliever alike, and reads the word for coming as passing over it, as a person arrives at a well (see [[passage/quran-28-23|al-Qasas, verse 23]]), which he takes to mean crossing the Sirat (see [[concept/sirat|the Sirat]]). He says this is his reading, on which scholars have other views, and says that Jahannam is the first level and is mentioned most. He adds the prayer of the believers to be turned from Jahannam (see [[passage/quran-25-65|al-Furqan, verse 65]]), and on the verse about those who invent lies about God or deny the truth (see [[passage/quran-29-68|al-Ankabut, verse 68]]) says that lying about God includes not keeping to His command. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:51:30}
- On the verses of al-Zumar in which the disbelievers are driven to Jahannam in groups and its gates open on them, with its keepers asking whether messengers had not come (see [[passage/quran-39-71|al-Zumar, verse 71]], [[passage/quran-39-72|verse 72]]), the sheikh says the arrival is sudden and without welcome. He reads the hadith that the Fire says is there any more until God places His foot, and it says enough, as meaning not a foot as a limb but the last of its group, comparing a group of locusts, and says the report is not meant to liken God to anything. (needs_source: the hadith of the Fire asking for more and the foot is told without a chain or a book) This is his reading on a question on which the schools of theology differ. He reads the proud as entering in humiliation (see [[passage/quran-40-60|Ghafir, verse 60]]), the people who think ill of God (see [[passage/quran-48-6|al-Fath, verse 6]]), and those who persecuted believing men and women and did not repent, who receive the punishment of Jahannam and of burning (see [[passage/quran-85-10|al-Buruj, verse 10]]), explaining persecution as humiliating, slandering and torturing them. He relates that Jahannam is brought with seventy thousand reins, each pulled by seventy thousand angels, and has necks like camels that pick up its people one by one, and places it in Sahih al-Bukhari. (needs_source: the hadith of the seventy thousand reins is told without a chain or number, and the book is the sheikh's attribution) He adds the verse on the disbelievers among the People of the Book and the idolaters (see [[passage/quran-98-6|al-Bayyina, verse 6]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:58:00}
- The sheikh says that the order of the levels is not fixed by the texts and is a matter of his own reasoning, resting on how often the verses recur and the kind of act. He gives Sa'ir as the second level and explains the word as a raging fire, like a mad dog in its fierceness, and gives the verse on those who devour orphans' wealth, who swallow fire in their bellies and will burn in Sa'ir (see [[passage/quran-4-10|al-Nisa, verse 10]]). He explains that orphans' wealth is spent from the day of the death of the father, for example on funeral tents, lights and meals, which belong to the heirs, and relates that Umar was shown a lamp whose oil was the orphan's. (needs_source: the story of Umar and the lamp oil is told without a chain or a book and is garbled) He adds hadith that the best of houses is one where an orphan is honoured, and that the one who cares for an orphan will be near the Prophet in Paradise like two fingers, and extends orphanhood to a child whose parents are absent or busy. (needs_source: the hadith on the orphan's house and on the carer being near the Prophet are told without a chain or a book) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:02:30}
- Continuing with Sa'ir, the sheikh reads the verses on those who follow their fathers' ways even if Satan calls them to the punishment of Sa'ir (see [[passage/quran-31-21|Luqman, verse 21]]), on the jinn who stray from God's command (see [[passage/quran-34-12|Saba, verse 12]]), saying it is specific to them, and on Satan calling his party to be among the people of Sa'ir (see [[passage/quran-35-6|Fatir, verse 6]]). He relates a saying about eight things that make a believer's prayer unanswered, such as claiming to love God without obeying Him and to love the Prophet without following his way, and seeing others' faults while ignoring one's own. (needs_source: the saying on the eight reasons a prayer goes unanswered is told without its speaker, a chain or a book) He adds that the people of Sa'ir include a group in Paradise and a group in Sa'ir (see [[passage/quran-42-7|al-Shura, verse 7]]), that the devils are among its inhabitants (see [[passage/quran-67-5|al-Mulk, verse 5]]), the cry of those who say they would not be there had they heard or reasoned (see [[passage/quran-67-10|al-Mulk, verse 10]], [[passage/quran-67-11|verse 11]]) and the chains and fires prepared for the disbelievers (see [[passage/quran-76-4|al-Insan, verse 4]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:05:30}
- For al-Hutama the sheikh reads the verses of al-Humaza (see [[passage/quran-104-1|al-Humaza, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-104-2|verse 2]], [[passage/quran-104-4|verse 4]]) and says the first word is one who slanders in a person's face and the second one who does so behind his back, which is his own account of terms the commentators explain in more than one way. He says the one who gathers and counts wealth and gives no almsgiving from it is warned of it, and describes the fire that reaches the hearts (see [[passage/quran-104-6|verse 6]], [[passage/quran-104-7|verse 7]]) as burning the skin, then the body, until it reaches the heart, after which the body is restored and the torment repeated. (needs_source: the description of the fire burning in order from the skin to the heart and the body being restored is stated without a text) He says that al-Hutama and Laza occur once each. (needs_source: the count of one mention each is given without a source) For Laza he reads the verses on a fire that strips the skin of the head and takes the limbs (see [[passage/quran-70-15|al-Ma'arij, verse 15]], [[passage/quran-70-16|verse 16]]) and the call to the one who turned away and gathered wealth and stored it (see [[passage/quran-70-17|verse 17]], [[passage/quran-70-18|verse 18]]), saying the Arabic word means the limbs that are not mortal if cut, and that storing wealth is likened to a bank account. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:10:00}
- On Saqar the sheikh recalls the verse on tasting its touch (see [[passage/quran-54-48|al-Qamar, verse 48]]), which the host places in al-Muddaththir, and says the verb of tasting may mean that a group is taken there for a given torment and returned to its own place (see [[passage/quran-37-68|al-Saffat, verse 68]]). He tells the story of al-Walid ibn al-Mughira (see [[person/al-walid-ibn-al-mughira|al-Walid ibn al-Mughira]]), called the perfumed flower of Quraysh and the provision of the caravan, who listened to the Prophet recite, said that it was neither poetry nor the speech of soothsayers, then was pressed by Abu Jahl (see [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]) to say something against it and called it magic that is passed on, for which he is promised Saqar (see [[passage/quran-74-25|al-Muddaththir, verse 25]], [[passage/quran-74-26|verse 26]]). (needs_source: the story of al-Walid and Abu Jahl is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled around the names of his sons, among whom the sheikh says that one became a Muslim (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]])) He closes with the verses in which those in Saqar say they were not among those who prayed, fed the poor, took part in idle talk and denied the Day of Judgement (see [[passage/quran-74-42|al-Muddaththir, verse 42]], [[passage/quran-74-43|verse 43]], [[passage/quran-74-44|verse 44]], [[passage/quran-74-45|verse 45]], [[passage/quran-74-46|verse 46]]) and that no intercession will help them (see [[passage/quran-74-48|verse 48]]), and ends with a prayer for rescue from the Fire. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep33 01:15:30}
## Episode 34: the levels of the Fire, part two: al-Jahim, the tree of Zaqqum, the lowest level, the marks of the people of the Fire and a closing counsel
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on the Day when Jahannam is brought (see [[passage/quran-89-21|al-Fajr, verse 21]], [[passage/quran-89-23|verse 23]]), then recalls that the previous episode dealt with Saqar, Sa'ir, Jahannam and al-Hutama, and says today's subject is the claim that one who pronounces the two testimonies will not abide in the Fire for ever. The sheikh answers that this is the hope he holds out to viewers, and the host adds a saying of an unnamed righteous man who set Paradise and the Fire before his own soul and found that it wished only for one more day in which to repent. (needs_source: the saying of the unnamed righteous man is told without a name or a book) This is the presenters' framing, and the schools differ on the fate of those who die as monotheists. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:04:00}
- On al-Jahim the sheikh says the word occurs twenty-six times in twenty-eight surahs, (needs_source: the count of twenty-six mentions in twenty-eight surahs is a statistic given without a source) and that it is the place of the severest torment, possibly the level just before the last, where he says the scholars describe embers as large as mountains. (needs_source: the description of the embers of al-Jahim is told without a text) He adds that the Companions shook when it was named. This is his account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:05:30}
- Turning to Surat al-Saffat, the sheikh describes the dialogue of the people of Paradise recalling the world, one saying he had a companion (see [[passage/quran-37-51|al-Saffat, verse 51]]). He explains, as his own view, that every person has a companion, an angel for the one who walks the way of good and a devil for the one who walks the way of evil, and that the angels accompany the righteous into Paradise and enter on them from every door. He adds that each palace has four doors, for wives, for the righteous friends of the world, for the market of Paradise and for the young attendants. (needs_source: the four doors of every palace in Paradise are described without a text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:08:30}
- The sheikh reads the believer's words to his companion that he doubted the Resurrection (see [[passage/quran-37-53|al-Saffat, verse 53]]), the invitation to look (see [[passage/quran-37-54|verse 54]]), the sight of him in the midst of al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-37-55|verse 55]]) and the words that he nearly brought him to ruin and that, but for the favour of his Lord, he would have been among those brought forward (see [[passage/quran-37-56|verse 56]], [[passage/quran-37-57|verse 57]]), followed by the question whether they will die no more (see [[passage/quran-37-58|verse 58]], [[passage/quran-37-59|verse 59]]) and the great triumph (see [[passage/quran-37-60|verse 60]]). He describes the human and jinn devils as those who lead one away from prayer and from spending, giving the example of a man who puts a land deal before the noon prayer. To the host's question whether the scene has already happened, he answers that God speaks of the future as if it had occurred, since it has occurred in His knowledge. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:12:30}
- The sheikh moves to the verses asking whether such a reception is better than the tree of Zaqqum (see [[passage/quran-37-62|al-Saffat, verse 62]]), which is made a trial for the wrongdoers (see [[passage/quran-37-63|verse 63]]). He gives two readings of the trial, one that it refers to the remark of Abu Jahl (see [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]) that the dates of Yathrib are Zaqqum, and one that the tree is itself a severe test for the unbelievers. (needs_source: the remark of Abu Jahl about the dates of Yathrib is told without a chain or a book) He says the tree springs from the base of al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-37-64|verse 64]]) and that its fruit is like the heads of devils (see [[passage/quran-37-65|verse 65]]); asked how an unseen thing is likened to something unseen, he answers that the believer who hears this has a clear picture, and he recalls that people also feared the Antichrist without seeing him. As a light aside he tells of a woman in Baghdad who asked a jeweller for a ring bearing the image of the devil. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:17:30}
- He describes the people of Zaqqum eating until they fill their bellies (see [[passage/quran-37-66|al-Saffat, verse 66]]), choking, being given a mixture of boiling water to drink (see [[passage/quran-37-67|verse 67]]) and then returning to al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-37-68|verse 68]]). He says the verses give the reason, that they found their fathers astray and hurried in their footsteps (see [[passage/quran-37-69|verse 69]], [[passage/quran-37-70|verse 70]]), and that most of the earlier peoples went astray too, though warners were sent (see [[passage/quran-37-71|verse 71]], [[passage/quran-37-72|verse 72]], [[passage/quran-37-73|verse 73]]). This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:19:00}
- The sheikh argues that the verses about following one's fathers apply first to unbelievers and also to Muslims who let custom outweigh worship, and gives everyday examples: a family meeting at which a pious woman is accused of exaggeration for not shaking a male relative's hand, which he says the Prophet never did with a woman, and a wife kept in a harsh marriage by a tribal custom that forbids divorce. (needs_source: the saying attributed to Abu Hanifa on women and greeting is garbled in the transcript and cannot be checked) He adds that the verses concern those who die unbelievers and that God's mercy embraces Muslims who held to monotheism. These are his opinions on contested questions of conduct, recorded as his view and not as a ruling. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:25:00}
- Reading the verses of Surat Ghafir, the sheikh recalls that eight angels carry the Throne (see [[passage/quran-69-17|al-Haqqa, verse 17]]), and says four glorify God for His forbearance after knowledge and four for His pardon after power, and that the second formula shows pardon is meant to follow ability. (needs_source: the two glorifications of the four angels each are told without a chain or a book) He then argues that mercy is already present in this world, in revelation, scholars, mosques, provision and family, and that gratitude for it is owed. The recording is garbled where he gives examples of killing and a remark on Hiroshima, and these are not summarised here. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:29:00}
- On the verse that the angels who carry the Throne glorify their Lord and ask forgiveness for the believers (see [[passage/quran-40-7|Ghafir, verse 7]]), the sheikh says the angels were given this task after Adam was created, and relates the report that angels at a gathering of remembrance, asked by God what the people seek, answer that they seek Paradise and refuge from the Fire and that they would seek forgiveness more if they saw them. (needs_source: the report of the angels at a gathering of remembrance is told without a chain or a book) He relates a saying that, if one is tried by hardship and endures, then ease comes and people forget one another. (needs_source: the saying on hardship followed by ease is told without a chain, and the name of the Companion in the same passage is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:32:30}
- A long digression follows on cafes and sitting in the street, where the sheikh recalls the report that the Prophet told people to avoid sitting on the roads and, when told they could not, to give the road its due, which the sheikh lists as lowering the gaze, returning the greeting, enjoining good and forbidding wrong. (needs_source: the report on the due of the road is told without a chain or a book) The host answers with humour, and the sheikh holds that the age has its needs but that the aim is to come near to God. He then returns to the verse that the angels ask God to forgive those who repent and follow His way (see [[passage/quran-40-7|Ghafir, verse 7]]) and says protection from al-Jahim is for those who both repent and follow the path. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:37:30}
- On the verses of manacles, al-Jahim, food that chokes and a painful punishment (see [[passage/quran-73-12|al-Muzzammil, verse 12]], [[passage/quran-73-13|verse 13]]), the sheikh links the pain of the Fire to the pain of wronging others, such as an employer who hounds a worker or children who fail their parents, and says that the first step of reform is conduct with God and then with people. He reads the verses of the chain of seventy cubits (see [[passage/quran-69-30|al-Haqqa, verse 30]], [[passage/quran-69-31|verse 31]], [[passage/quran-69-32|verse 32]]) and states that the body of the damned is enlarged, that the unbeliever's molar is like a mountain and his skin a three-day journey. (needs_source: the description of the enlarged body of the damned is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:39:30}
- On the verse that al-Jahim will be shown to those who see (see [[passage/quran-79-36|al-Nazi'at, verse 36]]), the sheikh says the levels of the Fire are levels and not only degrees, and that the torment is measured by the extent of the wrong done, from wronging a person to wronging a family, a village or a city. He then reads the verses that whoever transgressed and preferred the life of this world will find al-Jahim as his abode (see [[passage/quran-79-37|verse 37]], [[passage/quran-79-38|verse 38]], [[passage/quran-79-39|verse 39]]), and takes the example of one who postpones prayer for an appointment, with a report that Moses asked God who is most beloved to Him and was told it is the one who hastens to His pleasure. (needs_source: the report of Moses and the most beloved servant is told without a chain, and the name of the narrator is garbled in the transcript) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:42:00}
- Taking the verses on al-Jahim when it is kindled (see [[passage/quran-81-12|al-Takwir, verse 12]]) and on the wicked who are in al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-82-14|al-Infitar, verse 14]]), the sheikh defines wickedness as disregard for God's command and arrogance toward Him, and tells a story of an angel putting a question to a ruler about a servant who disobeys his master, to which the ruler answers that he would drown him. (needs_source: the story of the angel and the ruler is told without a name, a chain or a book, and the identification of the ruler is garbled) He adds the verses that they will surely see al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-102-6|al-Takathur, verse 6]]). The passage on Surat al-Mu'minun that follows is too garbled to summarise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:44:30}
- The sheikh turns to the lowest level, which he calls al-Hawiya (see [[passage/quran-101-9|al-Qari'a, verse 9]], [[passage/quran-101-10|verse 10]], [[passage/quran-101-11|verse 11]]) and links to the lowest depth of the Fire (see [[passage/quran-4-145|al-Nisa, verse 145]]). He lists as its people Pharaoh and his people (see [[passage/quran-40-46|Ghafir, verse 46]]), those of the table who denied after it was sent (see [[passage/quran-5-115|al-Ma'ida, verse 115]]) and the hypocrites, and tells how the disciples asked for a table (see [[passage/quran-5-112|al-Ma'ida, verse 112]]), that it came down daily with every kind of food and that they hoarded it and then disbelieved. (needs_source: the daily table and the hoarding are told as a story without a chain or a book) This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:47:00}
- On the hypocrites he says their leader prayed the five prayers behind the Prophet, paid almsgiving and fasted but hid unbelief, and that a hypocrite today would deny the label. (needs_source: the name of the leader of the hypocrites is garbled in the transcript and must be settled against a source before it is attached) He gives as the marks of the hypocrite the report that when he speaks he lies, when he promises he breaks his word and when he quarrels he is wicked, and discusses lying, saying the reports allow it only in war, in reconciling two people and in speech between spouses, and that a lie told to a child is recorded against the person, with a report of Aisha (see [[person/aisha|Aisha]]) and her relative. (needs_source: the report of Aisha and the lie told to a child is told in a garbled form, and the three exceptions are stated without a text) He adds that the angel of good deeds withdraws from a liar because of the smell of the lie, and that the worst thief is one who steals from his prayer. (needs_source: the withdrawal of the angel from the smell of a lie and the thief of prayer are told without a chain or a book) This is his reading, and the scholars differ on the permitted lies. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:51:30}
- The sheikh then distinguishes the Arabic word for abiding with and without the word for ever, and says that abiding without it is a limited abiding, for the sinful among the monotheists whom God brings out of the Fire for the testimony in their hearts, while abiding for ever belongs to the unbelievers. (needs_source: the verses the sheikh has in mind for the two forms of abiding are not identified in the transcript) The host asks for a summary of the seven levels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:53:30}
- On Sijjin, the sheikh recalls the verses that the record of the wicked is in Sijjin, an inscribed book (see [[passage/quran-83-7|al-Mutaffifin, verse 7]], [[passage/quran-83-8|verse 8]], [[passage/quran-83-9|verse 9]]), and that the stones sent on a people were marked (see [[passage/quran-11-83|Hud, verse 83]]), each stone marked with the name of the one it would strike. (needs_source: the claim that the stones bear the name of the one struck is stated without a text) He says scholars place Sijjin in the seventh earth, where a soul that the angels cannot lift sinks, passing each earth in turn. (needs_source: the journey of the soul through the earths to Sijjin is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:55:00}
- He describes Wayl as a valley of Jahannam from which the Fire itself seeks refuge a hundred times a day, (needs_source: the description of Wayl and the hundred refuges is told without a chain or a book) and links it to those who are careless of their prayer (see [[passage/quran-107-4|al-Ma'un, verse 4]], [[passage/quran-107-5|verse 5]]) and to those who deny the Day of Judgement (see [[passage/quran-83-10|al-Mutaffifin, verse 10]], [[passage/quran-83-11|verse 11]], [[passage/quran-83-12|verse 12]]), explaining that denial can be of word or of deed, as when a Muslim who accepts the Day does not pray, steals, drinks or wrongs people. A discussion of one who never prays, with a reference to Imam Ahmad and the annulment of a marriage, is garbled in the transcript and is left out. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:58:00}
- On the verses that the one who denies the Day is a transgressor and sinner who says the verses are tales of the ancients (see [[passage/quran-83-12|al-Mutaffifin, verse 12]], [[passage/quran-83-13|verse 13]]), he says that a sin makes a black spot and that, as spots accumulate, a veil covers the heart so that truth looks like falsehood (see [[passage/quran-83-14|verse 14]]). He then reads that they will be veiled from their Lord (see [[passage/quran-83-15|verse 15]]) and will enter al-Jahim (see [[passage/quran-83-16|verse 16]]), while the record of the righteous is in Illiyyin (see [[passage/quran-83-18|verse 18]]). (needs_source: the report of the black spot on the heart is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:59:00}
- The sheikh cites a hadith, which he ascribes to al-Bukhari, of the people of the Fire as every harsh, violent and arrogant man, and explains the terms for the rough and the hard of heart, adding that God punishes only the rebel who refuses to say there is no god but God. (needs_source: the hadith of the people of the Fire is ascribed to al-Bukhari in the talk and its wording has not been checked against the collection; the saying that only the rebel is punished is told without a chain) He recounts the report that those whose ears are filled with the praise of others are among the people of Paradise, and the report of a funeral at which the people praised the dead and the Prophet said it was due, because they are God's witnesses on earth. (needs_source: the report of the funeral and the praise of the people is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:01:30}
- He recalls the report that the hearts of those who enter Paradise are like the hearts of birds, and that a tender heart, once admired, is mocked in the present age, then the story that Bilal and Mu'adh ibn Jabal (see [[person/bilal-ibn-rabah|Bilal]], [[person/muadh-ibn-jabal|Mu'adh ibn Jabal]]) were asked about their prayer by the Prophet, Bilal saying he asked God for His pleasure and Paradise and refuge from His wrath and the Fire, and the Prophet saying that this was what they too were murmuring about. (needs_source: the hearts like the hearts of birds and the story of Bilal and Mu'adh are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:03:00}
- He adds that one who fasts a day for God is put seventy years from the Fire, and that the one who cuts the ties of kinship is kept from Paradise. (needs_source: the report of the fast and the seventy years and the report on cutting kinship are told without a chain or a book) He answers a question on why it is named Jahannam by saying the frowning of the Fire is figurative. Summarising the levels, he says Laza consumes the hands and feet, because the feet did not go to the mosque nor the hands to good, and that Saqar takes flesh and leaves bone, with its people those who did not pray, feed the poor, kept to the company of idle talk and denied the Day (see [[passage/quran-74-42|al-Muddaththir, verse 42]], [[passage/quran-74-43|verse 43]], [[passage/quran-74-44|verse 44]], [[passage/quran-74-45|verse 45]], [[passage/quran-74-46|verse 46]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:05:00}
- He says al-Hutama crushes the bones and reaches the heart, because the heart held pride, vanity and conceit, and that its people are those who attack others' honour, and he sums up al-Jahim as having embers each greater than the world. (needs_source: the claim that a single ember is greater than the world is stated without a text) He says that a believer who enters is brought out after a time, which he sets against the fifty-thousand-year day, while al-Hawiya admits no exit. This is his account and the scholars differ on duration. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:07:30}
- Asked about food, drink, clothing and places in each level, the sheikh says he has given general headings and hopes to write a detailed book. He mentions a valley of the Fire called the valley of regrets (see [[passage/quran-2-167|al-Baqara, verse 167]]), a place of psychological anguish where no fire is seen, which he places in the lowest level. (needs_source: the valley of regrets and its place in the lowest level are described without a text) He adds that Wayl for the cheats in measure (see [[passage/quran-83-1|al-Mutaffifin, verse 1]]) is more than a matter of merchants. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:10:30}
- Closing, the sheikh says that after about twenty years of finding it hard to complete this subject he finished with the help of his co-presenter, and that the next episode will turn to Paradise. As counsel he gives the saying of Sahl ibn Abdallah (see [[person/sahl-al-tustari|Sahl al-Tustari]]) to work for God according to one's need of Him, for the world according to one's stay in it, for the Fire according to one's endurance of it and for the hereafter according to one's stay in it. (needs_source: the saying of Sahl ibn Abdallah is attributed to him in the talk without a book) He adds that if the wrongdoer knew what the wronged holds against him he would hold back, and urges people to deal with one another by grace and not by strict justice only. The host ends with verses of poetry on wronging others. (needs_source: the verses of poetry at the close are not attributed to a poet in the talk) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep34 01:14:30}
## Episode 35: the keys of Paradise: contentment, mercy, prayer, patience, gratitude, remembrance, supplication, reflection and the keys of the Fire
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on the gardens of al-Firdaws as a lodging (see [[passage/quran-18-107|al-Kahf, verse 107]], [[passage/quran-18-108|verse 108]]), then says that souls breathe out after the episodes on the Fire and love to hear about Paradise and mercy. The sheikh observes that the people of Paradise were fearful in the world (see [[passage/quran-52-26|al-Tur, verse 26]], [[passage/quran-52-28|verse 28]]), unlike the one who was glad among his family over this life (see [[passage/quran-84-13|al-Inshiqaq, verse 13]]), and that the believer's fear is what leads him to Paradise. The host proposes speaking of the keys before entering the eight doors, and the episode is titled the keys of Paradise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:03:30}
- The host asks whether the words Paradise, forgiveness, good pleasure and al-Firdaws are synonyms, and the sheikh likens them to an orchard whose trees and fruits each have their own taste but belong to one whole. He ties mercy to mutual compassion, so that whoever shows mercy is shown mercy, makes forgiveness conditional on repentance and return, and says that between the servant and his Lord lies only an answered prayer. (needs_source: the report on answering the obedient and the sinful and the saying "if you did not sin" are told in the episode without a chain or a book) This is his account of the terms. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:06:30}
- He then explains good pleasure (ridwan) as God's being pleased with the servant in the world, then in the barzakh, then in the shade of the Throne on the Day of Resurrection, then through intercession, then on entering Paradise, and holds God's good pleasure to be the greatest of these (see [[passage/quran-9-72|al-Tawba, verse 72]]). He relates that God tells the people of Paradise that He has bestowed His pleasure on them and will not be angry with them after it. (needs_source: the report of God's address to the people of Paradise is told without a chain or a book) He also tells that Jibril came to the Prophet and said to give Abu Bakr greetings and tell him that his Lord was pleased with him and ask whether he was pleased, and that Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]) wept and said he was pleased with his Lord. (needs_source: the report of Jibril and Abu Bakr is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:08:00}
- On contentment with God, the sheikh says the servant is content with what his Lord has assigned him because the Creator knows his good best, that contentment is the highest degree of certainty and is followed by entrusting and then submission. He cites Zakariyya's prayer that his Lord make him well-pleasing (see [[passage/quran-19-6|Maryam, verse 6]]), the believer of Pharaoh's family who entrusted his affair (see [[passage/quran-40-44|Ghafir, verse 44]], [[passage/quran-40-45|verse 45]]), the prayer of Yunus in the belly of the fish, and God's command to Ibrahim to submit, to which he answered that he had submitted to the Lord of the worlds (see [[passage/quran-2-131|al-Baqara, verse 131]]). He tells that Umar ibn al-Khattab (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]) turned back from entering a land where plague had broken out and said he was fleeing from God's decree to God's decree. (needs_source: the report of Umar and the plague is told without a chain or a book) He also tells that al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) refused to pray against one who had wronged him, since that would add a second wrong. (needs_source: the report of al-Hasan al-Basri and the prayer against the wrongdoer is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:12:30}
- The host asks whether contentment is not weakness and reliance on others, and the sheikh answers that one who knows his affair is in God's hand gains self-respect (see [[passage/quran-63-8|al-Munafiqun, verse 8]]) and contentment descends as coolness and peace on his heart, adding that when the tree of certainty is fixed in a person's heart and he relies on his Lord, the whole world helps him. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:12:30}
- Asked about his love of the verses of mercy, the sheikh says it is true, and that the first blessing is that God made him a Muslim without his asking. He cites the aphorism of Ibn Ata Allah (see [[person/ibn-ata-allah-al-iskandari|Ibn Ata Allah al-Iskandari]]) that one who wants to open the door of hope should look at what comes from God to him, and one who wants to open the door of fear should look at what comes from him to God. (needs_source: the wording of the aphorism attributed to Ibn Ata Allah was not checked against his book) He relates a saying of Abu Hazim that his hope in God with his sins outweighs his hope with his deeds. (needs_source: the saying of Abu Hazim is told without a book and his name is not verified in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:15:00}
- The sheikh gives examples of the Prophet's mercy to creatures: the trunk that yearned for him, a camel that complained of being loaded beyond its strength so that he told its owner to fear God, and a doe that asked leave to nurse her young and return, whom the Prophet vouched for and who came back. (needs_source: the reports of the yearning trunk, the camel and the doe are told without a chain or a book, and part of the transcript is garbled) He cites the mission as a mercy to the worlds (see [[passage/quran-21-107|al-Anbiya, verse 107]]) and the saying that he was sent to ease and not to make hard. (needs_source: the saying that he was sent to ease is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:17:30}
- He relates a speech of the Prophet to the Ansar after the division of spoils, asking whether they were not content that others went home with plunder while they went home with the Messenger of God, at which the people wept and said they were content with him. (needs_source: the speech to the Ansar is told without a chain or a book, and the occasion is not named in the transcript) He describes the Prophet as the model of humanity that God shaped and a mass of mercy, seen in his plea of "my community, my community" on the Day of Gathering. (needs_source: the report of "my community, my community" is told without a chain) This is his description. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:20:00}
- The sheikh then turns to the keys in detail. Whoever prays the two cool prayers enters Paradise; those who walk to the mosques in darkness will have light on the Day of Resurrection; whoever prays on time is of the people of Paradise; whoever enumerates God's ninety-nine names enters Paradise, which he explains as acting by their meaning and not mere repetition. He tells of a man who removed a thorny branch from the Muslims' road and was forgiven, cites the saying not to despise any good deed even a sheep's hoof, explains the hoof as a small gift, and holds that exchanging gifts between people is not bribery. (needs_source: these reports on the two prayers, the walkers, the names, the thorny branch and the hoof are told without chains or books) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:22:30}
- He says that whoever goes to the mosque morning or evening, God prepares for him a lodging in Paradise each time, that if God punished the people of the earth only the people of the mosques would be spared, that God receives with joy the servant who heads for His house, and that one waiting for prayer is in prayer. (needs_source: the reports of the lodging, the sparing of the mosque people and God's welcome are told without chains or books) He mentions the supplication against worry, grief, incapacity, laziness, cowardice, miserliness, the burden of debt and the domination of men, says anyone who said it had his distress relieved, and urges people to turn to prayer when the heart is tight. (needs_source: the supplication against worry is told without a chain and its narrator is not named in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:26:00}
- He says that one who stays in God's company loses nothing, and that the door is open to one who sins, with no intermediary. He then tells the story of Sa'd ibn Abi Waqqas at al-Qadisiyya (see [[event/battle-of-al-qadisiyya|Battle of al-Qadisiyya]]): that he directed the battle lying on his stomach because of an ailment in his body, and that Abu Mihjan al-Thaqafi (see [[person/abu-mihjan-al-thaqafi|Abu Mihjan al-Thaqafi]]), who was bound in the tent over wine, asked Sa'd's wife to free him, rode out on Sa'd's mare and fought, then returned and bound himself as before. He adds that on the first day the Muslims had neglected the sunna of the tooth-stick, were beaten, and then restored it. (needs_source: the story of Abu Mihjan and the army's tooth-sticks is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is confused over the horses and details of the battle) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:29:00}
- He relates that Sa'd asked al-Bara ibn Malik (see [[person/al-bara-ibn-malik|al-Bara ibn Malik]]) to swear an oath on his Lord that He open the cities of al-Mada'in, that al-Bara prayed two rak'as and swore, and that the conquest came before sunset, relying on the saying of the dishevelled, dusty one in two worn garments who, were he to swear by God, God would fulfil it. He says Sa'd was one whose prayers were answered but judged al-Bara stronger on this occasion. (needs_source: the report of al-Bara, his oath and the conquest of al-Mada'in is told without a chain or a book, and the text is confused) He adds that Ibn Umar (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abdallah ibn Umar]]) used to gather the children of the quarter to say amen to his prayer on Friday nights. (needs_source: the report of Ibn Umar and the children is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:31:30}
- On the key of "there is no power and no strength but with God" the sheikh says it is a treasure from beneath the Throne that repels seventy kinds of affliction, the lightest being worry. He tells of the son of a Companion who was taken captive by the enemy until his shackles loosened and he escaped, taking their flock home. (needs_source: the merit of the phrase and the story of the captive son are told without a chain or a book, and part of the transcript is garbled) He holds that people wrongly think provision and victory lie in the hands of creatures when they lie in the hand of the Creator. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:33:30}
- He makes patience the key to victory, and tells that a man consoled Ibn Abbas (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdallah ibn Abbas]]) on the death of his father al-Abbas with words meaning be patient and we shall be patient with you, for the flock's patience follows the shepherd's, and that God's reward is better than al-Abbas and God is better than al-Abbas. (needs_source: the words of condolence to Ibn Abbas are told without a chain or a book, and their wording is confused in the transcript) He makes gratitude the key to increase (see [[passage/quran-14-7|Ibrahim, verse 7]]) and says gratitude is of the same kind as the blessing: knowledge is used to serve people, standing is used to serve people, and wealth is given in charity. He tells that Aisha (see [[person/aisha|Aisha]]) saw a crust of bread that had fallen, took it, ate it and said to treat God's blessings well. (needs_source: the report of Aisha and the crust is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:36:30}
- He links gratitude to the town that was secure and at ease with its provision coming in abundance and denied God's favours, so that God made it taste the garment of hunger and fear (see [[passage/quran-16-112|al-Nahl, verse 112]]). He relates that when the Muslims conquered Cyprus a Companion wept, whom the transcript names Abu Dharr, probably in error, saying that its people had been handed over, necks and lands, when they disobeyed God's commands. (needs_source: the report of the weeping at the conquest of Cyprus is told without a chain or a book, and the Companion's name is confused in the transcript) He mentions the victory of the Romans after their defeat within a few years (see [[passage/quran-30-2|al-Rum, verse 2]], [[passage/quran-30-3|verse 3]], [[passage/quran-30-4|verse 4]]) and says the whole matter is in God's hand. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:39:00}
- He makes remembrance the key to love, and cites the divine saying that God is with His servant when he remembers Him in himself and in company, and that whoever draws near by a handspan He draws near by an arm's length. (needs_source: the divine saying on remembrance is told in the episode without a chain or a book) He calls "there is no god but God" the basic key of Paradise. He makes piety the key to success, its first condition being the purifying of the self (see [[passage/quran-87-14|al-A'la, verse 14]]). He holds that children's memorising the Qur'an is a second life for their parents after death, and tells that the father is clothed in a robe of honour because of his son who memorised it, ending with the words God knows best. (needs_source: the report of the robe of honour for the father is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:41:00}
- He makes supplication the key to an answer, citing the verse "call upon Me and I shall answer you" and its warning to those too proud to worship (see [[passage/quran-40-60|Ghafir, verse 60]]), and holds that leaving supplication is pride and reliance on money, while allowing treatment by a doctor. He gives the manners of supplication, humility and privacy (see [[passage/quran-7-205|al-A'raf, verse 205]]), praising God and invoking blessings on the Prophet first, then believing that the answer comes when and as God wills. He relates that God is angry with one who does not ask Him. (needs_source: the report of God's anger at one who does not ask is told without a chain or a book) He warns that it is the devil who closes the door by saying the prayer was not answered. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:43:30}
- He makes reflection the key to faith, citing the verse on those who reflect on the creation of the heavens and the earth (see [[passage/quran-3-191|Al Imran, verse 191]]) and the verse "and in yourselves, will you not see?" (see [[passage/quran-51-21|al-Dhariyat, verse 21]]), and counts reflection as worship. He mentions al-Aqqad's book on reflection as an Islamic duty (see [[person/abbas-mahmoud-al-aqqad|Abbas Mahmoud al-Aqqad]]). He says a pharmacologist who works on lawful medicines serves Muslims, and holds that the devotion of a group of the community to study of the religion (see [[passage/quran-9-122|al-Tawba, verse 122]]) does not remove the need for a balance of specialisations. These are his opinions. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:47:00}
- Next is the key of entering upon God, love in Him. He relates that the Prophet sat with Abu Bakr, Umar, Uthman and Ali, and said to Abu Bakr that no one had done them a favour that he had not repaid except him; to Umar that he had been hard on them in the pre-Islamic time until God favoured him with guidance; and to Uthman that had he a third daughter he would marry her to him (see [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]]); that he told Abu Musa (see [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]]) to give Uthman news of Paradise with a trial to befall him; and that Jibril commanded him to marry Ali (see [[person/ali-ibn-abi-talib|Ali ibn Abi Talib]]) to Fatima (see [[person/fatima|Fatima]]). (needs_source: the reports of the Prophet's gathering with the four and of Ali's marriage are told without chains or books, and the wording is confused in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:50:00}
- He makes steadfastness a key, relating that a man asked the Prophet for a saying on Islam about which he would ask no one after him, and was told to say I believe in God and then be steadfast, and he cites the verse on those who say our Lord is God and then stand firm (see [[passage/quran-41-30|Fussilat, verse 30]], [[passage/quran-41-31|verse 31]]). (needs_source: the report of the steadfastness saying is told without a chain and its narrator is not named in the transcript) He tells that Usayd ibn Hudayr (see [[person/usayd-ibn-hudayr|Usayd ibn Hudayr]]) was reciting when tranquillity descended and that the Prophet told him it was tranquillity sent down for his recitation. (needs_source: the report of Usayd and the tranquillity is told without a chain or a book) He says hearts can be veiled by sins. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:54:30}
- He makes kindness to creation the key to mercy, relating the man who gave water to a thirsty dog and was forgiven. (needs_source: the report of the man and the thirsty dog is told without a chain or a book) He makes effort and seeking forgiveness the key to provision (see [[passage/quran-71-10|Nuh, verse 10]], [[passage/quran-71-11|verse 11]]), refers to the master supplication for forgiveness without completing its text in the transcript, and holds that the best supplication is what comes from the heart. (needs_source: the master supplication for forgiveness is alluded to without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:56:00}
- He relates of Ibn Masud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdallah ibn Masud]]) a bedouin who said in his love for God that if he had a donkey he would let it graze with his Lord's donkey, another who asked God for rain, and a prayer he made over a thief that God bless him if he was needy and make it his last sin if he was not. (needs_source: the reports of Ibn Masud, the two bedouins and the thief are told without chains or books, and the transcript is confused) He says Abu Dharr (see [[person/abu-dharr-al-ghifari|Abu Dharr al-Ghifari]]) walks alone, dies alone and will be raised as a community by himself. (needs_source: the report of Abu Dharr is told without a chain) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:58:00}
- Then the key of preparing for the hereafter is watchfulness and effort, and he cites the saying that if you reach the morning do not wait for the evening and if you reach the evening do not wait for the morning. (needs_source: the saying on the morning and the evening is told without a chain or a named narrator) He says the key to every good is watchfulness for the hereafter and the key to every evil is love of the world and long hopes. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:59:00}
- He counts the keys of the Fire as associating others with God, pride and turning away from God, with wine among the major sins, and relates that the drinker is given the clay of khabal to drink, the juice of the people of Hell, while the wine of Paradise has no intoxication (see [[passage/quran-37-47|al-Saffat, verse 47]]). (needs_source: the report of the clay of khabal is told without a chain or a book) He counts the loosing of the gaze as a key to indecency, hypocrisy, aggression and lying as keys to sin, and miserliness and cutting kinship as the key to avarice, distinguishing miserliness from avarice. These are his opinions, and scholars differ over the limits of the two terms. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:03:00}
- At the verse on God's reaching the right way while some ways turn aside (see [[passage/quran-16-9|al-Nahl, verse 9]]) he recalls the straight path and God's warning against the other ways (see [[passage/quran-6-153|al-An'am, verse 153]]) and says that crooked ways are made by people. A passage on work practice and the age is garbled, with one sentence repeated, and is not summarised. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:04:30}
- The host closes by asking what the coming episodes will cover, and the sheikh lists the doors of Paradise, its soil, ceiling, springs, rivers, trees, palaces, food, drink, light, the description of its people, the houris, those given its good news, its degrees, the verses and the hadiths about it, ending with the vision of the Noble Face. The host asks for a supplication in an hour of answer, the sheikh prays for forgiveness, guidance, a good end, the highest Firdaws and the Prophet's company, and the host announces that the next episode is on the doors and degrees of Paradise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep35 01:07:00}
## Episode 36: the doors and degrees of Paradise, the pillars of its bliss and the verses on its people: repentance, restraining anger, pardon, sincerity and truthfulness
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on the righteous in bliss on couches, given a sealed wine whose seal is musk (see [[passage/quran-83-22|al-Mutaffifin, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-83-26|verse 26]]), then welcomes the sheikh. They say that after the episode on the keys of Paradise they now come to its doors, and that the scent of Paradise should lead to action and not only to listening. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:02:00}
- Asked whether the road to Paradise is easy or hard, the sheikh answers that it is hard. The host asks whether the doors are real or a figure of speech, and the sheikh points to their mention in al-Zumar (see [[passage/quran-39-73|al-Zumar, verse 73]]) and says that several pillars must be set out before the doors. His first pillar is that Paradise is God's merchandise, and he relates the saying that God's merchandise is dear and that God's merchandise is Paradise. (needs_source: the saying on God's merchandise is told without a chain or a book) He adds that God planted a garden of Paradise with His own hand. (needs_source: the report that God planted a garden with His hand is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:03:30}
- His second pillar is that the greatest bliss rests on four supports: no fear of the future and no grief over the past, what they wish given by saying glory be to You (see [[passage/quran-50-35|Qaf, verse 35]], [[passage/quran-10-10|Yunus, verse 10]]), and looking at the face of God (see [[passage/quran-75-22|al-Qiyama, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-75-23|verse 23]]), which he calls the highest station and for which faces are made radiant by obedience. He relates that before entering the people of Paradise cross the basin of al-Kawthar, drink, and are cleansed of rancour (see [[passage/quran-15-47|al-Hijr, verse 47]]), so that even one who was wronged feels no resentment towards the wrongdoer. They then praise God who removed grief and gave them a lasting home where no toil or weariness touches them (see [[passage/quran-35-34|Fatir, verse 34]], [[passage/quran-35-35|verse 35]]) and where they hear no idle or sinful talk, only peace (see [[passage/quran-56-25|al-Waqi'a, verse 25]], [[passage/quran-56-26|verse 26]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:06:30}
- The sheikh next addresses women, saying that a woman in Paradise will marry only one with whom she is pleased and is not forced to marry a man who made her life hard in the world.
