He tells that Umm Salama (see [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]]) asked about a woman who had more than one husband and was told that she chooses the best of them in character. (needs_source: the report of Umm Salama's question and the answer is told without a chain or a book) He says there are two kinds of women in Paradise, those whom God created there and the women of the world, and that the earthly wife is raised above the houris by her deeds (see [[concept/hur-al-ayn|houris]]). He relates a description that one houri, were she to spit in the salt sea, would make it sweet. (needs_source: the description of the houri and the sea is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is partly garbled) He also says that if a believer's wife harms him in the world, a houri tells her to fear God, since he will soon be with them. (needs_source: the saying of the houri to the believer's wife is told without a chain or a book) These are his views, and the status of women and houris in Paradise is a topic on which scholars write at length and differ in emphasis. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:10:00}
- On the doors, he says they are real: a lean racehorse would cross the width of the door in forty years, and the doors stand open once the Prophet has been the first to be admitted and the door has been opened to him. (needs_source: the report of the width of the door and of the opening of Paradise to the Prophet is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled at the knocking) He says there are eight doors but that Paradise has degrees, each with its own door, and that between one degree and the next lies the distance between heaven and earth. He tells that a man asked the Prophet about his son who had died and was told that his son had reached the highest Firdaws, the Prophet's own degree, whose roof is the Throne of the Merciful, and he urges listeners to ask God for al-Firdaws al-A'la (see [[concept/firdaws|al-Firdaws]], [[concept/darajat-al-jannah|degrees of Paradise]], [[concept/abwab-al-jannah|doors of Paradise]]). (needs_source: the report of the man's son and the highest Firdaws is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:14:30}
- To see who enters, he turns to the Book and reads the verse on those who believe and do righteous deeds and have gardens beneath which rivers flow (see [[passage/quran-2-25|al-Baqara, verse 25]]). He holds that faith has no standing without righteous deeds and that a heart full of remembrance shows in the limbs, and he rejects the claim that faith is a matter between a person and his Lord alone. This is his view, and scholars discuss at length how deeds relate to faith. On the fruits that look alike but taste different (see [[concept/paradise-fruits|fruits of Paradise]]) he says the colour is the same and the taste differs, and that there is no sweat, menstruation, spittle or mucus in Paradise, only a scent like musk, and the spouses are purified. (needs_source: the description of Paradise as free of bodily waste is told without a chain or a book) He leaves the question of relations between spouses to God's knowledge. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:17:00}
- At the verse on those killed in God's way being alive (see [[passage/quran-2-154|al-Baqara, verse 154]]) the host asks who is a martyr. The sheikh says the first degree belongs to those killed in battle, and names others counted as martyrs in the reports: one who dies in a fire, of the stomach, of plague or crushed by a beast, and the host adds that a man who goes to work and dies is not among them. (needs_source: the list of the kinds of martyr is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is partly garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:18:00}
- He relates a story of Sa'id ibn Zayd (see [[person/said-ibn-zayd|Sa'id ibn Zayd]]), whom the transcript calls one of the ten promised Paradise and the husband of Fatima bint al-Khattab: that his brother made him sleep in the tent, that he cried out in his sleep, and that on waking he told of a dream in which a herald under the Throne announced God's pleasure with him, and two riders with a third took him to Paradise, where he saw women like moons and was told they were servants, then met three, two like the full moon and one like the sun, who said she was his wife in Paradise and that he would be with them soon. He then says that within about three days of the dream, while fasting on a Thursday, a stray arrow struck him at sunset, and his brother asked where the fast-breaking was; he answered that it was at the seat of truth with a Sovereign able to do all things (see [[passage/quran-54-55|al-Qamar, verse 55]]). (needs_source: the story of Sa'id, the dream and the arrow is told without a chain or a book, the transcript is garbled and the details of his death were not checked against his biography) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:23:00}
- At the call to hasten to forgiveness and a garden as wide as the heavens and the earth (see [[passage/quran-3-133|Al Imran, verse 133]], [[passage/quran-57-21|al-Hadid, verse 21]]) he says the first step is repentance (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]), which is renewed again and again. He tells that a man asked the Prophet where the Fire is if Paradise is as wide as the heavens and the earth, and was told that it is where God wills, as the night comes where the day was, and that the man asked Abu Bakr and Umar the same and was answered the same. (needs_source: the report of the question about the Fire is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is confused over who was asked) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:24:30}
- He explains the next verse, on those who spend in ease and in hardship, restrain anger and pardon people (see [[passage/quran-3-134|Al Imran, verse 134]]): to spend at the moment of gratitude and the moment of trial, and to forgive as Abu Bakr did with Mistah (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/mistah-ibn-uthatha|Mistah ibn Uthatha]]), when God asked whether they did not love that God forgive them (see [[passage/quran-24-22|al-Nur, verse 22]]). He compares anger to a water skin that fills and swells and must be tied at its mouth so that it does not pour out at once on those in front of one, and says restraining anger is a quality God does not deny the pious (see [[concept/kazm-al-ghayz|restraining anger]]). He relates a saying that one who is asked to be pleased and is not pleased is a devil, and one who is asked and is not angry is a donkey. (needs_source: the saying on the one who is asked to be pleased is told without an author or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:27:30}
- He says anger is from the devil and the devil is from fire, and that a person in anger should first stop and think, and he speaks of the heat of anger in the chest. A passage on what to do when angry is garbled and repeated and is not summarised. He applies this to divorce, saying from his own experience of more than thirty years that many divorces come from anger, and advising that when two sensible people cannot continue and arbitration has failed, they part in kindness as they entered, without anger, harm or blows, and that a father or mother who tells a child not to speak to a divorced parent should be dissuaded. These are his counsels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:31:00}
- On pardon he says it must be from strength and not from weakness, and recalls the Prophet at the conquest of Makka, when he asked the Qurayshi what they thought he would do with them, they answered a noble brother and the son of a noble brother, and he said as Yusuf said to his brothers that there is no blame on them today (see [[passage/quran-12-92|Yusuf, verse 92]], [[event/conquest-of-mecca|conquest of Makka]]), after which, he says, two hundred of them declared the testimony of faith. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet's words at the conquest and the number of two hundred are told without a chain or a book) He holds that character has predatory, devilish, earthly and angelic strands, and that faith feeds the angelic. He tells of a servant who recited the verse of restraining anger, pardon and the good-doers to his master phrase by phrase, and the master answered each and set him free; the master's name is garbled in the transcript and is not given. (needs_source: the story of the servant and the verse is told without a chain or a book, and the master's name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:34:00}
- He tells of a bedouin who tied his camel outside an orchard and slept; the camel got loose and spoiled the trees, the owner struck it dead, the bedouin killed the owner, and when he was to be punished he asked leave to settle his affairs, and a Companion stood surety for him; the man returned at the last moment and the heirs of the dead pardoned him, saying that they feared it would be said that the people of pardon had gone. (needs_source: the story of the bedouin, the surety and the pardon is told without a chain or a book, the surety's name is uncertain in the transcript and the text is garbled) He calls this the school of Muhammad, in which one man shows chivalry, another loyalty and the heirs pardon, and says the community must return to it and that preachers should concentrate on training conduct in mosques, universities and schools. This is his appeal. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:37:30}
- At the verse on those who after a sin remember God, ask forgiveness and do not persist (see [[passage/quran-3-135|Al Imran, verse 135]], [[passage/quran-3-136|verse 136]]) he holds that one who seeks forgiveness is not persisting even if he returns a hundred times, and relates a divine report in which God tells His angels each time that His servant knows he has a Lord who forgives, and bears witness that He has forgiven him. (needs_source: the divine report of the servant who sins and seeks forgiveness again and again is told without a chain or a book) He likens the sinner to a sick child who must be given the medicine kindly, and tells of a man who heard a verse on the hearts of the believers submitting while on a rooftop and turned back (see [[passage/quran-57-16|al-Hadid, verse 16]]); the man's name is garbled in the transcript and is not given. (needs_source: the story of the man and the verse is told without a chain or a book, and his name is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:41:00}
- He quotes an aphorism that God may open for you the door of deeds and close the door of acceptance, and may let a sin befall you so that you repent and it becomes a way to arrival, without naming its author, and relates the saying that if you did not sin God would remove you and bring a people who sin and seek forgiveness. (needs_source: the aphorism and the saying are told without an author, chain or book) He stresses good company, and says that one who works in a place against the law should ask whether sons of the Companions would approve of it, and should know that if he waits and repents he will be given the same sum lawfully, since God makes a way out for the one who fears Him and provides from where he does not reckon (see [[passage/quran-65-2|al-Talaq, verse 2]], [[passage/quran-65-3|verse 3]]): plant the tree, then take the fruit. He relates that Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf (see [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]]) said that he was sure that if he lifted a stone he would find gold beneath it. (needs_source: the report of Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf's certainty is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:44:00}
- He turns to the chambers of Paradise whose outside is seen from within and whose inside from without (see [[concept/ghuraf-al-jannah|chambers of Paradise]]), prepared, he relates, for the one who feeds, softens speech, keeps up fasting and prays by night while people sleep. (needs_source: the report of the chambers and their people is told without a chain or a book) He explains feeding as feeding one's wife and children lawfully, softness of speech as meeting a brother with a smiling face, fasting as three days of each month, and says some Muslims frown as if gravity were piety, while the Prophet smiled until his back teeth showed and the Companions played with one another yet were men in battle. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet's smile and of the Companions throwing melons is told without a chain or a book) He recalls that the Prophet, given a choice between two matters, chose the easier unless it was sinful, and tells preachers to give ease and not hardship, glad tidings and not repulsion, against making religion a collection of the most cautious opinions. (needs_source: the saying on choosing the easier of two matters and the command to give ease are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:47:30}
- He reads the verse on those who emigrated, were driven out, harmed in God's cause, fought and were killed (see [[passage/quran-3-195|Al Imran, verse 195]], [[passage/quran-4-100|al-Nisa, verse 100]]) and says emigration today means leaving sin for obedience, passivity for work and idleness for diligence. He says that seeing non-Muslims hold the reins of civilisation stirs his zeal, and that he wants a renaissance of the community with rulers and ruled together, opposing no one. These are his opinions. He then lists early sufferings: that Umm Salama's son was pulled by his arm when she emigrated (see [[person/umm-salama|Umm Salama]]); that Zaynab, the Prophet's daughter, bled for six years, as he tells it, after being cut down from her camel (see [[person/zaynab-bint-muhammad|Zaynab bint Muhammad]]); that Sumayya was the first woman martyr (see [[person/sumayya-bint-khayyat|Sumayya bint Khayyat]]); that Ibn Masud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdallah ibn Masud]]) was beaten for reciting al-Rahman before Quraysh; and that a Companion captured by Quraysh said he would not wish the Prophet pricked by a thorn in his place, the name being garbled in the transcript. (needs_source: these reports of Umm Salama's son, Zaynab, Sumayya, Ibn Masud and the captive Companion are told without chains or books, and the transcript is garbled and mixes details) He mentions that at al-Hudaybiyya the Prophet sent Uthman to Quraysh when Umar was judged too stern for the task (see [[event/treaty-of-hudaybiyya|Treaty of al-Hudaybiyya]], [[person/uthman-ibn-affan|Uthman ibn Affan]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:52:00}
- A passage in which he speaks about egoism and a remark attributed to al-Hasan al-Basri is garbled and not summarised. He then reads that obedience to God and His Messenger is the frame for entering Paradise (see [[passage/quran-4-13|al-Nisa, verse 13]]) and the verse on secret talk with nothing good in it except charity, kindness and reconciliation between people (see [[passage/quran-4-114|al-Nisa, verse 114]]), and says all of these must be done for God alone. He holds that God accepts only the deed that is sincere and correct, which he explains as done for God alone and in line with the Book and the Sunna (see [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]). (needs_source: the saying that God accepts only the sincere and correct deed is told without a chain or an author) On the verse of the believing doer of good (see [[passage/quran-4-124|al-Nisa, verse 124]], [[passage/quran-3-85|Al Imran, verse 85]]) he says the believer is the one who believes the deed is for God alone. This is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:55:00}
- He tells that the Prophet, when the daughter of Hatim al-Tai was among the captives, said that had her father been a Muslim they would have asked mercy for him, and freed the captives for his sake (see [[person/safana-bint-hatim|Safana bint Hatim]]), and that the Prophet was sent to perfect noble character, which her father loved. (needs_source: the report of Hatim's daughter and the saying on perfecting character are told without a chain or a book) On marriage he says one should accept in a suitor both religion and character, and that gloom of countenance is a defect even in the devout, citing the travel supplication against gloom of sight. He adds that the Companions joked, and that Nu'ayman used to play pranks, once selling a man as his own slave to a stranger, and the Prophet smiled when told. (needs_source: the report of Nu'ayman and the prank is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled and loops) He says the Prophet's conduct towards the Companions was a complete message and, as Aisha said, his character was the Qur'an. (needs_source: the saying of Aisha on the Prophet's character is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:58:00}
- At the verse on the day when the truthful benefit from their truth (see [[passage/quran-5-119|al-Ma'ida, verse 119]], [[passage/quran-33-8|al-Ahzab, verse 8]]) he says truthfulness is in deed as well as word (see [[concept/al-sidq-truthfulness|truthfulness]]), and tells how al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]), nursed in his infancy by Umm Salama, was brought to Umar and prayed over, and relates a saying of his that he learned four things: that his provision will not be taken by another, that his deed will not be done by another, that God sees him at every moment, and that death awaits him. (needs_source: the report of al-Hasan al-Basri's infancy and the four statements are told without a chain or a book) He speaks of truthfulness in giving, of bearing hurt as Abu Bakr did with Mistah, and of patience (see [[passage/quran-20-132|Ta-Ha, verse 132]], [[concept/sabr|patience]]), quoting lines of verse by a poet the transcript names as Abu Kabir al-Hudhali (see [[person/abu-kabir-al-hudhali|Abu Kabir al-Hudhali]]) and a couplet that this world is an hour to be made obedience and the soul is greedy and trained by contentment. (needs_source: the verse and its attribution to Abu Kabir al-Hudhali are not checked and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:03:00}
- He reads that the people of Paradise will say praise be to God who guided them to this (see [[passage/quran-7-43|al-A'raf, verse 43]]) and notes the courtesy of not boasting of one's knowledge or teaching. He stresses that none enters Paradise by deeds alone, since the door is opened by mercy and the degrees are divided by deeds, and that when the Prophet was asked if even he did not, he said not even he unless God covered him in mercy. (needs_source: the report that no one enters Paradise by his deeds and the saying on dividing the degrees are told without a chain or a book) He mentions the long shade in which a rider travels a thousand years (see [[passage/quran-56-30|al-Waqi'a, verse 30]]) and says the Prophet spoke of how large it is. (needs_source: the report of the rider in the shade is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:05:00}
- At the verse on the first forerunners of the emigrants and helpers and those who followed them in good (see [[passage/quran-9-100|al-Tawba, verse 100]]) he says that when worries crowd in one should, after seeking forgiveness, live in the world of the Companions, and that their mention in a gathering relieves hearts. (needs_source: the saying that the mention of the Companions relieves hearts is told without an author or a book) He adds that one who sees the trials of others finds his own lighter, and that following the righteous predecessors well is the path of those with whom God is pleased, who gain the great triumph. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:08:00}
- He relates that God will say to the people of Paradise that He will cast His pleasure on them and never be angry with them, which the scholars take as the meaning of the verse that God's pleasure is greater (see [[passage/quran-9-72|al-Tawba, verse 72]]). (needs_source: the report of God's address to the people of Paradise is told without a chain or a book) He explains the verse on those who do good having the best and more (see [[passage/quran-10-26|Yunus, verse 26]]) as the best being Paradise and the increase, on one reading of some commentators, the vision of God's face, and says it also means excellence in work and giving more than is due (see [[concept/ihsan|ihsan]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:10:30}
- He reads the verse on those who are patient seeking God's face, keep up prayer, spend secretly and openly and repel evil with good (see [[passage/quran-13-22|al-Ra'd, verse 22]]), and the verse that the Qur'an guides to what is straightest and gives glad tidings to believers who do righteous deeds (see [[passage/quran-17-9|al-Isra, verse 9]], [[passage/quran-20-75|Ta-Ha, verse 75]]), insisting that faith is joined to righteous deeds and that people differ in degree by them. He cites avoiding the major sins as the condition for the covering of lesser ones (see [[passage/quran-4-31|al-Nisa, verse 31]]) and, at the verse that doers of good are not wronged even by a speck (see [[passage/quran-4-124|al-Nisa, verse 124]]), explains the thread in a date stone, the speck on its back and the husk, saying that a person owns not even these except by God's leave. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:13:30}
- The host asks for a supplication and the sheikh prays that no sin be left unforgiven, no sick person unhealed, no hardship unmade easy, no worry, grief or tight heart unrelieved, that God grant repentance before death, ease at its moment and honour after it, righteous spouses for daughters and sons, the protection of the Muslim lands, and the return of al-Aqsa Mosque and the lands of Islam. The host thanks him, bids the viewers farewell and the opening verses are recited once more. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep36 01:15:30}
## Episode 37: the cup of the righteous in Surat al-Insan: servants of God, feeding the poor, the orphan and the captive, fear of the day, silk, vessels of silver, the sale of selves and wealth, and the gardens of Eden
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on Paradise brought near to the pious (see [[passage/quran-50-31|Qaf, verse 31]], [[passage/quran-50-34|verse 34]], [[passage/quran-50-35|verse 35]]), then asks whether the viewers wish to be among the people of Paradise, who have set traits, many roads and eight doors, some entering by all doors and some by one. He welcomes the sheikh, who says that after the previous episode, on the roads and deeds that lead to Paradise, they now walk within it, in its bliss, dress and drink. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:02:30}
- The sheikh says a true believer must know that God disturbs his life in the world as a mercy, through trials, illness, narrow provision, harm from people and talk against his honour, so that he leans on nothing and turns to God in every difficulty. He adds that the most tried are the prophets and the friends of God, who are the closest to Him, and that trial is no sign of distance from God. He urges that complaint be made to God alone, as Ya'qub did (see [[passage/quran-12-86|Yusuf, verse 86]]), and says that knowledge is fear and not the quantity of narration. (needs_source: the saying that knowledge is not the abundance of narration but fear is told without an author or a book) Trials, he says, keep a "hot line" between the heart and the doors of heaven, while ease makes a person forget and transgress (see [[passage/quran-96-6|al-Alaq, verse 6]], [[passage/quran-96-7|verse 7]], [[passage/quran-18-24|al-Kahf, verse 24]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:06:30}
- He tells of the father of Jabir (see [[person/jabir-ibn-abdullah|Jabir ibn Abdullah]]), who set out for battle though lame, was told he was excused, and went; that the Prophet saw Jabir weeping at his father's head and said that God had spoken to the father without a veil, asked him to wish, and that he wished to be returned to the world to be killed again; and that God answered that it is decreed that none return, but that He would let him look at His face morning and evening (see [[passage/quran-50-29|Qaf, verse 29]]). He says this man had a knowledge others lacked, namely that God showed him things as they truly are. (needs_source: the story of the lame man, Jabir's father and God's speaking to him is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled and mixes details) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:09:00}
- He names patience as the first trait of the people of Paradise, since it is half of faith (see [[concept/sabr|patience]]), and turns to the verse on the righteous drinking from a cup whose mixture is camphor (see [[passage/quran-76-5|al-Insan, verse 5]]). He explains al-abrar as those who are true in their dealing with God, or, as some say, those who fulfil covenants, the first being the covenant with God, which is kindness to His creatures whom He supports, so that people should be merciful to one another. On the word cup he says the Arabs use it for a cup full of wine, and quotes the opening line of a pre-Islamic poem, named in the transcript as by Amr ibn Kulthum, though the name is garbled (see [[person/amr-ibn-kulthum|Amr ibn Kulthum]]); the wine of Paradise differs from that of the world. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:10:30}
- He relates that neither a habitual drinker of wine nor a dayyuth will enter Paradise, and that the Prophet defined the dayyuth as one who sees ugliness in his family and approves it. (needs_source: the report on the one addicted to wine and the dayyuth is told without a chain or a book) He laments a loss of protective jealousy among some people, says he said in a lecture that he wished it would reach the level of Abu Jahl (see [[person/abu-jahl|Abu Jahl]]), and recalls an exchange between Abu Jahl and his wife, which the transcript garbles and which is not summarised. He recites the line of the poet Antara (see [[person/antara-ibn-shaddad|Antara ibn Shaddad]]) on lowering his gaze until his neighbour is hidden, and says the Prophet was sent among the Arabs to complete the noble traits they already had, such as chivalry, manliness, courage and keeping covenants and neighbours. (needs_source: the saying that the Prophet was sent to complete noble character is told without a chain or a book) These are his views on society. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:13:30}
- On the camphor of the cup (see [[passage/quran-76-5|al-Insan, verse 5]]) he says it is a fragrance and not a liquid, and that the drink comes from a spring from which the servants of God drink, causing it to gush forth (see [[passage/quran-76-6|verse 6]]). He contrasts servants of God with servants of the world, citing the verse on servants sent against Israel (see [[passage/quran-17-5|al-Isra, verse 5]]) and the saying that wretched is the slave of the dinar and the dirham. (needs_source: the saying on the slave of the dinar and the dirham is told without a chain or a book) He says that wishing to show the trace of God's blessing in good clothes is not arrogance, which is rejecting the truth and looking down on people, and that a person who puts on a garment knowing it is God's provision is forgiven past sins. (needs_source: the report on showing God's blessing, on arrogance, and on the garment and forgiveness is told without a chain or a book) He adds that one should not forget one's share of the world while building for the next (see [[passage/quran-28-77|al-Qasas, verse 77]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:16:00}
- He says a servant in Paradise walks with a staff of gold set with emerald and strikes the ground, whose soil is pure musk, and springs gush from it, and that Paradise is in tiers, the distance between a level's floor and ceiling being seventy years of travel, so that a person may roam the upper levels as he roamed in the world with his thought, his heart in love of God and in fear of Him. (needs_source: the description of the staff, the springs and the seventy years between levels is told without a chain or a book) He tells that when God created Paradise He told it to speak, it said that the believers have succeeded, and God said it was blessed as the abode of kings, and he adds that the poor man in the world to whom none gave attention but who, if he swore by God, would be answered, is a king of the world to come. (needs_source: the report of Paradise's speech and of the poor man whose oath is fulfilled is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:19:00}
- Turning to the traits of the servants of God, he reads that they fulfil vows and fear a day whose evil is widespread (see [[passage/quran-76-7|al-Insan, verse 7]]) and that they feed the needy, the orphan and the captive for love of food (see [[passage/quran-76-8|verse 8]]), saying they love the food and still give it, which is better than giving what one does not love. He distinguishes the needy (miskin) as the one whom need has stilled so that he does not ask, taken by the ignorant for rich through his restraint, from the poor (faqir), who has nothing and asks. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:20:30}
- A stretch of the audio loops and is not summarised. He then relates that Ibn Abbas (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdallah ibn Abbas]]) said the poor enter Paradise half a day, which is five hundred years, before the rich, because the rich are held to account, and that he tested the poor by asking who has breakfast, then lunch, then a second garment, concluding that one who has these is not poor. (needs_source: the report of Ibn Abbas on the poor entering before the rich and his test of poverty is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says the grateful rich person and the patient poor person are both praised, and that the grateful rich has further doors of reward in spending, which is his opinion. He adds that Islam gives to the poor in need and not the professional beggar, and that the orphan in the verse is the needy orphan, though an orphan may be rich. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:24:30}
- He relates that the Prophet said that he and the guardian of an orphan will be together in Paradise, even the orphan of a non-Muslim, in the highest Firdaws (see [[concept/firdaws|al-Firdaws]], [[concept/yatim-fiqh|the orphan in Islamic law]]). (needs_source: the report on the guardian of the orphan and on the orphan of a non-Muslim is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He advises people to sponsor an orphan personally, paying a share of their pocket money, hosting him on holidays and visiting him, and says that giving to institutions that prove false does not forfeit the reward, since the reward goes with the intention. He says the captive in the verse is any prisoner, naming those held in Palestine and Iraq, and that one should help with food, prayer, bearing his problem and making him feel that good remains in the world. These are his appeals. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:26:30}
- On those without wealth to give, he relates that the poor Companions came to the Prophet saying the rich pray, fast and give, and that he taught them to glorify, praise and magnify God thirty-three times each after prayer, after which the rich did the same and the poor said so again and he answered that this is God's bounty. (needs_source: the report of the poor and the rich and the thirty-three glorifications is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is partly garbled) He adds a tale from the Children of Israel: in a year of famine a man looked at the mountains of sand and wished they were food to give out, and God told Musa to tell His servant that He had accepted his charity (see [[person/musa|Musa]]). (needs_source: the tale of the man in the famine and Musa is told without a chain or a book) He draws the lesson that intention decides everything, that God has no need of a partner in a deed, and that the saying that actions are by intentions opens the books of hadith, which he says Ibn Hajar in Fath al-Bari counts as one of four pillars (see [[concept/niyya|intention]], [[person/ibn-hajar-al-asqalani|Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani]], [[work/fath-al-bari|Fath al-Bari]]). (needs_source: the saying that actions are by intentions and the claim that Ibn Hajar counts it among four pillars are not checked against the text) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:28:30}
- At the words that they feed for God's face and wish for neither reward nor thanks (see [[passage/quran-76-9|al-Insan, verse 9]]) he criticises those who give and then wait for thanks, and recalls that Abu Bakr was angry with Mistah yet swore to spend on him more (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]], [[person/mistah-ibn-uthatha|Mistah ibn Uthatha]], [[passage/quran-24-22|al-Nur, verse 22]]). He says each draught of anger, patience and harm swallowed in the world makes one drink in the hereafter from the cup mixed with ginger or camphor, as one reaps what one sows. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:30:30}
- He explains the fear of a frowning, distressing day (see [[passage/quran-76-10|al-Insan, verse 10]]) as the fear of a bad end, of those wronged who will fall on one, and of the angel of death coming before repentance (see [[concept/khawf|fear]]), and relates that Aisha asked the Prophet about those who give what they give with hearts trembling (see [[passage/quran-23-60|al-Mu'minun, verse 60]], [[person/aisha|Aisha]]) and was told they pray and fast and fear that it will not be accepted. (needs_source: the report of Aisha's question on the verse is told without a chain or a book) He tells that when the verse on the pure drink was recited before Sahl al-Tustari he moved his lips as if tasting it (see [[person/sahl-al-tustari|Sahl al-Tustari]]). (needs_source: the story of Sahl al-Tustari and the verse is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:32:30}
- Explaining that the believer's deed is like a letter to a great man, written with care, he relates that the Prophet told Abu Musa al-Ash'ari (see [[person/abu-musa-al-ashari|Abu Musa al-Ash'ari]]) that he had been given one of the pipes of the house of Dawud, and that Abu Musa answered that had he known he was heard he would have embellished it. (needs_source: the report on Abu Musa's recitation is told without a chain or a book) A tale of a woman and the mispronunciation of a prayer at Badr is garbled and not summarised. He then appeals to those responsible for education to set one day a week on which only classical Arabic is spoken in schools, which is his proposal. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:34:30}
- On the next verse, that God guards them from the evil of that day and gives them radiance and joy (see [[passage/quran-76-11|al-Insan, verse 11]]), he says the believer lives between two fears, of a past deed and of a future he does not know, and should do his work well and see his own good deeds as little. He notes the severity of a day itself being called frowning and says joy shows on the face of one at peace with God, and tells of an imam of the Sunnis flogged over the creation of the Qur'an who smiled and said that the others saw the flogger while he saw the Lord of servants; the name is garbled in the transcript and is not given. (needs_source: the story of the imam who smiled under the lash is told without a chain or a book, and his name is garbled) He cites the poet Iliya Abu Madi on not making one's life bitter. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:37:30}
- He says the believer should be worried if some days pass without trial, as a sign that his account with God needs review, and cites the verse to enjoin prayer on one's family and be patient (see [[passage/quran-20-132|Ta-Ha, verse 132]]) and the verse not to obey one whose heart God made heedless (see [[passage/quran-18-28|al-Kahf, verse 28]]). Of television serials he says one is asked about one's life and youth, and that the rights that fall in times can be made up, as missed prayers can, but the time lost cannot. (needs_source: the report that none moves on the day of judgement until asked about life and youth is told without a chain or a book) A garbled couplet on bringing back yesterday is not summarised. On livelihood he says it is guaranteed (see [[passage/quran-51-22|al-Dhariyat, verse 22]]), as the birds go out hungry and return full, and relates a saying of Umar that God gave the community honour through Islam. (needs_source: the saying on relying on God as the birds do and the saying of Umar on honour through Islam are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:42:30}
- On the reward of patience as a garden and silk (see [[passage/quran-76-12|al-Insan, verse 12]]) he says natural silk is forbidden to Muslim men and lawful to women, even in some threads, and that artificial silk is not silk (see [[concept/silk-rulings|rulings on silk]]), and tells that a Companion's brother, seen in a dream, said he had been held on account of silk thread in his cap. (needs_source: the dream of the Companion's brother and the silk thread is told without a chain or a book) At the couches with neither sun nor bitter cold (see [[passage/quran-76-13|verse 13]]) he explains zamharir as intense cold, the couch as a seat with sides, and the near shades and low fruit (see [[passage/quran-76-14|verse 14]]); and he relates that a man who never saw hardship will, dipped once in Paradise, say he never knew any. (needs_source: the report of the man dipped in Paradise is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:46:00}
- On vessels of silver and goblets of crystal-like silver (see [[passage/quran-76-15|al-Insan, verse 15]], [[passage/quran-76-16|verse 16]]) he says gold and silver vessels are forbidden in the world, and that in his view a vessel wholly or mostly of gold or silver, and spoons and knives of them, are forbidden, while his remark on plating is garbled. This is his opinion and not a ruling. He says the vessels are served by others and measured to what each will drink, so that nothing is left, and that the glass of Paradise is silver in colour and clear as glass, made not of the earth's silicon but from musk, the only likeness being the name. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:50:30}
- He explains that the cup is mixed with ginger (see [[passage/quran-76-17|al-Insan, verse 17]]) and drawn from a spring named Salsabil (see [[passage/quran-76-18|verse 18]]), relating Ibn Abbas's saying that nothing of the world is in Paradise but names, and holds that no one tires of Paradise because it is varied. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Abbas that there is nothing of the world in Paradise but the names is told without a chain or a book) On the immortal youths (see [[passage/quran-76-19|verse 19]]) he says they may be the children of Muslims who died young, but that he leans to their being created by God so that no one's own child is taken from him. He reads of great bliss and a great kingdom (see [[passage/quran-76-20|verse 20]]) and the seat of truth (see [[passage/quran-54-55|al-Qamar, verse 55]]), explaining that every seat that passes is false and the true seat is with the King who does not pass, and recalls Harun al-Rashid's words at death (see [[person/harun-al-rashid|Harun al-Rashid]]). (needs_source: the words attributed to Harun al-Rashid at his death are told without a source, and the transcript loops) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:55:30}
- Of garments of green silk, thin and thick, and bracelets of silver (see [[passage/quran-76-21|al-Insan, verse 21]]) he says silver is for men and gold for women, and that the purifying drink comes from God Himself. He relates that there is a day like Friday on which angels bring an invitation, perfumed rain falls, and God asks the angels whether they have fed, given drink to and clothed His guests, and that the people gather as they gathered on Fridays. (needs_source: the report of the day like Friday and God's questions to the angels is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He adds that one who leaves three Fridays without excuse has his heart sealed. (needs_source: the report on leaving Friday prayer is told without a chain or a book) He imagines asking Khalid ibn al-Walid about the Battle of al-Yamama and the Prophet about the night of the cave (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]], [[passage/quran-52-25|al-Tur, verse 25]]), which is his own imagining of what the verses promise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:00:30}
- He says the drink is pure because no human made it and it did not flow through any river, pure of the wrongs of the world, and reads that this is a reward and their effort is appreciated (see [[passage/quran-76-22|al-Insan, verse 22]]). He says the traits listed need patience and constancy and not a single burst of effort. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:02:00}
- He turns to the verse that God bought from the believers their selves and wealth for Paradise (see [[passage/quran-9-111|al-Tawba, verse 111]]) and explains: God is the buyer, the believers the sellers, the price Paradise, and self and wealth are a trust. Hence suicide is forbidden because one does not own one's self, and spending money on drugs or wine is a breach, as a bank cashier may spend only as the owner permits; he cites the trust offered to the heavens (see [[passage/quran-33-72|al-Ahzab, verse 72]], [[concept/amana|trust]]). A passage on Ibn Taymiyya and men at his door is garbled and not summarised (see [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]). He recalls the poem in which al-Farazdaq praised Zayn al-Abidin before Hisham ibn Abd al-Malik at the Black Stone (see [[person/farazdaq|al-Farazdaq]], [[person/ali-zayn-al-abidin|Ali Zayn al-Abidin]], [[person/hisham-ibn-abd-al-malik|Hisham ibn Abd al-Malik]]), though how he applies it is garbled. (needs_source: the story of al-Farazdaq's poem is told from memory and its link to the lesson is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:10:00}
- He reads the verse that this is a reminder and for the pious a good return (see [[passage/quran-38-49|Sad, verse 49]]), calling it the true reminder, and says truth is heavy as it will be heavy on the scale (see [[concept/taqwa|piety]]), and that it must be spoken gently, since one way of presenting truth harms both it and the speaker. He relates that a man who kissed the wife of a neighbour came to the Prophet and was told that good deeds remove evil ones (see [[passage/quran-11-114|Hud, verse 114]]). (needs_source: the report of the man and the neighbour's wife is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:12:30}
- At the gardens of Eden with doors open to them (see [[passage/quran-38-50|Sad, verse 50]], [[concept/jannat-adn|gardens of Eden]]) he says Eden means a dwelling, and that it is a garden God planted. He defines piety by the report that Umar asked a man whether he had walked a thorny path and what he did, and he answered that he tucked up his garment and picked his way, saying this is piety. (needs_source: the report of Umar and the thorny path is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript does not name the man asked) He tells of al-Hasan al-Basri's fear of God, likened to a man on a plank in a stormy sea, and says fear gives birth to love (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]). He says all eight doors are open, that every door of the world opens and shuts except the door of repentance, and that when that door opens the doors of mercy and prayer open too (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]], [[concept/abwab-al-jannah|doors of Paradise]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:16:30}
- At the reclining in them calling for fruit and drink (see [[passage/quran-38-51|Sad, verse 51]]) he says their call is glory be to You (see [[passage/quran-10-10|Yunus, verse 10]]), and mentions rivers of honey and wine, setting them against polluted waters of the world, and the episode ends with a gap in the transcript. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:18:00}
- The host asks for a supplication and the sheikh prays that the gathering be blessed and the parting kept from sin, that no sin be left unforgiven, no sick person unhealed, no hardship unmade easy, no debt unpaid, no one astray unguided and no dead unpitied, that God help those striving in His cause, give righteous spouses to daughters and sons, and take them in Islam, and that they see God's face and meet the Prophet at the basin, drinking from his hand. The host notes that the supplication is their practice in the programme, congratulates the sheikh on joining a body of jurists in India, as the transcript puts it, bids the viewers farewell and the opening verses are recited once more. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep37 01:20:30}
## Episode 38: the last to leave the Fire, wellbeing and contentment, the fiqh of priorities and work, Surat al-Rahman: the two gardens and what is below them, and Surat al-Waqi'a: the companions of the right and the foremost
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on the immortal youths who go round among the people of Paradise (see [[passage/quran-76-19|al-Insan, verse 19]], [[passage/quran-76-20|verse 20]]), then welcomes the sheikh, says the talk has returned to the Fire as they draw near Paradise, and asks who is the last to leave the Fire for Paradise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:02:00}
- The sheikh relates from Sahih Muslim (see [[work/sahih-muslim|Sahih Muslim]]) that the last to leave the Fire is a man among the sinning monotheists who looks right and left and sees no one, asks his Lord to bring him out and not make him the most wretched of creatures, and crawls out; he sees a tree with shade and water and asks to be brought near it, then a more beautiful one, then a third at the gate of Paradise, and each time God reminds him that he promised to ask for nothing more, then excuses him and lets him in. He is given a kingdom the size of the world, says its owners came before him, and God promises him a hundredfold. At the end the man says something in his joy at which the Prophet laughs, and the sheikh says Ibn Mas'ud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Mas'ud]]) laughed as he told it, saying he had seen the Prophet laugh, for the Lord laughs at His servant. The sheikh says he narrates the hadith by its sense and that those who want the wording should go to Sahih Muslim. (needs_source: the report of the last to leave the Fire is attributed by the sheikh to Sahih Muslim and told by its sense, and the transcript is garbled in its details and in the narrator's name, so it is not verified) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:08:30}
- The sheikh draws the lesson that a servant must not despair of God's mercy, tire of supplication or think anything too great for God's power, and should ask Him for whatever he wishes. He says Musa told God he was ashamed to ask for something small and God answered that he should not be ashamed to ask even the price of salt for his food. (needs_source: the report of Musa asking the price of salt is told without a chain or a book) He says a delayed answer may be a mercy so that the servant keeps standing at God's door, and that refusal from God is a kindness while a gift from a creature may be a deprivation, which is his explanation. He adds that this man had served out his punishment and was not among the unbelievers who ask that their Lord put an end to them (see [[passage/quran-43-77|al-Zukhruf, verse 77]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:11:00}
- Asked whether Paradise compensates for deprivation in the world, he says the believer is not deprived: one who drinks water aware of God's favour and thanks Him for making it sweet lives a pleasure the unaware person misses, and wellbeing is a garment wider than the body, covering religion and the world, so that a man who sees his children praying while his neighbours' children do not has seen his wellbeing. He relates the saying that whoever wakes secure among his people, sound in body, with his day's food, has the world gathered for him. (needs_source: the saying of whoever wakes secure among his people is told without a chain or a book) He mentions a man who said the goodness of food comes from wellbeing and not from the baker or the cook. (needs_source: the story of the fasting man and his remark on the goodness of food is told without a speaker or a book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:13:00}
- He says God meant the troubles of the world to loosen the servant from creatures so that nothing distracts him from God, and relates from Ibn al-Jawzi (see [[person/ibn-al-jawzi|Ibn al-Jawzi]]) that a sorrowful person who reads Surat Yusuf with certainty is relieved by God. (needs_source: the saying attributed to Ibn al-Jawzi on Surat Yusuf is told without a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says happiness is inside, not outside, and that a believer at peace with God has a contentment the people of the world could not buy with billions; that God wrote in the womb whether a person is wretched or happy by His prior knowledge and that the servant writes his own wretchedness or happiness by his deeds, which is his view of destiny (see [[concept/qadar|qadar]]). He cites the promise to Adam in Paradise that he will not go hungry or thirsty (see [[passage/quran-20-118|Ta-Ha, verse 118]], [[passage/quran-20-119|verse 119]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:16:00}
- On securing children's future he reads the verse on those who would leave weak offspring (see [[passage/quran-4-9|al-Nisa, verse 9]]) and relates that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) was asked about leaving his seven daughters without money and said he had taught them Surat al-Waqi'a, that if they were righteous God befriends the righteous and otherwise he would not give them what helps them to sin. (needs_source: the story of the seven daughters and Surat al-Waqi'a is told without a chain, and its attribution to Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz is garbled in the transcript) He says wealth is called mal because it inclines people away from the truth, that wealth and children are the adornment of worldly life (see [[passage/quran-18-46|al-Kahf, verse 46]]) and a trial (see [[passage/quran-8-28|al-Anfal, verse 28]]). A person should ask God, if made rich, to make him thankful and, if made poor, patient, and both the thankful and the patient are in Paradise (see [[concept/shukr|shukr]], [[concept/sabr|sabr]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:18:00}
- He reads the verse on trial in wealth and selves (see [[passage/quran-3-186|Al Imran, verse 186]]) and says a person tests himself by seeking beyond his means and that a little that suffices is better than a lot that makes one rebel. This is no call to abandon the world but to take what one wishes with a pure intention for God and keep wealth in the hand and not the heart. He recalls that God bought from the believers (see [[passage/quran-9-111|al-Tawba, verse 111]]) and that wealth is a trust. Then he tells of Sulayman and four sparrows: the first said would that creatures had not been created, the second that they were created to learn why they were created (see [[passage/quran-51-56|al-Dhariyat, verse 56]]), the third to act on what they know, and the fourth to be sincere in what they do. He says the sincere are in great danger and that the sincere person asks his Lord about the degree of his sincerity (see [[concept/ikhlas|ikhlas]], [[person/sulayman|Sulayman]]). (needs_source: the story of Sulayman and the four sparrows is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:20:00}
- He says a person's provision and lifespan are written while he is a foetus in his mother's womb, and relates that the angel of death stands at the door and tells the mourners he is under orders and has not taken a dirham from the dead man's provision nor a moment from his life, and that he will return to them until none is left. (needs_source: the words of the angel of death at the door are told without a chain or a book) He relates that whoever seeks forgiveness often, God gives him relief from every worry and a way out of every strait, and that "there is no power and no might except with God" is a treasure from beneath the Throne that repels seventy doors of affliction, the least of which is worry. (needs_source: the two reports on seeking forgiveness and on "no power and no might" repelling seventy doors of affliction are told without a chain or a book) He urges a return to the Prophet's words with certainty. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:22:00}
- He then says people are drowning in worldly pleasures because these became ends after being means, and that worry should be about prayer and fasting and the show and conceit in them. He gives the example of a client who trusts his lawyer and forgets the case, and says true reliance is to take the means and then leave the matter to God without abandoning work; one whose shop burned goes to the market to make good the loss, and in the same way seeking forgiveness is the road to pardon as work is the road to wealth (see [[concept/tawakkul|tawakkul]], [[concept/istighfar|istighfar]]). The wording breaks down where he speaks of the houris, and it is not summarised. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:26:00}
- He says the community has awakened and returned to its sincere scholars and preachers, that it is a community that falls ill but does not die, and that God took charge of preserving its religion (see [[passage/quran-15-9|al-Hijr, verse 9]]). He relates a saying attributed to Umar ibn al-Khattab that victory over the enemy comes through obeying God while they disobey Him, and if both are equal in disobedience they overcome us in equipment and numbers (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]). (needs_source: the saying of Umar on victory through obedience is told without a chain or a book) He says a person must first win over himself and that Satan overcomes only the weak in faith, citing the exception of the sincere (see [[passage/quran-15-40|al-Hijr, verse 40]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:28:00}
- On the fiqh of priorities he says that one who performs Hajj every year while in debt, or while others need marriage or a gladdened heart, errs, because an obligation comes before a voluntary act and the second and third Hajj are voluntary. He says wealth is God's and must be placed where it pleases Him, which is his view. He relates that a poor man came to the Prophet to beg, and that the Prophet sold for him a bowl and a rug for two dirhams, told him to spend one on food for his family and one on an axe head, fitted a branch to it himself and said, go and gather firewood and let me not see you for fifteen days. (needs_source: the report of the poor man, the bowl, the rug and the axe head is told without a chain or a book) He says begging is lawful only in extreme poverty, crushing debt or blood-money, and that the Prophet said of a calloused hand that it is a hand God and His Messenger love. (needs_source: the two reports on begging and on the working hand are told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:30:30}
- He recalls that the prophets practised trades: Zakariyya was a carpenter and Musa tended sheep for ten years (see [[person/zakariyya|Zakariyya]], [[person/musa|Musa]], [[passage/quran-28-27|al-Qasas, verse 27]]) and the Prophet tended sheep, and says the servant is God's servant and not the government's or the job's, and that gathering firewood corresponds today to looking for available work. He relates that Khalid ibn al-Walid (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]), ordered by Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]) to join the army in Syria, chose the desert road, watered the camels and tied their muzzles to hold the water, and when the army was thirsty slaughtered a camel and took the water from its stomach, so that he reached the Romans by surprise without losing a man. (needs_source: the story of Khalid's desert crossing, watering and slaughtering the camels is told without a source, and the transcript is garbled in its details) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:34:30}
- He digresses to the verse on a people who barely understand speech (see [[passage/quran-18-93|al-Kahf, verse 93]]) and says Muslims who take God's light will grasp this world and the next, and calls for Muslims to be moderate and God-minded, knowing what comes first and what later, and not to spend themselves finding fault with each other. He then turns to Surat al-Rahman as the "bride of the Qur'an", and says the people of Paradise enjoy it as fruit. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:36:00}
- At the verse on one who fears the station of his Lord (see [[passage/quran-55-46|al-Rahman, verse 46]]) he says the one who fears has two gardens because of his fear of God and his true faith, having passed two stations, fear and works, and that fear breeds love, as a child fears his father and loves him (see [[concept/khawf|khawf]]). He relates that the Prophet recited Surat al-Rahman to his Companions and they were silent, and said their brothers the jinn answered better, for at each "which of your Lord's favours will you deny" (see [[passage/quran-55-13|verse 13]]) they said that of none of Your favours, our Lord, do we deny, and Yours is the praise. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet reciting Surat al-Rahman and the jinn's reply is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says a reader should answer yes at the verse asking whether God is able to give life to the dead (see [[passage/quran-75-40|al-Qiyama, verse 40]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:38:00}
- He explains "dhawata afnan" (see [[passage/quran-55-48|al-Rahman, verse 48]]) as two gardens of branches, with two flowing springs (see [[passage/quran-55-50|verse 50]]) and two kinds of every fruit (see [[passage/quran-55-52|verse 52]]), saying the variety prevents boredom. He tells of animals that come to the people of Paradise and offer themselves as food and then return to their pasture, but the wording breaks down and it is not summarised. He explains the couches lined with brocade (see [[passage/quran-55-54|verse 54]]) as istabraq, thick silk, and sundus, thin silk, and says the fruit hangs near for whoever wants it, with no effort in picking. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:42:00}
- On "restraining their glances" (see [[passage/quran-55-56|al-Rahman, verse 56]]) he says it describes a woman who confines her gaze to her husband, and digresses to modesty, saying modesty is the character of Islam, that there is no shyness in seeking knowledge according to the scholars, and that religion is wholly modesty (see [[concept/haya|haya]]). He says the jinn enter Paradise because the Prophet was sent to humans and jinn and that they are in a veil from humans, that the angels enter on the people of Paradise from every gate, and that in this world a believer is surrounded by angels. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:45:00}
- At "as if they were rubies and coral" (see [[passage/quran-55-58|al-Rahman, verse 58]]) and "is the reward of goodness anything but goodness" (see [[passage/quran-55-60|verse 60]]) he says God gives justice, grace and what is beyond grace: justice is that the doer of good receives for his good, grace is a good deed multiplied from tenfold to seven hundredfold, and beyond grace is eternity. He explains the eternity of the people of the Fire by saying God looked at the believer's record and knew that if he were kept in the world forever he would obey forever, and at the unbeliever's and knew he would disbelieve forever, so the punishment matches his intention (see [[passage/quran-6-28|al-An'am, verse 28]]). This is his own explanation and not a ruling, and it is recorded here as a view attributed to him. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:47:00}
- He says one who plants a bad tree reaps only bad fruit, and that God made faith dear to the heart of one who followed the way of the Book and the Sunna (see [[passage/quran-49-7|al-Hujurat, verse 7]]), so that the servant has a share in it. He turns to "and below these two are two gardens" (see [[passage/quran-55-62|al-Rahman, verse 62]]), noting that "below" can be read as lower in rank or nearer the Throne, and says Paradise has a hundred degrees with a hundred years' journey between each pair. (needs_source: the report of a hundred degrees and the distance between them is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says the four gardens differ in degree. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:51:00}
- He explains "dark green" (see [[passage/quran-55-64|al-Rahman, verse 64]]) as so intensely green it nears black, and "gushing" (see [[passage/quran-55-66|verse 66]]) as water welling up with force, and notes that they hold fruit, palm and pomegranate (see [[passage/quran-55-68|verse 68]]), the palm being named separately because the Arabs know it. Then "good and beautiful ones" (see [[passage/quran-55-70|verse 70]]) and "houris confined in tents" (see [[passage/quran-55-72|verse 72]]): he says hur is the plural of hawra, a woman with intensely white and intensely black eyes, and that "confined" means guarded in the tents. He relates that the tent, as told to Umar, is a hollowed pearl the size of the world. (needs_source: the report of the tent as a hollowed pearl the size of the world and its attribution to Umar are told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says the gaze is where trouble begins. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:55:00}
