- At "reclining on green cushions and beautiful carpets" (see [[passage/quran-55-76|al-Rahman, verse 76]]) he says abqari is tied to Wadi Abqar, which the Arabs said the jinn inhabited, and means something never seen the like of, and the sura closes with "blessed is the name of your Lord, Owner of Majesty and Honour" (see [[passage/quran-55-78|verse 78]]), saying God rewards little work with abundant good. The host asks why the Qur'an so often describes sitting, eating and the houris, and he answers that people of the world take these blessings mixed with worry, whereas for the people of Paradise there is no fear and no grief. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:59:00}
- He turns to Surat al-Waqi'a and says it divides people into three groups at the resurrection and at the departure of the soul (see [[passage/quran-56-4|al-Waqi'a, verse 4]], [[passage/quran-56-5|verse 5]], [[passage/quran-56-6|verse 6]], [[passage/quran-56-7|verse 7]]): the companions of the right, the companions of the left and the foremost (see [[passage/quran-56-8|verse 8]], [[passage/quran-56-9|verse 9]], [[passage/quran-56-10|verse 10]]). He notes that God did not say "what are the foremost" but "those are the nearest" (see [[passage/quran-56-11|verse 11]]), and relates that al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) asked his Lord to make him one of the companions of the right since the foremost had gone. (needs_source: the saying of al-Hasan al-Basri on the foremost and the companions of the right is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He says "a multitude of the earlier and a few of the later" (see [[passage/quran-56-13|verse 13]], [[passage/quran-56-14|verse 14]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:02:30}
- He relates that the Prophet asked his Companions whether they would be pleased to be a quarter of the people of Paradise, then a third, then a half, and that the people of Paradise are one hundred and twenty rows of whom his community is eighty. (needs_source: the report of the rows of Paradise and his community being eighty of one hundred and twenty is told without a chain or a book, and the transcript is garbled) He then mentions the woven couches (see [[passage/quran-56-15|al-Waqi'a, verse 15]]), reclining on them (see [[passage/quran-56-16|verse 16]]) and the immortal youths going round (see [[passage/quran-56-17|verse 17]]), and says the Prophet used to spread his gaze over those sitting with him so that each thought he was the one meant. (needs_source: the saying that the Prophet spread his gaze over those sitting with him is told without a chain or a book) The transcript ends in repetition and the opening verses are recited again. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep38 01:06:00}
## Episode 39: the servants of the Merciful in Paradise: humility and the arrogant, night prayer, moderate spending, pure monotheism, the sanctity of life, repentance, truthful witness, righteous offspring, the lofty chamber and supplication
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on the servants of the Merciful who walk on the earth humbly and spend the night prostrating and standing (see [[passage/quran-25-63|al-Furqan, verse 63]], [[passage/quran-25-64|verse 64]]), then welcomes the sheikh and asks whether the people of Paradise are groups with set names and qualities or a single kind of pious believer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:03:00}
- The sheikh answers that people in the world are in ranks by what they know (see [[passage/quran-58-11|al-Mujadila, verse 11]]), that no one should claim to know everything, and that it is a fault that millions of Muslims today give rulings on what they do not know. He says the ranks of the Hereafter match how little a person was sunk in love of the world, and that the world and the Hereafter cannot be loved together unless the world is made a means to the Hereafter (see [[passage/quran-28-77|al-Qasas, verse 77]]). He cites a supplication he attributes to Ibn Ata Allah on being ignorant even in one's knowledge, and relates that when the Prophet was asked which places are best and which worst he said he would first ask one more knowing. (needs_source: the supplication attributed to Ibn Ata Allah and the report of the best and worst places are told without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He says the highest rank is that of those called on the day of resurrection to enter without reckoning, who praised God in every state. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:06:30}
- He says the servants of the Merciful are a special group in Paradise defined by set qualities, like a certificate earned in the world, hard but easy for one whom God helps. He suggests each viewer take paper and pen and measure himself against each quality as it is explained, as a ministry sets a timetable and five-year plan for a project, and says the days of the pilgrimage are a good time to start. On those below the age of accountability he says some scholars hold that the children of Muslims who die young are in Paradise and others that every child who dies before puberty is, Muslim or not, because no one was charged with them; these are different scholarly views and he gives both. He adds that one should rejoice when someone is guided, such as a woman who covers herself or a man who gives up drink and turns to God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:10:30}
- The first quality is humility in walking (see [[passage/quran-25-63|al-Furqan, verse 63]]). He says the arrogant do not enter Paradise (see [[concept/kibr|arrogance]] and [[concept/tawadu|humility]]), and defines arrogance as rejecting the truth and belittling people: such a person takes advice only if it suits him, otherwise is seized by pride or shifts his own faults onto others, as psychologists say, and claims that he alone made and did everything. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:12:00}
- He tells of Musa (see [[person/musa|Musa]]) addressing the Children of Israel and being asked whether anyone on earth knew more, then being sent to the meeting of the two seas (see [[passage/quran-18-60|al-Kahf, verse 60]], [[passage/quran-18-65|verse 65]]). (needs_source: the opening of the story, Musa's speech and the question put to him, is told without a chain or book) He draws lessons in courtesy from Musa's request to follow al-Khidr (see [[person/al-khidr|al-Khidr]]) in order to be taught (see [[passage/quran-18-66|verse 66]]), from al-Khidr's warning that he could not be patient (see [[passage/quran-18-67|verse 67]], [[passage/quran-18-68|verse 68]]), Musa's promise (see [[passage/quran-18-69|verse 69]]) and the condition not to ask (see [[passage/quran-18-70|verse 70]]), then the ship, the boy and the wall, and the closing words that he did none of it of his own accord (see [[passage/quran-18-82|verse 82]]). He asks whether any clever mind should claim to know better than that. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:15:30}
- He recalls the one who had knowledge of the Book and offered to bring the throne before Sulayman could rise (see [[passage/quran-27-39|al-Naml, verse 39]], [[passage/quran-27-40|verse 40]]; see [[person/sulayman|Sulayman]]) and says many commentators take him to be Sulayman himself, though the rule has exceptions. (needs_source: the attribution to many commentators is stated by the sheikh without naming any, and the identity of this person is contested among commentators; the transcript is garbled) He quotes Sulayman's answer that this is from the favour of his Lord to test whether he is grateful or ungrateful, and says the proud, as in the report of whoever drags his garment out of pride, will be dragged in the Fire. (needs_source: the report of the one who drags his garment is told by sense without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:17:00}
- On pride in daily life he gives the husband, wife, neighbour, employee or manager who says he does not take advice, and says each side must come a step toward the other; he points to the two litigants before Dawud (see [[person/dawud|Dawud]]), one holding ninety-nine ewes and the other one (see [[passage/quran-38-22|Sad, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-38-23|verse 23]], [[passage/quran-38-24|verse 24]]), and says many partners wrong one another. He calls pride in one's honour and prestige a deception of Iblis (see [[person/iblis|Iblis]]), and recalls pre-Islamic feuds over prestige in which, he says, thousands were killed. (needs_source: the number killed in the feuds is a figure with no source, and the war is not named clearly in the transcript) He says some people leave a will that a relative not attend their washing or funeral, and that in his view such a will need not be carried out; this is his opinion and is not recorded as a ruling. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:19:30}
- He tells of al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) answering a man who spoke ill of another by asking whether he had taken part in the conquests of Persia, Rome or Africa, and saying that the enemies of God were safe from his tongue but not his brother's honour. (needs_source: the exchange with al-Hasan al-Basri is told without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He then advises answering anger with good (see [[passage/quran-41-34|Fussilat, verse 34]], [[passage/quran-41-35|verse 35]]) and, in a quarrel, taking the other aside to say one is upset, since the enemy then becomes a close friend, and only the patient are given this. He adds a story of his own: when he was insulted he thanked the man, saying he had taken his good deeds, and he says Muslims should be humble with believers and strong against unbelievers (see [[passage/quran-5-54|al-Ma'ida, verse 54]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:26:00}
- On wealth and provision he says God made some above others in provision (see [[passage/quran-16-71|al-Nahl, verse 71]]) and that the rich are trustees of money that belongs to God and the poor His dependants. He adds that people are the poor in need of God (see [[passage/quran-35-15|Fatir, verse 15]]), that wealth and children are a trial (see [[passage/quran-64-15|al-Taghabun, verse 15]]), like gold tested in the fire, and that a blessing used for God's pleasure grows while one not used is taken and given to another. He urges asking God for His bounty rather than envying (see [[passage/quran-4-32|al-Nisa, verse 32]]) and mentions the account of the leper and the blind man, which breaks off in the transcript. (needs_source: the report of the leper and the blind man is told by sense without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:28:00}
- Humility, he says, is not frailty: no one splits the earth or reaches the height of the mountains (see [[passage/quran-17-37|al-Isra, verse 37]]), and all are descended from Adam and the dust. He relates that the Prophet calmed a trembling bedouin by telling him to take it easy, he was only the son of a woman who ate dried meat in Mecca, and that Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]), when a lamp went out in the mosque, rose himself to light it and told a guest he went as Umar and came back as Umar. (needs_source: the report of the bedouin and the account of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz and the lamp are told without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He mentions a poem a friend sent to a colleague raised to high office, which is not reproduced here. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:25:30}
- He recalls the two men in the parable, one with two gardens (see [[passage/quran-18-32|al-Kahf, verse 32]], [[passage/quran-18-33|verse 33]], [[passage/quran-18-34|verse 34]]): the owner says that his garden will never perish and that he does not think the Hour will come (see [[passage/quran-18-35|verse 35]], [[passage/quran-18-36|verse 36]]), his companion urges him to say what God willed and that there is no power but with God (see [[passage/quran-18-39|verse 39]]), and the garden is ruined by a bolt from heaven or the water sinking (see [[passage/quran-18-40|verse 40]], [[passage/quran-18-41|verse 41]], [[passage/quran-18-42|verse 42]]), and he finds he has no group to help him (see [[passage/quran-18-43|verse 43]], [[passage/quran-18-44|verse 44]]). He ties this to the man who turns away when favoured (see [[passage/quran-17-83|al-Isra, verse 83]]) and says anyone who is proud of wealth, learning or rank will have it taken. He relates a divine saying that greatness is God's wrapper and pride His garment and whoever contends with Him in them He will cast into the Fire. (needs_source: the divine saying on greatness and pride is told by sense without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:31:30}
- Asked why arrogance and not another vice is named first, he says some of God's attributes are of beauty and are to be imitated, like mercy and forgiveness, and others of majesty, like the Proud, the Compeller and the Subduer, which belong to God alone and which a creature may not take (see [[concept/asma-al-mutakabbir|al-Mutakabbir]]). He says pride was the first sin, when Iblis said he was better than Adam (see [[passage/quran-7-12|al-A'raf, verse 12]]). He relates that the Prophet saw a mark of pride in a man who came in dripping from ablution and whom the Companions had praised, and that humility is the door to the house of faith. (needs_source: the report of the mark on the man's face is told by sense without a chain or book) He explains that the verse says servants of the Merciful because mercy is a name of this world, in which God has mercy on believer and unbeliever (see [[concept/asma-al-rahman|al-Rahman]] and [[concept/asma-al-rahim|al-Rahim]]); this is his reading. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:35:30}
- He notes the verb is in the present: they walk, always. He tells of Ibn Mas'ud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Mas'ud]]) walking with followers behind him and turning to say that this is an abasement for the one followed and a trial for the follower. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Mas'ud to his followers is told by sense without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He reads the ignorant of the verse as those who fail to honour the standing of a scholar, elder or ruler, and says the believers answer them with peace, meaning that they neither wish them harm nor answer rudeness with rudeness. He describes a long story of a sword and a strange word whose meaning he is asked, which is garbled and not recorded. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:40:00}
- He says Hasan al-Basri sat among his young students who listened as if hearing for the first time, and calls this courtesy in learning. He relates that al-Shafii (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafii]]), then fifteen, sat in the circle of Malik (see [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik ibn Anas]]) when a man swore that his wife was divorced unless she was fairer than the moon; Malik said she was divorced, and al-Shafii recalled the verse that man was created in the best form (see [[passage/quran-95-4|al-Tin, verse 4]]), so she was not. (needs_source: the account of al-Shafii and Malik and the oath about the moon is told without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:42:00}
- On peace in word and in state, he tells of a man of the Prophet's family, named in the transcript in a garbled form that seems to be Zayn al-Abidin, who for forty years left food, drink, clothes and money at the doors of the poor before dawn without being known, until his body was washed after death and the rope marks of carrying food were found. (needs_source: the account is told without a chain or book, the name and the place (given as Kufa) are garbled, and the identity is not verified) He says that to speak to others from a sense of one's own rank is arrogance, and warns against wronging those with no helper but God, such as an orphan or a widow, whom he sees as more vulnerable than a divorced woman. This is his observation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:45:30}
- The second quality is night worship (see [[passage/quran-25-64|al-Furqan, verse 64]]; see [[concept/qiyam-al-layl-fiqh|the night vigil]]). He says the verse says they spend the night, not that they sleep, and puts prostration before standing, since a servant is nearest his Lord in prostration, which he relates as a saying of the Prophet. (needs_source: the saying that the servant is nearest his Lord in prostration is told by sense without a chain or book) He adds that high blood pressure is only regulated by long prostration and humility in prayer, then says God knows best. (needs_source: the claim about blood pressure and prostration is stated without a source and is a medical claim) He says the angels see the house of someone who prays at night as people on earth see the stars. (needs_source: the report about the angels and the stars is told by sense without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:48:30}
- He tells of Ata ibn Abi Rabah (see [[person/ata-ibn-abi-rabah|Ata ibn Abi Rabah]]), who shared the night in three parts with his mother and his wife, then in halves with his wife after his mother died, and after she died prayed the whole night for twenty years without his lamp going out. (needs_source: the account of Ata, his mother and his wife is told without a chain or book, and the name is given in a garbled form) He says the Lord descends in the last part of the night to the lowest heaven and asks if anyone has a need, and that God may delay an answer because He wants His servant to stay at His door and likes to hear his voice. (needs_source: the report of the descent is told by sense without a chain or book) He says the angels miss one who prayed at night and fell ill or travelled and pray for him, and he cites a third report on spreading peace, feeding food and praying while people sleep, which breaks off. (needs_source: the reports on the angels asking after him and on entering Paradise in peace are told by sense without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He adds a remark on those who keep Ramadan vigils while earning unlawfully and speaking ill of people, whose blessing, he says, drains away. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:52:00}
- He points out that the verse says "their Lord", for the lord is the one who rears and brings them up to love the night vigil. Next comes the supplication that God turn away the punishment of Hell (see [[passage/quran-25-65|al-Furqan, verse 65]], [[passage/quran-25-66|verse 66]]), which he says must be followed by deeds and not be words alone. Then moderation in spending, neither wasteful nor stingy (see [[passage/quran-25-67|verse 67]]), and he says the wasteful are brothers of the devils (see [[passage/quran-17-27|al-Isra, verse 27]]); he adds that a rich smoker is wasteful and a poor smoker foolish (see [[passage/quran-4-5|al-Nisa, verse 5]]). This is his view and is not given as a ruling. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:54:00}
- Next is not calling on another god with God (see [[passage/quran-25-68|al-Furqan, verse 68]]; see [[concept/tawhid|monotheism]] and [[concept/shirk|associating partners with God]]). He says the verse says God, not Lord or the Merciful, because Allah is the name specific to tawhid, and that if a convert said there is no god but the Forgiving he would not be a Muslim. He offers an observation on the letters of the name: remove the first letter and it reads for God, remove the next and it reads for Him, then he. He says the verses on worship are about one hundred and ten out of about six thousand verses, about three percent, and that the rest, ninety-seven percent, concern dealings with people. (needs_source: the count of worship verses and the percentages are a statistic with no source, and the transcript gives the total in a garbled form) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:56:00}
- Next is not killing the soul God made sacred except by right (see [[passage/quran-25-68|verse 68]]), together with not committing adultery (see [[concept/zina|zina]]). He says the loss of the world is lighter to God than shedding innocent blood, and that right means the matter goes to the one in authority who carries out the penalty, not a private person; he relates that a bird killed for no use will complain to God on the day of resurrection and that a woman entered the fire over a cat she starved. (needs_source: the report of the bird complaining and the woman and the cat are told by sense without a chain or book, and the transcript is garbled) He notes the one who does these things will meet punishment doubled on the day of resurrection (see [[passage/quran-25-69|verse 69]]) and abide in it in disgrace, and that this is also reckoned in this world, then comes the exception for the one who repents (see [[passage/quran-25-70|verse 70]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:59:30}
- Asked whether such a person is still a believer, he says faith is present but slips away at the moment of the act, as in the report that the fornicator is not a believer while he fornicates, and returns afterwards, and that this does not put a disobedient Muslim outside the community of Islam; this is his view, and the faith of the sinner is a contested question among Muslim schools. He gives the conditions of repentance, three, or four where the right of another person is involved, and says that one who is punished under the prescribed penalty is cleansed. (needs_source: the report that faith leaves the sinner at the time of the act and the saying about those punished under the penalty are told by sense without a chain or book) He begins the account of al-Ghamidiyya who was stoned and a drop of her blood fell on the lip of Khalid ibn al-Walid (see [[person/khalid-ibn-al-walid|Khalid ibn al-Walid]]), who recoiled, and the transcript breaks off before the answer. (needs_source: the account is told by sense without a chain or book, and the passage is garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:02:00}
- He says God does not merely erase the sins of the one who repents but replaces them with good deeds, and that in the account he gives God makes the limbs, the land, the time and even the recording angels forget, so that no witness remains but God, who is the Forgiving and Merciful. (needs_source: the claim that the limbs, the land, the time and the recording angels are made to forget is told without a chain or book) He cites the legal maxim that the burden goes with the gain, glossed as paying something and receiving more in return, and says "God is ever Forgiving, Merciful" means He always was and still is (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:05:00}
- Another quality is not bearing false witness (see [[passage/quran-25-72|al-Furqan, verse 72]]; see [[concept/false-testimony-zur|false testimony]]). He relates that the Prophet, reclining, sat up to name false witness and false speech among the great sins and repeated it until the Companions wished he would stop. (needs_source: the report of the Prophet repeating false speech is told by sense without a chain or book) He says it is not only in court, and gives the case of someone who certifies a man as a woman's guardian so she may go on the pilgrimage, saying the end and the means must both be lawful and that the one who did not know bears no sin. This is his view. He adds that the servants of the Merciful, when they pass by idle talk, pass with dignity, and that they do not turn away from the verses of their Lord deaf and blind (see [[passage/quran-25-73|verse 73]]), but reflect on the heavens, the earth and the signs of divine justice when a wrongdoer is repaid in this life. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:07:30}
- Another is the prayer for spouses and offspring who are the coolness of the eyes and a model for the pious (see [[passage/quran-25-74|al-Furqan, verse 74]]). He says this means children placed in the service of the faith who learn the Qur'an and the veil early, and relates the saying to play with a child for seven years, teach him for seven and befriend him for seven, then loosen the rein; he says a daughter should not be kept unmarried when a suitable husband comes. (needs_source: the saying on the three seven-year periods is told without a chain or book and its attribution is not given in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:08:30}
- He turns to the reward: the lofty chamber for their patience, welcomed with greeting and peace (see [[passage/quran-25-75|al-Furqan, verse 75]], [[passage/quran-25-76|verse 76]]), which he calls the highest place in Paradise, with chambers above chambers (see [[passage/quran-39-20|al-Zumar, verse 20]]; see [[concept/ghuraf-al-jannah|the chambers of Paradise]] and [[concept/darajat-al-jannah|the degrees of Paradise]]). He says the closing verse shows that God would not care for them but for their prayer (see [[passage/quran-25-77|verse 77]]) and that some belittle supplication. He relates that nothing turns back the decree but supplication and nothing lengthens life but kindness to parents, and that whoever does not ask God, God is angry with him. (needs_source: the reports on supplication and the decree, on kindness to parents and on God's anger at one who does not ask are told by sense without a chain or book) The episode ends with the sheikh's long prayer for the sick, the debtor, the traveller and for good spouses for children, and the opening verses are recited again. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep39 01:13:00}
## Episode 40: seeing God in Paradise, the Mount and Musa's request, the three views on who sees Him, the gathering place, the Companions and the vision, and a plan for reaching it
- The host opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and on faces that look toward their Lord (see [[passage/quran-75-22|al-Qiyama, verse 22]], [[passage/quran-75-23|verse 23]]), then welcomes the sheikh and says the programme is nearing its end, which both pleases and saddens them: pleasing because part of the trust given to them is done, saddening because they will leave a subject they have lived with. He then announces the episode on seeing God. The transcript here loops on a single phrase of praise. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:01:30}
- The sheikh says seeing God is the greatest bliss and the highest rank, reached only by the truthful believer, and that the scholars are agreed that the worst torment of the people of Hell is the veil from God's face and the greatest joy in Paradise is looking at it. He relates a saying of the people of knowledge that if the people of Hell looked on it they would forget their torment, and if it were veiled from the people of Paradise their life there would not please them for a moment. (needs_source: the saying of the people of knowledge about the people of Hell forgetting their torment is told without a name or a book) He says the rank of seeing has no colours of the gardens described before and is the place called Illiyyun (see [[passage/quran-83-18|al-Mutaffifin, verse 18]], [[passage/quran-83-19|verse 19]], [[passage/quran-83-20|verse 20]], [[passage/quran-83-21|verse 21]]), where the nearest ones are honoured by the lifting of the veils. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:04:30}
- He says the scholars of creed speak of this subject with great caution, because what comes to mind is not God (see [[passage/quran-6-103|al-An'am, verse 103]]), but that the Book and the Sunna establish the seeing of God's face in Paradise, which is the creed of Ahl al-Sunna wa al-Jama'a (see [[concept/ruyat-allah|the vision of God]]). Of the words "looking toward their Lord" he says "radiant" (nadira) means the freshness and glow of the faces of the blessed, and that the wording must be taken in its plain sense without turning it elsewhere; he links the glow to the state of faith and the halo of light spoken of in earlier episodes. A passage on gold and silver adornment is too garbled in the transcript to follow. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:07:30}
- He argues from Musa's request (see [[passage/quran-7-143|al-A'raf, verse 143]]; see [[person/musa|Musa]]): Musa, whom God spoke to without Jibril, would not have asked for what is impossible, since prophets are kept from idle speech, and it was a courtesy of the one spoken to. He notes that God's reply used a word of negation for the future and not the word "never", so, in his reading, the refusal is of seeing in this world and not of seeing at all, and God did not leave Musa in perplexity but pointed him to the mountain, greater and firmer than he, saying that if it stood firm he would see. He reads God's manifestation to the mountain as His light, which when it enters a servant's heart opens it, and relates that the mountain was levelled and Musa fell down senseless. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:16:00}
- He says one sign of the light in the heart is turning away from the abode of delusion and preparing for death. He relates that Abu Hazim, asked who is the most intelligent of people, said the one who leaves the world before it leaves him. (needs_source: the saying of Abu Hazim on the most intelligent of people is told without a chain or a book) He says the true king is the one who rules his own self and desires, and relates that Ibn al-Qayyim (see [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]]) said of his teacher Ibn Taymiyya (see [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]) that he overcame desire until it avoided him. (needs_source: the words attributed to Ibn al-Qayyim about Ibn Taymiyya are told without a book) Such a person, he says, sees things with the eye of religion: he takes loss of money, office or a young child's death as a mercy, as with the boy killed by al-Khidr (see [[passage/quran-18-80|al-Kahf, verse 80]], [[passage/quran-18-81|verse 81]]; see [[person/al-khidr|al-Khidr]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:15:00}
- He turns to Nuh, whom God cautioned against being among the ignorant when he asked about his son (see [[passage/quran-11-46|Hud, verse 46]], [[passage/quran-11-47|verse 47]]; see [[person/nuh|Nuh]]), and draws the lesson of not asking God for what He has not decreed: one who has done every cause and knocked at the door should stop complaining, and his certainty shows God's light in his heart. He praises Musa's courtesy in saying "show me" and not "let me see", asking for a measure of God's power and favour that would let him bear it, and concludes that the textual proofs show the seeing is possible. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:18:30}
- Asked who will see God, he sets out the views. A first view, which he ascribes to Ahmad ibn Hanbal (see [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]]) and some Hanbalis, is that only believers see Him in Paradise. (needs_source: the ascription of this view to Ahmad ibn Hanbal and some Hanbalis is told without a book, and the transcript is garbled) A second view is that sinning Muslims also see Him, which the transcript states in broken form. A third is that the hypocrites among Muslims see God at the gathering place and not in Paradise. He says he does not favour entering this academic dispute, and states his own position: God is seen at the gathering place by all, with fear and shame, and in Paradise with delight. This is a contested question and the views are his report of others. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:22:30}
- He relates from Sahih Muslim (see [[work/sahih-muslim|Sahih Muslim]]), on the authority of Abu Hurayra (see [[person/abu-hurayra|Abu Hurayra]]) as he gives it, that when the people of Paradise enter, God asks whether they want anything more, and they answer that He has whitened their faces and admitted them to Paradise and spared them the Fire, and then the veil is lifted and they are given nothing dearer than looking at their Lord. (needs_source: the narrator and wording of this report are as the transcript gives them and are garbled, and the narrator is to be checked against the book) He relates that Abu Bakr read "the best and more" (see [[passage/quran-10-26|Yunus, verse 26]]) as the best being Paradise and the more being looking at God's face, and that "neither dust nor humiliation covers their faces" is for those who honoured themselves by turning from sin. (needs_source: Abu Bakr's reading of the addition is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:25:30}
- To the question whether we will see our Lord on the Day of Resurrection, he relates the Prophet's report that people are not harmed in seeing the full moon or the sun with no cloud, so they will not be harmed in seeing Him; that on the gathering people follow what they worshipped, the sun-worshippers the sun and the others their idols; that God comes to the rest in a form they do not know, then in the form they know, and they recognise Him; that the Prophet and his community are first to cross the Sirat while he prays that God save them (see [[concept/sirat|the Sirat]]); that the thorns of al-Sa'dan on Jahannam are known only in size to God; and that God then lets those who did not associate anything with Him leave the Fire. He adds that on that day only the messengers speak (see [[passage/quran-20-108|Ta-Ha, verse 108]], [[passage/quran-20-111|verse 111]]), and that the hypocrites see God so that their regret and the unbelievers' is greater, which is his explanation of the wisdom. (needs_source: this long report is told by its sense in the transcript, without naming a book or narrator) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:31:00}
- He turns to the Companions and the seeing. He relates the Prophet's dream of drawing from a well, in which Abu Bakr (see [[person/abu-bakr|Abu Bakr]]) drew a bucket or two and then Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]) drew until the bucket turned large and the camels watered, and says Umar lived ten years and six months of justice, conquests and registers. (needs_source: the dream of the well is told without naming a book or chain) (needs_source: the figure of ten years and six months for Umar's rule is given without a source) He says the Companions in their ten years in Medina took part in about 27 expeditions, each taking about a month, so about eight campaigns a year, undertaken to clear the way for God's call; he asks whether such effort would be lost and says God rewards it. (needs_source: the figures of 27 expeditions and eight campaigns a year are given without a source) He contrasts the present with the blessings of the telephone, the internet and remote surgery and asks whether people give thanks. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:35:30}
- He says whoever does not see the truth in this world and keep to it will be veiled from God, that the truth is the Book and the Sunna, and that the disagreement of the scholars is in branches and not in the constants. He says the agreed ground is over 90 or 95 percent and the disputed ground under 5 percent, and that clinging to the 5 percent shows weak creed, little knowledge, pride, desire or the whisper of devils. (needs_source: the shares of 90 to 95 percent agreed and under 5 percent disputed are the sheikh's estimate without a source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:37:00}
- He says all who enter Paradise see God in their degree, and that the people of Illiyyun see His face morning and evening. (needs_source: the report that they see the face morning and evening is told without a chain or a book) He says that the further a person keeps from the world's immersion, the more he gets of God's generosity in the Hereafter, and that God keeps inspiring the dwellers with new wishes so they do not tire. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:39:00}
- Asked whether our form changes in Paradise so that we can bear the sight, he says people are raised in the form of their father Adam at sixty cubits, all of one height but with the faces known in the world, so that they recognise one another, and that God is seen in this form. (needs_source: the report of sixty cubits is told without a chain or a book) He argues, as his own view, that a cubit of 75 cm would make the body the height of two or three storeys, and adds a digression that human strength fell as tools were invented and urges young people to read widely. He criticises the Western custom of a bride changing her family name as his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:42:00}
- On the family in Paradise, he says people live and share in good as in the world, with privacy kept, and cites that God joins the believers' offspring to them without reducing their reward (see [[passage/quran-52-21|al-Tur, verse 21]]), the higher not coming down but the lower being raised, and that grudges are removed from their hearts (see [[passage/quran-15-47|al-Hijr, verse 47]]). Asked whether it is just that one of the foremost be joined by another, he says no one enters Paradise by his deeds but by God's mercy, with an office-service analogy, and that one should rejoice in obedience because God made him fit for it. (needs_source: the statement that no one enters Paradise by his deeds is told without a chain or a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:46:30}
- On the order of Paradise he says it is said to be a hundred degrees and that some scholars hold the number is that of the Qur'an's verses, though he has found no proof he could rely on. (needs_source: the hundred degrees and their equation with the number of verses are told without a source) He relates that the one who recited and acted by the Qur'an is told to read and rise, that the Qur'an pleads for its owner in the grave, and that one who recited and did not act has it as a case against him, and applies the verse on those who give short measure (see [[passage/quran-83-1|al-Mutaffifin, verse 1]], [[passage/quran-83-2|verse 2]], [[passage/quran-83-3|verse 3]]) to banks that take 12 to 20 percent on loans and pay little on deposits (see [[concept/riba|riba]]), which is his reading and a contested application. (needs_source: the interest rates he gives, 2 percent against 15 percent, are illustrative figures without a source) The transcript is garbled here. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:48:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:49:00}
- Above the highest degree is the Throne of the Merciful, and above it, he says, is what God knows. On whether the Prophet saw God on the night of the ascent he says scholars differ, some holding that Musa asked and did not see while Muhammad did not ask and saw, others that he did not see Him, and he does not take the question further. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:50:30}
- He uses an example of an unknown benefactor who sends a monthly allowance and pays one's debts, asking whether one would not long to meet him, and says the Lord deserves this more. He takes the verse on those who sell God's covenant for a small price (see [[passage/quran-3-77|Al Imran, verse 77]]), who will not be spoken to or looked at, and links it to the greeting of peace spoken from a merciful Lord (see [[passage/quran-36-58|Ya-Sin, verse 58]]) which all in Paradise hear; the people of Hell do not hear it. He relates that no one will be left without God addressing him, reminding him of his deeds, with no interpreter or veil between them, and names Adi ibn Hatim (see [[person/adi-ibn-hatim|Adi ibn Hatim]]) as the narrator. (needs_source: this report is told by its sense and with the narrator named only in a garbled form) He uses the example of a person proud of a portrait of an official who once greeted his grandfather, to show what it would mean to be addressed by God. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:57:00}
- On the way to this seeing he says what mostly keeps people from Paradise is the number of sins of the limbs and of the hearts, and that the worst is treating a sin as small and getting used to it (see [[concept/kaba-ir|the major sins]]). He relates that the believer sees his sin as a mountain above him and the hypocrite as a fly he brushes off. (needs_source: the report of the believer's sin as a mountain and the hypocrite's as a fly is told without a chain or a book) He recounts the Prophet's meeting with Adam on the night of the ascent, laughing at his right and weeping at his left, placing it in the sixth heaven in the transcript, a place that does not match the usual account. (needs_source: the place given for Adam in the sixth heaven does not match the usual report and has no source) He lists sins of the eye, the tongue and the ear, among them looking at what God forbade, scorn, backbiting and idle screen time, and says one will be asked about one's time, money and knowledge. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 00:59:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:03:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:06:00}
- He ends with a plan for reaching the seeing: sincere repentance, asking what pleases God and his Prophet, asking oneself whether one has visited the sick, joined kin, prayed at night, helped the orphan and the widow and given each his right in inheritance, and keeping the world as a bridge, for the world has children and so does the Hereafter. He says there is a Paradise in this world, contentment with God and what He decreed, and that whoever does not enter it will not enter the Paradise of the Hereafter. (needs_source: the saying that there is a Paradise in this world is told without a source or an attribution) He closes with a supplication for repentance, an easy crossing of the Sirat, a drink from the Basin and not being veiled from God's face, and the host thanks him and ends with verses of a poem on the world. The transcript then repeats the opening verses. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep40 01:12:00}
## Episode 41: the final episode, the sheikh's thanks, renewing the intention, the scholars and imams who shaped his life, his working method, and his messages to rulers, peoples, scholars, the media, the young and the family
- The host opens the last episode with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]), welcomes the viewers and thanks the management of Sharjah Television and its satellite channel and everyone there for giving the time to this conversational series. He introduces the sheikh (see [[person/omar-abdelkafy|Omar Abdelkafy]]) in a closing portrait as a preacher, asking who taught him and how he gathered what he gave in the programme. He says they spent about 41 hours in Al-Wa'd al-Haqq. (needs_source: the figure of about 41 hours is given by the host without a count) The transcript is garbled in places, so only what is clear is relayed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:03:00}
- The sheikh says it is their duty as preachers to raise the lamp of guidance as far as they can so that travellers to God may follow it, and hopes to be a small soldier in the army of those who defend the faith. He thanks the television management, the director (the name is garbled in the transcript and later appears as Khidr Abd al-Mutallab), the technical team and the viewers who listened, and prays for the host. The host says endings are painful and that the Prophet felt longing for what surrounded him, so that he named his belongings and animals, and that they sat in the studio through Ramadan, then Shawwal, into Dhu al-Qa'da. (needs_source: the Prophet naming his belongings and animals is told by the host without a chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:03:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:05:30}
- On living with the programme, the sheikh says a scholar in any matter of knowledge renews the intention every day so that it is purely for God's face, and that he would pause before leaving his house for the studio to ask himself why he was going; he finds it frightening for a person to mix the work of this world with the work of the next (see [[concept/niyya|intention]], [[concept/ikhlas|sincerity]]). He gives three observations: he feared for his sincerity; what steadied him was that whenever he opened the Qur'an to check a verse he opened on that very verse; and the effect of the programme on viewers in the east and west of the earth opened a door of hope, since the community is still awake and good remains in it. This is his account of his own experience. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:07:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:09:30}
- He names those who shaped him, living and dead. He recalls his first teacher in Upper Egypt in the 1950s, whom he names as Sheikh Abd al-Fattah Abu Zayd, saying the pupils raced to kiss his hand so that his perfume would cling to them and that he learned from him to be fragrant outwardly and inwardly; also the school principal he names as Sheikh Ahmad Makhluf, and the husband of his paternal aunt whose name is garbled (Sheikh Mahmud, the rest unclear), from whom he learned steadfastness on the truth and to take the Book and the Sunna as the reference. He names a language teacher who gave him a love of grammar, rhetoric and literary criticism; the name is garbled in the transcript. (needs_source: the names of his early teachers are garbled in the transcript and were not checked against another source) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:12:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:12:30}
- He says he never forgets his great sheikh Muhammad al-Ghazali (see [[person/muhammad-al-ghazali-modern|Muhammad al-Ghazali]]), who taught him to honour the intellect as a trust from God, and describes his humility and says that when he fell silent the gathering learned from his silence, and that he held firm in speaking the truth whoever differed and turned with the Book and the Sunna wherever they turned. He mentions Sheikh Muhammad Mutawalli al-Sha'rawi (see [[person/muhammad-mutawalli-al-shaarawi|Muhammad Mutawalli al-Sha'rawi]]), saying he felt it was not his tongue that spoke but his heart, that he saw him respect al-Ghazali despite their difference of thought, and that the love of millions of Muslims for him shows his standing with God. This is the sheikh's personal testimony, not a scholarly judgement. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:17:30}
- He mentions another sheikh, still living, whose name appears as Muhammad Nayil, a teacher of language and rhetoric, and describes al-Sha'rawi's humility before him, then laments that the young do not know the standing of scholars though some have begun to respect them again. He cites verses by an Arab poet on the loss of loved ones without naming him. (needs_source: the sheikh's name and the verses cannot be made out in the garbled transcript) He then lists scholar colleagues, naming among them the dean of the Faculty of Da'wa (the name is garbled, appearing as Mahmud Hamaya), Dr Jamal Abd al-Aziz, whom he calls a man of the righteous predecessors who came to live among them, and Dr Abd al-Hamid (his surname appears as Farmawi), professor of exegesis, and praises their humility. (needs_source: the names of these scholars as they stand in the garbled transcript) In places the transcript loops on a single word and is not followed. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:18:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:21:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:22:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:22:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:23:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:23:30}
- He turns to those who shaped him whom he read but never met. He says the Prophet is the model in small matters and great and promises viewers that the next episode will be on his description. He mentions Umar ibn al-Khattab (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]) and says some viewers counted that he mentioned him seven times in one episode, adding that a person who loves another mentions him often, and that the Qur'an coming down in agreement with Umar's view on several occasions is honour enough. (needs_source: the count of seven mentions of Umar in one episode is the viewers' and cannot be verified) He cites a saying of Ibn Mas'ud (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-masud|Abdullah ibn Mas'ud]]) that Umar's Islam was a victory, his emigration a conquest and his rule a mercy. (needs_source: the saying of Ibn Mas'ud about Umar is given without a chain and the wording is garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:24:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:25:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:25:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:26:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:27:00}
- He says he has lived with al-Hasan al-Basri (see [[person/al-hasan-al-basri|al-Hasan al-Basri]]) for more than thirty years, that many asked him for a book on him, and that he hopes young researchers in universities and research centres will write about the imams, who are models for sons and daughters. (needs_source: the length of his companionship with al-Hasan al-Basri is the sheikh's own estimate) He mentions Abu Hamid al-Ghazali (see [[person/al-ghazali-abu-hamid|Abu Hamid al-Ghazali]]) and says he read the Ihya' as a boy (see [[work/ihya-ulum-al-din|Ihya' Ulum al-Din]]) and was raised on its four quarters, on customs, acts of worship, the destructive and the saving; he sets aside its weak hadith since the author was not a hadith scholar, and in his view al-Ghazali was the greatest psychologist in history. He notes those who criticise Abu Hamid and says almost every scholar has drawn on his books. This is the sheikh's view of him, not a scholarly ruling. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:30:00}
- He turns to the four imams, singling out Abu Hanifa (see [[person/abu-hanifa|Abu Hanifa]]): he prizes the use of reason, and says Abu Hanifa was ahead of his age and innovators are always fought. He gives the question of someone who flies over Arafa and the question of the travel distance for shortening prayer if one rides something faster than an animal as examples of a jurisprudence that supposes cases not yet occurring. He mentions his care for his appearance, dress and mount, to show God's blessing on him. (needs_source: Abu Hanifa's care for his appearance as it is told in the transcript has no book) He tells the story of the imam's father, who ate a fruit that fell from an orchard and sought the owner to be forgiven or pay, and the owner required that he marry his daughter, described as blind, deaf and dumb; he accepted, and found she answered his greeting, and her father explained she was blind to falsehood, deaf to obscenity and mute to idle talk. The sheikh says she was Abu Hanifa's mother. (needs_source: the story of Abu Hanifa's father and the orchard fruit is told without a chain or book; it is a popular story and no text is given for it here) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:33:30}
- He mentions Malik (see [[person/malik-ibn-anas|Malik ibn Anas]]), al-Shafi'i (see [[person/al-shafii|al-Shafi'i]]) and Ahmad ibn Hanbal (see [[person/ahmad-ibn-hanbal|Ahmad ibn Hanbal]]) without detail, then al-Bukhari (see [[person/al-bukhari|al-Bukhari]]), whose scholarly precision moves him: he says he spent sixteen years gathering the hadith, refining them and writing only the sound, and that before writing a hadith he would wash, make ablution and pray two rak'as (see [[work/sahih-al-bukhari|Sahih al-Bukhari]]). (needs_source: the sixteen years and the washing and two rak'as before writing a hadith are told here without a chain or book) He says this left him cautious, checking with his own scholars before giving an opinion, and that an opinion released without checking sometimes troubles him at night. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:34:30}
- He says he respects Ibn Taymiyya (see [[person/ibn-taymiyya|Ibn Taymiyya]]) and grants his wide learning and that he deserves the title Shaykh al-Islam, yet his heart inclines to his pupil Ibn al-Qayyim (see [[person/ibn-qayyim-al-jawziyya|Ibn al-Qayyim]]); he explains the sharpness of Ibn Taymiyya's style by the injustice he met from rulers and people. He cites the hadith that God sends at the head of each century one who renews the faith for the community (see [[concept/tajdid|renewal]]), says renewal can be by an individual or a group, and counts Ibn al-Qayyim among them. (needs_source: the hadith of renewal at the head of each century is given here without a chain) He praises his book al-Fawa'id (see [[work/al-fawaid-ibn-qayyim|al-Fawa'id]]), saying each line holds three or four maxims, and al-Jawab al-Kafi (see [[work/al-jawab-al-kafi|al-Jawab al-Kafi]]) as the answer for one who sins, asks forgiveness and returns. He explains the name by saying the father was in charge of al-Jawziyya, which he calls a place. (needs_source: the sheikh's explanation of the al-Jawziyya in Ibn al-Qayyim's name is given without a reference) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:34:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:35:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:35:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:36:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:36:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:37:00}
- He gives a report of a devout man, attributed in the transcript to Sa'id ibn al-Musayyib or to al-Bukhari, the two being confused, who for fifty years never missed the first takbir in congregation or saw the back of a worshipper because he stood in the first row. He says people would offer condolences to one who missed the opening takbir for a day, and that the Companions offered condolences for three days to one who missed the congregational prayer. (needs_source: the subject of the report wavers between two names in the transcript, and the three days of condolence is told without a chain) He says this concerns Muslims, not non-prayers, and hopes Al-Wa'd al-Haqq will stir Muslims to return to God. He then names Tariq ibn Ziyad (see [[person/tariq-ibn-ziyad|Tariq ibn Ziyad]]) and Musa ibn Nusayr (see [[person/musa-ibn-nusayr|Musa ibn Nusayr]]), calling Tariq a man not of Arab origin but a Berber who reached some hundred kilometres south of Paris, and tells a saying put on one of them that had he known people lay beyond the mountain he would have crossed to them. (needs_source: reaching a hundred kilometres from Paris and the saying put on one of the two are told without a source, and whether it is Tariq or Musa is unclear) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:38:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:38:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:39:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:39:30}
- On his references and method, he says he has never read anything without memorising it nor memorised anything and forgotten it, which he counts a grace from God, and that he is not ashamed to telephone a colleague, a scholar or someone younger to confirm a piece of information, since the diver in the sea of knowledge is never ashamed to seek the truth. He mentions books, references and the computer for recalling information and documenting events, and going back to Lisan al-Arab, the dictionary and the people of the language for the meanings of words, and that the host prepared no set questions but sincere intentions and the audience's listening had an effect. This is the sheikh's own description of his work. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:39:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:40:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:40:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:42:30}
- To the objection that the programme leaned on threat and warning with too little encouragement, he replies that the levels of the Fire were the hardest episodes, that he gave headings and left researchers to debate them, and he hopes to issue them in a documented book. He compares his severity to a doctor giving a painful treatment for the patient's comfort, and holds that love, comfort and security grow on the tree of fear. He says he has seen some whose lives Al-Wa'd al-Haqq changed and set on the path, and many who listen but do not apply, which pains him, and he cites the verse on the hatefulness before God of saying what one does not do (see [[passage/quran-61-3|al-Saff, verse 3]]). This is the sheikh's view of his own method, contested by those who criticise the language of intimidation. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:42:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:43:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:43:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:48:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:50:00}
- On his family, he says his wife, sons and daughters bore with him and waived many of their rights over him because of lectures, meetings, reconciling disputes and dividing inheritances, and that he counts them partners in the reward and stays close to them even for a few minutes. He praises the voice of Sheikh Muhammad Siddiq al-Minshawi (see [[person/muhammad-siddiq-al-minshawi|Muhammad Siddiq al-Minshawi]]), heard at the head of the programme, whom he has preferred to other reciters since boyhood, saying he recites from his heart and one understands the Qur'an from his recitation, and he hopes programmes will be introduced by the voices of sincere reciters. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:46:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:47:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:48:00}
- He then gives his short messages. To rulers: may God help them, bring them closer to their peoples and give them righteous counsellors, citing a saying that God restrains by the ruler what He does not restrain by the Qur'an. (needs_source: the saying about the ruler and the Qur'an is given by the sheikh with no attribution or book) To the peoples: whoever wants rulers like Abu Bakr and Umar should be like Uthman and Ali, each beginning with himself. To scholars: give the call all your time and be like a tree that is pelted with stones and gives fruit. To preachers: do not be deceived by crowds or praise. To the media: look to what pleases God and His Messenger, and let the word, the look and the scene be true. To the young: do not waste your time, and follow Abd Allah ibn Amr, Abd Allah ibn Umar, Abd Allah ibn al-Zubayr and Muhammad ibn al-Qasim, conqueror of Sind (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-amr-ibn-al-as|Abd Allah ibn Amr]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-umar|Abd Allah ibn Umar]], [[person/abdullah-ibn-al-zubayr|Abd Allah ibn al-Zubayr]], [[person/muhammad-ibn-al-qasim|Muhammad ibn al-Qasim]]), and not those he calls the dregs who came in our time in farces that please neither God nor His Messenger. This is the sheikh's opinion and advice. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:50:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:51:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:53:00}
- To girls he says fear God, wear the hijab (see [[concept/hijab|hijab]]), read the Qur'an, obey your parents and be like Nusayba bint Ka'b (see [[person/nusayba-bint-kab|Nusayba bint Ka'b]]) and Asma bint Abi Bakr (see [[person/asma-bint-abi-bakr|Asma bint Abi Bakr]]). To wives: be patient with a husband who treats you badly and strengthen his good side. To husbands: be gentle and affectionate; whoever brings joy to his wife is, in his words, protected by God from the punishment of the Fire. (needs_source: the saying that bringing joy to a wife protects from the Fire is given by the sheikh with no chain or book) He thanks his wife for raising their children and prays that his sons and daughters grow well and keep the Book of God. He cites Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz (see [[person/umar-ibn-abd-al-aziz|Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz]]) as saying his soul longed for rule, then wealth, then the caliphate, and now longs for Paradise, and says his own work does not reach that but he hopes for what is with God and a good end. (needs_source: the saying of Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz on his longing is given by the sheikh with no chain or book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:55:30}
- The sheikh thanks the host, saying his questions drew out what had to come out, and hopes he will be a model for young presenters. The host closes by announcing that the next episode, on Monday, is on the description of the Prophet, that two further episodes are for calls from the public, and that the call numbers are shown on screen. He ends with verses that the transcript attributes to Abu Nuwas (see [[person/abu-nuwas|Abu Nuwas]]), in which a servant admitting his sins takes refuge in God's pardon. (needs_source: the verses are attributed to Abu Nuwas by the host, and the transcript spells the name in a garbled form) The transcript then repeats the opening verses and loops on a single phrase. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:55:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:56:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:56:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:57:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:57:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:58:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:59:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep41 00:59:30}
## Episode 42: a call-in episode after the pilgrimage: the awaited Mahdi, doubt about the acceptance of deeds, the deaths at Mina, the signs that repentance is accepted, God's attributes of beauty and majesty, trial, and balancing this world and the next
- The episode opens with the verses on the opening of Gog and Magog (see [[passage/quran-21-96|al-Anbiya, verse 96]], [[passage/quran-21-97|verse 97]]) and a poem of questions to creation, asking who made the physician fall ill, who guides the blind, who fed the bee and who split the date stone, and ending that God sees the one who does not see Him; the poet is not named. The host greets the viewers and welcomes the sheikh (see [[person/omar-abdelkafy|Omar Abdelkafy]]), who has come straight from the pilgrimage, saying he prayed the Friday prayer in the Sacred Mosque and travelled from Mecca to Jeddah, then to Al-Ain, Dubai and Sharjah, so the recording began a quarter of an hour late. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:00:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:01:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:02:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:02:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:03:00}
- The host explains that this episode takes the viewers' and listeners' calls by telephone, on television and on the satellite and radio channels of the broadcaster, and that the audience in the studio has stayed silent for a long time, which he hopes is reflection. He introduces a stand-in director for the series while the usual director is away; the name appears in the transcript as Mustafa Zazu and is not certain. (needs_source: the stand-in director's name is garbled in the transcript) Advertisements for the sponsor, a developer and a soft-drink company, and the breaks are not summarised. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:04:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:04:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:05:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:06:00}
- A caller from Cairo says the sheikh once avoided a question on the awaited Mahdi and asks him to clarify, since such matters can weaken some people's faith. The sheikh answers that the minor and major signs of the Hour as he has presented them come either from the Qur'an or from al-Bukhari and Muslim, that he found nothing in the two collections supporting the awaited Mahdi (see [[concept/al-mahdi|the Mahdi]]), and that he did not deny the matter but did not find his heart at rest on it.
