He says God sends at the head of each century one who renews the faith (see [[concept/tajdid|renewal]]), which may be one man or a group, and that Muslims in their weakness want someone to carry the burden for them, although all are responsible for supporting the faith. (needs_source: the hadith of renewal at the head of each century is given without a chain) This is the sheikh's own position on the Mahdi, which is contested among scholars; he says he spoke only of what he held with certainty. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:14:30}
- The same caller says she is committed and draws near to God, yet doubts whether her deeds are accepted. The sheikh says the believer should hope and be certain that God has accepted what He made easy for him, since the Generous pardons when He reckons. (needs_source: the exchange in which an Arab asks who will reckon him and is told that God will, and the Arab answers that the Generous pardons when He reckons, is given without a chain) He cites the saying of scholars not to rejoice that obedience came from you but that God made you fit for it. (needs_source: the saying of the scholars on rejoicing in obedience is given without a name or book) He adds that the Generous accepts the little and magnifies it, forgives much, changes evil deeds into good (see [[passage/quran-25-70|al-Furqan, verse 70]]) and loves those who remember God and seek forgiveness after an error (see [[passage/quran-3-135|Al Imran, verse 135]]). {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:14:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:15:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:15:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:16:00}
- On the crush at Mina the day before, the sheikh prays for the dead and speaks of easing the rites for the crowds. He says the Prophet never chose between two matters without taking the easier, that he told his own group and the callers who reached him that they might throw the pebbles before noon and then leave the place for others, and that he holds that scholars should make this concession for the millions (see [[place/mina|Mina]], [[concept/ramy-fiqh|stoning]], [[concept/hajj-rulings|the rulings of the pilgrimage]]). He offers comfort to the families: the dead are raised in the state of pilgrim sanctity, and he recalls a man in the Farewell Pilgrimage (see [[event/farewell-pilgrimage|the Farewell Pilgrimage]]) thrown by his mount, of whom the Prophet said to wash him, not perfume him, and that he is raised saying the pilgrim's call. (needs_source: the report of the man killed by his mount in the Farewell Pilgrimage is told without a chain or book) He calls those who died after completing the rites and heading to stone fortunate. This is the sheikh's view on a contested question of rulings (founder-held: opinion rulings). The year of the crush is not stated. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:16:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:16:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:17:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:17:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:18:30}
- He relates that a woman came to the Prophet after her son died in a battle and asked whether he was in Paradise so she could be patient, and was told that her son was in the highest Paradise, and she said she would be patient. (needs_source: the report is told without a chain and the name of the woman and of her son are garbled in the transcript) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:18:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:18:30}
- Several callers thank the sheikh and say that people close to them changed after hearing the programme, and a caller living in Canada regrets not meeting him on his visit there and asks him to continue with other programmes. Another asks that he give a commentary on the Qur'an, and the sheikh answers that he will try. These are the viewers' own reports and are not checked. Some ask about contact numbers, recordings of the series and posting episodes on the programme's website, which the host passes to the production team. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:19:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:19:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:20:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:20:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:21:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:44:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:44:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:45:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:45:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:58:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:58:30}
- A caller asks what the destruction of the Kaaba and the raising of the Qur'an to heaven is, as a sign of the Hour. The sheikh says the report is mentioned but the hadiths on it did not reach the degree of soundness, so he stopped at it and counts them not strong. He says the series gave headings, not every angle, and thanks those who add to or correct it. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:23:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:24:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:34:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:34:30}
- Asked how a person can rise in character, in forgiving one who wrongs him and joining one who cuts him off, the sheikh says patience comes by training oneself, that it goes against the self's whim, and he gives the example of visiting a relative who has cut him off: if he is driven away, he has not driven the relative from God's mercy. (needs_source: the saying that knowledge comes by learning and patience by practising patience is garbled and is given without a source) He says a scholar was asked why he tired himself and answered that it was for its rest, and that Ibn Abbas wanted to be told to go back at a door so as to apply the verse (see [[person/abdullah-ibn-abbas|Abdullah ibn Abbas]], [[passage/quran-24-28|al-Nur, verse 28]]). (needs_source: the scholar's answer and the report of Ibn Abbas are told without a chain and the transcript is garbled) He says the self is a liar and, if left alone, is lost, and that the Prophet is the measure: one asks in small and great matters what he would have done, and the answer helps even those without learning. He adds that people struggle to keep going and proposes using the new Hijri year to begin a plan, with new conduct. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:26:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:27:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:27:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:28:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:28:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:29:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:29:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:30:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:30:30}
- To a question on why the Prophet's body is to be left for an hour after death, he says this is a reported matter known to God and that the Companions did not ask why. He gives the example of Aisha (see [[person/aisha|Aisha]]), who did not ask what her place in Paradise would be, and says women should ask what to do to enter Paradise, not measure the next world by this one. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:30:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:31:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:31:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:32:00}
- He says women have been wronged by some men, husbands, fathers and brothers, for instance over inheritance, and that this wrong comes from customs and not from the faith, and he tells the patient believing woman that she will enter Paradise and that every one of the seventy-two wide-eyed houris will serve her as a handmaid (see [[concept/hur-al-ayn|the houris]]), singing that they will never perish, never be displeased and never depart. (needs_source: the number of seventy-two houris as handmaidens of the believing woman and the song of the houris are given without a chain or book) This is his statement and is contested. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:32:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:32:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:33:00}
- Asked how a believer can judge himself on the lists of the people of Paradise and of the Fire, which he finds in himself, the sheikh says that one who finds all the traits of the people of Paradise and none of the others is deluded, while the devout person finds the traits of the Fire in himself and burns with fear and finds those of Paradise and hopes, and that between fear and hope love leads him on (see [[concept/khawf|fear]]). This is his answer. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:33:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:33:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:34:00}
- Another caller asks how to know that repentance has been accepted. The sheikh lists signs he says he gave before: the doors of obedience open even if one does not walk towards them, and the doors of sin close; a small sin seems great and one blames oneself for the small and the great; one counts a little blessing as large and one's own worship as small; and one fears for the great sin. He adds that God forgives a servant who regrets his sin, and that one feels in repenting that God has decreed it (see [[concept/tawba|repentance]]). (needs_source: the signs of accepted repentance are given by the sheikh as his earlier teaching without a book) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:37:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:37:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:41:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:41:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:42:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:42:30}
- A caller asks about the Names and Attributes of beauty and of majesty. The sheikh says God's attributes are of two kinds (see [[concept/asma-allah-al-husna|the beautiful names]]): attributes of beauty, such as the Forgiving, the Loving, the Merciful and the Provider (see [[concept/asma-al-ghafur|al-Ghafur]], [[concept/asma-al-wadud|al-Wadud]], [[concept/asma-al-rahman|al-Rahman]], [[concept/asma-al-rahim|al-Rahim]], [[concept/asma-al-razzaq|al-Razzaq]]), by which a person should adorn himself and draw close in love; and attributes of majesty, such as the Subduer, the Compeller, the Exalter and the Humbler (see [[concept/asma-al-qahhar|al-Qahhar]], [[concept/asma-al-jabbar|al-Jabbar]], [[concept/asma-al-muizz|al-Mu'izz]], [[concept/asma-al-mudhill|al-Mudhill]]), which a person should fear and should not take on, since God uses them to put down the arrogant and the tyrants. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:46:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:47:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:52:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:52:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:53:00}
- To a caller who asks about the jihad of women, the sheikh recalls that when the Prophet saw the women of Medina weeping for their dead, he said that Hamza had none to weep for him (see [[person/hamza|Hamza ibn Abd al-Muttalib]]). (needs_source: the report of the Prophet's words on Hamza's having no weepers is told without a chain or book) He says a woman's jihad is in bringing up children like Hamza on love of Islam and of the Book and in her patience, and that the world looks to Muslims more than they look to it; he says he told Muslims in Canada, the week before the pilgrimage, that people there need them more than they need the people. This is his view. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:49:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:49:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:50:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:53:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:53:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:54:00}
- On a question of the signs of the Hour about a war between the Romans and the Muslims on horses, the sheikh says the present civilisation may perish through its people's wrongdoing or may last with other weapons for war, so one should not dwell on it, and he recalls the owner of the garden who thought it would never perish (see [[passage/quran-18-35|al-Kahf, verse 35]], [[passage/quran-18-42|verse 42]]), the earth taking its adornment (see [[passage/quran-10-24|Yunus, verse 24]]) and the blessings promised to a believing town (see [[passage/quran-7-96|al-A'raf, verse 96]]). He says he is glad when viewers open books and read, which was one aim of the research. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:54:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:54:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:55:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:55:30}
- After the break the sheikh answers a viewer on trial (see [[concept/ibtila|trial]]), where the caller had heard him say that a person who passes two or three days without trial should check his reckoning with God. He says faith is word and deed and that mere words are a claim; he cites the verse on being left to say they believe without being tried (see [[passage/quran-29-2|al-Ankabut, verse 2]]); that the prophets left knowledge and trial, so whoever takes knowledge without trial turns away, since true knowledge is fear and not abundance of narration. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:00:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:01:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:05:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:06:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:06:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:07:00}
- He gives the saying that a hired wailer is not like the bereaved mother, to describe the preacher who speaks as a job, against the one who carries the burden and whose heart burns. (needs_source: the exchange between a son and his father on why people weep at the father's words, as told here, has garbled names and no chain) He recalls Ata ibn Abi Rabah (see [[person/ata-ibn-abi-rabah|Ata ibn Abi Rabah]]) saying he would be sure the pilgrims' pilgrimage was accepted had Ata not been among them, (needs_source: the words of Ata are garbled in the transcript and given without a chain) and the account of Umar (see [[person/umar-ibn-al-khattab|Umar ibn al-Khattab]]) who missed the dawn prayer, when Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf led the people (see [[person/abd-al-rahman-ibn-awf|Abd al-Rahman ibn Awf]]), and was found fainted by a wall after hearing a woman reciting the verse that the punishment of the Lord will occur (see [[passage/quran-52-7|al-Tur, verse 7]]). (needs_source: the story of Umar's fainting at the verse of al-Tur is told without a chain and the details are garbled) {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:07:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:08:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:08:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:09:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:09:30}
- He concludes that trial is tied to worship and uprightness, that a servant who is not tried may be insignificant to God, and that the afflicted should rejoice, since God may thereby change his evil deeds into good and raise his rank. This is his view; others stress patience and avoid reading hardship as a sign of status. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:09:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:10:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:10:30}
- To a caller asking how a Muslim balances this world and the next in his heart, he says many do not grasp that Islam does not divide deeds into worldly and otherworldly: eating, drinking, sleeping and rest, done with the intention of pleasing God, become deeds of the next world (see [[concept/niyya|intention]], [[concept/dunya|this world]]), unlike, he says, what is understood in the West. This is his own account. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:10:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:11:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:11:30}
- The host reads out the broadcasting times, Monday and Friday in the evening with repeats on Sunday and Wednesday at midday, UAE time. A viewer asks what to do to enter Paradise; the host answers that one listens to Al-Wa'd al-Haqq and applies what was said. The sheikh gives his own testimony that parents in Canada told him they open the broadcaster's channel for their children without fear. The host announces that the next Monday and Friday episodes will take the calls of viewers and listeners only, for two hours, with no input from the studio. {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 00:51:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:11:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:12:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:13:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:13:30} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:14:00} {cite:assabile-omar-abdelkafy ep42 01:14:30}
